# Deadstab: project handoff

Deadstab is a top-down arena shooter that runs in the browser on desktop and phones. Each map has its own setting; Deadwater Outpost is a western frontier town. This file is the single source of truth for anyone (person or AI) picking the project up. **Keep it current: any change that adds or changes a system updates this file in the same change.**

## Standing rules from the owner

These come first and are never removed.

- **Never push or pull the repo unless the owner says so.** No commits either unless asked.
- **Omen integration notes (owner, 2026-09-26):** keep [CLAUDE_HANDOFF_OMEN.md](CLAUDE_HANDOFF_OMEN.md) current with every change in this worktree: final behavior and balance, every new/shared file touched, verification and merge considerations. It is the running handoff for combining this work with Claude's map changes. Update it before handing a change back; do not overwrite the other checkout's shared files or publish/send notes without the owner's instruction.
- **Sightline is implemented in this weapon worktree:** the owner approved the amber loaded-round cue, long diagonal back sling and Sidekick holster. [SIGHTLINE_DESIGN.md](SIGHTLINE_DESIGN.md) is its final behavior reference; `CLAUDE_HANDOFF_OMEN.md` tracks all shared-file edits for combining with map work. No commit or merge is authorized.
- **Nothing in the game mentions the developer tools until they are unlocked** (pause, Shift+P, code). Keep it that way: no hints, toasts, labels or key reactions before the unlock. Practice overrides never go online.
- Do not control the user's visible game tab for testing. Use a separate hidden browser tab and leave their session alone.
- New weapons or damage effects must carry an explicit damageType through lethal hits and define their death reaction in src/effects/death-reactions.js. Preserve weapon drops for every death. Electricity leaves a charred fallen body; fire leaves a fallen skeleton (sometimes with snapped or missing limbs and the brains spilled beside a cracked skull, see gore.js); bullets leave an intact body with a head wound; ordinary explosions use directional gore scatter. Sheath's blade leaves a fallen body with one or two arms cut off and thrown (`blade`, `bladeDraw`). Add reaction and cleanup coverage for new effects.
- **Every Ballast kill is the headless kneeling death** with directional blood (owner's rule, replacing the old 85%-in-0.45 s burst requirement; `recordBallastDamage` still records the hits).
- **Performance work targets the Performance and Potato presets.** Playtesting happens on phones and an Intel MacBook, not on the owner's high-end PC (RTX 5090), which is not representative.
- **"Optimize" means finding headroom and spending it on looks, never removing things.** Measure first. Merge, batch, cache or build lazily, and use what that saves to add detail that is cheap to draw.
- Explain decisions in plain language: how a system works, not just that it works.

## Design (the owner's intent; keep new work consistent with it)

### Look
- **Low-poly, flat-shaded primitives** (boxes, cylinders, icosahedra), a western frontier town in muted warm desert colours, seen from a **top-down camera** (slightly tilted from the south). No lettering anywhere in the world. Age shows through missing planks, tilts, tone shifts and seeded wear, never text or decals of words.
- **Readable from above:** anything the player must notice goes where the camera sees it: upward faces, the hat brim, the floor. Roofs fade right out when you are inside; interiors are shrouded from outside.
- **Gore is stylized, not realistic:** solid low-poly shapes in blood reds, bone and char, flat-shaded like the world; no anatomical textures. It should be obvious what it is (brains are pink folded lobes, bones are ivory, char is cracked with glowing seams), and varied (not every skeleton is whole).
- **Blood is world history:** it stays until a map reset (one stain and one body per player, capped drops), and it moves with the world (a crate's blood goes when it breaks; players who walk through it carry it and leave prints).
- **Graphics presets:** Extreme is Quality plus, never minus. "Optimize" means find headroom and spend it on looks; test targets are phones and an Intel MacBook.

### Interface
- **Panels:** dark grey (about #232827) with a pink accent (`--ui-accent`, #e8afb9); solo pause and settings cards are cream (#f0e4c9). Headings are lowercase ("paused", "lobby", "choose weapon"). The main action is pink (GO, START ROUND highlight).
- **Buttons:** big stretched display lettering, fitted before the first paint (plain text never flashes), with the same letter width across buttons shown together; a menu's buttons alternate left and right alignment. Anything destructive takes two presses ("PRESS AGAIN TO RESET"). Nothing in solo mentions multiplayer, and nothing mentions developer tools before they are unlocked.
- **Copy:** tutorial and hint text is lowercase, plain, minimal punctuation, keys as keycaps (`[SPACE]`, `[E]`). Controls: Space/LMB shoot, E the weapon's other action, Q dodge (Left Ctrl until v147), R reload, X / C weapon abilities, Shift/RMB aim in, M map, N sound, Esc pause, Tab scoreboard; menus use E to confirm and Q to go back.
- **HUD:** top right three round icon buttons (icons centred, 38 px) in the button ink: map (folded map, M), sound (speaker, N; off turns it pink with a bar struck across) and menu (three bars, Esc); keys live in their titles. Top left the title, then "map · mode" on one line, then the room badge, in one block so nothing overlaps; health top centre; weapon panel bottom centre. Nothing floats over players' heads (names live in the lobby, scoreboard, kill feed and death screen). Overlays that are glanced at during play (the Tab scoreboard) do not dim the game and are slightly see-through, with text shadows for contrast.
- **Kill feed:** your name blue, others pink; "X killed Y", "**X one shot Y**" (from full health to dead in one hit), "Y died". The death screen says "killed by X" / "one shot by X".
- **Directional damage (2026-09-26, preview r11):** `ui/damage-indicator.js` shares the pink gunfire arc's canvas drawing, with a red arc 24 px further inward and a shorter angular span. The owner requested more presence: its stroke is now 7.5 px (pink 12 px), minimum strength .75, hold 160 ms and fade 2/s, lasting about 0.54–0.66 s. It appears immediately; simultaneous bearings remain independent. Fire/other damage without a source draws eight separate red arcs with 38% angular gaps, pulsing together every 0.72 s from quarter to full strength. Continuous burn ticks refresh its life without restarting the pulse; its separate state cannot fill the gaps or sustain a stale hit bearing. It works with muted sound and clears on pause, death and respawn. `playerDamage` carries optional normalized `sourceDX/sourceDZ` toward the hit origin (opposite impact force); bot/arena curses and streams fall back to the caster's position. Projection keeps a level plane on hills; the center stays empty and both canvases ignore pointer input. The additive fields travel through existing event packing. Tests cover bearing, pulse cadence during continuous burning, gaps, overlapping directional hits, cleanup and network/bot delivery.
- **Weapon pick:** rounded, slightly see-through squares, five to a row, only picture and name, lifting on hover or pick; a timer bar on top (not in solo); pink GO.
- **Moving damage totals (2026-09-26, preview r12):** outgoing damage numbers catch up to the target's latest hit position every 0.3 s (`OUTGOING_ANCHOR_BEAT`, `ui/outgoing-feedback.js`). The same DOM node, cumulative total, subtotal/addition, birth time and expiry remain intact; rapid pellets/stream ticks update a pending location rather than moving the number every tick. This shared path covers Static, Nominal, Ballast and Omen, practice targets, bots and online opponents. After damage stops, it stays at the last hit location; visibility uses the updated anchor. Your own incoming damage number already follows the rendered player. Tests cover all weapons, repeated relocation, separate targets, same-volley grouping and clearing.

### Title screen blood (the owner's brief, all of it in one place)
The title (`title-screen.js`, the main menu's home page) is built to this; every point is something the owner asked for:
- **Source:** it comes from inside the hole of the **d** in "deadstab" (first asked for the a; changed to the d).
- **On the letters:** across the bottom of **every** letter, each letter bleeding its own way (higher on some letters and parts, lower on others, not one smooth line across the word; wet edges like the first sample; no splatters on the letters; where it joins the drips it is seamless), reaching about **a third of the way up** them, **uneven** from place to place (higher here, lower there), messy.
- **Gravity:** it hangs off the word as if pulled to the bottom of the screen; the parts that droop **droop a lot** (long drips), but it drips from **only a few places**, not everywhere.
- **Buttons are solid:** blood oozes onto the buttons below, gathers on their tops, oozes and drips off their edges, and runs down onto the next button; some buttons get **splatter and stains** as it lands.
- **Liquid, but 2D:** it should read as liquid, yet stay **flat and 2D**: no shiny/3D light effect, no highlights or bevels. **Cartoony like the game's gameplay design, but also somewhat real and messy.**
- **Colour:** close to the **in-game blood** (gore.js `#8c1c2a` family): a realistic red that fits the mood, **not bright red but not too dark either** (the first, near-black `#4b0c12` was too dark).
- **Animation, not simulation:** it plays as the screen opens, then stays in that settled state and **keeps playing as a repeating animation** (drips keep forming and falling).
- **Layout:** don't change the size of the word "deadstab"; the **game version sits above** the word. Buttons PLAY / GAMEMODES / SETTINGS / EXIT in the menu style; they animate and click but do nothing, EXIT goes back to the dev menu.
- **Still to come:** the loading screen (not started; the owner said title first).

- Intro: nothing shows ahead of the spreading pour. The underside band and the crevice fills are clipped to `#title-reach` (a strip round each source, widening at the pour's speed, `reachAt`), drip sites grow in as the blood reaches them, and the smear lies flat below a letter until it arrives there (no red hairline under it).

### Play
- **Omen Covenant UI and tendrils (2026-09-26):** Covenant's full name fits inside its desktop and touch HUD dials (smaller word type only; countdown digits retain their size). Its ready/active dial, cursor timer and touch button use purple with a matching glow; its last-second rupture cue still pulses coral/ivory. All caster/victim sigils use `sigilTendrilPoint` with different reaching lengths, independent crossing waves and speeds, anchored inner roots and inward-moving energy. Caster streams still turn purple to red. Points and draw batches are reused; quality presets spend more segments without new materials. Tests cover independent motion, anchored roots, palette, inward flow and cleanup; browser checks cover label bounds, desktop/touch colours and the timing cue.
- **Omen curse presentation (2026-09-26):** the caster's E prime is the purple six-point intake seal with a red inner circle. The victim's red five-point sigil is 25% wider (`CURSE_SCALE` in `omen-view.js`), including its last-second ring, contraction and dissolving strokes; the blast damage radius is unchanged. E and X marks play a deep hollow click on landing and every `OMEN.curseBeat` (1 s), independently of the half-second damage ticks. `omenCurseBeat` comes from the caster's simulation at the target's current position, is shared online through `host-session.js` / `main.js`, and uses the weapons mixer and normal hearing distance. No repeating voice/timer survives expiry, rupture or death; ordinary curse-hit audio stays quiet so it cannot double the rhythm. Tests cover cadence, position, network forwarding, stopping, palette/size and muted/paused playback.
- **Solo target health override:** unlocked Developer tools > World > Target health (solo), in both Settings and the floating window, offers Normal / 100 / 250 / 500 / 1,000 HP for practice boards and hay dummies (including moving boards). Changing the value refills standing targets and clears their bullet damage; downed targets keep their respawn timer and return at the chosen health. Map reset/restart retains the choice. Normal, resetting dev options or locking the tools restores each map-authored default (including the tutorial's lighter targets). `Simulation.practiceTargetHealth` / `syncTargetHealth` apply it only to practice targets; robot/player proxies are excluded. `dev-wiring.js` rejects it online, and online/prediction sims ignore it. `tests/health.test.js` covers health, respawn, reset, tutorial defaults and proxy/online isolation; `tests/dev-window.test.js` covers both controls.
- **Practice targets:** every board target 250 health, every hay dummy 300 (`RULES.targetHealth` / `dummyHealth`; Deadwater's range-b no longer has its own 500). The tutorial keeps lighter ones (`TUTORIAL_TARGET_HEALTH`: 100 / 75) so lessons stay short. They **show damage as they break**, Balanced and up, more stages and pieces on Quality and most on Extreme (see Rendering).
- **Players:** 500 health, distinct weapons (Static electric orbs, Nominal rifle + grenade, Ballast double-barrel shotgun with Scatter), dodge stamina per weapon. Every damage source carries a `damageType` and every death has a reaction (see Deaths); **every Ballast kill is headless**.
- **Targets break by what hit them:** bullets leave holes, everything else only cracks and breaks them (target-damage.js); targets face varied directions, never away from the camera.
- **Blood follows force:** shots on players bleed (never Static's stream or single orbs), bullets build up the longer you keep hitting, blasts bleed most. Walking through blood soaks you; **you respawn clean** (your body keeps the blood).
- **Modes:** multiplayer rounds (FFA, practice, 1V1, 2V2, 2V2V2, 3V3, 4V4), up to 8 players, host-run lobby with host-only settings; FFA respawn wait 6 s by default, weapons change only after dying; practice changes weapon any time. A host can play alone, even without the matchmaking server. **Every gamemode outside Multiplayer is singleplayer against AI (bots) until at least the beta.**
- **Sound carries a set distance** (`HEARING` in audio.js, `hearingLevel(d)`): others' sounds are full volume within 11 m (about half the screen), then fade exponentially: 5% at about 3x that distance, silent past ~1.2%. Each event gets its own distance gain. Your own sounds are always full.
- **Syphon (FFA, host setting `syphon`, on by default):** a kill gives the killer back half the health they were missing (`SYPHON_SHARE` in arena.js), with a green "+N" and a sound.
- **Aim without a mouse:** arrows and swipes lock onto targets; against players the lock chases (a runner pulls ahead) and holding an arrow leads them. A mouse is never assisted.

## Sightline (weapon worktree, 2026-09-26)

**Revision 18:** Scoped reach is 1.46× normal camera height, about 10% farther than revision 17; the 45-degree cone and slow turn are unchanged. E setup is 1.33 s (previously 2 s), with a swift 0.28 s draw followed by crouch/bipod deployment; E cancels and firing waits for setup. The stand/return remains 0.42 s. Sightline arms have fixed upper/forearm lengths and a compact receiver grip; the rifle clears torso/hat through draw, return and both reloads. Scope-disabled interiors/crops also disable the laser and precise aim, using the unscoped bloom cone. Cursor unprojection uses the 1.28 m sniper firing height, muzzle compensation uses its own 0.40 m lateral/1.73 m forward offset, the held barrel yaw is straight, and the beam attaches to the animated muzzle. Sightline cursor smoothing uses the responsive hip profile while scoped; the slow camera/cone remains independent. Full merge inventory and verification: `CLAUDE_HANDOFF_OMEN.md`.

**Revision 17 supersedes older scope/laser values below:** 45-degree view, 1.325× camera height (half prior reach), cone/camera turn limit 0.35 rad/s with 1.1 rad/s² acceleration; laser and shot aim remain responsive. Zoom is unavailable in interiors and standing/burning crops. Sightline and Sidekick share two dashes; refill is 5% shorter (1.52 s/charge, unchanged 0.6 s delay). X can replace a normal Sightline reload with a fresh full Breach load when ready. `src/weapons/sightline-flight.js` shares elevated straight trajectories, cursor endpoints and height-aware cover between bullet and laser: low rocks can be cleared, breakables pierced, high bridge crossings stay above deck, lower shots remain underneath; true terrain/tall-cover intersections stop the shot. Beams draw after transparent bridges with depth testing, have continuous cores instead of terrain bends, and preserve Breach highlights. New shoulder routing/sling offset/hand interpolation clear torso and hat throughout draw/return/reload. Sightline/Sidekick bloom brackets and red fill share one firing plane so bridge-edge height samples cannot twist the quadrilateral. The ordinary terrain-flight implementation used by other weapons is unchanged. See the current design and handoff for verification.

Fifth weapon, sniper plus semi-automatic Sidekick. Revision 16 names the sniper Sightline in both ammo rows, ready/empty/reload status, accessibility text, controls and tutorial; internal rifle fields remain unchanged. The red laser appears only while aiming the crouched Sightline; standing Sidekick aiming uses its bloom cone, including remote players. Full current behavior: [SIGHTLINE_DESIGN.md](SIGHTLINE_DESIGN.md). The pure state machine and physical rounds are in src/weapons/sightline.js; tuning is SIGHTLINE in config/gameplay.js. Revision 14 names the sidearm Sidekick throughout the HUD, controls and tutorial and reduces each shot from 35 to 27 damage through the shared player/bot/host tuning. Internal protocol fields and damage-type identifiers stay compatible. Revision 15 changes Sidekick to a 25 ± 2 integer damage roll (23–27), once per shot and preserved through impact. Standing: 10-round, 23–27-damage Sidekick, .28 s click cap, 2 s reload. E alone toggles stationary crouched rifle stance; ordinary rifle reload is 4.2 s and cancels if stood up. Rifle damage is an integer 493–505, a .16 s direction commitment then a 114 m/s projectile; all breakable props are pierced, solid cover/terrain/players stop it. Aimed range is 10% past maximum scoped view whether or not scope was used. Hip shots use cursor distance with wide bloom. Rifle sounds carry 1.5x farther.

Revision 15: E starts a cancelable 2 s setup that blocks rifle fire; standing/drawing Sidekick takes 0.42 s visually. R or either reload exits scope and requires releasing/repressing aim (v0.992a: no longer; a reload takes the aim down only while it runs, see Touch > Sightline's stance). RMB/Left Shift gives an 80-degree scope at 2.65x camera height, with the player near the opposite screen edge (direction/aspect-based lead at 76% of rear reach). Elevation adds 0.6% per metre, capped at 6%, to camera and projectile reach; turn limit 0.55 rad/s, acceleration 1.8 rad/s². Scenery outside is gray through prewarmed world-space shading in src/render/scope-shading.js; no CSS backdrop/mask and no extra render pass. Fog excludes the added vertical camera distance. remote players are hidden by renderer visibility and targets/projectile/effect/bird/crow materials share scope uniforms in interior-visibility.js. Hidden actors continue simulating. New batches are prewarmed; Sightline's bounded instanced streak batch carries laser, rounds and fading wind wakes. Do not gray the HUD or freeze hidden simulation.

X loads the rifle for 4.2 s without auto-crouching. Standing X holsters the Sidekick and permits movement during rifle loading, then returns to the Sidekick. E can change stance during loading. A completed special stays armed while slung. Crouched LMB/second X fires; 50 s cooldown from commitment. Direct damage 793–805 combined once; blast 300 center / 250 at 1.3 m / 40 at 4.5 m, cover-aware and with shooter immunity. A small muzzle flash/smoke burst can hurt close opponents for up to 80 damage within 1.5 m, cover-aware; the landing blast keeps the grenade effect. Amber vents/chamber, locking plates and pale vapor remain on the slung rifle until firing. Both weapons appear in the selection photo, using the existing studio. Sidekick sits in a leather holster when stowed; the rifle is diagonally slung clear of the hat, using ZYX rotation. Continuous draw/holster, crouch/stand, bolt/shell (plain or amber), Sidekick magazine and hand poses accompany the actions. Regular laser segments follow terrain and remain readable when zoomed; a loaded Breach gets a wider bright-red core and moving amber energy. src/ui/sightline-ammo.js keeps both magazines visible in animated large-active/small-stowed rows. Rifle ammo is a large flat horizontal sniper-round silhouette with left-to-right reload fill and amber X coloring. Exact cached frustum math replaces scope-edge searches; laser terrain/cover paths are reused until pose, stance, aspect, ground or collider array/length changes. The laser/wind batch explicitly restores visibility after warm-up and hides when empty.

Protocol 13 carries new inputs, loadout, remote pose and rounds; client prediction includes crouch. Bots complete rifle reloads and react to a visible aimed rifle with evasion and solid cover, never tracking a hidden laser. Seven tutorial lessons and matching touch/keyboard controls use existing systems. Explicit sightlineShot/sightlinePistol/sightlineBlast damage types select head-wound bodies or explosion scatter; all preserve the dropped loadout and cleanup. Tests: tests/sightline.test.js. Revision 15 full suite: 919 passing; hidden desktop/touch browser checks passed on Potato, Performance and Extreme (software rendering; not device FPS claims).

## Engine, language, tooling

| | |
|---|---|
| Engine | **No game engine.** The rendering library is **three.js 0.180**; the game loop, physics, input, UI and audio are all hand-written. |
| Language | JavaScript (ES modules, no TypeScript). HTML and CSS for every menu and HUD. |
| Build | **Vite 7** |
| Tests | Node's built-in runner, `node --test tests/*.test.js` (about 485 tests) |
| Package manager | pnpm on the owner's PC and in CI; npm works too |
| Deploy | `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` builds and publishes to GitHub Pages on every push to `main` |
| Networking | **The game server** (`server/`: Node, **ws 8.18.3** WebSockets) for JOIN and HOST; **PeerJS 1.5.5** (WebRTC, P2P) behind the developer tools. Both behind one transport interface; see Multiplayer |
| Audio | Synthesized in the browser (Web Audio). There are no sound files. |

### Run and build

```
pnpm install          # or npm install
pnpm dev              # dev server on http://127.0.0.1:5173
pnpm test             # all unit tests
pnpm build            # production build into dist/
pnpm server           # the game server on 127.0.0.1:8787 (server/; see Multiplayer > The game server)
```

Dev-only URL shortcuts:
- `?play=1&weapon=static|rifle|shotgun&map=deadwater|tutorial|dry-creek|hill-test` starts straight into a match.
- `&course=basics` picks the basics tutorial.
- `&start=<buildingId|farm|propType>` spawns somewhere specific.
- `&server=ws://127.0.0.1:8787` plays online on a local game server (`pnpm server`, with `ALLOW_ANY_ORIGIN=1` if the page is not on 127.0.0.1:5173). It rides along on every online reload.
- `&peerhost=127.0.0.1:9000` uses a local PeerJS signalling server for peer-to-peer play (developer tools).

Player-facing links: `?join=CODE` joins a room on the game server (`&p2p=1`: a peer-to-peer room), `?host=1` opens the host page.

**Claude artifact build.** This is a single self-contained HTML page for claude.ai. `vite.artifact.mjs` builds without code splitting. A small bundler script then inlines the JS and CSS into one file that is published as an artifact. The artifact host provides its own page shell, so viewport settings in `index.html` may not apply there.

**Version:** `src/version.js` is the one place the version lives (`0.66a`, the a is alpha). The browser tab reads `deadstab v0.66a` (`TAB_TITLE`); keep `index.html`'s `<title>` in step when it changes.

## Where the code lives

There are three copies. Know which one you are in.
- **GitHub `main`**: what players get on GitHub Pages.
- **The owner's PC** (`E:\ai slop games\game1`): his git checkout. Changes reach it as patch files in `Claude outputs/`, applied with `git am --3way`.
- **A Claude working copy**: where most recent work happens. It is uncommitted until the owner asks.

## Folder structure

Files are grouped by what they do; a module keeps its name when it moves, so
"`renderer.js`" in these notes is `src/render/renderer.js`. `find src -name x.js`
finds anything.

```
index.html          all menu, HUD, settings and panel markup (and the loading screen)
CHANGELOG.md        player-facing notes per release, newest first: add a section with every version push
src/                game code, one job per module
  main.js           entry glue: game state, keyboard/mouse/touch input routing, the fixed-step loop, menus flow
  bootstrap.js      loads the game behind the loading screen
  simulation.js     the rules: one Simulation object, stepped at 1/60 s from an input list
  settings.js       presets (GRAPHICS), saved settings, adaptive resolution, render budget
  audio.js (+ audio-hollow.js, hollow-ambience.js: Hollow Wick's soundscape and crows), items.js, maps.js, map-kit.js, crops.js, tutorial*.js, viewport.js, version.js,
  aim-assist.js, aim-damping.js, auto-range.js, keyboard-aim.js, target-lock.js, target-radius.js,
  box-index.js, playable-area.js, storage-migration.js, online-play.js, remote-players.js
  config/           EVERY tunable: gameplay.js (movement, Static/orbs/hex RULES, RIFLE, SHOTGUN, GRENADE,
                    aim assist), controls.js (touch feel), network.js, match.js
  maps/             one file per map (plain data); terrain/ holds the baked ground of maps with hills
                    (generated by tools/bake-terrain.mjs: never edit by hand)
  net/              online play: transports, protocol, host and client sessions
  render/           the 3D view: renderer.js (WorldView), crisp-output.js, extreme-post.js,
                    extreme-surfaces.js (ground detail, weathering, cloud shadows), shader-savings.js,
                    interior-visibility.js, frozen-transforms.js, bake-colors.js, arc-batch.js,
                    blob-shadows.js, camera-framing.js, shadow-snap.js, map-look.js, pick-view.js,
                    terrain-mesh.js (the drawn hills and retaining walls), ground-lift.js,
                    ground-layers.js + hill-shade.js (the ground's colours and hill shade),
                    water-mesh.js (a stream's water), ground-marks-view.js + ground-marks-atlas.js
                    (the ground marks: one draped mesh on a painted atlas, one instanced mesh of bits),
                    world-build.js / warm-up.js / vision.js (WorldView methods, see Rendering),
                    prop-instances.js (breakable props in batches), ...
  effects/          effects-detail.js (DetailFX), electric-effects.js (Static arcs and pooled pulses),
                    dust-trail.js, dust-devils.js, birds.js, crows.js + crow-rules.js (Hollow Wick), blood-*.js, gore.js, death-*.js,
                    water-field.js + water-effects.js + water-drips.js (the stream's field, effects, drips),
                    surface-marks.js (+ terrain-marks.js on hills), target-damage.js, remote-corpses.js
  world/            what the map is built from: world-details.js, roadside.js, rail-depot.js,
                    interior-details.js, detailed-interiors.js, room-furniture.js (where furniture stands,
                    and its colliders), building-finishes.js, crop-view.js,
                    approach-paths.js, heightfield.js (the ground under a map with hills),
                    terrain-bake.js (its bake, Node only), terrain-details.js (ground scatter on hills) and
                    ground-marks.js (where the ruts, puddles, prints, spills and leaf drifts lie; Hollow Wick),
                    hollow-life.js (Hollow Wick's moving life: the goat, the washing, effigies, shutters, sign, smoke)
  weapons/          rifle*.js, shotgun*.js, grenade*.js, ballast-damage.js, weapon-photos.js (tools only)
  bots/             robots (dev-tools test for now): nav-grid.js (walkable grid, A*, cover floods),
                    robot-brain.js (senses, plans, fights, teams up; outputs player input), robot-profile.js (skill and style), bot-match.js (robots and sides in a
                    solo game, joined to your sim like the multiplayer host joins players), robot-model.js
                    (the tin gunslinger, sparks, wrecks)
  ui/               menus, HUD and touch: title-screen.js, menu.js, settings-panel.js,
                    mobile-settings.js, touch-controls.js, touch-layout.js, weapon-hud.js,
                    ability-hud.js, aim-overlay.js, health-hud.js, damage-feedback.js, death-screen.js,
                    lobby-*.js, dev-*.js (dev-wiring.js: the game's hooks), toast.js, watermark.js, fire-indicator.js, map-cards.js, dom-writes.js (cached HUD writes),
                    haptics.js (touch vibration), wake-lock.js (screen stays on in play), ...
  styles/           style.css (base), menu-theme.css (menus, HUD), mobile-controls.css, tutorial.css,
                    loading.css
  assets/           shipped pictures (thumbnails, weapons)
server/             the game server (Node; runs HostSession with no local player): index.js (HTTP + WebSocket,
                    the 60 Hz loop), rooms.js, room.js (one room: leader, map moves), admin.js (the owner's
                    page), admins.js (who may use it), bans.js, config.js (limits), deploy/ (VPS setup:
                    SETUP.md, install.sh, update.sh, admin.sh, systemd units, Caddyfile)
tests/              one test file per system (node:test)
tools/              dev-server helpers (dev server up unless noted):
                    smoke.mjs [--map id] [--walk [--spot s]]  every preset (and Test Hill); --map runs that map on all 5; --walk walks a route on a terrain map (see Hills)
                    program-check.mjs shaders linked during play (Static X, blasts); expect none
                    perf.mjs [preset] [weapon] [map] [x z] | --map id --spot name|x,z --preset all|a,b  cost per frame by phase, draw calls, triangles, programs; PROFILE=1 adds a profile
                    draw-count.mjs    draws per frame on a served build
                    load-profile.mjs [quality] [cpuSlowdown]  where loading time goes (world build, warm-up)
                    death-shaders.mjs [quality]  programs linked at your first death, per death kind
                    capture-thumbnail.mjs / capture-weapons.mjs remake the menu pictures
                    bake-terrain.mjs  (no server) bakes every map's terrain into src/maps/terrain/
                    flat-frames.mjs <dir> [map] [refDir]  frozen frames on all 5 presets, compared
                                      with a reference (pixels and program keys)
                    terrain-frames.mjs <out> [map] [port]  frozen frames, cost and programs on a terrain map
                    terrain-spots.mjs (a module) named places per map for perf.mjs --spot and smoke --walk
.github/workflows/  Pages deploy (the game server updates itself from main: server/deploy/update.sh)
vite.config.js      normal build; vite.artifact.mjs is the single-file artifact build
```

## Major systems

### Simulation: the rules
- **Sight lines** (`canSeeEntity` → `sightBlocked`): only the colliders that block sight, cached per collider list, each rejected by its world bounds before the slab test. Target lock and aim assist ask three rays per target per step; `lockCandidates` (main.js) now tests range and screen first and sight last. **Gameplay sight is `sim.sees()`** (`canSeeEntity` plus, on a map with hills, the ground: see Hills); `canSeeTarget` goes through it, and `canSeeEntity` alone is the views' wall-and-room rule.
- **Files:** `simulation.js` holds the rules; their numbers are `RULES` in `config/gameplay.js`. The weapon rules are in `rifle.js`, `shotgun.js` (+ `shotgun-pressure.js` visuals, `ballast-damage.js`) and `grenade.js`. Maps and colliders are in `maps.js`, crops and fire in `crops.js`, and `playable-area.js` holds the map bounds.
- **How it works:**
  - `sim.step(input)` advances one fixed 1/60 s tick from a plain input object (move, aim, fire, dodge, abilities).
  - It pushes **events** (`hit`, `kill`, `propBreak`, `dodge`, and so on) that the renderer, audio and HUD react to via `sim.drainEvents()`.
  - It knows nothing about three.js or the DOM.
- **One player per sim** (`sim.player`). Multiplayer keeps it that way: one sim per player sharing one world through `net/arena.js` (see Multiplayer). `respawn(at)` gives a fresh body and loadout without touching the world; `worldAuthority` says whether this sim burns crops.

### Hills (terrain maps: the "2.5D" ground)
Positions stay x/z; the height of the ground under any point comes from one shared grid. A map opts in with a `terrain` spec. Every other map is exactly flat (`FLAT`) and runs exactly the code it always did: every height rule is skipped behind `ground.flat` / `dy === 0` guards (locked by tests/golden-flat.test.js and tools/flat-frames.mjs).
- **Files:** `world/heightfield.js` (the runtime ground: `FLAT`, `Ground`, sampling, sight, round stops, the authored edges), `world/terrain-bake.js` (spec to grid; Node only, never in the game), `tools/bake-terrain.mjs` (writes `src/maps/terrain/<id>.js` and `index.js`), `map-kit.js` `groundFor(map)` (one Ground per map id, shared by every sim, robot and view; FLAT without terrain), `render/terrain-mesh.js` (the drawn ground and the retaining walls), `render/ground-lift.js` (`groundY`, `hilly`: the view side's height helpers), `render/water-mesh.js` (a stream's water), `maps/hill-test.js` (Test Hill, the dev-only proving ground), `maps/hollow-wick.js` (Hollow Wick, being built). Numbers: `TERRAIN` in `config/gameplay.js`.
- **The grid:** 0.5 m apart, whole millimetres (Int16), baked in Node from the spec and shipped as text (each point predicted from its neighbours, the difference as a zigzag varint, base64: about a byte a point), so every browser and the host read exactly the same numbers. **After changing a map's `terrain` or moving a building that has `baseY`, run `node tools/bake-terrain.mjs`**: tests/terrain.test.js re-bakes and fails with "re-bake" until you do. `heightAt` is bilinear and includes decks (a bridge, a log) exactly: level to each deck's outline (`deckAt`), then straight down, so there is no ramp beside a deck; `drawnHeightAt` is the ground as drawn (no decks); `gradientAt` is the slope (0 on a deck). `ground.grid` (the drawn ground with each deck raised a grid step past its outline) is only for the highest-ground tile bounds and Extreme's ground texture; the lowest-ground bounds come from the drawn ground.
- **The spec** (documented at the top of terrain-bake.js): bounds, a base height and a gentle noise, carves (valleys), levels (plateaus, or pits with `lower`: each side falls away at its own grade and the highest wins, so a retaining wall fades out where a gentle side wraps round its corner; `cliffs` sides drop at 8:1 and become walls, `openings` are steep without a wall so a walled ramp can go through, `banks` are earth banks (they fall at `bankGrade`, 0.6, with no wall: the owner wants a lot fewer walls, so a side a body need not be stopped at is a bank), knolls, paths (ramps and cuttings, pulled to their own heights), the water channel (its banks rise from the bed to `top`, or without one to the ground beside them; `surface` is where the water stands, below), fords, decks, pads (each its own `margin` and `blend`), and `maxSlope` (below). A level with `overWater: true` is laid after the streams and fords (Hollow Wick's mill dam: stone faces as `cliffs`, its top a walk across the stream). **Buildings with `baseY` stand on flat pads** (the footprint plus 1.5 m, blended out over 2 m or `padBlend`); the building, its interior, roof, details and the room's shroud are lifted by baseY, and the camera rides at baseY inside.
- **Steep edges are authored, never extracted from the grid:** each level `cliffs` side and each segment of the spec's `edges` polylines is a thin `playerOnly, terrainEdge` box (mapColliders). **Wherever one of the spec's paths crosses a level's cliff side, the wall has a gap** the path's width plus half its shoulders across (`heightfield.js` `pathGaps`; a slanted crossing gets a wider gap; a stub under 0.3 m between a gap and a corner is left out): lanes, step flights and gullies go through walls with no extra authoring, and the path pulls the ground in the gap to its own height. A level side under a higher level is best an `openings` side (steep, no wall), so its falloff does not spill round the corner below the wall. They stop bodies and robots (so nav and spawns follow with no new code); sight, shots and blasts go by the ground instead. They are drawn as dry-stone retaining walls with lighter caps (the colliders at least 0.6 m deep, a cliff's drop/8 + 0.3; the wall meshes 0.45-0.5 m, inside them). The caps are laid as flat stones, 2-3 per 0.9 m piece, each its own length, a little narrower than the cap (at most the wall + 0.06 m), set off the line, raised or sunk a few cm and turned a hair, in the map's `wallCap` tone, two darker and one mossy (`wallMoss`, default #5d6647) (v0.975a: one continuous pale cap read as a concrete kerb from the camera above); seeded, merged into the static batches like the rest. The hill fence (`makeHillFence`) follows the ground: where the ground strays over 0.3 m from a post-to-post line the span gets posts every 1.25 m and a piece still that far off (a wall's face) is left open; a span running into a stream stops at the bank top with its own post. Tests walk every standable point of every terrain map: nowhere a body can stand is steeper than 1:1, nor steeper than an orb climbs (`orbRise` 0.8, so bodies and orbs agree: a pad cut more than about a metre into a slope needs a wider `padBlend` or an authored wall; a smoothstep bank's steepest point is 1.5 x the cut / the blend), and no slope over 30% runs on for more than 8 m (4 m before the earth banks; a deck's sides, within 1.1 m, are drops by design and are left out).
- **`maxSlope` (terrain-bake.js `limitSlopes`):** where features meet (a path cut into a bank, a knoll by a level, a pad's blend), ground can come out steeper than the rules allow. With `maxSlope` in the spec (Hollow Wick: 0.78) the bake lowers what is too steep: every step to a grid neighbour east/west/north/south is kept to maxSlope/sqrt(2) per metre, so the bilinear slope is at most maxSlope any way it faces. Only the higher side is lowered (paths and hollows keep their heights; a cut bank recedes). Not across a wall (a step between two grid points either side of a cliff or edge line is the wall's, touching counts), not on a wall's own face (from 0.1 m inside its line to just past its drop: `edge.face`, only for walls not centred on their line; lowering the ground just past a wall's line would tilt the grid cell that holds the line into ground a body can reach), never inside a pad (footprint plus margin); ground under a deck is kept no higher than the deck. Without `maxSlope` the grid is exactly as built (Test Hill). To author: bake, then run the terrain tests (or draw the slopes: every reachable point steeper than 0.8 and every run over 30% for more than 8 m); fix what is left in the spec (a wall, a gentler grade under 0.3, a wider shoulder, a path's gap), not by raising maxSlope.
- **Water: wade anywhere** (owner, 2026-09-26; `ground.water`, `waterAt`, `waterDepthAt`, `wetAt`, `flowAt`, `bankDistance`): no barriers and no water colliders. Each `water` channel's `surface: { up, down, damX, flow }` is the level the water stands at: `up` upstream of the dam at x `damX`, `down` below it; `flow` 1 runs east, -1 west (Hollow Wick's stream runs west, right to left on screen: up 0, down -0.3, dam at x 14). The depth is the level less the ground a body stands on, so it varies with the bed (Hollow Wick: about 0.1 m at the ford to 0.7 m in the west). `WADE` (config/gameplay.js; share = depth / `WADE.depth` 0.5, at most 1): walking is `slow` 22% x share slower, and `current` 30% x share quicker with the flow, slower against it (`sim.wadeFactor`, the cosine of the step with the flow; across it only the 22%); a dodge goes `dodge` 35% x share shorter; stamina refills `recharge` 45% x share slower. `wetAt` (over 3 cm): no dust, footprints or ground marks there. Robots' nav squares cost more with depth (up to +1). `bankDistance(x, z)`: how far outside the top of the nearest banks (the fence stops at the banks by it). FLAT has none of it. **Decks** (a bridge, a log, a footbridge: `terrain.decks`, `deckAt`) are two levels. A body walks onto one only from ground within `WADE.step` (0.45 m) of its top, so each deck's ends run on until the bank is within a hand of its top (the tests check every deck); from the water it wades in underneath (`p.below`, `sim.updateStance`) and stands on `drawnHeightAt` (`sim.standY`) until it leaves the outline or the ground under it rises to within a step of the top (then it is up on it). **Its ends are shut underneath** (v0.980a, owner: you walked straight up out of the stream through the bridge's end): where the ground comes within `WADE.under` 0.7 m of the top (the bridge's abutments, 1.1 m in from each end), a body under the deck goes no further toward that end: `updateStance` drops the part of the step along the deck (`deckAxis`: its long way and middle, from the outline) and the push that way, so it slides along the abutment, out from under a side, and climbs the bank round the end (the up-on-it rule above is left only as a fallback; tests/wading.test.js checks both ends of every deck). Stepping off a deck's side drops it into the water. `below` is sent in player states (protocol 11) and robots' `others()`; sight from or to a body under a deck takes its own ground (`sim.ownGround()`, the optional endpoint heights of `sightClear`), a round fired from under one starts over the ground there (`round.oy`), and the deck never cuts a sight line (only the drawn ground does); rounds fly over or under it (above). The view draws a body under a deck on the drawn ground (a death there carries `below`: the corpse lies in the water, `ground-lift.js floorY`), and **what is made down there is drawn down there too** (v0.975a): `ground-lift.js groundY` asks the view's `underNear(x, z)` (renderer.js: inside a deck's outline with the body nearest the point, you or another player within `UNDER_REACH` 2.5 m, wading under that deck; only asked while somebody is under one: `playerUnder`, `remote.anyUnder`), so effects (DetailFX and the electric arcs through their `floorAt`, sparks, smoke, blood, gore) follow the wader; things that last or travel keep the side they were made on: Static's orbs (`userData.under`, from the orb's first frame), its launch beams and flight streaks (`under`), casings and shotgun shells (your `below` when ejected) and dropped magazines (a WeakMap in rifle-view.js). Someone up on the deck by you keeps theirs on the deck (the nearest body decides). tests/wading.test.js. The deck you are under turns see-through (opacity 0.22, a quarter second each way: `crossing-decks.js` `updateCrossingDecks`). **Robots** walk the bridge, ford and footbridge and wade the stream; the fallen log (1.4 m) is narrower than the 0.5 m nav grid can thread (stage 2: a nav link, crossed one at a time). **Slopes:** walking is up to 7% slower straight up a 30% slope and up to 4% quicker straight down one (`slopeFactor`); dodges, knockback and launches are unchanged.
- **The stream's look and what moves on it** (`render/water-mesh.js`, `effects/water-field.js`, `effects/water-effects.js`, `effects/water-drips.js`; tests/water.test.js). `WaterField` (no three.js) holds each `terrain.water` channel as half-metre sections: `sample(x, z)` gives the level (`surfaceLevel`: `up` upstream of `damX`, by the channel's `flow` sign), the depth over the ground you would stand on (decks count), where it is along and across, and the flow (`dx, dz` point downstream: a channel's `dir` is its flow sign, -1 on Hollow Wick; `FLOW` 0.55 m/s scaled by width, slower at the banks, quickening over the 4 m above the weir). It is the view's only: wading itself (the slowdown, the current) is the simulation's `WADE` above. **The surface:** one ribbon per channel (17 columns, a row every half metre, the weir's face as two rows), **see-through on every preset** (owner, 2026-09-26: opacity `WATER_OPACITY` 0.8, no depth write, `renderOrder` -1 so fog sheets and a fading deck draw over it), per-vertex depth and signed flow speed (the current's bands slide downstream; the `waterDir` uniform rolls Extreme's swell the same way). Three index sets over the same vertices (`WATER_LOOK.mesh`: full on Extreme, every metre on Balanced and Quality, every other column too on Potato and Performance); one patched material ('water-surface') whose ladder is uniforms (`setWaterLook`), so a preset change links nothing. It draws dark #232a2c, lighter toward the banks, a pale wet line where it thins, the ford's pebble bed (#5a605a) tinted in by depth and drawn as pebbles from Balanced, a dull sky sheen, current bands (Performance up) and on Extreme a 2.2 cm swell (`swellAt` mirrors it for floaters). The stones in it are the crossings' (`map.crossings.stones`, drawn by crossing-decks.js; a map without them gets seeded rocks). **Effects** (`WaterEffects`, `view.waterFX`, made by buildWaterMesh so every pool is in the scene for the warm-up): five instanced pools (streaks, rings, patches, drops, floaters), each skipped while empty, plus DetailFX puffs; only the stretch within 26 x 19 m of the camera is alive. `WATER_DETAIL`: Potato streaks only; Performance splashes and wader ripples; Balanced rings round the stones, crossing posts, the ford's riffles and the weir (a line at the foot of the dam's downstream face: a terrain dam level with `overWater`), plus leaves (litter browns and canopy yellows/oranges, never red) coming in from upstream; Quality foam, weir churn and blood that drifts downstream; Extreme more of everything. Hooks in renderer.js: `waterFX.event(e)` at the top of `event` (a rifleImpact in water splashes instead of dust; pointImpact, scatter, explosion as a dome of spray, grenadeExplosion as a spout, playerDeath as a bleeding source, mapReset), netEvent (others' Scatter and deaths), `blood()` from `bleed`, `sprayColour()` at the top of `walkingDustColor` (steps, dodges and blasts in water kick up spray), `update()`, `clear()` in `reset`. You, other players and robots wade: a ring and splash per step, a bigger splash on a dodge, slow rings standing still. **Drips** (`WaterDrips`, Balanced and up): stepping out of the stream you drip for about 2 s. For later: `addSource('wheel' | 'pile' | 'rock', x, z, options)`, `driftBody(body, dt)`, `onSound(kind, x, z, strength)` (wadeStep, wadeDodge, splash, spout, plop; audio not wired yet).
- **Sight is mutual:** `ground.sightClear(a, b)`: a's eye (1.35 m) to b's body (0.9 m), or b's eye to a's body; the ground cuts a line only where it rises 0.5 m or more above it, **except at a retaining wall, which cuts at its full height** (a wall mask: the grid points under each wall's collider grown half a metre, `wallAt`/`cutAt`; lines there are walked in quarter metres, as round stops are, so both find the same narrow lip). So from the foot of a wall you cannot see, lock or shoot onto the ground just past its top. The pair is put in a fixed order first, so (a, b) and (b, a) take the same samples; one look at the highest ground in the tiles under the line's box skips the walk when nothing there can reach either line (most lines). No walk runs past 2 km (a runaway position returns at once, clear). `sim.sees` uses it for target lock, aim assist, the mouse volley pull, robots' eyes and team callouts; `obstacleBetween` (the lock's let-go test) counts a crest as a wall. **The view hides anyone the ground hides from you** (practice targets and other players; your own side always shows).
- **Rounds fly over the ground** (owner, 2026-09-26: they must not go into a hill when aimed at a different height; Nominal's bullets, Ballast's pellets, Scatter's shells and Static's spray lanes; `ground.flight`, `flightAt`, `flightReaches` in heightfield.js; `rifle.js` `roundOnGround`, `roundSees`, `roundMeets`): at fire time a round works out its **flight**: a line that starts over the ground its shooter stands on (`oy` when wading under a deck; `lead`, the shooter-to-muzzle distance, lets it rise up to that far toward a bank's rise before the barrel, so a round fired at the foot of a bank does not start inside it) and follows the ground under it every quarter metre (`FLIGHT_STEP`), climbing and dropping no more than `TERRAIN.roundClimb` (1 m per m), and not dipping into a hollow it would climb straight back out of within `FLIGHT_SPAN` (6 m: a gully, a ditch, the stream's channel; it looks ahead only for going down, so a wall ahead is met, never climbed early). The round flies `TERRAIN.roundHeight` (0.74 m) over that line and ends where the ground rises into it (`stop`: a retaining wall's face or a rise steeper than it climbs; on both terrain maps every stop is at a wall). Decks: met within `WADE.step` of their top it flies over them, from lower down under them. **A hit is physical:** a body is hit when the round passes it between its feet and `TERRAIN.bodyTop` (1.9 m) above them (`roundSees(sim, round, target, s)`, `s` metres along the path), so over a crest it hits bodies the shooter cannot see (sight is only for lock, assist, robots' eyes and drawing), close under a ledge it is coming down from it flies over them, and a wall is cover; low cover (1.5 m or less) meets it only at its own height, walls and buildings always. Scatter's small shells carry on from their big shell's flight height where it split. Robots' `shotClear(..., ground)` asks `flightReaches` (a crest a round flies over is no cover; walls are). The view draws every round on the same flight (`ground-lift.js` `roundFlight`: the round's own on the host, worked out once from where it set off on a mirrored one). Ground hits push `impactMark`/`rifleImpact` with `ground: true`; `stop` travels in snapshots and the mirror keeps drawn rounds short of it. **Lightning and beams bend over the ground too:** electric arcs keep 0.32 m over the ground between their ends (their glow in four pieces when bent), Static's orb-flight beams are chains of 1.5 m pieces, and the volley beam's core and halo are tubes along the ground (`renderer.beamTube`); flat maps draw them exactly as before.
- **Orbs** float over the ground and stop against a rise steeper than 0.8 m per metre (a wall's face); a parked orb also stops at a drop that steep (it does not drift off a ledge). Placing one skips a lane, and a spot, up such a rise (a `wall` event, no ammo). **A volley launches only the orbs its owner can see** (`sim.launchableSeeds()`: every orb on a flat map); with none it is a quick shot. Robots count launchable orbs too. A volley's refocus onto a body near the cursor skips a body the ground hides. **Blasts:** orb volleys, grenades and Scatter bursts are measured in 3D and do nothing behind a crest (`blastReach`: a victim already out of reach on the flat is skipped before any walk, since 3D is never shorter; every blast asks it of every prop on the map); the hex and Surge's break use 3D distance only (`Simulation.reach3`: like walls, crests do not stop them). **Grenades:** `y` is a world height; the old arc is laid over the ground from where it left the hand to where it lands (`grenadeBase`: lobbed off high ground it keeps that height until it is past the edge, lobbed up onto high ground it is up there by the time it arrives, so it clears the lip it was thrown over), it drops where it meets a rise on the way, and the retaining walls are left to the ground test.
- **Robots:** `shotClear` takes the ground (by the rounds' flight: walls are cover, crests are not), eyes use `sees`, nav squares cost more on slopes (+50% at 30%, +75% from 45%), and the off-screen box leans with the height between them and you (`offScreen(..., dy)`: 0.82 m less reach north per metre up, the sides a little less; exactly the old box at dy 0). **Robots never fire from off your real screen:** besides the box, when the player's screen shape is known a robot must also be on it (`camera-framing.js` `onScreenOf(aspect, dx, dz, dy, side, end)`, the camera's own projection, with 0.5 m side and 2 m end margins), so a tall phone held upright is covered. The shape comes from `view.camera.aspect` each frame: practice sets `bots.viewAspect`, the host `session.setAspect`, and a joiner sends `aspect` in its input (rounded to 0.001, clamped 0.42-3.6 by protocol.js); unknown (0) falls back to the box alone.
- **Camera and drawing:** the camera's focus rides the ground (about 4/s); the pixel snap works in the view plane (the old ground-level branch at y 0); `aim()` marches the cursor's ray to the ground (+0.7) in quarter metres and halves down; `screenPoint(x, z, y)` takes y above the ground there, which fixes every HUD overlay at once. **Effects keep y above the ground under them and add it where they are drawn** (DetailFX, the particle pool, dust trail, blood, drops, bullet and blast marks, footprints, weapons' rounds and casings, corpses, gore, robots' scrap, electric arcs); anything that holds a world height (a gun's muzzle) takes the ground off first. Pools of blood, prints and burns lie on the slope (`tiltToGround`); base and team rings, the aim chevron and the kill ring too (`layFlat`, for flat markers built lying down that ride a turned figure), and Potato's blob shadows. **Rounds are drawn at their height over the ground under them** (rifle rounds, pellets and their spent ghosts, Scatter shells), from the ground their shooter stood on, gliding over a wall's edge no steeper than 1:1 (`ground-lift.js` `glide`/`roundGlide`, a little state per round) and, where the ground takes them, into it over their last metre. Blood thrown at a retaining wall stains it from its foot and flies over it from the top (`castToWall` with the ground). The view's sight checks to practice targets are kept until either end moves (`seenFrom`). A restart puts the camera's focus at the ground's height. Extreme's dust band and the fog sheets follow the ground (one height texture, extreme-surfaces.js groundHeights, made at load on terrain maps on every preset). Tumbleweeds and dust devils are Deadwater's and stay off terrain maps. **Water** (`render/water-mesh.js`): one ribbon per channel at the height the water stands (`surface: { up, down, damX, flow }`: a short drop at the dam; upstream is west of it for flow 1, east for -1), dark and a little glossy, lighter toward the banks (vertex colour), running under the banks and on past the fence; uv x = metres along, y = -1..1 across for the flow effects to come. Decks are built by `crossing-decks.js` (stage 2 crossings, below), one merged mesh each (`view.deckMeshes`), over the whole outline except where a bank rises over the top; the one you are under fades to see-through. The drawn player drops from a deck to the water quickly but not in one frame (`renderer.playerY`, reset on restart). **The fence on a terrain map** (`world-build.js` `makeHillFence`): Deadwater's posts every 5 m standing on the ground, rails post to post up and down the slopes, and no post or rail on a stream's banks (the stream flows on past it; the playable edge still stops everyone there).
- **Ground detail on hills** (`world/terrain-details.js`, from world-build.js `makeHillTerrain` right after the three detail groups are made, and after `buildTerrainMesh`, whose mesh it reads): dry grass tufts, flattened fieldstones, twigs, dead weed stalks and leaf drifts. Leaves are litter browns only (#8a6a3e / #7a5a34 / #6e4a2c): red on the ground reads as blood.
  - **Groups and cost:** one InstancedMesh per kind per group, on `bakedMaterial('plain')` with instanceColor (Deadwater's ground-cover material, so the same light, Extreme weathering and cloud shadows; one instanced program variant, warmed at load). No shadows cast, no per-frame work. Every group is built at load on every preset (about 0.1 s on Test Hill). The kinds' shares go 20% into `performanceDetails`, 30% into `groundDetails` and 50% into `extraGroundDetails`, so Potato shows none, Performance 20%, Balanced 50%, and Quality and Extreme all of it.
  - **Placement:** seeded from `map.scenerySeed`, with one sequence per kind and soft 7 m value-noise clumping.
  - **Keeps clear of** (`detailClearance`): anything more than 16 m outside the playable outline (`nearFence`), paths, the stream's channel and banks, fords, decks, slopes over 0.5, the wall mask and wall colliders, building footprints and pads, the spec's own pads, crops, props, fences, targets, and anything off the grid.
  - **Heights:** flat pieces lie 1.2 cm over the higher of the grid and the loaded RTIN mesh (looked up through its triangles). Upright ones root 3 cm under the lower of the two. A later `refineTerrain` never buries or exposes them.
  - **Map data:** `groundDetail: false` turns the layer off. Otherwise `groundDetail = { density, kinds: { tufts|stones|twigs|stalks|leaves: { colors, density } }, regions: [{ poly, density, kinds: { <kind>: multiplier } }] }`; where regions overlap, the last one in the list wins. Base densities per 100 m² are in `DETAIL_KINDS`. Hollow Wick: thick grass and stalks in the hollow and the field, the town's yards (1.1: trodden grass at the edges, gravel stones x1.4, twigs, the village trees' leaves x1.2; it was 0.45 and read bare next to Deadwater's streets, map-ui audit), leaf litter and twigs deep in the woods. The leaves' litter browns are #8a6a3e, #7a5a34 and #5a4632 (the third was #6e4a2c, only 6.3 from the rust coat). The ground marks' straw and spilled grain are faded the same way (render/ground-marks-atlas.js `STRAW_COLOURS`, `GRAIN_COLOURS`, and world/ground-marks.js `BIT_COLOURS`; v0.980a: the bright straw read as an Amber hat), and tests/hollow-wick-look.test.js holds every one of them to the hats' and coats' contrast.
  - **Test Hill:** thickens leaves 3x and halves tufts in its hollow. Its layer adds 5 / 10 / 15 draws and about 11k / 27k / 54k triangles on Performance / Balanced / Quality and Extreme.
  - **Tests:** tests/terrain-details.test.js.
- **Ground marks on terrain maps** (`effects/terrain-marks.js`, used by `surface-marks.js` only when `hilly(view)`; Deadwater keeps its DecalGeometry path untouched): a DecalGeometry projected onto the RTIN tiles cost 1-5 ms a mark (it clips every triangle of a 32 m tile) and cut a burn off at a tile's edge, so on terrain maps the ground is never raycast or decalled. `SurfaceMarks.hit` first finds where the ray meets the drawn ground from the height grid (`groundAlong`: one lookup straight down, else a quarter-metre walk), raycasts only what stands nearer (walls, floors on pads, decks, props, targets, roofs: these still get decals, as before), and otherwise returns a stand-in `{ terrain, point, distance }` hit, which `stamp` sends to `TerrainMarks.mark`. Marks up to `QUAD_MAX` (0.6 m, bullet marks) are instances of one lying quad (one InstancedMesh with the decals' own soot material) tilted to the drawn ground's slope. Burns are draped grids (`drapeBurn`: 9x9 vertices, up to 17x17 so they stay within 0.5 m of each other, at `drawnHeightAt`): a triangle over a retaining wall's drop (steeper than 1.6:1) or bent more than 8 cm off the ground is left out, and a triangle cutting under a crest has its corners raised. Then all of it is lifted by `view.terrainError` (the preset's RTIN error) + 6 mm, with the material's polygonOffset, so it is never under the drawn mesh and at most a few cm over it. Both are fixed rings built at Extreme's size in the constructor, so they are in the scene before the warm-up draws them (the only new program is the soot material's instanced variant). Each preset uses up to `TERRAIN_MARK_CAP` (quads 120/240/480/900/900, burns 10/16/28/48/48). Only the changed slot is uploaded (`addUpdateRange`). They sit at renderOrder -1 like the decals, at the scene's top level (so the AO pass leaves them out), and `clear()` empties them. An explosion's 24 radial soot rays skip ground hits (they land inside the burn), but the ground still stops them. Nothing marks the stream's water (`wetAt`; its splash comes with the stream's effects). Cost per mark: a bullet mark about 0.02 ms and an 8 m burn about 0.15 ms of geometry in node (was 4-5 ms and 1.3-2.5 ms); tests/terrain-marks.test.js.
- **The mesh:** RTIN (the "Martini" method) in 32 m tiles, within 4 / 3.5 / 2 / 1 / 1 cm of the grid (Potato to Extreme); neighbouring tiles even out the errors on their shared edge so there are no cracks. Colour is not in the mesh: one world-space texture read per pixel (see **Ground look** below); it receives shadows and casts none. A vertex beside a retaining wall's drop takes its normal and colour from its own level's side (`riseAcross`), so the ground at a wall's top and foot stays level (no dark saw teeth along the walls). The mesh is built for the preset the map loads with; a switch to a finer preset rebuilds its tiles into the same group (`refineTerrain`, same material: no new programs).
- **Ground look (terrain maps):** the terrain material is white; its colour is one world-space RGBA texture baked at load and read once per pixel (`render/ground-layers.js`; RTIN puts few vertices on flat ground, so tints baked per vertex blurred into triangles).
  - **Layers, in order:** `map.palette.ground`, then `map.groundLayers` in order (`[{ poly: [[x, z], ...], colour: '#hex' or a list mixed by slow noise, feather (m, 1), mix (0..1, 1), noise (0..1, .3: ragged edge and patchiness) }]`: woods litter, stubble, turf, yards), then banks on steep ground (`terrainLook.bank`, `bankMix`; a retaining wall's drop is not a bank), then damp below `terrainLook.dampBelow` (0) over `dampDepth` (1.2) and, when `terrainLook.hollow` is set, the hollow colour below `hollowBelow` (-1) over `hollowDepth` (1), both with noise-broken edges, then `map.terrain.paths` (palette.road or the path's `colour`/`colourMix`; about 0.6 m soft edges that wander ±0.25 m and a broken worn fringe; 2-D paths are colour-only; a path with `colourMix: 0` shapes the ground but is not drawn, and ground detail does not keep clear of it: Hollow Wick draws only the main road, its branches to the field and the bridges, owner 2026-09-26), then a slow tone variation (`terrainLook.variation`, .06). Colours are mixed in linear light and stored as sRGB bytes.
  - **Texel size and cost:** `GROUND_TEXEL` gives .5 m (Potato, Performance), .35 (Balanced) and .25 (Quality, Extreme): 440k texels for a 172 x 160 m map. Each layer and path is drawn only over its own box and near its own segments, so a 172 x 160 m map bakes in about 0.2 s at .25 m here.
  - **Hill shade** (`render/hill-shade.js`, pure): 1 m texels over the drawn heights. From each texel it walks toward the sun, compares the steepest rise with the sun's slope through a soft band (far crests give softer edges) and blurs once. It rides in the colour texture's alpha (one read). It dims only the sun's direct light: `hillShadePatch` wraps `getDirectionalLightInfo`/`getShadow` with macros instead of editing three's lights chunk, so the sun takes min(shadow map, hill shade), Potato just multiplies it in, sky light is untouched, and later hooks (Extreme's clouds and ground variation) still find every #include they patch.
  - **Patches** chain through `material.userData.patches` (`addPatch`: one onBeforeCompile, key `look|ground-layers|hill-shade-groundSunLeft`), so there is one material and one program per preset.
  - **Presets:** a finer preset re-bakes the colour into the same uniform (no new program); a coarser one keeps it. `view.groundLook` holds the texture, the shade bake and the bake times.
  - **Props and players (not done yet):** they can take the same shade through a RedFormat texture of `groundLook.shade.data` plus `hillShadePatch(uniforms)` (instancing handled), or `shadeSampler` per object, on terrain maps only.
  - **Sun:** it is per map: `look.sunOffset` (map-look.js, default Deadwater's (-24, 40, -18)) drives the sun's position and basis, the shadow camera's near/far (`shadowDepth`), the blob lean (`sunLean`) and this shade.
  - **Tests and tools:** tests/ground-look.test.js; `node tools/terrain-frames.mjs <out> hill-test <port>` gives frozen frames, frame cost and program counts per preset.
- **Multiplayer:** PROTOCOL_VERSION 11 (`below` in player states). The host sends a fingerprint of the map (`maps.js` `mapHash`: its data and its ground) in `welcome`; a joiner on another map or build of it is turned away. **Every number is rounded to the micrometre first** (`hashNumber`): numbers worked out as a map loads can differ in the last bit between browsers (Deadwater's rounded outline uses Math.hypot, and Firefox's hypot is not Chrome's or Safari's), which must never turn anyone away; tests/terrain.test.js checks no map number sits near a rounding tie and that nudging every approximated Math function a few ulps leaves every fingerprint the same. A mirrored round's object taken over by a new round drops the old one's `stop` and `surge` (`projectiles.js` STALE: a stale Surge glow no longer sticks to a later round).
- **Tests:** tests/terrain.test.js (the bake is up to date and repeats, the text form round-trips, pads are flat, the gradient, mutual sight, round flights (never into the ground before their stop, never steeper than 1:1, stopped only by walls; across the gully, down a slope, over and under the bridge), decks, no reachable steep ground, the slope limits, engine-independent fingerprints), tests/wading.test.js (Hollow Wick's stream: no barriers, depths vary, with/against/across the flow, dodges and stamina in water, every deck's ends on its banks, on/under/off decks, `below` over the network), tests/hills.test.js (slopes, walls, the ramp, sight, rounds over the ground (over a crest, into a wall, off a ledge), pellets into a wall, blasts in 3D and at a crest, Scatter never hitting a hidden body, the volley refocus, orbs, grenades over lips both ways, blood on retaining walls, the off-screen box, the network, runaway inputs), tests/golden-flat.test.js (Deadwater replays byte for byte: every weapon, a robot fight, a hosted match; re-record only for an owner-approved change, noted in its header). Frames: `node tools/flat-frames.mjs <dir> deadwater <refDir>` compares all 5 presets' pixels and program keys with a reference taken before a change.
- **Measuring terrain maps (tools/perf.mjs, tools/smoke.mjs, tools/terrain-spots.mjs).** Named places to stand live in `SPOTS` in tools/terrain-spots.mjs, per map id. Each is `{x, z, look?}`; `look` is a point the aim is turned towards.
  - Test Hill has start, plateau, hollow, hilltop and walk.
  - Hollow Wick has start, walk (the scarecrow field), summit-into-hollow (the widest view), north-woods (the densest), town, bridge, s5-life (the farm's back yard: the goat and the washing) and s5-ground (the green, where the ground marks are thickest).
  - A map's `walk` spot is where the walk check starts. It must have an open 9x9 m square to its north-east plus room to its north-west.
  `node tools/perf.mjs --map hill-test --spot plateau --preset all [--port 5173]` teleports the player through the dev-only `window.__capture` (sim + view) and cuts the camera. For each preset it prints a JSON line: whole-frame draw calls and triangles (every pass, taken from `renderer.info` reset around `drawFrame`), median frame ms over frames that drew, idle ms at the spot, program count, frame counts and errors. The old positional form `perf.mjs [preset] [weapon] [map] [x z]` still works.
  `node tools/smoke.mjs --map <id>` runs the usual smoke on all 5 presets. `node tools/smoke.mjs --walk [--map hill-test] [--spot s]` starts a practice game at the walk spot and holds WASD along north, east, south, west, then diagonals north-west and back. It moves to the next preset at the start of each leg, so all 5 run. It fails on a page or console error, a NaN position, the drawn player more than 0.25 m below `ground.heightAt`, or a leg that moved less than 1 m. The rules are `judgeWalk`, tested in tests/terrain-spots.test.js. It prints a JSON summary.
  Legs are timed in game seconds (`sim.time`), because SwiftShader can stall for seconds after a preset change. Everything is SwiftShader, so compare numbers only against each other on the same box.
- **Hollow Wick, stage 1 (the ground):** the whole terrain from the layout (levels, the terraced slope, the lip as two walled levels, the graveyard terraces with gaps for the aisles, the east gully as a walled cutting, the woods' knolls, paths, the stream with five crossings, building pads), the irregular fence, wading (no barriers), the water ribbon and plank decks you can walk on, wade under and step off, earth banks for most of the old walls, a darker brown ground and only the main paths drawn. Also stage 1: the draped ground colours and hill shade with the low west-south-west sun, the ground-detail layer, instanced marks, and the measuring tools (below).
- **Hollow Wick, stage 2: breakables** (task s2-breakables; owner: "many different breakable assets like the barrels or crates in Deadwater, except new things that are cool and unique"). Ten types in `world/hollow-breakables.js` (`HOLLOW_BREAKABLES`, spread into `PROP_TYPES`; models by `makeHollowBreakable`, one dispatch line in `makeProp`): `pumpkin` (uncarved), `ciderKeg` (on a trestle), `appleCrate`, `grainSacks`, `chickenCoop` (empty), `beeSkep` (on a stool), `stoneware` (salt-glazed crocks and a jug), `tinLantern` (pierced tin on a post, candle lit), `cordwood` (split logs; the name `woodpile` is the field props' solid pile, so it is not reused), `squashBarrow`. Health 5 like a barrel, except the lantern 3 and the coop, barrow (8) and cordwood (12); none is walk-over. 84 placed in `maps/hollow-wick-breakables.js` (spread into `hollowWick.props`; ids `hw-b<n>` by position in the list, so new ones go at its end): by the tavern, the houses' yards, the smithy, the barn, the mill, the fork, the farm, the woodshed, a few near each base as light cover, uncarved pumpkins on the field's west half, and (v0.975a, the stage 4 audit found stretches with none) lanterns, crates, grain and crocks at the crossings' ends, windfall crates by the old orchard and cordwood at the woods' edges. A breakable is drawn at its data `scale` (a big pumpkin, a small one; map-kit scales its collider too) and settles to the low side of a slope (`world/settle.js`). **Placement rules** (`tests/hollow-breakables-rules.js`, checked by the test): inside the fence, out of the stream's banks, off decks, retaining walls and every path (drawn or not), slope at most 0.35 at every corner, 1 m off the building pads' walls and 2.5 m clear in front of their long sides (doors), 3 m from a base's centre, 2 m from a target, not overlapping each other. Re-run the test after moving buildings or paths.
  - **How they break** (`effects/breakable-effects.js`, `HollowBreakFX`, made on the first break by one line at the top of `renderer.breakProp`, stepped before `updateParticles`, cleared with the map): each type has a recipe. Pumpkin: orange chunks and pale seeds, a pulp splat that fades (28 s), chunks lying in it. Keg: staves and hoops, amber cider thrown in arcs for 0.9 s, a dark wet stain that dries (60 s), the tin cup rolls off. Apple crate: slats, and the apples roll downhill and stay. Grain: sacking tatters, grain pours for 1.3 s into a heap that stays. Coop: planks and shingles, feathers that drift down (fall at most 0.55 m/s, swaying) and some stay. Skep: straw coils, and a dark swarm circles, widens and drifts off. Stoneware: grey and cobalt shards, the lid spins off like a coin and settles flat, big shards stay. Lantern: tin bits, the post falls, and the candle burns on the ground 5.5-7 s, guttering, then goes out in a curl of smoke (DetailFX embers and glow, and a flicker of the shared fx light when it is idle). Cordwood: logs tumble, roll across their length downhill and stay. Barrow: squash roll off and stay; the wheel runs off on its rim and topples flat.
  - **Cost:** flying pieces are the view's own particles (the tinted white pool, the preset's cap; `DETAIL` scales counts: Potato .4 to Extreme 2). What stays (rollers, stains, heaps, splats) is **one** instanced mesh for the whole map (flat-shaded Lambert with instance colour: the same program as DetailFX's chunk pool, nothing new to compile), at most `LEAVINGS_CAP` (Potato 48 to Extreme 320, oldest first), hidden when empty, matrices rewritten only on frames something moves, grows or fades. Rollers stop at walls, buildings, fences and retaining walls (the sim's colliders), within 2.6 m (the wheel and lid a little further), and sink where the stream starts. The model's lit candle is one shared `MeshBasicMaterial` (merged with the prop batches).
  - **Sounds** (`effects/breakable-sounds.js`, one hook in audio.js's propBreak branch): a wet thunk and seeds (pumpkin), staves and glugs (keg), apples thudding (crate), a tear and a hiss of grain, a crack and a rush of feathers (coop), a swelling buzz (skep), stoneware and the lid's coin spin-down, ringing tin (lantern), a slowing cascade of knocks (cordwood), thumps and a rattling wheel (barrow). A dash adds the body's thump first. Landing pieces use the existing clatter ('wood', 'clay', 'plant').
  - **Tests:** tests/hollow-breakables.test.js (types registered with health and sizes; 60-90 placed, every type, all by the rules; each breaks in the sim with its event and comes back; effects run on Potato and Quality within caps and Potato throws less; apples roll downhill, stop within reach and stay; feathers drift, the swarm disperses; every type has its own sound).
- **Hollow Wick interiors (stage 2, `world/colonial-interiors.js`):** furniture you collide with, for the interiorStyle names shared with the buildings: `colonial-house` (capes, saltboxes, gambrel, farm), `colonial-lit-house` (the lit cape), `colonial-tavern`, `meetinghouse`, `smithy`, `barn`, `gristmill`, `shed` (woodshed; a `hearse` id gets the bier), `horse-sheds`, `tomb`. `detailed-interiors.js` dispatches to it (`interiorCover` -> `colonialCover`, `makeDetailedInterior` -> `makeColonialInterior`), and `interior-details.js` skips these rooms (its cookstove and hanging lamp are the wrong century). Period: a centre chimney with an open cooking hearth, crane and pot (no cookstove), trestle tables, ladder-back chairs, rope beds, blanket chests, dressers with pewter, spinning wheels, cradles; the tavern's hearth, bar cage, settle, casks and tables; box pews, a raised pulpit with its sounding board and a communion table; the smithy's forge and bellows (banked coals glow), anvil and quench tub; the mill's millstones in their tun with the hopper and the pit wheel, grain bin, bolter, sacks, a spare stone; the barn's stalls, mangers, mow and threshing floor; cordwood and a sawbuck; coffins on stone shelves. Abandoned mid-task; candles and pierced tin, never kerosene; no lettering anywhere. Walk-over touches (`extras`): in the tavern a tankard on its side in a dried stain and a dropped clay pipe (and a chair, a mug, a broom); in the meetinghouse the tithingman's rod across a pew and guttered candle stubs; the houses' own below. Doorways are every named door (centred, `doorWidth`) and every extra opening (`openings`: { side, offset, width }, the horse sheds' bays, the sheds' back doors): each keeps its own clear zone, and one narrower than the walkway needs a body through it but no walkway.
  - **Layouts are wishes, resolved per building:** each piece lists spots (first choice, then fallbacks; some are worked out from a piece already placed, like the anvil in front of the forge). A spot is taken only if it stays inside the walls (0.19 m wall half-thickness), clear of every door zone (1.2 m in from the door, its width plus 0.2 m a side: `doorZones`) and every open window's zone (below), off every placed piece, with no gap to a piece or a wall between 0.72 and 0.86 m (a body's width: where bodies jam), and if the room stays walkable (`roomCheck` on a 0.1 m grid: a 1.4 m walkway joins every door, no floor a body fits on is cut off, at least 60% of that floor lies within 1 m of the walkway, and no nook a robot's body fits in (`NAV_BODY` 0.46 m, RULES.radius + 0.08, as bots/nav-grid.js) is cut off from the doors; the fine grid is not the robots' 0.5 m squares, so the NavGrid test below is the judge: stage 4 left 10 squares of the lit cape and 9 behind the meetinghouse's back-right pews unreachable, and now the meetinghouse's pews (1.75 m) leave 1.25 m between them and the lit cape's table and the mill's spare stone stand where the squares get round them). A piece with no spot left is left out, so any door sides work (the tests try all 15). Rooms without a house story are mirrored by id (a house with a `variant` is laid out for its own walls). The result is cached per shape (style, variant, id, size, doors, windows, height).
  - **Windows stay shootable (s5-interiors):** every window that is neither boarded nor a fallen wall keeps a strip clear inside it, like a doorway: its width plus 0.1 m a side, `WINDOW_CLEAR` 0.9 m in, and down its middle (`WINDOW_LANE` 0.4 m) on to `WINDOW_REACH` 1.35 m (`windowZones`), so a round fired straight through a window flies 1.5 m past the wall line before any piece can stop it (stage 4 had furniture against about 19 open windows: sight went in, rounds stopped at the sill). Pieces take their next spot: the tavern's bar cage is shallower (1.65 m) in the back-right corner, its casks along the left wall, its cupboard front-left; the smithy's forge stands along the right wall 0.9 m forward of the back window, its bench front-left; the mill's bin (1.1 m) between the back window and door, its spare stone on the left wall; the barn's mow is two heaps either side of its low front window (the right one 2.26 m deep by the side window); the meetinghouse's pulpit window (back, middle) is shuttered (SPECS), and its outer-left pews stand in 0.12 m and stop short of the side windows.
  - **Colliders:** every piece is one axis-aligned box in the room's frame with a height (table 0.8, bed 0.6, pew 1.0, pulpit 2.0, hearth and chimney the room's height...); map-kit.js turns them into colliders (so nav, spawns, rounds and low cover follow). Small clutter (chairs left about, mugs, the broom, straw, bones, horseshoes, meal, the houses' shoes, poppet, shards, salt, prints) is drawn only and never collides.
