Nothing solid is drawn outside its own box: the cupboard's and dresser's cases stand at the back of a 0.6 m footprint so the door left ajar swings inside it (`ajar`), and a knocked-over chair is seated on the boards inside its table's footprint (`fallenChair`, used by the lit cape's table and the parlour's). Everything is built with view.box/cylinder in shared colours and merged by the static batch (floors and dust are two-triangle quads, `flat`); the glow materials add one draw each where used, and the laying-out's one lit candle uses the lantern's own material (no new draw). Draws inside a house on Performance stayed within one of stage 4's (saltbox 57 to 56, cape 51/50, gambrel 63/64, lit cape 66/66, saltbox-2 58/58, farm 58/57), and all sixteen buildings came to 149.8k triangles (153.9k before, with all the new detail).
  - **The houses tell six stories (s5-interiors, owner: "lots of detail in these interiors ... eerie horror movies and colonial america ... puritan type stuff"):** SPECS `variant` picks the room (`HOUSES` in colonial-interiors.js; each keeps its centre chimney under its roof's stack, first spots written for the house's own doors and windows, then every wall, `everyWall`): **laying-out** (the lit cape: the coffin across two chairs in the back corner, lid slid askew, a plate of salt on it, four tall candlesticks burnt to their sockets with one still alight; the mourners' chairs in a row facing it; the watchers' supper left on a table, its chair knocked over; the deathbed stripped to its ropes; a chest), **weaver** (the cape: the four-post loom with its cloth half woven, the great wool wheel, baskets of fleece, a linen chest, the bed), **kitchen** (the saltbox, whose lean-to was the kitchen: stew half cooked in the kettle on the crane with the ladle in it and bread burnt in the spider, the dough trough risen over, the churn and the salting tub, the work table with apples half pared, the dresser, the wood box), **nursery** (saltbox-2: the rope bed with the trundle pulled out, the cradle left rocked to one side before the hearth, a sampler on its stand (a pattern, no letters), the blanket chest), **parlour** (the gambrel: the long table laid for the whole family, every chair pushed back as if they all rose at once, two knocked over, the family Bible shut at the head beside a candle stub; the court cupboard with its pewter; a chest), **farm** (the root cellar's trapdoor thrown open, grain sacks, tools on their rack, the butchering table with its stained cloth). Their walk-over stories (`houseStory`), different in every house and sized to read from the room camera (about 18 m up; the pale things carry it: linen, salt, flour, wool, pewter): laying-out, the looking-glass over the coffin covered with a cloth, the dead man's buckled shoes set by the chairs, a daisy wheel burnt into the lintel over the back door; kitchen, herbs, dried apples and onions hung from a beam over the table, crocks on a shelf, a skillet and ladle on pegs, bare footprints in spilled flour walking to the hearth where they stop, a jug broken by the dresser, the broom; weaver, a cloak still on the peg rail with skeins beside it, the warping board, a skein wound round a chair back, scratches in the boards before the hearth; parlour, the pewter on its plate shelf, the whole family's hats and caps still on the pegs, a line of salt across the back threshold; nursery, children's tally marks scratched low on the back wall, little frocks on the pegs, a porringer and horn cup on a shelf, the rag poppet face down by the cradle, three pairs of little shoes by the back door; farm, the back door shut and barred from inside (drawn shut outside too: SPECS `shutDoors`), seed corn hung on the pegs, hooks over the butchering table, a slop bucket kicked over, an unlit lantern set down by the open cellar. Wall pieces go where the wall is free (`wallSpot`: not across a doorway or an open window, not behind a piece standing taller, not over another wall piece; the back wall first: its face is the one the camera sees; the front wall hides what stands behind it). A house with no `variant` gets the old one-room hall and its old extras (doll, shoes, scratches, chair, mug, broom).
  - **The fire is under the stack:** a chimney in SPECS listed `over: <kind>` stands over that placed piece's flue (`flueOf`; maps/hollow-wick-buildings.js its `at`/`across` are written into SPECS (v0.980a: worked out by `stackOver` and checked equal by tests/colonial-interiors.test.js; working them out at import laid out every room, 110-130 ms of every page load on every map; `stackOver` still fills in a stack left without `at`), so the shell and the crows read ordinary numbers): the tavern's right-hand chimney is over its hearth at the right end (5.35, was 2.6), the smithy's over its forge (3.35, -1.1; was -3.2 at the far end). (Neither room was mirrored: the hearth and forge were where the doors and windows left room.) Houses keep their centre chimneys.
  - **The sheds furnished (s5-interiors):** stage 4's horse sheds were empty and the hearse house held one stack (every spot was in a doorway). The horse sheds' stall boards stand between the bays against the closed front wall (1.1 m long, 1.4 m of floor left along the open side), a manger in three bays and the old chaise backed into the end bay (spots at both ends: the room is mirrored); the hearse house's bier stands in a back corner (either corner) with a coffin stood on end and the pall chest; the woodshed has its long stack on one side wall's back half, the short one opposite and the sawbuck; the tomb's second coffin shelf lies along its front wall.
  - **Tests:** tests/colonial-interiors.test.js (every style laid out and the stories' key pieces for all 15 door sets, inside the walls, doorways and open windows clear, heights set, no overlaps or body-width gaps, walkable for all 15 door sets, a Simulation body walking in from each doorway (the extra openings too) and round every piece; no room places fewer than three pieces; the six houses have six different stories and never the same piece in the same spot; each `over` stack is over its piece's flue; a rifle round fired straight in through every open window flies 1.5 m past the wall line (the shooter placed so the muzzle's line, RIFLE_MUZZLE, is the window's middle); every open square of the robots' NavGrid, built from `mapColliders`, inside every room is reached from each of its doorways 1.4 m wide or more).
- **Hollow Wick, stage 2: the trees** (`world/tree-kinds.js` kinds and colliders, pure; `world/trees.js` models; `maps/hollow-wick-trees.js` the places, written by `tools/place-trees.mjs` (seeded: re-run it, never hand-edit); `maps/hollow-wick-tree-rules.js` `spotProblems`, the placement rules the tool and the tests share; tests/hollow-wick-trees.test.js). A map lists `trees: { trees: [{ x, z, kind, s, yaw }], stumps, logs: [{ x, z, length, radius, yaw }], drifts }`. Hollow Wick: the North Woods (74, clumps and gaps, the knolls' crests open), the West Woods (36), an old apple orchard in loose rows along the back trail, bare village elms (town, lanes, south terrace), old trees by the meetinghouse, willows/alders on the stream banks, the fork's big maple with the map's one orange leaf carpet, plus stumps, fallen logs and leaf drifts (litter browns, never red). Nothing on any path or its shoulder, in the stream or on its banks, on pads, decks, bases (6 m), steep ground (over 0.35), near other builders' set pieces (`KEEP_CLEAR`: the hanging tree 4 m+, skeletons, the drag trail, reed beds...), within 0.9 m of anything solid (every non-tree collider: headstones, walls, props, building walls), on a building's pad (the buildings make their pads: `baseY`, `padMargin`) or within 2 m of a spawn point (`map.bases[].points`, `ffaSpawns`). (The fenced orchard behind the rear houses is the field props' `orchardTree`; the trees' orchard is the old rows along the back trail.) Re-run `node tools/place-trees.mjs` after moving any prop, building or spawn. **Gameplay:** each trunk is a square collider its own width, 2.4 m tall (cover: rounds, pellets, low grenades meet it); canopies never block. Stumps and logs are low colliders marked `lowTop`: `rifle.js roundMeets` takes their real top, so a round (0.74 m over the ground) flies over a stump or thin log and meets a big one (height 0.9 x diameter). Robots' nav paths through the woods are checked in the test. **Drawing:** trunks, limbs (open-ended 4-6 sided cylinders), roots, stumps, logs, drifts and the carpet are plain meshes in their own group, all casting, merged per 24 m cell by `view.batch()` (one draw per cell: under view.static, shadowBySize would split each cell into casting and non-casting); leaves are opaque flattened icosahedra in four InstancedMeshes (the North Woods in two halves, the West Woods, the rest) with a colour per clump: 8 draws with shadows, culled by part. **Tree fade** (v0.985a, owner: under a tree its whole canopy and branches should go see-through, fading into the branches at the trunk, with no dither noise): standing under a tree's crown, or behind it on the line from your head to the camera, fades THAT WHOLE TREE for everyone the view draws (the roofs' shared slot list, characters outside buildings; world/roof-fade.js `treeFade`, `TREE_FADE`). Every tree's meshes are stamped as they are built with `treeAt` (trunk x, z, crown radius from its limbs' and clumps' reach, ground y: `crownOf`); batch() carries the stamp into the merged cells as a per-vertex attribute (merge-transformed.js `stamp`; stumps, logs, drifts and the carpet carry radius 0 and never fade) and the clumps as a per-instance one. Leaves: a clump whose tree fades goes to `canopy` 0.85 see-through as a whole; the opaque canopy draw drops it by its vertices (placed outside the view: no discard, so the early depth test stays) and a blended copy of the part (`fadeOverlay`, sharing its instances) draws only the faded clumps. Limbs: faded `limbs` 0.8 from `to` 4.2 m over the ground at the trunk, easing back to solid at `from` 1.6 m, so the branches fade into the trunk and its foot stays cover; the opaque cell cuts them out and each cell's blended copy draws them. The copies show only on frames when one of their trees is faded (`near: 'trees'`: each copy's trees, from its stamps, tested against everyone's line toward the camera; `prepareFades`). Materials: the canopy's two ('tree-canopy-fade', its `|overlay`), and each baked material's solid and clear copies ('tree-limbs-N', 'tree-limbs-N-clear' in `view.bakedMaterials`, so presets reach them). The drifts and beds the trees lay (`TREE_DRIFT_COLOURS`: litter browns, the fork maple's burnt orange #c0612b and a faded ochre #94803e, was #b08a3c) are in the contrast test. Hooks: map-kit `mapColliders` (`treeColliders(map.trees)`), world-build `makeHillTerrain` (`buildTrees`), rifle `roundMeets` (`lowTop`), hollow-wick.js (`trees:`).
- **Hollow Wick, stage 2: the open ground (field, farm, yards, fences, set pieces)** (`maps/hollow-wick-props.js` data: `HW_PROPS`, `HW_CROPS`, `HW_GROUND_LAYERS`, `DRAG_TRAIL`, `CROSSING_EXITS`; `world/hollow-props.js` models: `HOLLOW_TYPES`, spread into `PROP_TYPES`, built by `makeHollowProp` from makeProp; tests/hollow-wick-props.test.js):
  - **The scarecrow field:** the west half cut and stood in corn shocks (solid, 1.7 m, block sight); the east half five patches of dead standing stalks (Deadwater's crop system: they block sight, not shots, rustle and burn; 6 x 8 m or 5 x 8, 2 m lanes, 11 m from every crossing exit, 3 m clear of every footpath). A field may set `rows`/`cols` (Hollow Wick 3 x 2 sections, each a draw; Deadwater keeps 8 x 7) and its `stalk`/`bed` colours. On hills every stalk stands on the ground under it, the bed is draped (`crop-view.js` `drapeBed`) and the flames rise from the ground.
  - **Pieces:** two scarecrows (sack head, no brim, a clear T, straw and grey cloth; one faces the dam, one the bridge road), the empty cross, the abandoned ox cart (1.4 m), the corn crib and haystack (tall, block sight), woodpiles (1.2 m) with chopping blocks, the well and sweep on the green, troughs, post-and-rail fences (front yards, the farm yard, the orchard) and split-rail worm fences (the pasture, the field's west edge) in ~3 m panels that follow the ground (`fence()` in the data: gaps where paths and doors are), fieldstone walls, boulders and piles, the fork's marker stone, a small orchard of bare apple trees, and reeds on the banks. Reeds are `screen` props: their box blocks sight only (`playerOnly` + `walkOver`), so you walk and shoot through them. **Stage 4 audit fixes (v0.975a):** flat-based pieces settle to the low side of a slope instead of hanging off it (`world/settle.js settle(view, p, g, w, d)`: the group drops by how much lower the ground is under its footprint, sampled at 75% of its half extents; hollow-props.js `SETTLE` types, the table tombs and big stones, every breakable); the graveyard's fieldstone walls step with the ground stone by stone (`fieldWall(..., lift)`); the hanged man is solid (a 0.5 m box under him in `hangingTree`'s colliders); the well sweep's counterweight stone is solid and the well is turned so it lies off the road; the boulders stay inside their collider (no random turn on the big rock); the cordwood's chopping block (outside its collider) is two splits lying in front; the corn crib's roof is shingle courses with a dark hole over bare laths; the trough is open-topped with the water below its rim.
  - **Collider heights:** a collision box's fifth number is its height (map-kit.js; fences 1.1, woodpiles 1.2, cart 1.4, crib 2.6, haystack 2.8); without it a prop box is 1.2 as before. Lying pieces (skeletons, the trail) have `collisionBoxes: []`.
  - **Set pieces:** the hanging tree (a gnarled bare oak, trunk collides) with a gory hanged man (gore.js palette, rotted, no team colours) swaying slowly and an empty noose on a second branch (its crows are the live flock's: effects/crow-rules.js); the drag trail (aged blood decals draped on the ground from the main path into the North Woods, stopping dead, leaves over parts of it: one mesh on a small canvas atlas); the body pile on the west bank (about twelve rotted bodies with skeletons and bones, merged; its collider (v0.985a, owner: "collisions are weird here") covers the heap itself, 4 x 1.9 m from its dry side to the water's edge, 0.9 m high (it used to stand north of the heap, stopping you short there while you walked into the bodies from the sides; tests/wading.test.js lets it onto the bank), the lower bodies lie in the water, which runs faintly red round it; the ground round it tinted by a ground layer); the North Woods skeleton half-sunk in leaves and the field skeleton with its sickle (bones only, ivory, each different); the body in the rocking chair on the gambrel's stoop, rocking.
  - **Moving pieces** (the hanged man, the chair) are their own merged groups under the scene, moved from their meshes' `onBeforeRender` by the page clock (no renderer hook; nothing under the static root moves). `p.lift` raises the chair onto a stoop if the building's stoop is higher than the ground.
  - Everything else is static scenery merged by `batch()`. Positions start from the layout's marks, moved where the ground needed (the crib to the flat, the pile up the bank off the west-flank path, the woodpile off the main road); the test checks every piece: inside the fence, dry, off every path's centre line and the drawn road, 2.2 m from every pad side's middle (doors), 5 m from base centres, big pieces on slopes under 0.36 and level within 0.45 m, fences off walls.
- **Hollow Wick graveyard and fieldstone walls (stage 2, s2-graveyard):** the burying ground on Church Hill's terraces and the low fieldstone walls elsewhere. Data: `src/maps/hollow-wick-graveyard.js` (`GRAVEYARD_PROPS`, `FIELD_WALLS`, `GRAVE_SLOTS`, `GRAVEYARD_MARKS`), **written by `node tools/graveyard-layout.mjs`** (deterministic; rerun it after any terrain, path or pad change, `--check` only prints; it prints the grave count, the solid share and the worst spot for cover). Types and models: `src/world/graveyard.js` (`GRAVE_TYPES` spread into `PROP_TYPES`, `makeGrave` from `makeProp`). Pieces: slate `headstone`s (rounded head with shoulders, four shapes, some leaning, lichen #8b8a80 and moss #56603f on stones only) each over its `headstoneStump`; `grave` (the sunken turf and the footstone, laid on the slope), `fallenStone`, `tableTomb` (`scale` .8: a child's), `bigStone` (a double stone), `fieldWall2..6` (dry-laid, about 1 m, lighter caps; a type per length), `railPlot` (granite posts, wooden rails, only the posts solid; unused while the treads are too narrow), `freshMound`, `openGrave` (the hole is drawn, the ground is not cut; the shovel in the spoil) and `graveSkeleton` (bones only, slumped on a double stone). **Colliders carry heights:** a prop type's `coverHeight` is its collider's height (map-kit `mapColliders`; headstone .9, table tomb .8, walls 1.0, stump .35), so the low-cover rule (rounds meet low cover only at their own height) works on them; dressing has `collisionBoxes: []` (no collider: walked over, never stops a round or an orb). **Headstones break** (health 5, like crates): slate shards and chips (`graveBreak`, from `renderer.breakProp`) and a crack and a heavy thud (`graveThud`, from audio.js); the stump is its own solid prop and stays as a little low cover (it stops bodies, orbs and level rounds). Drawn through the usual paths: the solid pieces merge into the static batches, the headstones are batched per 24 m cell by `prop-instances.js` (hidden on break), so the whole graveyard is a few draws. Rules (tests/hollow-wick-graveyard.test.js): graves lie east-west (headstone at the west end, x - .78, facing east) in north-south ranks at least 2.5 m apart (1.2 m along a rank), clear of every path and its shoulder (the aisles included), the pads, base B's spawn ground and ground steeper than 0.3; about 40% of the cover solid; no tread spot (level ground off the aisles, a metre across) more than 3.5 m from hard cover (table tombs, big stones, walls, the hill's buildings). **The terraces' treads are narrow** (the earth banks between them take 2-3 m each, the aisles 3.2-4 m): about 35 graves fit, not the planned ~150; widening the treads (retaining walls or steeper banks at the terrace fronts) and rerunning the tool gives more. Walls elsewhere (layout WALLS): broken pieces round the fork, the base screens (a-yard, b-trail, c-side, c-lip), the scarecrow field's north edge and a lane wall; each run is built only where it is clear (never across a path), so they come out broken. Since v0.975a a run is also built only 0.35 m clear of every other prop's solid collider and 0.8 m clear of the stalk patches (the tool reads the map's other props: before, two walls stood through the fork's own and others through a stalk patch, a corn shock and a stone pile); the props file's own two fork walls are gone (the tool's fork runs stand there).
- **Hollow Wick, stage 2: crossings and mill wheel** (s2-crossings; looks in `maps/hollow-wick-crossings.js` as `map.crossings`, models in `render/crossing-decks.js`, `world/mill-wheel.js`, merged by `render/parts.js`): the bridge is an open timber bridge (weathered grey #6f6a62 planks across, four stringers and a cross beam under them that you see wading under, fieldstone abutments at both ends, low curb timbers you walk over, kingposts with short braces only at the ends, just outside the outline); the log a fallen eight-sided bark trunk with its flat top at the deck's height, a flared root end, a splintered crown end, snapped branch stubs out of its flanks and underneath (never over the top), moss on top and its root plate torn up on the south bank beside its end (clear of the end); the footbridge three long planks on bank sills and two braced trestles in the water. **Rules (tests/hollow-wick-crossings.test.js):** each deck's walk surface (planks, trunk) tops out at exactly `deck.h` and runs only over ground under it; nothing along a deck's long sides rises more than `CURB` (0.12 m) over it and there are no colliders on or by a deck (owner, 2026-09-26: you step off its sides into the water, so no rails, whatever the old covered-bridge plan said); each crossing is ONE vertex-coloured mesh with its own material, so the see-through fade covers all of it (add a crossing's new parts to its `Parts`, never as a separate mesh). The stream works (one static mesh; the one solid thing in the water is the wheel with its axle and bearing post and the sluice, `crossings.solid` boxes that map-kit.js turns into `streamWorks` colliders, allowed by tests/wading.test.js): the mill wheel's stone bearing block, outboard bearing post, race boards and raised sluice gate, and stones in the stream (shallow spots only, never in the ford's lane or within 1.5 m of a deck). (A timber crib dam with a spill strip exists for maps that set `crossings.dam`; Hollow Wick's dam is the terrain's stone `dam` level, a walk across the stream with retaining-wall faces.) The undershot wheel (x 22, z 19.5, radius 1.45, axle into the mill's foundation at z 17.25) turns slowly with the flow (its bottom going west) and drips (an instanced ring of 14 drops, not on Potato); `updateCrossingDecks` runs the spill and the wheel. Draws: one per crossing, one for the works, the wheel, its drops and the spill (each casts shadows except the spill and drops).
- **Hollow Wick's detail bar** (`tools/detail-density.mjs`: pieces per 1000 m² of playable ground (target 35), period types (45), and 38 x 26 m screens under 25 pieces): v0.960a stood at 36.5 per 1000 m², 71 types, 198 of 523 screens under 25 (Deadwater, counted the same way without its detail layers, has most screens at 10-20). `tools/place-detail.mjs` (seeded; writes `maps/hollow-wick-detail.js`, spread last into props) fills the sparsest screens with fieldstone piles, boulders, chopping blocks and troughs under every placement rule the breakables and props tests hold (doorways, pads and each side's middle, spawns, bases, targets, the drag trail, set pieces, slopes and uneven ground, 1.2 m from trunks, off stalk patches, and 4.5 m from any other of its pieces and the map's own piles, boulders and blocks so they never heap together): it fills up to the bar, 25 pieces a screen (v0.975a; it used to stop at 20). A stone pile collides as a cross of two boxes (1.8 x 0.8 and 1.2 x 1.3 m, hollow-props.js), the heap's round shape: one 1.8 x 1.3 m box stopped bodies 0.3-0.8 m short of the stones at its corners (v0.980a, stage 5 review; the pass re-run with it). Chopping blocks go only within 4.5 m of wood (a woodpile or cordwood; otherwise a stone pile) and troughs only on ground with a gradient of 0.1 at most (v0.980a, stage 5 review: a block alone in a field and a trough on a bank made no sense). With stage 5 merged: 42 pieces, 43.4 per 1000 m², 99 types, 31 screens under 25 by detail-density's count (none under 19: the rest are by the mill pond and on the targets' slope, where nothing may stand; the tool's own count, which drops a screen it cannot place in, says 9). Re-run it after moving props, buildings, spawns or trees, then `node tools/place-trees.mjs`; `--check` writes nothing and exits 1 if the file is stale (what it would write differs).
- **Hollow Wick, stage 5: ground marks** (s5-ground; `world/ground-marks.js` decides where (pure, no three.js), `render/ground-marks-view.js` draws, `render/ground-marks-atlas.js` paints the pictures; map data `groundMarks` from `maps/hollow-wick-ground-marks.js`; tests/ground-marks.test.js). Marks lying on the ground that say what went on here. Most are found from the map itself (every woodpile, chopping block, cordwood stack, haystack, well, trough, barn, mill and shed door, fieldstone wall and building corner); the data file holds only what the map cannot say (which roads carts used, where rock shows through them, where a cart turned in or a horse stood, the trail of bare feet).
  - **Ruts:** a strip under each wheel, 1.44 m apart (a farm cart's gauge), wandering a little off the path's line: the town street and down the slope to the fork (`town-road`), the fork down to the bridge and on up to the field (`bridge-road`), up Church Hill to the meetinghouse (`meeting-road`, older and grassed), and an older second pair on part of the street (Balanced up). Each fades in and out at its ends, stops short of every deck and breaks over rock (`ledges`: grey ledge showing through the road at the slope's wall gaps, the hill road's climb, south of the bridge). Standing water glints in them now and then; the horse's prints run between the wheels (Balanced up).
  - **Puddles** (11): in the ruts' low points (scored along each road: where a ramp runs out onto level ground, then the bends; at most `puddles` a road, 6 m apart), two by the well (away from the sweep), one by each trough on its open side with churned mud, a wet patch and the drinkers' prints (only where the ground is level, a gradient of 0.08 at most: v0.980a, a puddle on a slope read as a stain). Never in the stream. The dull sheen of the overcast is painted in, and their vertex colours are a little over 1 (no glossy material, no new program).
  - **Prints:** hoofprints where a horse stood (`trampled`: shoeing by the forge's east side, the barn door, the tavern's front) and along two cart tracks (`cartTracks`: off the street into the barn's west doors, across the yard to the forge); the digger's boot prints round the open grave; and bare feet from the body pile down the bank into the stream (`barefoot`), the steps shortening, the last ones sunk and holding water, the last at the water's edge. Nothing comes out on the far side.
  - **Scatter:** straw fanned out of the barn's and horse sheds' doors and round the haystack; chips (with bark and kindling) in front of every woodpile and cordwood stack and all round every chopping block; the forge's ash heap raked out behind it and cinders thrown from its sides (`ash`); grain spilled at the mill's east door and dribbled toward the path (`grain`); fresh earth round the open grave and the new mounds; worn ground outside the other doors (not the tomb's). A dead fire ring (any prop type like fireRing, firePit, campfire) gets ash and char: the woods' fire ring (stage 5 dressing) has them.
  - **Leaves** (litter browns only): drifted against each fieldstone wall's north side (a north-south wall: its west side; the other side when that one is a retaining wall's lip) and into building corners (south corners first: the roofs hide the north ones from the camera).
  - **Rules** (`markRules`, checked by the test for every mark): the mark's middle, corners and edge middles inside the fence, out of the water (the ground 3 cm over the water level), 0.35 m off decks, off the retaining-wall mask, never inside a building (past its walls' inner faces), 1 m off the drag trail, clear of the set pieces it does not belong to (hanging tree, skeletons, rocking chair), on ground under 0.62; raised bits also never inside anything solid. Marks have no collider (walked over: the map's colliders are unchanged), so the solid pieces' rules (door aprons, path lanes, spawn points, base middles) do not apply: ruts lie on the roads and straw at the doors on purpose. A spill, drift or patch that does not fit is tried smaller (chips and soil also nudged) before it is dropped; `placeGroundMarks(...).rejected` lists what was dropped and why.
  - **Drawing:** every flat mark is a small grid (vertices at most 0.5 m apart) draped on `drawnHeightAt` plus the preset's mesh error and 6 mm (as terrain-marks.js), its corners raised where a flat triangle would cut under a crest and any triangle over a wall's drop left out. All of them are ONE mesh (about 8k vertices): MeshLambertMaterial with the atlas and vertex colours with alpha (fades and tints), transparent, no depth write, alphaTest .02, polygon offset, renderOrder -1.1 (under burns, blood and the drag trail). The atlas: one canvas, 1024 px (512 on Potato and Performance), four seamless 16 m rut strips and 38 cells, painted with shapes gathered by colour into one path each (about 50 ms); every see-through pixel then takes its cell's average colour (so mipmaps never darken a pale edge) and it is uploaded as a DataTexture. The raised bits (about 1,070 straws, chips, bark, kindling, cinders, clods and leaves) are instances of one six-faceted low cone on the baked 'plain' material with instance colours (the ground detail's program), in `performanceDetails` (hidden on Potato, left out of Extreme's AO pass).
  - **Presets:** each mark has a tier (`MARK_TIER`). Potato draws the stories (ruts, puddles, bare feet, straw, chips, ash, grain, ledges: 3.7k triangles), Performance adds prints, tracks, mud and leaf drifts (5.8k), Balanced up everything (8.8k); raised bits none / 22% / 58% / all. A preset change rewrites the index list (marks in draw order, those over the tier left out) and cuts the bits' count, from the meshes' own onBeforeRender when `view.qualityName` changes: no renderer hook, nothing rebuilt.
  - **Cost:** 2 draws, no shadows cast, no per-frame work, about 0.2 s at load (placement 70 ms, drape 25 ms, atlas 100 ms, SwiftShader box). On Performance at the town: +2 draws and +7k triangles; the decals change 6-7% of the screen's pixels at the busiest spots (one cheap Lambert fragment each). No program links in play (program-check: 0 on Performance and Extreme). Measuring spot: `s5-ground` (the green) in tools/terrain-spots.mjs.
  - **Hooks:** world-build.js `makeHillTerrain` (`this.groundMarks = buildGroundMarks(this, map)`, after the leaves), hollow-wick.js (`groundMarks: HW_GROUND_MARKS`), tools/terrain-spots.mjs (the `s5-ground` spot). Re-run the test after moving props, buildings, paths or the stream.
- **Hollow Wick, stage 5: life** (s5-life; `world/hollow-life.js` types, models and motion, `maps/hollow-wick-life.js` the places and the buildings' flags, tests/hollow-life.test.js with its placement rules in tests/hollow-life-rules.js). A few things that move, each subtle and cheap:
  - **The goat** (Black Phillip): a big black billy with long pale horns sweeping back, a beard and two amber eyes (a vertex colour over 1, so they catch the dusk light without a glowing material), in a small wattle pen in the farm's back yard (`goatPen`: four woven hurdles, 0.95 m colliders, low cover; 3.0 x 1.9 m inside, so every spot in it is within 1 m of a hurdle and nobody is ever spawned there; trampled mud, hay, straw, a bucket). `GoatMind` (pure, tested) grazes (neck down, pulling at the straw), looks up and about, flicks an ear or wags its tail now and then and takes a step or two, its footprint (horns and tail included) always inside the pen (`fit` shuffles it in as it turns). The nearest player the view draws (you, every visible avatar and robot) within 14 m (`GOAT.notice`) makes it stop, prick its ears and turn its head, then after a moment its body, to stare at them until they are past 16 m for 1.5 s. Cosmetic: nothing collides with it or hurts it.
  - **The washing** (`laundryLine`: two forked posts, 1.95 m colliders; the line and linens walked under; a basket and a dropped peg): a sheet, a man's shirt, a small child's gown and a dark apron hanging by one peg, each a small grid bent by the wind every frame (swinging out and lifting toward the hem, a wave along it, a billow, the hem trailing east), out of step with each other. Front and back are separate faces (the material is one-sided).
  - **Stick effigies** (`effigy`, no collider): six figures of pale lashed twigs (about 1.3 m, arms 0.9 m across, twine at the joints) hanging on strings from dead limbs reaching 1.4-2.2 m out of trees along the North Woods' track, 1.8-2.2 m off its middle, where nothing is between them and the camera (found by ray casts from the figure to the camera through the trees' limbs and canopies), turning slowly on their strings (`effigyPose`: a twisted string, round one way and back over 18-34 s) and swaying.
  - **From the buildings** (flags set by `withLife` in hollow-wick.js: a window's `loose` (its hinge side), a chimney's `smoke`): a loose shutter on the cape, the second saltbox and the farm, hanging from its one top hinge (tilted) and swinging out from the wall and back, now and then a gust swinging it well out or slamming it back against the wall with a bounce (`stepShutter`); the tavern's sign swinging gently on its hooks under the arm (`stepSign`, a pendulum pushed by the wind); a thin grey wisp from the lit cape's chimney, rising, leaning east, growing and fading (someone is home), fading with its own roof when that lifts (v0.980a: `ChimneySmoke` finds the flue's roof once it is registered and scales each puff by its opacity). The steppers step from the time a state was last stepped (`s.t`) to the clock's, so the view and the soundscape can both step it (v0.980a); each notes what the soundscape plays: a shutter's `bang` (the speed it met the wall at, over `SHUTTER.knock` 0.15 rad/s) and `bangAt`, the sign's `turn` (how far out it turned at a swing's end) and `turnAt`. Their state fields start as numbers (-Infinity), so nothing is boxed a frame. The air (`windAt`, `gustAt`, `blow`) is one slow swell with a gust in every 13 s window that reaches a place later the further east it is.
  - **How it stays cheap:** every moving thing is ordinary geometry in the static scenery's own vertex-coloured material (`view.bakedMaterial('plain')`: nothing new compiles) and the pieces near each other share one mesh: a region (the town, the farm, the woods; `LIFE_REGIONS`, the nearest wins), one draw each (and one in the shadow pass) with its own culling sphere. Its vertices are rewritten on the CPU from each piece's rest shape when the mesh is drawn (`onBeforeRender`: a region off screen costs nothing): a rigid piece (the goat's body, neck, head, ears, tail and legs; a shutter; the sign; an effigy) is one matrix, a linen a grid; the effigies' limbs are pieces that never move (written once). About 2,800 vertices in all; measured per rewrite (desktop, Node): the farm about 50-70 us, the woods 25, the town 12. Potato rewrites them 20 times a second, Performance 30, the rest every frame (`LIFE_DETAIL.step`). Nothing is allocated per frame: time and the players reach the pieces through objects (`clock`, `mind.player`, `AIR`), the poses build matrices in place from a Float64Array (`turnX/Y/Z`, `shift`), and all four linens share one shape of spec object, so V8 boxes no numbers either (the test measures the heap over 1000 frames). The smoke is one instanced draw of camera-facing soft quads (`ChimneySmoke`: its own small MeshBasicMaterial patched for a soft round edge and a per-puff opacity, program 'hollow-chimney-smoke', compiled with everything else at load), its puffs a pure function of time (`smokePuff`), drawn after the roofs' fade (`renderOrder` 60: a fading roof painted over it); `LIFE_DETAIL.smoke` puffs: none on Potato, 10 on Performance, 16/22/28 on Balanced/Quality/Extreme. Draw calls at 400 x 250 on Performance (whole frame, shadow pass included), before -> after: the `s5-life` spot (the farm's back yard) 116 -> 118, `town` 164 -> 165, `north-woods` 94 -> 96; the frame's draw time, life shown and hidden on alternate frames, differs by less than the noise (SwiftShader). `tools/program-check.mjs performance|extreme static hollow-wick`: 0 programs linked in play.
  - **Hooks:** map-kit.js (`LIFE_TYPES` into `PROP_TYPES`), world-build.js `makeProp` (`makeLifeProp`), hollow-wick.js (`HW_LIFE` into props; `withLife(...)` round the buildings), colonial-buildings.js (the import; `sash` leaves a `loose` window's static shutter out and marks its hinge, `userData.lifeShutter`; `tavernSign` hangs the sign and its hooks in a group marked `userData.lifeSwing = 'sign'` with its pivot at the arm; `chimney` returns its first flue's top and a `smoke` chimney's is passed on; `makeColonialBuilding` calls `takeLifeParts(view, g, smoke)` once the building is placed, which takes the marked groups out of the static group before the static merge), tools/terrain-spots.mjs (the `s5-life` spot). Placement rules (tests/hollow-life-rules.js, a copy of tools/place-detail.mjs's `problems` on top of the breakables' rules, the slope rule for solid pieces only) hold for every piece; re-run the test after moving props, buildings, spawns, paths or trees. Not done: sounds (a shutter's bang, the sign's creak, the goat's bleat would go through `sound.hollow`).
- **Hollow Wick, stage 5: the static dressing** (s5-props; `world/hollow-dressing.js`: `DRESSING_TYPES` spread into `PROP_TYPES`, models by `makeDressing` from one dispatch line in world-build.js `makeProp`; data `maps/hollow-wick-dressing.js`, spread last into the map's props so no other prop's index or id moves; rules `tests/hollow-dressing-rules.js`; tests/hollow-dressing.test.js). 29 period pieces of 24 types, each where it would have been used and left:
  - **Puritan justice at the meetinghouse:** the stocks (a plank bench, the two-board leg stock, a pair of worn buckled shoes still in the holes, rotten things thrown at them) and the pillory (the head-and-hands board on a post, its upper half left lifted a crack) at the summit's south-west corner by the west-flank path; the whipping post (worn pale at shoulder height, an iron ring and a frayed rope end, the earth trodden bare round it) beside the west door; a two-step granite mounting block at the portico's east end.
  - **The town:** a hitching rail on the street before the tavern (east of its door) and one by the smithy's west door (rubbed pale where the reins went, a cast shoe; the churned mud on the horses' side is ground marks, v0.980a: `trampled` patches in maps/hollow-wick-ground-marks.js, where the model's own flat patches floated on the slope), the smithy's grindstone on its trestle beside that, the forge's slag and cinders raked out east of the open forge, a hay wagon heaped with hay (a fork stuck in it) drawn up beside the barn's west doors, rain barrels under the eaves at three house corners (the cape, the lit cape, the second saltbox), a plough left where it stopped (two furrow ridges behind it) and a harrow north of the farm, a scythe leaning on the pasture fence.
  - **The graveyard's edge:** the parish bier outside the hearse house with a plain six-board coffin on it, open and empty (a strip of shroud caught on the rim); the coffin's lid leaning on the hearse house's west wall by the open grave; the digger's barrow of earth with a mattock across it by the open grave; an unlit pierced-tin lantern and its spilled wax on a table tomb.
  - **The stream and the mill:** a skiff pulled half out on the mill pond's south bank (stern afloat, water in it, an oar in it and one on the bank, the painter to a stake); a wicker eel pot at the water's edge below the dam, tied to a stake; a washtub with a carved wooden washboard (no metal: the period) and wet linen on the bank below the farm; two spare millstones leaning on the mill's east wall.
  - **The woods:** a ring of blackened stones round a long-dead fire (ash, charred sticks, small bones, a pot on its side) on the knoll's open crest in the North Woods; two cairns flanking the back trail where it enters the North Woods; in the West Woods by the west-flank path, a collapsed lean-to (the fork still standing, the ridge pole down, branches slid off, old bracken and a rag of blanket) and a deer's carcass under the tree beside it (ribs, a young buck's skull, scattered legs, scraps of hide, an old stain): whoever camped there never came back. Under the canopies these show as the canopy fades round you.
  - **Gameplay:** all solid scenery (health null, never breaks). Colliders carry honest heights: stocks .7, the pillory's and whipping post's posts 2.1 and 2.5, mounting block .7, hay wagon 1.5 (low cover), rain barrels .95 (their water, and the washtub's, just under the rim: v0.980a, it stood over them), grindstone 1, plough .6, bier 1.05, digger's barrow .6, washtub .45, millstones 1.1, cairns .7, lean-to .8, the coffin lid 1.4 (v0.980a: a 1.6 m board bodies walked into); the rowboat collides only with its dry part, the bow to just past the middle (.6; v0.980a: pulled half a metre up the bank so the box could reach back that far and a wading body no longer walks through the hull). **Knee-high pieces are `lowTop`** (v0.980a, stage 5 review: `rifle.js roundMeets` counts any box under a round's height as 0.94 m tall unless it is `lowTop`, so a round took a washtub for full cover and a body behind it could not be hit): the type's `lowTop: true` (map-kit.js passes it to the prop's colliders) makes a round meet the box only below its real top, as for stumps and logs: the stocks, mounting block, plough, digger's barrow, rowboat, washtub and cairns here, and the chopping blocks, troughs and the well sweep's counterweight (hollow-props.js) and headstone stumps (graveyard.js); and robots' `shotClear` skips a `lowTop` box lower than `TERRAIN.roundHeight`, so they shoot over what their rounds fly over (no `lowTop` on Deadwater: its replay is untouched). tests/hollow-dressing.test.js fires level rounds across every one from four sides. The hitching rails are `screen` props (map-kit makes their boxes playerOnly): they stop bodies and robots, rounds pass under the rail. Walked over (`collisionBoxes: []`): the slag heap, harrow, scythe, coffin lid, lantern, eel pot, fire ring and deer. Nothing new blocks sight.
  - **Placement rules** (`dressingProblems`; v0.980a adds two, from the stage 5 review): **no squeeze** (a piece's box is either under 0.7 m from a building's or a retaining wall, so nobody passes, or at least 1.4 m, a robot's lane: at 0.7-1.4 m a player slipped through where robots had to go the long way; `DRESSING_RULES.squeeze`; the grindstone moved to (18.9, 4.6), the hay wagon drawn up against the barn at x 45.9 with its ladders and dropped hay kept off the wall, the plough to (-46.3, 30)), and **window lanes outside** (a round fired out through an open window flies 1.5 m past the wall before anything that meets rounds can stop it, as windowZones keeps inside; a knee-high lowTop piece may stand there). The breakables' `placementProblems` and the detail pass's checks (copied from tools/place-detail.mjs `problems`: spawns 1.1 m, doorway aprons 2.4 m, 0.35 m from solids and 1.2 m from trunks, stumps and logs, level ground, pads, sides' middles, bases, targets, slopes, the drag trail, the set pieces), plus 3 m from every deck's end, out of the ford, 0.4 m from every grave and tomb, and no collider in the water. A piece that only makes sense touching something says so in its data and is checked for exactly that: `at: <building>` (leans on it, stands under its eaves or before it: that building's 1 m / door-side / pad rules give way to its real doorways' aprons and a gap to its walls that is either touching, under 0.15 m, or the usual 0.35), `bank` (the boat and the eel pot reach the water; the boat's box stays dry, its middle outside the banks), `on: [x, z]` (the lantern on its tomb, the scythe on its fence panel), `by: 'open grave'` (inside the open grave's keep-clear marks but 0.5 m clear of the grave and mounds). Where the breakables' rules measure a piece by its radius near a retaining wall, a long piece is measured again by its outline. The test names the rule a piece breaks; add a piece the same way.
  - **Cost:** about 10k triangles in all, nothing that moves, no per-frame work. Every part is a plain colour through view.box/cylinder/mesh (the polyhedra and extrusions given an identity index), so the pieces merge into static batches their 40 m cells already had: the same batch keys with and without them (the test builds the map both ways), so no new draws; no new programs (tools/program-check.mjs performance and extreme: 0). On Performance at the town spot: +11k triangles, the same static draws in the camera and the shadow pass; the north-woods spot: +6k. Detail bar (tools/detail-density.mjs): 38.3 -> 40.5 pieces per 1000 m², 71 -> 95 types, screens under 25 pieces 159 -> 107.
  - **Keep in mind:** `node tools/place-detail.mjs --check` reports its file stale once the dressing is in (the dressing fills screens it would fill); re-run it once stage 5 is merged, then the tests. After moving a building, path, spawn, tree or prop near a piece, run tests/hollow-dressing.test.js.
- **Hollow Wick, stage 3: the leaves** (s3-leaves; `world/leaf-carpet.js` placement and the carpet, `effects/leaf-fx.js` `LeafFX` the moving leaves, `buildLeaves(view, map)` from world-build `makeHillTerrain` after the trees and the ground detail, as `view.leafFX`; map data `leafLitter: { woods: WOODS }`; tests/leaves.test.js). Adds to stage 1's leaf bits and twigs (terrain-details.js) and the trees' drifts and the fork maple's carpet (trees.js), never repeats them.
  - **The carpet:** thick fallen leaves under the North Woods and the West Woods: a patch of four cupped leaves per instance (16 triangles), `CARPET_PEAK` 5.2 patches per m² at full, thickest under the crowns (`canopyAt`: within 3.4 m x the tree's scale of a trunk standing in the wood) and thinning over the last 4 m in to the outline and 1.5 m past it (`woodsAt`); thin (`TRACK` 22%) on the tracks through the woods (paths with `colourMix: 0`, woods-track and back-trail) and never where stage 1's `detailClearance` says no (worn paths, stream, pads, props, trunks, slopes over 0.5), so every way through stays readable. Litter browns with maple yellow, burnt orange and ochre mixed in (12% at the edge, up to 38% under a crown); never rust or red. About 4,070 patches, stored in a random order; a preset draws a prefix (`LEAF_CARPET`: Potato 14%, Performance 34%, Balanced 58%, Quality 82%, Extreme all), so about 9k / 22k / 38k / 53k / 65k triangles with both woods in view. One InstancedMesh per wood (2 draws, usually one in view), no shadows cast, no per-frame work, on `bakedMaterial('plain')` with instance colours (stage 1's ground-detail program: nothing new compiles). Placement takes about 0.25 s at load (most of it building stage 1's keep-clear test, shared with the kicks).
  - **Falling leaves:** leaves let go of canopy clumps (read back from the trees' instanced canopies) inside the camera's window (`LEAF_WINDOW` +-20 x +-15 m round the focus), `rate` per second with ten or more clumps in view, and drift down spinning and swaying with a light eastward air, land, lie 1-3 s and shrink away. Any canopy colour falls; rust and red shrink out by 0.45 m over the ground and never lie there. Pools (`LEAF_FX`): none on Potato, 18 / 32 / 54 / 84.
  - **Kicked up:** every body (you from `sim.player`, others and robots from `view.remotePlayers`, as the stream's wading reads them; not in the water, only in the camera's window) walking faster than 1 m/s through litter (`litterField().at`: the woods, less on tracks, 15% where the carpet keeps off; the maple's carpet; the drifts) lifts `step` leaves every 0.26 s, a dodge's start `dodge` leaves thrown along it; `renderer.explosion` (orb volleys, grenades) in litter throws `blast` leaves up to 6 m/s. Litter colours and the yellows and oranges only. They flutter down, settle and fade after 2-4.5 s. Pools 14 (Potato) / 28 / 48 / 76 / 112. (Each leaf's colour is parsed once and kept on it, `l.col`: no colour parsing per leaf per frame.)
  - **Cost:** the falling and kicked leaves share one InstancedMesh (a double-sided 8-triangle leaf, capacity 196, one draw only while a leaf is out, not frustum culled). A frame walks only the live leaves (a height lookup each) and a few bodies; measured under 0.3 ms at the 95th percentile on SwiftShader. A preset change (the view's `qualityName`) re-thins the carpet and trims the pools; `renderer.reset`/`clearDebris` clear them. `aoExcluded` leaves the carpet and the leaves out of Extreme's occlusion pass, like the ground detail.
  - **Hooks:** world-build.js (import, `this.leafFX = buildLeaves(this, map)`), renderer.js (`leafFX?.update(sim, fdt)` after the breakables, `leafFX?.blast(e)` in `explosion`, `leafFX?.clear()` in `reset` and `clearDebris`, the AO list), hollow-wick.js (`leafLitter`).
- **Hollow Wick, stage 3: crows and soundscape** (s3-sound). Glue: `src/hollow-ambience.js` (`hollowAmbience(map, view, sound)`, made in main.js right after the view, before the shader warm-up; null on every other map; main.js feeds it every event of yours and everyone else's, `update(renderDelta, sim)` per drawn frame while playing, `reset()` on restart).
  - **Crows** (`effects/crow-rules.js`: the rules, no three.js; `effects/crows.js`: drawn). `crowPerches(map, mapProps(map), heightAt)` lists every perch from the map data: roof ridges (every ~1.6 m, clear of chimneys, the forge's stack and the belfry), chimney caps, the meetinghouse's belfry cap, upright headstones, table tombs, rail-fence posts, split-rail stakes, wall tops, eight branches of the hanging tree (the three where its model once carried crows that never flew included: every crow there is the flock's now) and four torsos on the body pile (`BODY_PILE_PERCHES`, `favoured`: the first `CROWS.favoured` 2 crows are seated there, a crow picking a perch is drawn to them while they are short, `favouredIn` sends the nearest idle crow back every 8-20 s once nobody is within 9 m and 25 s after they were scared off, and the gathering never takes them; design: "crows sit on it and fly off when someone comes near"). `CrowFlock`: `CROWS.counts` perched per preset (Potato 5, Performance 8, Balanced 12, Quality 16, Extreme 20) plus up to 1-3 overhead crossings; a preset change adds or retires crows. **Scatter**: a shot's line (28 m along the shooter's aim), an impact, a hit, a break, a death within `CROWS.scatter` 6 m (blasts 6 m + their radius) sends them up: a climb away, a few turns round, then a glide to a free perch 12-34 m off and 9 m from every player and the scare. A living player within 5 m (a crow on the ground or a low perch) or 3.5 m (roofs and branches) does too; going into a building sends its roof's crows off (the roof fades) and no crow sits or lands on the roof over you (`usable()`); a broken perch prop's crow leaves and nobody lands there while it is down (`syncBroken(sim.props)` twice a second from hollow-ambience.js, besides the `propBreak`/`propRestore`/`mapReset` events, so a reset, a restore or a late join's snapshot all come right). Players the view hides from you (`view.remote.avatars` root not visible) scare nothing: a crow taking off would give them away. Another player's Static stream (`sprayStart`, all a remote stream sends) counts as gunfire. **Quiet**: after gunfire no idle caws for `CROWS.hush` 24 s; every 45-110 s (`CROWS.silence`) a last two or three caws and then all of them silent at once for 18-35 s (`silentUntil`), and with the soundscape's silences (`quiet(seconds)`, from `HollowSound.onSilence` via hollow-ambience.js). **The dead**: 7-14 s after a death (not in the water, never under a roof (`indoorsAt`: crows.js asks the buildings); later while the guns go on or someone stands over it) the 1-3 nearest crows come down round the body with a caw or two each, hop and peck (the bird tips forward) for 28-50 s, and go when anyone comes within 6 m. One body per player, as remote-corpses.js keeps them: a visit is keyed by whose death it was (main.js passes the slot through `otherEvent(e, shooter, slot)`; yours is `'you'`), so that player's next death drops the old visit and sends the crows at the old body off, while robots dying elsewhere leave yours alone. The flock flies on under the death screen (main.js updates it while `deathActive`). A crow that flew off the map comes back gliding in from 20 m off (`returns()`), never popping onto a perch in view. Crows far (30 m+) from every player are moved, one every 5-11 s, flying in from off screen to a perch 9-22 m from you, so a few crows still fill the screen. Drawn as two instanced meshes (bodies, wings: 2 draws, about 26 crows' worth allocated at load, no shadows, frustum culling off), 0.4 m long, 0.75 m across, body #2a2c30, wings and tail #1e2024, folded when sat, beating or held flying. `tools/program-check.mjs performance static hollow-wick`: 0 programs linked in play.
  - **Sound** (`src/audio-hollow.js`, `HollowSound`, attached as `sound.hollow`; audio.js calls it from `start` (Deadwater's desert wind is disconnected here and these beds take its place), `event` (gunfire you can hear ducks everything below at once, held 1.6 s, back slowly) and `update` (the beds follow you; your footstep in water is a wetter one, `step`; the water's own `wadeStep` sound is everyone else's only, so yours is never doubled). Ambient channel, through one duck gain: a colder, thinner wind with gusts (a swell, a thin whistle rising over it, a fall; `HOLLOW_SOUND.gust` 6-15 s apart), dry leaves near the woods and trees (louder in a gust), the stream's babble near the water, the weir's rush by the mill dam, softer indoors (`bedLevels(map, x, z)`). Placed one-shots (audio.js `hearingLevel`): crows' idle caws (duller far off), alarm calls and wing claps when they scatter, the rope creaking at each end of the hanged man's sway, the mill wheel's axle groan each quarter turn and its paddles slapping, a single bell toll from the belfry every 95-210 s (never in the quiet after gunfire), the loose shutters slamming against their walls and the tavern sign's iron hooks squealing (v0.980a; `swings`: the life's shutters and sign within `HOLLOW_SOUND.swings.reach` 45 m are stepped from here as well as by the view, whichever comes first, `lifeOf(view).stir`, so one behind you still bangs; a bang as loud as the square of how hard it met the wall, `slam` 1.4 rad/s full, carried 1.25x; the sign squeals only at the end of a swing wider than 0.03 rad; nothing stale plays after a pause), and a faint digging by the open grave (the graveyard layout's `openGrave`: a spade's gritty scrape and the dull fall of the throw, strokes 1.4-2.4 s apart in runs of 4-9, 6-16 s between runs, heard within 34 m; it stops when you come within 11 m and stays stopped until you are 20 m off and 25 s have passed: `HOLLOW_SOUND.dig`). **Silences** (design, owner's additions): every 70-160 s (the first after 45-100 s) the beds and every placed sound fall to 2% over 0.6 s, hold 3-6 s and come back (`silences`), never in the quiet after gunfire; the crows are told and stay quiet longer. Effects channel: the water's own sounds from `view.waterFX.onSound(kind, x, z, strength)` (wadeStep, wadeDodge, splash, spout, plop). Tests: tests/hollow-crows.test.js.
- **Hollow Wick, still to come (after v0.975a, stages 4 and 5 done):** a stronger dusk if my Lord wants it (see the dusk look's knobs), the graveyard's size (low terrace walls: the owner's call), the bases' long open sightlines (25-30 m: a layout question) and 31 screens still under 25 authored pieces (the sparsest 19, by the mill pond and on the targets' slope; the ground marks fill them to the eye but are not counted). (Done in v0.990a: the stronger dusk, and the release to the menus.) Asked for and not yet done: about twice the graves with varied turns, fewer blocking obstacles (light furniture and clutter breakable or walk-over where it makes sense), and varied turns on the other props. Known small gaps: a grenade thrown from under a deck flies and lands on its top (the simulation's grenade takes `heightAt`; weapons code); the graveyard holds about 33 graves (the terraces' flat treads are narrow; low terrace walls would make room, the owner's call); robots cannot path into a narrow way round some colonial furniture (tests/solid-furniture.test.js leaves those rooms to tests/colonial-interiors.test.js). (spawn-check.mjs now counts building walls as well as sight-blocking props: from above you see over a wall, but it stops every round.)
- **Not built yet:** the hidden-ground shade, low cover seen over from above, corpses tilted to the slope, flat rings (blast, hex) draped over hills. Props, players and robots standing in hill shade still get full sun (the hook is described under Ground look); faceted or smooth terrain shading is the owner's call.

### Hollow Wick's buildings (colonial style; stage 2)
- **Data:** `maps/hollow-wick-buildings.js`: `hollowWickBuildings(BUILDING_PADS)` makes one building per pad (same id, x, z, w, d, angle; `baseY` = the pad's h; the mill's `padMargin` .5 / `padBlend` 1.5), so the terrain spec has no `pads` of its own (terrain-bake.js `terrainPads` makes them from the buildings; re-bake after moving one). `hollowWickBuildingProps` adds the solid parts outside walls: the meetinghouse portico's half walls and the forge's hearth, anvil and tub (prop types in `world/colonial-parts.js` `COLONIAL_TYPES`, spread into `PROP_TYPES`).
- **Fields:** `style: 'colonial'` (drawn by `world/colonial-buildings.js`, never by Deadwater's `makeBuilding` path); `height` is the eave (the walls' collider height); `roof: { kind: gable | saltbox (ridge toward the front, the long slope north) | gambrel | shed (`high`: back/front) | mound, axis: x | z (the ridge runs along the width or the depth), rise }`; `chimneys: [{ at, across, kind: brick | stone, w, d }]` (or `over: <piece kind>` in place of `at`: stood over that piece's flue as the interior placed it, s5-interiors); `variant` (a house's story, s5-interiors: laying-out, weaver, kitchen, nursery, parlour, farm); `shutDoors: [{ side, offset, width }]` (a door drawn shut on the outer face, no opening: the farm's back door); `windows` (`boarded`: shuttered shut, solid; `lit`: glowing glass; `collapsed`: a fallen wall, a low heap); `features` (belfry, portico, tavern-sign, forge-stack, hay-door, loft-door, granite-face); `open` (open sheds: beams over the openings, no doors. For you inside one it is a room like any other since v0.980a (owner: the little sheds gave no zoom-in and no grey-out): the camera comes in (renderer `cameraRoom`), the shroud greys what its walls hide (vision.js), entities outside the sight lines are clipped (interior-visibility.js) and the fog frame counts it indoors; v0.975a had all of that skip `sim.interior.open`. To everyone else it stays outdoors: whoever stands in one opens the roof over them (roof-fade.js). The simulation's aiming rules always counted it as a room); `stone` (fieldstone to that height: the mill 1.8 m, the tomb all round to its eave); `stoopRun: { side: m }` (the stoop runs on that far past the door on its +along side: the gambrel's, where the rocking chair sits, `lift: .18`); `finish: 'boards'` (the barn). **`openings: [{ side, offset, width }]`** (map-kit.js `localOpenings`) adds doorways anywhere on a side, beside `doors` (one in the middle of each listed side, `doorWidth` wide): the horse sheds' four bays, the hearse house's and woodshed's narrow doors.
- **interiorStyle** names (shared with the interiors): colonial-house (capes, saltboxes, gambrel, farm), colonial-lit-house (the lit cape, a laying-out room: its front window at offset -2.6 glows; the lantern stands on that sill), colonial-tavern, meetinghouse, smithy, barn, gristmill, shed (woodshed, hearse house), horse-sheds, tomb. The forge has none (an open shed; its parts are props). Until the interiors' styles land, `detailed-interiors.js` gives these its generic fallback.
- **Drawing:** built in the building's frame and stood on the pad at baseY, turned. Walls are cut exactly as `buildingWalls` cuts them; clapboard is one strip per run of wall per board (about 0.26 m); windows are 9-over-6 sash (dark glass, a broken pane here and there, a shutter hanging or shut); doors are plank leaves standing open outward; house doors have a plank stoop under a shed hood. Shingles are laid in courses of runs 1.2-3.2 m long in three tones of the roof's colour; a missing one is a 0.25-0.6 m gap in its run showing the dark board (v0.980a: the whole run went, long black slots on the lighter roofs), a few are lifted. Everything above the eaves (roof, gables, chimneys, the belfry, hoods, the portico's pediment) is the roof group, registered exactly like Deadwater's roofs (`roofs`: doors, reach, depth prepass, one vertex-coloured material, two draws), so it fades when you go in or stand in a doorway. Roof tones (`COLONIAL`, map-ui audit: two near-identical dark greys, darker than the ground, made the town one slab): each building its own weathered shingle, lighter than before: `shingle` #4d4842 (older, dark: the lit cape, the hearse house), `shingle2` #5a554e, `silver` #6f6b64 (barn, saltbox, woodshed), `brownGrey` #5d544a (tavern, farm), `moss` #545440 (the cape and the mill, damp), `cedar` #6b4f3c (the one newer roof, saltbox-2), `soot` #403b36 (smithy, forge); the meetinghouse keeps its painted #9a968c, the tomb its turf. **Roofs never hide a character standing outside under them** (v0.975a, `world/roof-fade.js`, stage 4 audit: the portico's pediment and the stoops' hoods hid the hat of anyone on them, an opponent's too). Until s6-roofs that was a see-through patch about a metre round the point of the roof between the camera and each character's head (smooth since v0.985a); **now a roof fades by whole sections** (s6-roofs, owner: "make it bigger and make it make the whole front section of the roof transparent"). **Sections** (colonial-buildings.js, as the roof is built: `tag` marks each piece before the roof is merged): each slope of the main roof (`slope+` the roof's +Z side of the ridge, `slope-` the other: a gable's two, a saltbox's short front and long back, a gambrel's pair on each side as one; a lean-to's one `slope`), each gable end (`gable+`/`gable-`, the barn's hay door and the mill's loft door with theirs), each door's hood (`hood0`, ...), the portico, the belfry, the tomb's `mound`; a chimney goes with the slope it stands on, and one on the ridge and the ridge cap with the slope turned from the camera (the one facing more to the north, `eaveFacing`: the side they hide). Hollow Wick has 85. **One table:** every vertex of the baked roof carries its section's index (`roofSection`, a float attribute: `batch()` keeps sections apart and passes the number on, `bakeColors`' `stamp` writes it) into ONE uniform table for every roof (`view.roofSections.values`, `SECTION_FADE.slots` 256 as 64 vec4s read in the vertex shader; index 0 never fades; a roof that did not fit would never fade, with a warning). **Decided on the CPU once a frame** (`prepareFades` -> `updateSections`, before each render): for each character in the shared list (outside every building; since v0.990a an open shed's occupants open nothing either, so nobody's roof patch gives away someone in a shed: the one inside has the shed as their room and its roof lifts whole), first the roof's whole bounds, then each section's shapes in the building's frame (a slope or a gable end is a slab whose top follows the roof line, `thick` 0.3 m over it; anything else a box round its pieces): a section fades when the character stands under one of its boxes or it hides them from the camera, the line from their waist (0.9 m) or head (1.6 m) toward that view's camera coming within `reach` (0.3 m, a little under the body's 0.38 radius) of the section and being under its top there. So: under the eaves or hugging a wall, on a stoop, under the portico, or behind a roof (toward the top of the screen the camera looks in lower, and the line from someone far up the screen crosses the whole house, so both slopes go). A door's **hood takes the face of the roof on its side along** (the slope over an eave side, the gable end over a gable side), and so does the **portico** (the meetinghouse's front slope): at a door the whole front goes. A brim grazing a gable's overhang from a side door fades only that hood and gable end. **Eased:** a section moves to its target over `fadeIn` 0.16 s and back over `fadeOut` 0.24 s, smoothstepped, in real seconds (at most a tenth a frame); faded it is `strength` 0.85 see-through. **Drawn:** the roof's own material (`colonial-roof-sections`, both variants: opaque, and blended while it fades as a whole) moves a faded section's vertices outside the view (no fragments and no discard: the patch's per-pixel loop and discard are gone from every roof pixel), and its blended copy (`fadeRoofMeshes(..., sections)`: `fadeOverlay`, all sharing `roofOverlayMaterial`) draws only those, at 1 - fade x 0.85; the copy shows only while one of its roof's sections is faded or still fading (`near: 'sections'`) and stays hidden while the roof fades as a whole (you inside or in its doorway: renderer.js's `opacity` path, unchanged). A faded section still casts its shadow. The map card's picture is taken with the table cleared. **Gameplay guard:** a faded section shows the room under it from outside (its furniture is fine) but never who is in it: renderer.js `sees` adds `shownInside` (roof-fade.js): a remote player or robot inside a closed building with a sectioned roof is drawn only for someone inside that building or in its doorway (its roof lifting: `opacity` under 0.995), whatever its sections do; no sectioned roofs (Deadwater), nothing changes. Still seen through a faded section: that room's shots, effects and corpses. **Cost:** nothing allocated a frame (the numbers the tests work on live on two long-lived objects, CLIP and LINE: a number handed to a call the engine does not inline is boxed); about 2-3 microseconds a frame with one character by a house, 12 with eight (node). One draw per roof with a section faded, as the patch's copy was, but no longer while someone is merely near a roof (the town, camera pass: one draw and 2k triangles fewer; Potato 72 -> 71 draws); a front stoop draws the same as before. tests/roof-fade.test.js (the shader, the decisions, the ease, the copy, the guard), tests/hollow-wick-buildings.test.js (every roof piece in a section, room in the table, and what fades at the saltbox's front stoop and back eave, the cape's back stoop, under the portico, at the tavern's side door, in an open shed and in the street). **Ceilings (v0.990a fix, owner: "when running against a wall in some buildings, especially smaller ones, it lets me see inside"):** a whole section fading over someone outside under its eave (or behind the building) showed the room under it, and on a small building one or two sections are most of the roof (against a gable wall, often all of it). Each sectioned roof now has a boarded ceiling over its room (`ceiling` in colonial-buildings.js, `entry.ceiling`; one shared material, `ceilingMaterial`), out to the walls' middles and `CEILING_FADE.drop` (3 cm) under their tops, so its edges hide in the walls. It is registered like a blended copy (`registerOverlay(..., { near: 'sections', opaque: true })`): drawn only while one of its roof's sections is faded, never while the roof fades whole (you inside, or in its doorway), so it costs a draw only then (the town stays 71 / 162). The faded section still shows whoever stands under the eave (outside the walls); the room stays hidden. It opens a small hard-edged hole (`CEILING_FADE`: inner .4, outer .75, over the waist, only on the camera's side of the body) on the line to someone behind the building, who shows through the wall's patch. Tests: tests/hollow-wick-buildings.test.js (every roof has one, opaque, its own sections, shown against the cape's front wall, hidden in the street and when the roof fades whole). **Walls open too** (v0.980a, owner: someone against a north wall was hidden by the wall itself, a head shorter than it): every mesh of a colonial building's shell (walls, clapboard, trim, sashes, doors, stoops, the floor; not the interior pivot, not the roof group) carries `userData.wallFade`, which renderer.js `bakeKind` bakes as its own kind 'wall' (one more draw in each 40 m cell with buildings; the batch's onBeforeRender keeps the shared list current, and 'wall' gets the timber bump like 'timber'). Its material is `ditherFade(..., WALL_FADE)`: the see-through patch (the roofs' too until s6-roofs), a little bigger since s6-roofs (`inner` 0.65, `outer` 1.3 m; it was 0.5 and 1.05), but only more than `above` 0.9 m over the character's feet and only on the camera's side of the body (`ahead`: the wall behind someone standing in front of it stays whole); eight slots in the shared list, the first `count` in use (`roofFadeCount`: the shader loops over only those and skips the patch when nobody is outside). The patch's blended copy is each building's own (v0.985a: `shellOverlay`, a merged copy of just that building's shell in the batches' baked colours, material 'wall-overlay'; shown while someone's line passes within the patch's reach, `pad` `WALL_FADE.outer` + 0.1), since a copy of a 40 m cell would draw every house in it for one; the cost at the town: about one draw and 2-5k triangles while someone stands by a wall. Someone hugging a north wall now shows from the waist up. **North eaves run on less** (v0.980a, owner): `eaveRun(b, side)` (`EAVE`): an eave facing north (its outward way within 60 degrees of -z) runs 0.2 m past its wall instead of 0.45, a saltbox's long back 0.4 instead of 0.9 (tests/hollow-wick-buildings.test.js). `makeQualityDetails` skips colonial buildings (no sand drifts or door paths).
- **Numbers:** houses eave 2.6-2.9 m, ridge 5.5-5.9; the tavern 6.4; the meetinghouse eave 4.2, ridge 7.6, belfry top about 12.8 m above its floor (the landmark); sheds, the tomb and the forge under 4.2. All sixteen come to about 100k triangles (Deadwater's buildings: about 147k). At the town spot on Performance (400 x 250, shadows included) they add about 73 draws and 90k triangles (116 draws with them, 43 without): each roof is 2 draws, the walls and dressing merge into the static batches of each 24 m cell (plain and timber, casting and not), which later props share. The gambrel's pad is at 5.0 (was 5.1: its margin ran 0.1 m up into the tavern's corner 0.3 m away).
- **Tests:** tests/hollow-wick-buildings.test.js (every pad has its building at baseY and the same pads; houses have 2+ doors; every doorway opens onto 2 m of walkable dry open ground; no overlaps; paths keep off buildings, except two stage-1 pads on the ends of shape-only graveyard aisles: the tomb's corner 0.31 m into aisle-22, the horse sheds' 0.01 m into aisle-40; the renderer builds every building with one fading roof).

### Hollow Wick's dusk look, contrast, overhead map and card (s3-look, stage 3)
- **The dusk is light, not dark ground** (the map's `look`, render/map-look.js): overcast sky light (the overcast #8a8a86 brightened in linear light to #e7e7e1 at 1.95, bounce #807a6d), the low weak WSW sun #d9a070 at 1.72 seen through the west's dusk glow #b58a66 (`glow`, `glowMix` .4: mixed into the sun's colour), haze #8b8782 that starts inside the view (`fogNear` 34, `fogFar` 120 m of view depth; the camera is about 31 m off, so the far edge of the screen and the stream hollow grey a little), grey fog sheets #aaa69e, and on Extreme a duller grade that cools the shade (`grade`: warmth .03, shade .07, contrast .08, saturation .9; extreme-post.js takes it). The same on every preset: only light colours, the hemisphere, fog and (optionally) `exposure`; no pass, nothing per pixel. Hollow Wick keeps the default exposure (.98) so unlit effects (blood, glows, tracers) keep their exact colours; the brightness lives in the light intensities instead. New `BASE_LOOK` fields default to what the renderer always used (exposure .98, fog 70-130, no glow, no grade): every other map is lit exactly as before (tests/hollow-wick-look.test.js). **The stronger dusk (v0.990a, owner):** the light about a quarter down (sky 2.32 to 1.95, sun 2.1 to 1.72, glow .3 to .4, haze and fog sheets a shade darker): the ground reads about 72% as bright as the old daylight grey (the test holds it at 65-80%; it was 92-115%). Every coat, team colour and blood still clears its contrast floor on the ground and on what lies on it; to keep unlit blood clear of the leaves, the burnt orange that lies on the ground (the carpet, drifts, landed leaves: `CANOPY_GROUND[1]`, trees.js `CARPET`) is #c86a2c, a shade brighter than the canopies' #c0612b. Darker still breaks the coat floor (coat 4 at 7.9) before anything else.
- **Ground brightness:** on screen the ground is as bright as the stage 2 daylight (mean luminance within -8%/+15%; test), and shade keeps over 60% of the light (overcast).
- **Contrast rule** (render/look-contrast.js, tests/hollow-wick-look.test.js): each colour is taken to the screen through three's own maths (albedo/PI x (hemisphere + sun x its height x the hills' sun left), ACES filmic, sRGB; unlit things skip the light) and compared, as CIEDE2000, with every 1 m ground texel of the playable area (the real baked ground: ground-layers.js, not under decks or in the channel), in sun and in shade. Blood #8c1c2a (a splat, unlit, and gore, lit) and the AMBER/CYAN/VIOLET hats and rings (config/match.js) need 15 (plainly another colour even as a small spot); player coats (remote-players.js PLAYER_COLOURS) 8 (a big moving shape under a bright hat). Luminance alone would miss it: dark red on dark brown has nearly the same luminance. The olive coat #5c5f3a failed (6.1) on the map's own ground, so the non-owner colours moved away from it: base ground #625840 -> #665840, the ridge and town yards #6c6247 -> #746649, the burying ground's turf #5a5840 -> #625a42 (the owner's explicit palette was left alone; its grass #6e6a50 is itself only 6.9 from the olive coat, which is why the notes say keep large areas away from olive and rust). **The leaves that lie on the ground count too** (v0.975a, map-ui audit): every colour of the woods' carpet (`GROUND_LEAF_COLOURS`), the trees' drifts (`TREE_DRIFT_COLOURS`) and the ground detail's leaves meets the same rules in sun, half shade and shade: the carpet's maple yellow #c49a3a and ochre #b8923c (7.8 and 9.6 from the Amber hat) lie faded as #9c8a3c and #8e7a3a (still bright in the canopies and while falling), and the darkest litter brown is #5a4632.
- **Overhead map** (pause > map; src/ui/overhead-hills.js, `overheadMapSVG` hands any map with `terrain` to it; Deadwater's drawing is untouched and hash-tested in tests/overhead-hills.test.js): framed on the fence; the ground by height (base, then each level a step lighter), the ground layers (woods' litter, stubble, turf, yards), stalk patches, the stream's channel at its width with the ford, the drawn paths at their width (shaping-only paths as faint dashes), every retaining wall and cliff run (`groundFor(map).edges`, with the gaps where paths cross; stage 3 drew only the spec's `edges`, so most walls were missing), the levels built over the water as solid walkway (`overWater`: the mill dam, which read as open water), fieldstone walls and rail fences (from their collision boxes), the decks, the buildings turned on their pads, every tree's crown (the woods read as a mass of orange-edged crowns) and the trees that are props (the fenced orchard; the hanging tree as a dark 2.6 m landmark), the irregular fence and you. Teleport clicks still map through the SVG's own transform.
- **Map card** (maps.js MAP_LIST `card`, src/maps/hollow-wick-card.js): `{ thumbnail: { x, z, height, lift } }` (Hollow Wick's line over the picture, "Supper is still on the table. Nobody came home.", was removed in v0.992a, owner; a card `line`, if one is ever given, still shows as the map picker tile's title and over the picture on a Practice map card: menu.js `.map-line`, menu-theme.css), the picture is src/assets/thumbnails/hollow-wick.webp (map-cards.js), made by `PORT=<port> node tools/capture-thumbnail.mjs hollow-wick` (it now reads `card.thumbnail` and skips the vulture on a map without birds; it hides the practice targets; `SPOT=x,z,height DRY=1` tries another spot and writes only /tmp/thumbnail-<map>.png). The spot (v0.990a) is the meetinghouse whole, belfry and crow, the burying ground below it with the hearse house, and the woods' autumn line down the west side (-40, -18, 44; before, -30, -14 cut the meetinghouse in half). `lift` (1.3) brightens the card picture only (PIL brightness, and contrast by .27 of the lift): the dusk reads as a murky square at card size; `LIFT=` overrides it, a `SPOT=` try takes none. **Released in v0.990a (owner):** `menu: true`, so Hollow Wick is the Practice map page's second card, on the SOLO page and in the lobby's map list from any map; Deadwater stays the default (first in `multiplayerMaps()`); no longer under Developer tools > World > Map in progress.

### Interiors (furniture is solid; doors stay clear)
- **One list per room** (`world/room-furniture.js`, `roomLayout(b)`): where every piece of furniture stands in the building's own frame (x across, z front + / back -). The renderer draws from it (`interior-details.js`: the plain rooms' back counter and two stools, the theme's corner stove / sacks / spare wheel, the wall shelf, the big stove and its woodpile, the fallen chair and swept scraps) and `map-kit.js` `mapColliders` makes a collider of every piece that is not clutter (`solidFurniture(b)`: the styled rooms' `interiorCover(b)` plus the placed pieces). Nothing is drawn that a body walks through, and there is no second list to drift. `makeBuilding` no longer draws its own counter.
- **Solid, not cover** (owner, 2026-09-26: "make Deadwater furniture solid; make sure it won't block much of the gameplay"): the plain rooms' furniture stops bodies, robots, dashes and spawns, but rounds fly over it (`playerOnly`, like a window's sill), so the fights in these rooms play as before. Collider heights are the drawn heights (`FURNITURE`: counter .8, stool .56, stoves 1.2, sacks .8, wheel 1, shelf 1.5, woodpile .5). Styled rooms' cover (workshop, depot, station, freight hall, rail cars) still stops rounds. `walkOver` pieces (fallen chair, scraps, and the map's pots and potted plants) are never colliders.
- **Doors first:** inside every doorway a strip the door's width plus .3 m each side and 1.5 m deep (`DOOR_DEPTH`, 1.3 m clear of the wall) is kept empty, and a 1.3 m lane (`WALKWAY`) runs clear from each door to the room's centre, so every door reaches every other through the middle. A back door splits the counter in two either side of it; the things standing on the counter slide along onto a segment (`onCounter`) or are left out. Every other piece tries its candidate spots in order and is left out when none fits.
- **No pockets:** a piece is only placed if every bit of floor a robot fits on (a .5 m round body) is still reachable from a door (`hasPocket`, a flood over a .2 m grid). So no nook a body can wander into but a robot's nav grid can't route to, and no spawn lands where it can't get out.
- **Fixed on 2026-09-26:** the counter across the sheriff's and the boarding house's back doors (now two counters either side), the farmhouse's stove in its left doorway, Test Hill's plateau house counter and stool in its right doorway; the corner stove / sacks / wheel no longer stand inside the counter's end, nor the fallen chair and litter under it.
- **Tests:** `tests/solid-furniture.test.js` (Hollow Wick's colonial rooms have their own walkway, window and robot-reach tests in tests/colonial-interiors.test.js; every door and walkway clear on every map, a body walks from each door to the centre and on to each other door with the simulation's own movement, no pockets, spawns reachable, robots route in by each door and straight through opposite doors, colliders match the list). The golden replay was re-recorded for this (owner-approved).

### Movement
- **Where:** `simulation.js`. Speed, acceleration, braking and dodge values are in `RULES` (`config/gameplay.js`).
