- **Keys** (`GAME_KEYS` in `config/controls.js`; everything that reads or names them goes through it: main.js, rifle-input.js, items.js controls/hints/touch bindings, tutorial.js, ability-hud.js, Settings > Controls): **Space (or LMB) shoots**, **E is the weapon's other action** (Static places orbs, Nominal grenade, Ballast double shot), **Q dodges** (v147; it was Left Ctrl, see Dodge is Q below). Menus keep E confirm / Q back. From the moment a game starts, in every state, Ctrl shortcuts are blocked by a capture-phase keydown listener (Ctrl+D bookmark was firing while moving with D; Ctrl+wheel zoom too; text fields excepted), and since the browser keeps Ctrl+W/T/N, leaving the page mid-game asks first (`beforeunload`). **Key lock (v0.9b, `ui/key-lock.js`):** a keyboard game (not touch) goes full screen when it starts and holds the game's keys with the Keyboard Lock API (Chrome, Edge), Escape included, so Ctrl+W (dodge while walking up, back when dodge was Left Ctrl) no longer reaches the browser; Esc still pauses, holding Esc leaves full screen (then it stays out until the next game). A start with no click behind it (a page load) tries again on the first click or key. Setting: Settings > Controls > LOCK BROWSER SHORTCUTS (`settings.keyLock`, on). Other browsers keep the prompt. **Fullscreen or Windowed (owner, 2026-09-25; PC only):** Settings > Graphics > SCREEN > MODE (`settings.screen`, 'fullscreen' by default; a saved `keyLock: false` from before migrates to 'windowed'). Fullscreen: a game start goes full screen in any browser that allows it (`playFullscreen`, holding the keys too when LOCK BROWSER SHORTCUTS is on and the browser can), the first click or key on the menus goes full screen once a visit, and full screen now stays on through the menus (MAIN MENU no longer leaves it); someone who leaves it (holding Esc, F11) stays out until the next game starts. Windowed: never full screen, and the shortcut lock is greyed out (it needs full screen). Choosing a mode applies it at once (`applyScreen`). The SCREEN block is hidden on a touch-only device (`touchPlay()`: coarse pointer and no fine one), which keeps its own Settings > Mobile full-screen toggle.
- **Dodge:** costs stamina. Distance `RULES.dodgeDistance` 3.5 m (was 3.2). **One dodge per weapon (`RULES.maxStamina` 1), Ballast two (`SHOTGUN.dodges`)** (owner, v0.83). A dodge breaks through breakable props.
- **Dodge input buffer:** a dodge pressed up to `RULES.dodgeBuffer` (0.15 s) before it can happen (mid-dodge, a hair short of stamina) goes off the moment it can, instead of being dropped.
- **Direction-only aim** (arrow keys, WASD fallback, walking on touch) turns quickly with a little weight: `keyboardAimResponse` 9, `keyboardAimMaxTurn` 10 rad/s, `keyboardAimSpinUp` 24 in `RULES` (a quarter turn in about 0.25 s, a half turn in about 0.4 s). The touch move stick eases direction changes over `MOVE_STICK.smoothing` (0.055 s).
- **Target lock for players without a mouse** (`target-lock.js`, wired in main.js: `lockMode`, `lockCandidates`, `lockTarget`). Keyboard-only aim, and touch (unless aim assist is off in Settings > Mobile). **Idle by default:** aiming is the ordinary kind (arrows or walking turn the aim, a finger drags the cursor). Candidates are alive, visible (`sim.canSeeTarget`: not under another building's roof; from indoors only out through doors and windows, the same sight lines as the dark interior shading; not behind sight-blocking cover), on screen and within 18 m; online only other players.
  - **Players and robots (v0.83, owner: "lock on accurately and smoothly"):** they are `mover`s. **Nothing is ever locked for you** (owner): a target is locked by an arrow press or swipe that way (the next one that way if locked; with none that way, held arrows just turn the aim, so the cursor walks onto a target), or by **turning your character to face it** (main.js `facingLock`: within about 7° or 0.7 m of your facing, on screen, seen, not behind a wall or indoors). One you let go of by hand is not taken again until you turn well off it. Locked, the aim sits on them (a .035 s smooth-damp that only takes out jitter, led by their velocity × (smoothing + one tick), so it is not a step behind); the old "chase" lag is gone. It drifts off the way they were going when they **dodge** (then waits `relockDelay` .16 s after the dodge and glides back in `relockGlide` .13 s) or when a **solid obstacle** is between (walls, buildings, fences: `Simulation.obstacleBetween`, never breakable props; 1.4 s behind one and it lets go), and it **disengages** at once when they go into a building you are not in (`buildingAt`) or off the screen. Held arrows still lead them by hand (an offset that eases back). Measured on a moving robot: under 1° off.
  - **Keyboard:** arrow directions are taken from where the cursor (aim dot) is now. From idle, an arrow press locks onto the target that way from the cursor; with none that way it just aims as usual. While locked (the cursor is on the target), an arrow moves to the next target that way; with none that way the lock lets go and the arrow turns the aim as usual.
  - **Touch:** a swipe on the aiming side is an arrow press. The cursor never jumps under the finger. From idle, a 42 px swipe locks onto the target that way from the aim dot; with none that way, the rest of that drag moves the cursor by the finger's movement (trackpad style). While locked, a swipe moves to the next target that way (one per swipe, another every 140 px of a long drag); none that way goes idle. A tap fires at the locked target (idle: at the tapped spot).
  - Consistency: offline (practice targets), once the aim arrives on the target it stays exactly on it. **Online (players) it chases instead** (`chase` in `update`): the point moves at most `chaseSpeed` 5.5 + `chaseCatchUp` 3 × (metres behind) m/s, so a walker is kept up with but a player running flat out (7.2 m/s) or dodging pulls ahead and the aim lags. Held arrows push the point that way at `nudgeSpeed` 7 m/s (at most `nudgeReach` 2.5 m past the target) so a skilled player leads by hand; online an arrow with no other target that way keeps the lock (it is leading, not letting go). Touch gets the chase without the nudge. A locked target briefly out of sight (a post, a doorframe, the screen edge; `hold` 0.6 s, alive and within 1.2x range) keeps the lock. If it dies or breaks, or stays hidden longer, the nearest other candidate takes over; with none left it goes idle.
  - The aim point sweeps to a new target on a critically damped spring (`glide` 0.09 s smooth time: eases in and out, keeps its speed if the target changes mid-sweep), and while locked the body faces the sweeping point directly, so body, cone and dot move together. From idle a target just beside the cursor still counts as "that way" (2 px). The sim's aim assist is off while locked (the input already points at the target). A mouse never uses it. `#world` has `touch-action:none` so a swipe is never taken as a page pan.
- **Aim assist** (`aim-assist.js` + `auto-range.js`, numbers in `AIM_ASSIST` / `AUTO_RANGE` in `config/gameplay.js`). Levels: **touch** strongest (finger, walking or stick; can be turned off in Settings > Mobile, `settings.aimAssist`), **keyboard** lighter, **mouse none** (`assistMode()` in main.js).
  - Only visible targets count (`sim.assistTargets()` → `canSeeTarget`, range check first).
  - Acquire: the player's own aim roughly points at a target (acquire cone, `AUTO_RANGE.halfAngle`). Players rank first; practice targets stand in for players.
  - Range: the aim reach slides out to the target (a finger's own point is overridden while locked).
  - Stick: the aim is bent toward the target by `pull` every tick, so strafing or a moving target does not drag it off. Movement never breaks a lock (only `hold`, death or range does).
  - Let go: turning your own aim away builds `resist`; the pull fades with it and at `release` the lock drops, and that target is ignored for `cooldown` unless you aim straight back (`reacquire`).
  - The aim dot turns **red** only when the dot is visibly over a target you can see: inside its on-screen outline from feet to top (`aimOnTarget` in main.js, `.reticle.on-target`). A lock alone does not turn it red. While locked on touch the dot shows the assisted aim, not the finger.
  - Online the host runs it with the joiner's input (`autoRange` travels in inputs), so it is the same for everyone.

### Weapons
- **Sheath (owner's brief, 2026-09-28; eighth weapon, id `sheath`):** a wide, straight **white broadsword** with a plain crossguard, kept in a **black sheath at the left hip**. Full design and change note: [SHEATH_DESIGN.md](SHEATH_DESIGN.md). Every number is `SHEATH` in config/gameplay.js.
  - **Files:** `weapons/sheath.js` (rules: `stepSheathMobility` runs in every sim, the joiner's predicting one too, because Gold Rush and the Draw-cut move the body; `stepSheath` does the damage where rules are decided), `sheath-motion.js` (the eleven-number poses and every swing's keys), `sheath-model.js` (model, `poseSheath`, `applySheathBody`), `sheath-view.js` (trails, hits, Gold Rush, the Draw-cut's tell, wake and gold slash), `ui/sheath-screen.js` (Gold Rush tint, Draw-cut streak: page markup, no renderer cost).
  - **Slashes:** 60–70 per hit (`damage` 65 ± `damageRoll` 5), one swing every `interval` .46 s (1.9x Ichor's), `windup` .1 s then a `hitWindow` .08 s in which a body is cut **when the blade reaches its side of the arc** (a right-to-left cut takes the right-hand body first; the hit-stop lands as the blade is seen to pass), everyone in the arc once per swing. Reach 2.45 m (+.3 overhead, +.1 sweep), arcs 1.35–2.35 rad per swing. About 3.3 s to kill 500 (Nominal ~3.6 s). Walking while slashing at `attackMove` .85. Never mid-dash: a press during a dash (or during the swing cooldown) is `queued` and goes as soon as it can. Two dashes at Ichor's refill (`dashRechargeScale` 1.12).
  - **Swing choice (`nextSheathSwing`):** seven swings (0 flat right-to-left, 1 backhand, 2 falling diagonal, 3 rising diagonal, 4 overhead, 5 wide sweep, 6 the draw-slash out of the sheath). Each starts on the guard the last ended on (`from`/`to` sides), never repeats the last one, avoids the one before when it can, and is otherwise random (no loop). The first swing out of the sheath is always the draw (`drawWindup` .14). Back in the sheath after `sheatheDelay` 1.5 s idle.
  - **E Gold Rush:** `rushDuration` 3 s at `rushSpeed` 1.35, `eCooldown` 12 s. You can attack during it. Your screen gets a gold tint (sheath-screen.js; edges more than the middle). Everyone sees a gold ribbon at your feet and gold afterimages (`p.sheath.rush` in the snapshot).
  - **Walking:** `outMove` .88 with the sword out, `sheathedMove` 1.02 sheathed; a swing uses `attackMove` .85 (the slower one, not both); Gold Rush multiplies on top. A draw-slash started while moving (speed > 1) is stretched by `drawMoveSlow` 1.25 (wind-up and swing alike, so the pose still meets the hit). HUD: no ammo row, so `#weapon[data-weapon=sheath]` keeps a 114px min-height (the others' height) and the stacked E/X dials stay on screen.
  - **E Gold Rush:** `rushDuration` 3 s at `rushSpeed`, `eCooldown` 12 s. It draws the sword (`s.out`, event `draw`) and keeps it out while it runs (no idle sheathe); no out-walking penalty while it runs (a swing is still `attackMove`). Slash reach × `rushReach` 2 (`sheathReach(variant, rush)`; robots' slash range follows). Look: `sheath-extension` in the blade (sheath-model.js, added after `compact`): a see-through gold blade (normal-blended gold body, additive core and edge rails) from the guard to twice the blade's length, plus a white flash shell; shared materials, so it is driven only by visibility and scale in `poseSheath` (shown while the rush runs and the blade is out: grows in over .12 s with a .1 s flash, thins away over .22 s after). sheath-view.js `extensionFx` (local model and every remote avatar's pack) throws a white-gold burst on pop-in, motes while out, and ~46 rising gold motes along it as it dissolves. Swings made during a rush (`sheathSwing.rush`) draw their trail at the extended tip, gold with a white edge and a second gold band. Death drops skip it (transparent parts are not copied).
  - **X Draw-cut (rev. 2, 2026-09-28):** phases `back` → `tell` → `dash` → `strike` → `flourish`. The direction is fixed at the press (the facing then, normalised). `back`: a hop of `xBackDist` 1.5 m over `xBack` .12 s through `sim.movePlayer` (walls stop it); a drawn sword goes back in (`s.out` false: it always starts sheathed). `tell`: `xTell` .17 s rooted; the line is measured here (`sheathDrawCutLength` from where the hop ended: collider grid; breakables and walk-over clutter do not stop it; walls, fences, rocks, low walls and the map's edge do; on hills a rise the body cannot take) and `sheathDrawTell` shows it to everyone. `dash`: `xDashSpeed` 52 m/s along the line, through bodies for the whole dash (simulation.js skips the body push-outs while `sheath.x.phase` is `dash`, and `sim.phasing` still works for callers), never walls; each tick `drawCutSweep` cuts what lies within `xWidth` .75 m of the stretch covered (plus `xLead` .6 m ahead; the last tick reaches the line's end): 290–310 (`bladeDraw`) once per body (`cut.hitIds`), breakables cut apart, crops felled. A body that steps off the line before the blade reaches it is missed (contact ≥.29 s after the press, ~.43 s at the far end). It lands `xShort` .55 m short of the end; `strike` roots for `xStrike` .3 s, then the flourish (`xFlourish` .5 s, walking at .6) snaps it into the sheath. No dash charge, no dash look. `xCooldown` 35 s. Snapshot: `cut`, `cutT`, `cutDX/DZ`, `cutLen`, `cutSX/SZ` (line start), `cutTr` (travel), `cutBack`.
  - **Blade blood:** people only (target `kind` 'player'), `bloodPerHit` 1/8 per hit, drawn in four stages (four small meshes toggled by visibility, so avatar clones never share a stage); cleared on respawn.
  - **Look:** the arms are the RiflePose two-handed rig (hand-and-a-half grip; sheathed, the right hand hangs and the left rests on the throat). Poses turn the torso, lean, bank, dip and step onto the front foot (`body.position.z`). Trails are rebuilt from each swing's own keys through the same body/gun transforms, so they follow the point exactly at any frame rate: a white band thick in the middle, tapering at both ends, fading .13 s after the blade passes; heavy swings and the draw add a faint second edge. Hits: a .05 s hit-stop on the attacker's blade (the pose is held back, then catches up), a camera nudge (`view.kick`) along the swing, the body flinches (tilt, set afresh each frame), blood thrown along the swing through Ichor's pooled drops (stains, caps per preset), sparks and a clang off props, yellow-blue sparks off robots. Draw-cut: a dust kick and a gold glint (a turning four-point star at the body) on the hop; the tell's pulsing gold ground line with chevrons and an end ring, run out over the set and eaten by the dash; the dash's gold wake at chest height with gold afterimages; on arrival the gold slash, a huge crescent laid on the dash's own path (straddling the line, a slight bow, rising at one edge: it sits where the cut happened), textured with an engraved gold band (diamonds, scrolling vines, leaves, dots, a hatched rule; drawn once on a canvas, `goldSlashTexture`), sweeping in along the dash in .07 s, holding .6 s and burning off from the start end by 1.6 s, the engraving drifting; under it two thin gold edges split apart and a gold ground scar, gold and white shards, gold and white rings where it lands; the screen streak is gold. One dynamic ribbon mesh (vertex alpha), one line batch, one ghost batch, four pooled slash meshes (own geometry and material, one shared texture); all in the warm-up.
  - **Menu picture (weapon-photos.js `sheathPhotoModel`, photo-only):** the sword half drawn with the scabbard over the point half of the blade, along the card's diagonal like Ichor's: bevelled, fullered blade with a gold mark on the ricasso; crossguard with flared capped quillons, langets and a gold-inlaid centre; diamond-wrapped grip between ferrules; faceted pommel with a gold collar; scabbard with a riveted, gold-edged locket, gold trim and inlaid diamonds, a banded middle with a ring, strap and buckle, and a pointed steel chape.
  - **Sounds (audio.js `whoosh`, `ring`):** band-passed swept noise whooshes timed to each strike, a band and sweep per swing plus a random pitch and swell, and a low push of air; a scrape and ring on the draw, a slide and click on the sheathe, a meaty slice on people, metal on robots, a dull clang on props, a shimmer and rush for Gold Rush, and for the Draw-cut a click as the guard is thumbed free on the hop, a rising gold shimmer through the tell (a warning others hear), a tearing whoosh for the dash, then a crack, a long ring and a gold chime.
  - **Death (`blade`, `bladeDraw` in death-reactions.js, `severed`):** a fallen body with gore, one arm (sometimes both; the Draw-cut both more often) cut off at the shoulder with a red stump and bone, the arms thrown the way the slash went (`GoreBurst` with `{arms}`). The weapon drops as always. Remote bodies match (remote-corpses.js passes the avatar's colours).
  - **Online:** protocol **17**: `sheathE`, `sheathX` (predicted: in `movementInput`), `p.sheath` in player states, the loadout, and the replay rebuilding a Draw-cut in progress (client-session.js). Shared events: `sheathSwing`, `sheathHit`, `sheathClang`, `sheathSheathe`, `sheathRush`, `sheathRushEnd`, `sheathDrawBack`, `sheathDrawTell`, `sheathDrawDash`, `sheathDrawCut`. (The dash is continuous, so robots' bodies interpolate across it like any move.)
  - **Robots:** slash in reach (never pressed mid-dash), Gold Rush to close on a far target or to get away when low, the Draw-cut down a clear line inside its reach, measured from where its hop back would end (at once when it would finish them). The line check runs only when it is about to go.
  - **HUD / touch / tutorial / dev:** no ammo row; the status line says SHEATHED / DRAWN / GOLD RUSH / DRAW-CUT; E and X dials; touch RUSH and DRAW (no RELOAD, no AIM). A three-lesson tutorial (slash, gold rush, draw-cut). Dev options (after unlock): E and X ready now, blood the blade.
- **Omen (local implementation, 2026-09-26):** fourth selectable weapon, including Deadwater Outpost practice, solo robots, the tutorial and online weapon selection. `weapons/omen.js` owns its pure rules; `OMEN` in `config/gameplay.js` holds every gameplay number. The body is an oxblood, matte, split-claw relic around a suspended crimson diamond, held in one hand with a faint forearm aura. `omen-model.js` is shared by the local player, remote players/robots and the menu photo studio. Its shipped `assets/weapons/omen.webp` uses the same camera, lights, framing, dimensions and compression as the other cards.
  - LMB/Space: straight orange diamonds, 36 damage through 12 m, falling to 22.5 at 22 m, ending at 26 m; one every **0.42 s**, 4 rounds, R reloads in 1.8 s. Owner's latest balance change slows fire from 0.34 s and reduces every damage value by 10%, including random variation and the overlap cap. E primes the next shot for **5 s** (`primeWindow` / `primeLeft`): a larger red diamond, 49.5 impact. Firing or expiry starts the 10 s cooldown; expiry consumes exactly one unused round and is processed before firing at the same endpoint. An empty/reloading weapon cannot prime. A landed shot leaves one E curse for exactly 3.5 s, **9–12.6 damage every 0.5 s** (10.8 ±1.8, uniform rolls in 0.9 steps on the damage authority). **E again** detonates only that active E mark, including while reloading or cooling down: 135 direct damage, rising to 171 during its last second, with an independent **±13.5** roll. `curseTickVariance` / `curseBlastVariance` affect E only; `curseRollStep` preserves the previous distribution at 90% damage. Early E presses neither refresh a prime nor queue a burst before impact; C has no Omen action. Splash stays 2.5 m, 67.5 down to 18, with no extra own splash on the marked victim.
  - X (covenant): three total homing diamonds, 45 impact each, divided across visible enemies (two enemies get 2 + 1), with a 40 s cooldown. Limited turning, 1.5 s flight and no loop back let a dodge evade them. Curses tick for 10.8 damage every 0.5 s. Four seconds from launch to press X again and rupture X marks for 135–171 direct damage depending on timing. Repeated hits never stack/refresh a curse; overlapping ruptures cap at 198 per victim. E and X marks keep their own detonation binding. At expiry they become inert before input is handled; caster death cancels marks and in-flight diamonds. Expiry/death dissolves them, never detonates them.
  - `omen-view.js`: three prebuilt instanced batches draw faceted diamonds, sparks and linework; one extra pooled smoke batch shares `effects-detail.js`'s puff material. Dark oxblood outlines contrast the bright etched rings, forked points, stars and inward streams; X connections carry travelling red pulses. Floor segments follow terrain and below-deck stance. Rupture contracts/glows for 0.11 s, then throws wider jagged rays, rising red petals, fragments and rolling smoke (6 puffs on Potato up to 30 on Extreme), all gone by 1.65 s. The larger visual burst does not enlarge the damage radius. Layered audio adds the crack, bass impact and long falling tail. Curse ticks carry `omenCurse` without `electric`; renderer routes hit/kill and robot feedback to a dedicated crimson climbing pulse, never Static's blue aftershock. Death reactions/drops still run normally.
  - Omen visual detail: the shared relic has raised claw ribs, rivets, inset cuts, a carved top seal and jagged pommel, merged into its existing solid draw. Every shot forms a red vortex and heart at the muzzle, with brighter diamond facets, hot tips, orbiting chips and muzzle/impact sparks. `LOOK` in `omen-view.js` increases detail through Quality and Extreme, adding core/side beam strands. Red E/X wakes follow their sampled curved flight/height for 0.14–0.21 s; 96 reusable paths of 24 samples return on expiry/reset. Only occupied buffer ranges upload. All meshes/shaders are prebuilt and warmed; effects borrow the existing light. A primed E has its own purple six-point intake seal around a red inner circle: curved streams draw purple energy from roughly 2–3 m away, becoming magenta then red as they enter the circle. Bright packets move inward over dim paths; higher presets add finer curves and annulus cuts without new meshes/materials. Activation pulls a purple ring inward; expiry scatters purple strokes and a red inner ring. The seal dissolves in 0.25 s after use/cancellation; the optional `omenPrime` snapshot carries it to other players/robots and expires stale state after 0.3 s. Victims retain their red five-point curse sigil. Tests cover the spatial colour transition, inward flow, all five presets and cleanup.
  - Omen HUD: four square red diamond ammo pips, including red reload fill. `ui/omen-state.js` supplies the same clocks to standard filling ability dials and smaller timers beside the aim cursor during priming or active curses; no separate orbiting rim. Standard ready pink / charging yellow / active blue / cooldown red states. Tenths show the 5 s prime, 3.5 s E mark and 4 s X window. Both priming and rupture show E. On touch, CURSE handles both presses and shows the live curse timer, AIM is restored, and COVENANT is X. Both dials fit the mobile layout. Settings, key rebinding descriptions and HUD hints use the same actions.
  - Detonation cue: `omenReadouts` marks `optimal` only for a landed curse in its final `OMEN.lateWindow` second (E or X). The HUD and cursor fills pulse coral to hot ivory every 0.48 s, with a red glow; touch CURSE/COVENANT buttons get the same colour cue. The filling circle and numeric countdown remain intact, without a separate orbiting rim. No cue for an unfired prime, a missed X volley, expiry or removed marks; changing weapons clears it. Reduced-motion keeps the colour without pulsing. Tutorial copy explains the coral cue.
  - Tutorial (`tutorial.js`, Gamemodes > Tutorial > Omen): seven automatically advancing lessons using the existing card, progress meter and HUD highlights — diamonds, reload, aim in, curse, rupture, last-second rupture and covenant. Keyboard and touch each get their own prompts. Late timing is credited from the burst's timing power, independent of its random damage roll. Only this active range course returns spent cooldowns once no prime/flight/curse remains; live timing and ammo costs stay real.
  - Network protocol **12** adds the two ability inputs (`omenPrime` handles both E presses; `omenVolley` handles both X presses), Omen loadout, diamonds and caster-owned target-id marks. Lost-input repeats clear both one-shot presses. Bot brains use the same rules and wait to rupture near expiry. Explicit death types: `omenShot` intact head-wound body, `omenCurse` charred fallen body, `omenBlast` directional scatter; all retain the dropped weapon. Tests in `tests/omen.test.js` cover timers, damage roll bounds, dodge escape, splash, shields, proxies, packets, terrain and effect/death cleanup; tutorial coverage also lives in `tests/tutorial.test.js`.
  - Integration: developed in isolated `codex/omen` from `6ad2d18` (v0.985a); map authors' files are untouched. No commit/pull/push is authorized by the build request. Merge the weapon changes with current map work only when requested; retain both sides of shared renderer/simulation/main/AGENTS edits. The release version has not been bumped. Preview captures and the standalone playtest HTML live under ignored `output/omen/`.
- **Aim through the muzzle (v0.9b, `aim-damping.js` `muzzleBearing`, `MUZZLE_LATERAL` rifle .27 / shotgun .20):** the trigger guns' muzzles sit to the right of the body and shots leave parallel to the facing, so the body turns left of the cursor's bearing by asin(lateral / distance): the barrel's line (shots, Ballast cone, Nominal band) runs through the cursor at every range. Applied to mouse aim (`AimDamping.apply(..., lateral)`, its aim point on the barrel line), target lock (main.js) and robots (brain aim). `rifleAim` now lays the barrel parallel from the muzzle (the old 8 m convergence floor left close shots up to ~0.2 m beside the cursor; Ballast had no correction at all). Test: tests/rifle.test.js.
- **The X abilities' names (owner, v0.83):** Static's is the **hex**, Nominal's the **nova**, Ballast's the **blast**. Everything the player reads uses these (touch buttons HEX / NOVA / BLAST, HUD hints, Settings > Controls, the tutorials, the dial, which shows the name when ready, dev options). The code keeps its older internal names: `surge` (surge.js, `SURGE`, `sim.surge`, `surgeStart` events) is the nova, `scatter` (scatter.js, `SCATTER`, `sim.scatter`, `scatter*` events) is the blast. Don't rename one without the other.
- **Static** (`simulation.js`, visuals in `electric-effects.js` + `arc-batch.js`):
  - Hold E to place orbs, LMB/Q to launch a volley or a quick shot.
  - C is the lightning stream.
  - X deploys the hex, and X again pulses it (Hex Pulse).
  - The orb count sets volley damage (`ORB_VOLLEY_TOTALS`). **Volleys (1 to 12 orbs, quick shot included: impacts and blast) hit 1.75x** (`VOLLEY_BOOST`, v0.83); the hex and the stream are unchanged, and what an orb can break on the way (`pierceBudget`) is as before.
  - **Mouse volley assist** (`MOUSE_VOLLEY_ASSIST`): mouse players (no auto-range) have a volley's landing point drawn 6% of the way onto the nearest target they can see within 0.6 m of the cursor (v0.9b, owner: very subtle, the volley lands where the cursor is; 18% / 0.9 m before). Touch and keys keep their own aim assist, lighter since v0.9b (`AIM_ASSIST` pull touch .6, keyboard .3; .85 / .5 before).
  - **Volley lands at the cursor (v0.9b):** orbs overshoot the aim point by only `launchOvershoot` .2 m (was .55, which made them visibly converge past the cursor), and the refocus onto a target in the line of fire (`interceptCorridor` .5, was 1.1) only happens for a target within `interceptReach` 1.5 m short of the cursor (it used to pull a volley aimed far past a target back onto it). Friendly targets are never assisted or refocused onto.
  - **Launch profile (v0.9b, `ORB_LAUNCH` start 7, top 64 m/s, ramp .11 s):** an orb leaves slowly so the throw reads, then ramps and lands fast (18 → 38 m/s before), `launchDistance`/`launchDuration` from it. Placed orbs drift out at `driftSpeed` 1.5 (.72). Each launched orb leaves a thin pale-blue beam along its path for `BEAM_LIFE` .32 s (`effects/orb-beams.js`, one additive InstancedMesh; the fade is the instance colour; from `fromX/fromZ` on `impactMark` / `pointImpact`). The C stream: 8% less damage (`sprayInnerDPS`/`OuterDPS` ×.92) and its orbs used 20% faster (`sprayAmmoTime` .25/1.2).
  - **Enemy orbs a deeper blue (v0.9b):** other players' and robots' orbs draw with `enemySeedMaterial` (dark blue, darker aura) unless they are on your side (`ProjectileMirror.lists(now, isEnemy)`: solo, ally slots; online, the slot's team vs yours).
  - **Splash** (`explode`) is measured to the edge of a body (a player half inside the blast is hit; it used to need its centre inside), only walls and buildings shelter from it (crates, barrels and other breakables don't), and a body is sheltered only when its centre and both sides are behind a wall. Owner's report: splash felt inconsistent.
  - The hex ring spreads at `hexSpeed` 3.6 m/s (was 2.4). **Unpulsed, it holds at full size (`hexRange`), still turning, for `hexLinger` 1.6 s before it fades (v0.9b, owner); X still pulses it there.**
  - **Hex shield (v0.9b, owner):** anyone inside a deployed hex (spreading, holding, or spinning after the pulse) takes nothing from shots that come from outside it: every sim gets `shields` (every hex in the game: `Simulation.hexShield()`, handed round by BotMatch.before and Arena.before) and `Simulation.hit` drops a hit on a target inside a shield when the shooter is outside it (`hexBlock` event: a small crackle). **Teammates walk into each other's hex** (only other sides are pushed out: `pushHexVictims` compares `victim.team` with `player.team`, set on proxies and players in team games).
  - **Hex zaps** (the spinning edges, `RULES.hexEdgeDamage`) hit 15 harder than before (`HEX_ZAP_BONUS`, added after the hex boost: 31.92 → 46.92).
  - **Hex mark on the orb bar** (weapon-hud.js `hex-mark`, x-ability.css): a thin bar with a small X after the 10th orb (`RULES.hexCost`), dim until you hold that many, pink once you do.
- **Nominal** (rifle; `rifle.js`, `rifle-view.js`, `rifle-pose.js`):
  - LMB/Q fires: tap for single shots, hold for auto.
  - RMB/Shift aims in.
  - R reloads (**20 rounds** since 2026-09-29, owner: "reduce nominals ammo in a clip down to 20"; 28 from v0.83; **1.95 s**). The old X (40-round magazine) is gone; **X is Surge** (below). 22 damage per bullet (16 at the far falloff), one shot every 0.165 s. All menu/tutorial/HUD text reads these from `RIFLE` in config/gameplay.js.
  - E throws a grenade (`grenade.js`). **Grenades do +50 over their base (`GRENADE.bonus`), +100 when thrown during Surge (`surgeBonus`).**
  - **Surge** (X; `weapons/surge.js`, numbers in `SURGE` in config/gameplay.js, look in `effects/surge-view.js`): 2 s power-up (`charge`), then 5 s (`duration`) of 2x bullet damage, no ammo use and no reloads, **v0.9b: ~30% less damage taken (`taken` .85×.82), +26.5% move speed (`speed` 1.1×1.15), and the cone 40% tighter hip and aimed with a lighter recoil kick (`spread` .6, `rifleSpread(..., surging)`, `kickRifle` scale)**; when it ends the magazine is full (owner, v0.83; was empty); 50 s cooldown from the end. The charge breaks breakables within `breakRadius` 3.2 m. Events `surgeCharge` / `surgeStart` / `surgeEnd` (shared online; robots use it too). **Look:** yellow ray strips spiral in on the ground (one InstancedMesh), kicking up dust, turning white as they converge; a flash, ring and pillar at 80%; then the body and gun are redrawn bright white and jitter (one shared white material), lit by the borrowed `view.fxLight` on Quality/Extreme (a second PointLight halved fps, so never add one). **The nova (v122, replaces the plain ring when the power lands):** `SurgeView.nova`: a sunburst of 14 jagged, forked rays (white heart to gold to amber) that flares out and turns as it fades, a smaller one counter-turning under it, and a torn, crackling wave front with motes racing out past them; drawn once into two canvases (`novaTexture`), three pooled sets of flat additive sprites (no new shader; checked). **White body (v121):** the body's own meshes are hidden under an opaque over-white shell (color 1.35, not tone-mapped; a see-through shell let darker colours flicker through as it shivered) with a soft additive halo 12% larger; only meshes showing at the time are copied (not a flash in a hidden group); originals come back when it ends. **Surge rounds:** a louder, heavier report with an electric zing (`Soundscape.rifleShot(surge)`), a white additive beam up to 2.4 m behind each round (rifle-view `beams`), a bigger white glow, a brighter muzzle light and a small view kick.
  - **Hip-fire recoil:** each hip shot knocks the whole cone slightly off line at random (`kickRifle`: up to `RIFLE.recoilKick`, building over a burst via `recoilBuild`, capped at `recoilMax`, settling at `recoilSettle`/s). Bullets fly along the knocked cone and the HUD brackets (aim-overlay.js) draw it there, so the guide shakes a little while firing and stays honest. Aimed in (RMB/Shift) adds none and settles fast (`recoilSettleAim`). `sim.rifle.sway` / `kick` travel with the rifle state online.
- **Ballast** (double-barrel shotgun; `shotgun.js`, `shotgun-view.js`). **No charging since v0.83** (owner: charge and store removed everywhere: tutorial, controls, HUD, cursor ring, input, protocol):
  - LMB or Space: one press, one shell (holding does not fire again). Shift or RMB aims in (cone .30 → .18 rad).
  - Every shot the same: `SHOTGUN.range` 6.8 m (7.5 until v0.9a: owner, "a bit" less), `shellDamage` 287 (300 until v0.9b; +15 first shell) if every pellet lands point blank, a gentler fall-off (`shotgunFalloff`: 1 to 15% of the range, .71 at 60% (.75 until v0.9b), .25 at the end) so mid-range counts. Measured (single shell, hip / aimed in): 1 m ~285 / 290, 3 m ~200 / 235, 4 m ~130 / 195, 5 m ~85 / 150. E aimed in point blank still one-shots (owner). Launch `SHOTGUN.recoil` 5.4 (a double ~7 m of travel). `SHOTGUN.look` sizes the flash, sound and shake of every shot.
  - E fires both shells. R reloads (2 shells, `SHOTGUN.reload` 2.5 s).
  - **Blast look, v0.9b (owner: lots more, most on Quality and Extreme, `fx.level` ≥ 1.3):** readied, embers swirl into the gun with a closing ring; fired, a white-hot core, a wide shock ring, a cone of 14-30 sparks along the volley, a rolling bank of dark smoke, embers and grit; in flight, a heat haze round each shell with sparks thrown off behind (and embers off the big ones); splits get an orange ring and glow; each small explosion adds a short pillar of fire, a wider heat ring and flying slag (`scatter-view.js`, all from the shared DetailFX pools, no new draws or shaders).
  - **Scatter** (X; `weapons/scatter.js`, numbers in `SCATTER`, look in `weapons/scatter-view.js` + `EffectsDetail.miniBlast`): X readies it (the aim cone turns into its wide red cone with an arc where the shells split; the gun vents steam, `shotgunPressurized`), X again fires 5 big red shells across ±0.55 rad. Each flies 5.5 m (or to whatever it hits) and splits into 4 that fan out and fly on to 11 m, each ending in a small explosion (1.4 m, walls shelter, props break). Big shell hit 40, small 14, each explosion up to 18 (down to 30% at the rim); they stack. A big shell that hits a body splits into it (its four burst on them: ~165 for one big shell at mid-range). Capped at `SCATTER.max` 460 per target per Scatter (owner: 420-480; reached point blank). 40 s cooldown from firing (owner). Uses no shells. One InstancedMesh draws every shell (yours and others'); trails, flashes and blasts come from the shared effect pools; no shader is built mid-game (checked Balanced and Extreme). Shells travel to other players in snapshots (`pack().scatter`, mirrored by id).
  - Each shot **launches the player backward** through `blastVX/blastVZ` + `ballastLaunch`, and a launch smashes props on contact.
- **Deaths:** `death-reactions.js`, `death-view.js`, `death-corpse.js`, `death-bones.js`, `ballast-blood.js`, `gore.js`, `remote-corpses.js`.
  - **The death shot:** the camera zooms onto the body over `DEATH_ZOOM` (2.2 s) and then holds still on the gore; the death screen comes in at `DEATH_MENU_DELAY` (2.6 s), on the right (at the bottom on narrow or portrait screens), and the held frame slides so the body sits in the open half (`cameraFrame(aspect)`), so you can look at what is left of you while the respawn counts down.
  - **Gore** (`gore.js`, low-poly and flat-shaded like the world; amount by `GORE_DETAIL` per preset): wounds go on the side that faced the killing hit, which is the side facing up once the body has fallen. Gunshot/Ballast: a soaked chest, entry wounds with torn flesh, blood runs down the coat. Impact: a soaked, torn patch. **Electric (charred):** a cracked char texture whose seams glow orange and cool to dull red (`charMaterial`, emissive map), the chest burst open with the ribs showing, the skull through the face, a forearm burnt to the bone, embers. **Fire (skeleton):** charred and seared flesh left on the bones (`skeletonRemains`); **not always a whole skeleton**: about half the time 1 to 3 limbs have snapped (two pieces askew with splinters) or one is gone, and about 45% of the time the skull cracked open (knocked askew, fragments) and the **brains lie on the floor past the head** (`spilledBrains`: two pink folded hemispheres, groove, torn bits, wet smear). **Explosion:** `GoreBurst` throws severed limbs (cloth, ragged red end, bone sticking out; a boot or a hand), a torso piece with ribs and flesh chunks with the bones and organs.
  - **Draw cost:** every gore piece is merged (`compact`: one lit and one unlit vertex-coloured mesh; shared source geometry untouched, merged geometry freed with `disposeMerged`). A dressed corpse is 2 extra draws; a burnt skeleton becomes 2 draws once it has fallen (1.1 s), an explosion's bones/organs and `GoreBurst` pieces become 2-3 once landed (2.4 s); the scatter pool is one mesh. Wall and floor placement uses the collider boxes (`blood-surfaces.js`: `castToWall`, `floorHeight`), never a raycast into the merged world meshes.
  - **Dropped weapon** is darker (`darkenWeapon`: its own material copies at 60%).
  - **Other players' bodies** (`remote-corpses.js`): the same reactions from their `playerDeath` event, in their colours, built from a loose copy of their avatar (`RemotePlayers.looseBody`, head and legs tagged `deathPart` so headless/kneeling work), their weapon dropped beside them, darker. One per player (next death replaces it), cleared by a map reset (`clearDebris`) and `reset`.

### Rendering
- **`renderer.js` (WorldView):**
  - Builds terrain, roads, buildings with enterable interiors, props, crops and fences.
  - Draws each frame: camera follow with pixel snap (`camera-framing.js`), roof fades, interior vision shroud, particles and shadows.
  - Helpers include `world-details.js`, `roadside.js`, `rail-depot.js`, `interior-details.js`, `detailed-interiors.js`, `crop-view.js`, `birds.js`, `dust-trail.js`, `surface-marks.js`, `shadow-snap.js`, `interior-visibility.js` and `vision-polygons.js`.
- **Batching:** static meshes are merged per 24 m cell (`batch()` + `merge-transformed.js`); on a map with hills per 40 m cell (v0.960a: Hollow Wick's town went from 183 to 159 draws on Performance, the North Woods from 107 to 85, for about a quarter more triangles). Empty particle pools are hidden each frame (a zero-instance draw still binds its program).
  - Plain flat-coloured materials are **baked into vertex colours** so different colours share one draw.
  - Roofs are never baked: they fade their own materials. (A colonial roof's pieces merge only within their section and carry its number into the bake: s6-roofs, see Hollow Wick's buildings.)
  - **Casters and non-casters share a mesh** (v0.980a): `batch(view.static, true, { mergeCasters: true })` no longer splits a cell by `castShadow`; the casting parts go first and the merged mesh's `userData.castCount` (indices, or vertices if not indexed) marks their end, and three's `onBeforeShadow`/`onAfterShadow` (bake-colors.js `castOnly`/`drawAll`, `castersOnlyInShadow`) narrow the geometry's draw range to them for the shadow pass only: the camera draws the cell once, the shadow map gets exactly the casters as before. Only the static scenery opts in: roofs, props and characters keep the split in `batch()` because later steps rebuild those meshes (bakeColors, prop-instances.js) and would drop the hooks. Each roof is baked into one mesh the same way (`bakeColors(roof, { castersFirst: true })`: its shingles never cast), one draw where it was two. Hollow Wick on Performance, camera pass: the town 121 -> 109 draws, the North Woods 89 -> 84, the meetinghouse 101 -> 93; a still frame differs from before in 0.1% of pixels, at edges where two surfaces meet (draw order).
  - **Opaque roofs draw first** (`ROOF_OPAQUE_ORDER` -3, renderer.js): opaque draws are sorted by material before depth, and the scenery's baked material is older than the roofs', so every floor, table and hearth under a roof was shaded and then painted over; drawn first, the roof's depth turns them away before they are shaded (on GPUs that test depth early). A blended roof (inside, at a door) still draws at `ROOF_PREPASS_ORDER` + 1.
- **Reusing geometry:** reuse existing geometry, materials and pools rather than allocating per frame. `arc-batch.js` draws every electric arc in 4 calls.
- **Frozen transforms** (`frozen-transforms.js`): the scene's own matrix update is off, and the static roots (buildings/scenery, ground cover, quality details) plus every breakable prop group are frozen: their world matrices are computed once and three.js skips walking them each frame (~0.86 → ~0.28 ms of matrix work here). A prop group that moves (hit wobble, restore grow-in) calls `updateMatrix()` on those frames only. **Anything added under a frozen root never animates;** give moving things their own group under the scene.
- **Merged characters:** (the Static gun's two glowing rails share one material and throw no shadow: one draw where two, v0.980a) the player's body merges into a few meshes (legs, head and the rest, keeping `deathPart` tags for death reactions; the gun and Static arm stay separate because they move and hide), and each target's board is one mesh. `batch()` keeps parts with different `deathPart` apart.
- **WorldView is split into mixins** installed with `Object.assign(WorldView.prototype, X)`: `world-build.js` (terrain, roads, buildings, fences, props, players, targets, ambient), `warm-up.js` (shader warm-up), `vision.js` (vision shroud and interior sight). A method moved there still uses `this` exactly as before; tests that read renderer source read these files too.
- **Fair camera** (`fairFov(aspect)` in camera-framing.js): every screen shape sees about the same amount of map as 16:9 at 40°: wider screens don't see further sideways and tall phones don't see less across (the FOV grows as the screen narrows, capped). Indoor fit (`interiorCameraHeight`) uses the current FOV.
- **Shared box geometry:** boxes of the same size share one geometry (`userData.shared`, bounding sphere precomputed); `batch()` never disposes shared geometry. Cut world build time noticeably on slow CPUs.
- **Breakable props in batches** (`prop-instances.js`): prop parts sharing a material in a 24 m cell merge into one mesh; each prop's own meshes move to layer 1 (kept for bullet-mark rays, which enable layer 1, and decals). When a prop moves, breaks, is restored or cleared, `propInstances.sync(id, group)` rewrites only its vertex range (collapsed to a point when hidden). Any new code that moves or hides a prop must call it.
- **Loading (v0.995a, owner: "improve loading times across everything"):** measured with tools/load-profile.mjs (CPU slowed 4x, SwiftShader): Deadwater Performance ~20.5 s to playable, Hollow Wick ~26 s, of which shader compiles are a third to a half (parallel on real GPUs) and building the world most of the rest. Taken out: the title's blood intro no longer measures its letters or runs its goo filters behind the loading screen (`title-screen.js` `active()` waits for `body.loading` to go; it plays when the menu is seen; ~0.9 s at 4x on Hollow Wick); Deadwater's Quality-only street dressing (`makeQualityDetails`) is built only for Quality/Extreme or on the first change up (`setQuality`), not hidden at every load; canvas textures draw paths, not thousands of single strokes (the crop beds' 4200 leaves in 3 paths and their 330 scratches in 1, also redrawn while fields burn; the sand and wood relief maps one path per shade, same random sequence), and the Hollow Wick drag trail's canvas is made for reading back (`willReadFrequently`: 0.7 s at 4x to under 0.08). WorldView's build on Deadwater 6.5 s -> 5.8 s at 4x; Hollow Wick's load about 3 s shorter at 4x.
- **Shader warm-up (no first-use hitches)** (`warmPrograms`, `warmRack` in warm-up.js; started from main.js as `ready = view.warmProgramsParallel()`, which bootstrap awaits; it uses KHR_parallel_shader_compile when the GPU has it). Deaths are staged too: fake gunshot, explosion, fire, Ballast and electric deaths are stepped through their phases behind the loading screen and their materials kept (`warmDeathMaterials`, also indoor-clipped copies), so the first death builds nothing on Extreme. Every shader any preset, weapon or event needs is built *and drawn once* behind the loading screen, so no orb, grenade, blast, arc, death or building entry builds one mid-fight (checked: zero programs created or first used during play on all 5 presets x 3 weapons, with buildings, damage, death and respawn). How:
  - The weapon views (`RifleView`, `ShotgunView`, `GrenadeView`) are made in the constructor, before the warm-up, not on the first frame.
  - `warmRack()` adds a tiny stand-in for each material the view holds that nothing shows yet (walks the view's fields; skips shadow-pass depth/distance materials) plus the kinds a blast or thrown grenade makes on the spot (basic transparent/additive/double-sided, smoke, Lambert flat and smooth), each also in its indoor-clipped variant. Their materials are **kept**, not disposed: disposing the last user of a program destroys it and the real effect would build it again.
  - **Everyone else's guns** (v0.990a, owner: frames lost playing a robot): a robot's or another player's gun (remote-players.js `gunModel`, one shared model per weapon, cloned per avatar) uses materials the world's scope shading never wraps, and nothing in the scene at load drew them, so each weapon's gun built its shader the first time one came on screen, and hidden parts (the Sidekick's magazine, shown on a reload) again later: 50-400 ms stalls in a headless VS ROBOTS run (Performance, Deadwater and Hollow Wick, every robot weapon). `gunStandIns(view, weapons)` builds every weapon's shared model and the warm-up puts one of each in the rack (hidden parts drawn too); later guns are clones sharing those materials, so nothing compiles. Checked with a scratch VS ROBOTS program probe (the robot brought to you, 24 s per weapon): 0 programs linked in play on both maps. Test: tests/remote-gun-warm.test.js. (The robots' own thinking and stepping measured ~0.1 ms a tick, the odd path search up to ~8 ms.)
  - The pass shows every non-light object (hidden pools, the grenade in the hand) with culling off, compiles, then **draws once into a 1-pixel scissor**. A draw, not only a compile: a shader's first draw is where the driver is made to finish it and where the shadow pass builds its depth variants. Lights are left alone (their count is part of every shader). The indoor-clip variant is applied to the particle pools, effect pools and the arc batch first.
  - On Extreme the scene draws into the composer's linear target, and output colour space is in every shader key, so the warm-up draws into `post.composer.readBuffer`, and runs again when the post pipeline finishes loading. It also draws the AO pass's strained denoise once (`post.setLight(true)`, its Poisson denoise at `strainedDenoiseSamples` into a 1-px scissor, then `setLight(false)`): the material keeps both programs, so the first time a slow device drops into strain nothing compiles mid-game (map-ui audit; checked: 198 programs after load, none added strained and back).
  - Production builds set `renderer.debug.checkShaderErrors = false` (reading a shader's log forces the driver to finish it mid-frame); dev keeps it and logs a failed warm-up to the console.
  - On Extreme the warm-up also draws the scene through the AO pass's normals material **with the pass's own exclusions** (`ao._overrideVisibility()`: it hides lights, lines and points, and the light count is part of every program key). Without that, each effect pool's first appearance built an AO normals program mid-fight (the first Static X).
  - **Pooled effects** keep their materials, so no program lookup, interior patch or compile happens when they fire: Static X pulses (`ElectricEffects.takePulse`/`prewarm`, 8 built at load) and blasts (`WorldView.takeBlastKit`/`prewarmBlasts`: ring, core, four puff materials and the puffs so far, up to 8 kits kept; a preset change empties the pool because the smoke material's kind follows the preset). A new pooled effect should follow the same shape and hand its meshes to the warm-up rack.
  - **Roof shaders held (v0.990a):** each kind of roof material's two programs (opaque, and blended for a whole-roof fade) are drawn at the warm-up on kept stand-ins (`warmRoofKept`: clones given the roof's own hooks and key, which `clone()` drops). A colonial roof is made transparent, so its opaque program used to be built by the first roof to come into view, mid-game when the spawn showed none, and three destroys a program when its last material leaves it.
  - **The scope's grey only on the world (v0.990a merge):** Sightline's `ScopeShading` hooks world materials at the warm-up, and a hook changes the program key. It is handed only the scene's children as they stood before any staging (`apply(scene, world)`), never the warm rack or the staged deaths: those stand for materials play makes fresh or clips indoors later (an orb's aura, a death's parts), and a hooked stand-in warmed a program nothing in play asks for (Static on Deadwater built 3-4 programs mid-game). A world thing made again in play (a tumbleweed rolling in) is hooked as it is made (`spawnTumbleweed`). Keys are order-dependent (scope then indoor clip is not indoor clip then scope): hook a material the same way in the warm-up as in play.
  - Known gap: on Extreme, the AO pass's normals shader for the death effects is built at the first death (one shader). DeathView builds its meshes on first use.
  - To check after adding an effect: `node tools/program-check.mjs [quality] [weapon] [map] [port]` (dev server up) plays two Static X presses and orb blasts and lists every program linked after load with the object that needed it; on a map with hills it also visits every named spot (tools/terrain-spots.mjs), wades the stream (a splash, drips on the way out) and breaks a dozen props. Expect none (Hollow Wick: none on Potato, Quality and Extreme, v0.960a).
- **Frame-rate savings that keep the picture identical** (checked by pixel diff of frozen frames, and `tests/render-savings.test.js`):
  - `shader-savings.js` patches three.js's shader source at import (renderer.js imports it first). **Shadow early-out:** three 0.180 filters shadows by hand, 17 shadow-map reads per pixel on PCF (Performance/Balanced) and 16 on PCF Soft (Quality/Extreme), on every receiving pixel including the whole ground. It now reads the filter's corners and middle first and only reads the rest when they disagree (a soft edge); the probe reads are reused, so edges are exactly as before. **Dark effects light:** `fxLight` stays in the scene (its count is in every shader) but its lighting is skipped while its colour is black (a uniform test, free on a GPU). Re-check both on a three.js upgrade: in dev the module throws if three's code no longer matches.
  - **Empty draws skipped** (a wrapper on `renderer.renderBufferDirect`): instanced effect pools with no instances and batches with an empty draw range were bound and drawn every frame, about a quarter of all draw calls. The warm-up sets `drawEmpty` so the pools still get their shaders built at load.
  - **One draw per rigid part** (`bake-colors.js`, `bakeColors`): parts' colours move into vertex colours on one white material of the same kind. Rifle 15 draws → 1; Ballast about 35 → 4 (body, swinging barrels, each shell); each roof about 5 → 2 (casters and shingles), one fading material per roof. Child groups (moving parts) are left alone.
  - **Ground drawn last, top layer first** (`orderGround`, renderOrder .5–.95): after the buildings and props on it, and the map's ground before the wide slab under it, so a GPU's depth test skips covered ground pixels instead of shading them. Surface marks (-1) and crops (-2) still draw before it, sand marks (1) after.
  - Draw calls on an ordinary frame (960x540 test): Performance about 122 → 92, Balanced 140 → 109, Quality 211 → 180.
  - Headless testing note: SwiftShader (CPU) runs both sides of a branch and has little early-z, so it cannot show the early-out, light skip or draw-order gains; measure those on a real GPU. Compare pictures with frozen A/B frames in one evaluate (render, toggle, render), never across runs: dust, birds and wind make separate runs differ by up to 25% of pixels.
- **Roofs:** the corner posts stop just under the eave and the front fascia and side-door lintels sit below the roof line (they poked through at the corners as small blocks). Each roof has seeded wear (by building id, the same every game): a few missing shingles showing the dark underlayer, lifted shingles, a newer board patch; on metal, rust patches and a lifted sheet end. It uses only the roof's own colours.
- **Target damage** (`target-damage.js`, `DAMAGE_STAGES`): two kinds that never mix. **Bullet damage** only from real bullets (Nominal rounds, Ballast pellets carry `bullet: true`; `sim.hit` counts `target.bulletHits`, zeroed on respawn): holes with pale chipped rings in a board, dark punctures with straw tufts in a dummy, one batch per two hits (`HITS_PER_BATCH`). **Breaking** by health lost to anything (the C stream, orbs, the hex, grenades, fire, any future ability; bullets too): a board gets gouges, cracks from the rim (branching when worse), splinters, then a split edge with a piece hanging; a dummy tears, straw bursts from the head and an arm and spills at its feet. Balanced 2 breaking stages (66%, 33%), Quality 3, Extreme 4 with twice the pieces; on Quality and Extreme each new stage also throws debris off the target (`set` returns the stages just broken; renderer `TARGET_DEBRIS` chunks). Built the first time a target is hurt (and again if the preset changes), seeded by its id, each stage and batch merged into one or two draws (`compact`); hidden again at full health. Potato and Performance show none. **Facing:** every target and dummy is turned by `targetYaw(id)` (seeded, within about 60 degrees either side of the camera, never turned away, so its face reads from above).
- **Blob shadows** (`blob-shadows.js`): Potato has no shadow map, so soft patches under props, the player, targets and other players stand in for it, leaning the way the map's sun throws the real shadows (map-look.js sunLean; a lower sun leans and stretches them further). Two draws.

- **Sheriff's office (Deadwater):** a door on every side (owner, v0.9b; it had only the front).

### Map look (light and haze per map)
- **Where:** `map-look.js`. `BASE_LOOK` holds the default sky/bounce hemisphere light, the sun, and the dust haze (fog + clear colour). A map overrides any of it with a `look` block in its own data, next to `palette` (e.g. `look: { warmth: .15 }` or `look: { sun: '#ffd9a0' }`). `mapLook(map)` gives the finished colours and the renderer builds its lights and fog from them, so a new map needs no renderer changes to get its own mood.
- **Warmth** pulls light toward amber (red stays, green eases, blue drops most) in proportion, so brightness barely moves. Deadwater, Dry Creek and the training range are all `warmth: .15`.
- Only the world's lights and haze change: the HUD, menus and unlit effects keep their colours. Every preset lights the world with these lights, so it applies to all of them. Extreme's grade pass sits on top as before.

- `sunOffset` ({ x, y, z }: where the sun stands from the camera's focus; default Deadwater's (-24, 40, -18)) drives the sun's position and shadow basis, the shadow camera's near/far (`shadowDepth`: a lower sun reaches further toward itself), Potato's blob-shadow lean and the birds' shadows (`sunLean`), Extreme's footprints and the hills' shade.
- `fog` ({ colour, highlight, opacity, lowBias }): the drifting fog sheets' two tones and strength (default Deadwater's tan dust '#d0ba8e'/'#e8d6ac' at .27, not warmed); a map giving only colour gets a highlight a third of the way to white; lowBias (terrain maps) gathers them over low ground. See render/fog-sheets.js.
- `exposure`, `fogNear`/`fogFar`, `glow`/`glowMix`, `grade` (s3-look): the tone mapping's exposure, the distance haze's start and end, a horizon glow mixed into the sun's colour, and Extreme's grade over its defaults. Defaults are the old fixed values. See Hollow Wick's dusk look.
- `birds: false` (map data, not look): no birds (Deadwater's vultures, swifts and finches fly at fixed world heights; Hollow Wick has crows only). Deadwater's street and desert dressing (world/world-details.js makeQualityDetails: ruts, ripples, pebbles, handbills, tumbleweed...) is skipped on hills maps.

### Graphics presets
- **AUTO: the preset picked for the device (v0.995a, owner: "make the game auto select between performance, balanced and quality for users device at start of game"; `device-tier.js`, tests/device-tier.test.js).** Settings > Graphics > PRESET has **Auto** first and it is the default (`settings.qualityAuto`; a save from before it counts as automatic only if its preset is still the device's old default: Performance on touch, Balanced elsewhere; choosing any preset by hand turns it off). At each start main.js reads the graphics chip's name from a throwaway WebGL context (`gpuInfo`: `WEBGL_debug_renderer_info`; before the game's own context, since screen multisampling is fixed when a context is made) and `detectTier` picks from **Performance, Balanced, Quality** only: software drawing (SwiftShader, llvmpipe) Performance; phones and tablets Performance, flagship chips (Adreno 730+, Immortalis, Mali-G77+/G7xx/G9xx, Xclipse 9xx) and 8-core Apple tablets Balanced, never Quality; NVIDIA Quality (GTX 9xx and older, MX, GT: Balanced); AMD cards Quality, AMD graphics inside the processor Balanced; Apple M chips Balanced (Pro/Max/Ultra Quality), Safari's "Apple GPU" Balanced; Intel Performance (Iris Xe Balanced, Arc Quality); unknown Balanced; 4 cores or fewer, or 4 GB or less, cap it at Balanced, 2 GB at Performance. **Learning:** `AutoQualityWatch` (main.js frame loop, running and visible only) measures drawn frames over 20 s windows; an automatic preset under 72% of its frame target (the FPS cap, at most 60) steps down one at the **next** start (`settings.qualityAutoStep`, -1 or -2, saved; never mid-game, a switch would stall the fight), and a step already taken climbs back after 90 s smooth (97%+). The settings note reads "Auto: <preset> for this device." and the demanding-preset warning is left off for Auto. Tools that set a preset save `qualityAuto: false` (smoke, perf, program-check, capture, frames), else Balanced would become Auto.
- **Changing preset without the freeze (v0.995a, owner: "changing quality settings causes a brief pause/freeze"):** a change from Settings (or the developer window) goes through `changeGraphics` (main.js) and `WorldView.changeQuality` (renderer.js): nothing is drawn from that moment (`holdRender`: `render()` returns early, the last frame stays on the canvas), a small **APPLYING GRAPHICS** pill shows (`#graphics-busy`; still, not spinning: a CSS animation keeps the compositor busy in the same process that builds the shaders, which measured the warm-up at 8.6 s against 3.4 s in SwiftShader), then one frame and a task later the preset is applied (`setQuality(name, { deferWarm: true })`) and its shaders are built through `warmProgramsParallel` (three's `compileAsync`, `KHR_parallel_shader_compile`: the driver's own threads, the page left free) before drawing resumes. Changes made meanwhile are folded in (the last one asked for wins; `wantedQuality`). Without the extension (SwiftShader) the build is still one block, behind the pill. Programs of presets used before stay cached, so switching back is quick. Measured (SwiftShader, Deadwater, Performance to Quality): the old synchronous path 4.6-6.3 s of frozen page; the new one ~1.2 s of preset work plus the compile, which a real GPU with the extension does off the page.
- **Sun shadows fit the screen** (`render/shadow-snap.js` `shadowFrame`, `fitShadowBox`, `shadowBoxOver`, `settleShadowBox`, `SHADOW_FIT`; renderer `fitShadow`, run on each shadow update just before the texel snap; tests/shadow-coverage.test.js). The shadow camera used to be a fixed 42 x 30 m box, which left the north-west corner of a 16:9 Deadwater screen, the top and bottom of a tall phone, and the wider views over hills without shadows (a visible line where shadows stopped short of the edge).
  - **Across the map:** each update takes the view's four corner rays between the lowest ground in view and 8 m (`receivers`) above the highest. On terrain maps these heights come from the ground's 4 m tiles (`minInBox`/`maxInBox`), looked up over the widest view the map's height range allows. The corners are turned into the light's frame and boxed, plus 1.2 m because the view moves on between updates. A caster that shadows a point is on the same line to the sun, so only the receivers matter here.
  - **Depth:** it reaches toward the sun until above the tallest caster (`casters`: 16 m over the map's highest ground) and away from it past the lowest ground in view. The bias is a fixed 5 mm of depth (`bias / (far - near)`). `map-look.js` `shadowDepth` now gives only the first frame's near/far.
  - **Roll:** the box is turned about the light, through the shadow camera's `up`, to the whole-degree roll that hugs the view best for the screen's shape. It is chosen again only when the aspect changes, and `sunBasis` follows it for the snap. Deadwater at 16:9 is now 28 x 43 m, smaller than the old box yet covering the whole screen.
  - **Stability:** edges move in whole metres and depth in 2 m. A box is kept until it no longer holds the view or is 1.3x too big. On terrain maps a box that must change takes 1 m of slack all round: about 2 changes on a 290 m walk across Hollow Wick at 16:9, and 7 on an upright phone. Each change shifts every texel once. Flat maps settle once. Potato skips all of it.
  - **Map sizes:** Balanced 1280 (was 1024) and Quality 2560 (was 2048) keep the 45-55 m boxes on hills as sharp as before. Performance stays 768 (phones) and Extreme 4096.
  - **Tall roofs:** `SHADOW_FIT.receivers` (8 m) covers every Hollow Wick roof but the meetinghouse's belfry (about 12.8 m over its floor): at the very edge of the screen its top can miss a shadow. Raise it if that ever shows.
- **Crisp output (Performance, Balanced; `crisp-output.js`):** the world is drawn off-screen at the tier's usual internal size (same cost as before), then one full-screen triangle writes it to the canvas at a higher output ratio (`CRISP`: Performance up to 1.5x CSS, Balanced up to 2x, each capped in total pixels). Performance's pass does FXAA-lite (it had no antialiasing: stair-stepped, crawling thin lines); Balanced keeps 4x multisampling, now on the off-screen buffer (the screen context is created without it for these tiers). Both add contrast-limited sharpening scaled by the stretch (none at 1:1, clamped to the neighbourhood so no halos), fixing the soft look of the browser's plain stretch. Colours are unchanged: the buffer is flagged `isXRRenderTarget` so the scene's shaders tone map and encode sRGB as for the screen, stored as plain RGBA8 (an sRGB format would encode twice) and copied as is. Adaptive resolution now resizes only the off-screen buffer, never the canvas. The camera's pixel snap uses the off-screen height. A tier with antialiasing (Quality) on a page that started without screen multisampling also goes through it (4x, no stretch). Potato and Extreme draw as before. The pass's draw adds to `renderer.info` rather than replacing the frame's count.
- **No blank frames:** adaptive resolution never resizes the canvas between a draw and the screen showing it (a resize clears the canvas, which showed the brown page colour for a frame every time the scale stepped). `setResolutionScale` only records the new scale; `render()` applies it right before drawing. A window resize draws straight away. A lost WebGL context (phones out of GPU memory) stops drawing until it returns, then rebuilds the shadow map and Extreme's buffers; two losses within a minute on Extreme step down to Quality with a notice.
- **Low input lag (frame pacing):** after each frame a WebGL 2 fence is set (`fenceFrame`); while the GPU is still drawing the previous frame, main.js skips drawing this one (`gpuBusy`, `RenderBudget.hold`) instead of queueing it, so the next frame is drawn from fresher input. The game and input keep running; waits are capped at 120 ms. The aim dot updates every display frame, drawn or not.
- **Adaptive resolution:** Performance and Balanced may drop to 70%, Quality to 85%, Extreme to 80% (`ADAPTIVE_FLOOR`). Still short of the target at the floor, the tier is *strained* (`setStrain`): shadows redraw at two thirds of their rate, and on Extreme the AO runs at 38% scale with a lighter denoise and bloom a size down. It lifts after 4 s of healthy frames.
- **Faded roofs:** (Hollow Wick's colonial roofs also fade a section at a time for anyone outside under or behind them: see Hollow Wick's buildings.) Inside a building, or stepping through one of its doorways under the eaves (`roof.reach` bounds, `roof.doors`), its roof fades right out (about 0.1 s); the roof switches to its see-through shader a few steps before a door (`doorGap < 3`), not on the threshold, where the switch and the driver's first draw of it stalled big roofs (the freight hall, the west depot) as the player walked in and is then not drawn at all (`roof.group.visible` off below 1.5%). It used to rest at 9.5% as a faint ceiling, but on some real GPUs that rendered far heavier, shingle rows and all (seen on the workshop in two sessions; SwiftShader never showed it). During the fade a roof lays down its depth first (depth-only copies, render order 50) and then its colour (51), so overlapping surfaces are painted once; that relies on two shaders giving identical depth, which drivers don't guarantee, hence the full fade-out.
- **Where:** `settings.js` → `GRAPHICS`. Five presets: **Potato, Performance, Balanced, Quality, Extreme**.
- **Extreme** is Quality plus, never minus (a test enforces "never less of anything"):
  - **Post-processing** (`extreme-post.js`, loaded only when Extreme is picked, disposed when it is left): half-resolution GTAO ambient occlusion (eased down to 0.4 indoors so rooms keep their warm light), bloom on near-white effects only (soft-knee, capped so the Static stream glows instead of whiting out), and one grade+sRGB pass. The scene is still tone mapped per material while drawn (the composer's buffers are flagged as output targets), so effects that skip tone mapping keep their exact colours. The AO draws its own depth/normals without glows, particles, lines, faded roofs, birds and ground cover (`WorldView.aoExcluded`). Reusing the main depth instead was tried and dropped: lopsided AO and grey screen edges.
  - **Surfaces** (`extreme-surfaces.js`, shader injection behind an `EXTREME_SURFACE` define so other presets compile the old shader): world-space ground variation and pebbles against the 4 m tile repeat; dust on the lowest half-metre of objects, streaks on walls, worn patches on roofs and tops.
  - 4096 shadow map at 60 updates/s; 2048 ground texture; anisotropy 16; cylinders built with twice the sides (applies to the map as loaded; switching to Extreme mid-game keeps the models).
  - Lit, flat-shaded birds with coverts and primaries and a soft ground shadow thrown along the sun (`birds.js`, `rich`). Their lit colour is the unlit colour x .38 plus a little of it as emissive: brighter (.72) washed them into the warm ground under Extreme's sun and grade.
  - Long tapered rifle tracers (`RIFLE_QUALITY.extreme.trailLength`), more sparks/smoke/impact debris, denser arcs (`ARC_DETAIL.extreme`), more dust, blood and blast smoke, a 400-per-colour particle pool.
  - Adaptive resolution may lower Extreme to 80% when frames drop; Quality stays fixed.
  - **Cloud shadows** (`extreme-surfaces.js`, `CLOUDS`): soft shadows of high clouds, about 50 m across, drift east over the whole map (with the tumbleweeds' wind). They dim only the sun's light (`exSunLeft` after `getDirectionalLightInfo` in the lights chunk), so shade stays shade. `tickExtremeSurfaces(elapsed)` from `WorldView.update`. A few noise reads per pixel, no pass.
  - **Dust devils** (`dust-devils.js`, `DUST_DEVIL`): every 35-70 s a whirl of sand lifts off open ground upwind and wanders across the view for 13-19 s, dying out against anything solid; not indoors. Made of DetailFX's own puffs and grit (a puff or chunk with `devil` set circles the devil's centre, climbing and widening): no new meshes, materials or draws.
- **Detail effects** (`effects-detail.js`, `DetailFX`, `view.fx`): one shared layer of sparks, embers, lumpy smoke/dust puffs, flashes, shock rings (round and hexagonal), light columns, grit and streaks, nine instanced draws in all, counts scaled by `FX_DETAIL` (Potato none → Extreme 1.8×). Hooked to gunfire (`muzzle`), impacts, explosions and grenades, Static contacts (`electric`), the X pulse (`hexPulse`), the C stream's contact points, orb-to-orb links (`ElectricEffects.onContact`, more links per strike on Quality/Extreme via `ARC_DETAIL.links/linkGap`), shells, footsteps and dashes, burning crops. Puffs thin out right around the player (`fx.clearZone`). Pools are shown during `warmPrograms` so their shaders compile at load. Views take `view.fx || NO_FX` so tests without a view still run.
- **Orb blast sizes** (`orbBlastScale` in `effects-detail.js`): each orb that lands makes the blast look one step bigger: 2-3 orbs a small pop, 4-7 medium, about 10 the full fireball, and 12+ a bit past that (it stops growing at 16). It shrinks what used to be a fixed size: smoke puffs, how far sparks, clods and embers fly, the flash brightness, the renderer's hit burst and the blast light. The outer ring only shows from 4 orbs up. The radius and damage come from the simulation (`explosionFor`) and don't change. Grenades pass no scale, so they keep their full size.
- **Ballast shells** bounce, skid and roll to rest on their side, trailing smoke; Extreme builds them with a crimp and brass rim. **Pellets past the red zone** fly on as cosmetic copies (`ShotgunView.ghosts`) until they hit something solid or run 34 m: no damage, nothing breaks.
- **Footprints on Extreme** are pressed prints (sole, heel, tread, a lip of sand) lit from the sun's side, and last 7 s.
- **Fog sheets** (`render/fog-sheets.js`, the old "dust wisps"; world-build.js `makeAmbient`/`updateAmbient` only build and feed them): 1/2/3/5 big blended sheets (Performance/Balanced/Quality/Extreme, none on Potato) drawn with the map's `look.fog` (map-look.js: two tones, opacity, lowBias; Deadwater's tan dust at .27 by default). **The clearing** round the player and the aim point is not a circle. Its edge wanders with direction (a two-lobe and a three-lobe pattern, each turning slowly) and with a two-octave world-space grain. Each centre has a wake that trails it (up to 4.5 m for the player, 3 m for the aim) and closes in behind. It is measured where the pixel's view ray passes the centre's height, so it sits on the player on screen. The old inner radii (2.2 m and 1.2 m) are always clear. `clearingAt` is the shader's sums in JS, for the tests. **Flat maps**: the sheets ride with the camera across the screen as before. **Terrain maps**: a sheet forms in the view or just ahead of it (the camera's smoothed velocity, up to 7 m). It drifts east at 0.3-0.6 m/s and hangs at a fixed world height: `formationGround` (the 7th lowest of 9 samples across its footprint, never below its middle) plus 1.2-2.6 m. Per pixel it is drawn only where there is air under it: it fades out where the ground comes within about a metre, thins to 40% when 4-8 m above it, and is up to 1 + lowBias as thick over low ground (the lowest 5% of the map's heights up to its median, `lowRange`). It reads extreme-surfaces.js `groundHeights()`, the same height texture as Extreme's dust band, now made at load on terrain maps on every preset. So a sheet drifting into a hill thins against the slope instead of slicing through it, and one over a hilltop stays there as you walk down. With lowBias, up to three places are tried for each new sheet and the lowest wins, so the fog gathers in the stream hollow. A sheet the view has left, off screen by a margin, forms again ahead at opacity 0 and fades in: nothing pops. One program per map ('dust-wisp', or 'dust-wisp-ground' with FOG_GROUND), warmed at load. Tests: tests/fog-sheets.test.js. Headless capture note: SwiftShader keeps the GPU busy, so the game skips view.update and the sheets freeze. Step `view.update(sim, dt, false, 0, sim.player, 1)` by hand before judging them.
- **Interior shroud** repaints on any frame the camera moved (it used to lag the walls on entering a room). It never greys the room you are in (v0.980a, owner: grey patches on the walls where no window was): besides the openings' cones (laid at 0.7 m over the floor), `vision-polygons.js` `roomBox(room)` clears the room's whole box as the camera sees it, floor to eaves and 0.3 m out past the walls (six faces; `projectVisionPolygon` takes a point's own `y`). `interiorPolygons` is unchanged: interior-visibility.js reads its XZ quads (tests/vision-polygons.test.js); **roofs** count as solid for AO until half faded, so a closing roof no longer shows the room's outline.
- **UI sounds** (`ui-sounds.js`): a clack on buttons, a lower falling one for back/close/Esc, on their own audio context so they work while the game is paused.
- **Blood when a player is shot** (`blood-drops.js`, `WorldView.bleed`, from `hit`/`kill` events with `targetKind: 'player'`, so every screen sees it for every player): a red spray of particles along the shot (instead of the pale hit burst) and drops that stain the floor, walls and crate sides where they land (walls from the collider boxes; a drop on a wall often runs to its foot too). **Not** for electric hits (Static's stream, single orbs, hex); Static's volley blast and grenades count and bleed `BLEED.blast` (2.6x) more (`blast` flag on the hit). **Bullets build up:** each hit on the same player within `BLEED.window` (1.4 s) bleeds `step` more, up to `max` (3.2x); several pellets in one tick share one burst. Hit events now carry `damage` and `blast`. All drops are instances of one quad (one draw), `DROP_CAP` per preset (90 to 1000, oldest first); a crate's drops go when it breaks (`dropProp` on `propBreak`); all go on a map reset or restart (`clearDebris`, `reset`).
- **Walking through blood** (`blood-wading.js`, you respawn clean: `WorldView.cleanPlayer` on respawn and restart): every player (you in renderer.js, others in remote-players.js, each with a `Wading`) walking through a pool (a death stain on the ground, a body, an explosion's pool: `WorldView.poolsOnGround`) soaks up `level` (1.6 per second while moving in it) shown in three merged stain stages on the body (`makeBloodStains`: boots; legs and coat hem; up the coat, shoulders, arm and hat brim, which is what the top-down camera sees; the brim's flecks are tagged `head` so a headless death leaves them out). It dries over 150 s and is gone on a respawn. For up to 8 steps after leaving a pool, each step leaves a red boot print, fainter each time, on the very same step as the print in the dirt and the same size and shape (renderer `updateFootprints` asks `Wading.takePrint()`; `BloodDrops.print` draws a sole-and-heel texture drawn with the dirt print's own formula, tread lighter, in its own instanced ring `PRINT_CAP`; indoors too; a map reset clears them). The hat gets more as it soaks (brim flecks from stage 2, more brim and the crown's top at stage 3), and so does the gun in the hand (`makeGunStains`: flecks on its top faces from its own bounds, rebuilt for each weapon, a few at stage 2 and more at 3). A body keeps the stains it died with (the corpse copies the visible stages). **Floating-fleck fix (v0.9b):** the arm's flecks ride the Static arm itself (`makeBloodStains(body, staticArm)`, arm-local), so they hide when the Nominal/Ballast IK arm replaces it (they used to hang beside the shoulder), and the gun's flecks are laid from what is really shown (`traverseVisible`; the hidden models' bounds put flecks in the air).
- **Blood on death** (`blood-splatter.js`): every player death (yours, and every other player's online via `netEvent`) lays a splatter on the floor under the body, thrown along the killing hit: pool, lobes, streaks and droplets on one textured quad (three canvas shapes, shared), grows in over 0.35 s, stays 60 s, fades over 4 s. Capped per preset (`SPLAT_CAP`, Potato 6 to Extreme 20), oldest first. Not cleared on restart or respawn. **Balanced and up** (`FINE`): 512 px textures (256 below), a fine mist of droplets, a wet highlight and a drying rim; more flying drops (scatter 80/120/160, Ballast 40/60/80). **Blood on walls:** along the hit (and 20 degrees either side; all round when there is no direction) the nearest fixed wall (collider taller than 1.5 m, not a prop) within 2.4 m; the nearest takes a vertical splat (spray with runs dripping down, bigger when closer), owned and faded like the floor stain.
- **Muzzle light:** rifle and Ballast shots flash the existing effects light (warm, a few hundredths of a second) on every preset that has it; no new shader variants.
- **Defaults:** phones default to Performance, desktop to Balanced.
- **What the presets set:** pixel ratio and scale, shadow map size and refresh rate, particle caps, textures and bump relief, and which detail layers are shown.
  - Performance: 768 shadow map (was 512), 256 ground texture (was 128), two-thirds of the ground cracks. Potato: blob shadows (see Rendering).
  - Dense ground cover is only built on Balanced and above; it's built on demand if you raise the preset.
- **Adaptive resolution** (`AdaptiveResolution`) lowers the 3D buffer on Performance and Balanced when fps drops.
