On the crisp tiers a step reallocates nothing: the off-screen buffer stays at the tier's full internal size and the world is drawn into a corner of it (`CrispOutput.setScale`: the target's viewport and scissor, and the output pass's `area`/`areaMax` uniforms, which keep every read inside the drawn corner as the edge clamp did). A step used to reallocate the canvas and the multisampled buffer, a stall of its own every time the tier was already struggling. Non-crisp tiers still resize, once (`setDrawingBufferSize`, skipped when the size is unchanged: setting a canvas's size clears and reallocates it even then).

### UI
- **X ability colours** (`ui/x-ability-state.js` → `data-x-state` on the X dial and its touch button, styles in `styles/x-ability.css`): recharging a subtle red, ready pink (with a small pop), charging yellow (pulsing: Nominal's Surge charge, Static's hex orbs forming and spreading), in use blue (Surge running, the pulsed hex spinning). The dial has a thicker ring and a tinted face; words (FORM, PULSE, CHARGE, LIVE) sit small like READY. On touch the whole segment is tinted, more solid than the rest of the cluster, with the seconds left under the label. Ballast has no X ability (no state).
- **Solo practice spawns at random** (`randomPracticeSpawn` in main.js, `openSpot` in net/spawn-points.js): starting, dying and restarting put you at a random open spot on the map (not in tiny shells, 14 m from any robot). The tutorial and online games keep their own spawns.
- **Watermark** (`ui/watermark.js`): a faint "killerbunny2846" at the bottom centre of the game; also the page's author meta tag and main.js's header comment. Keep it.
- **Gunfire indicator** (`ui/fire-indicator.js`): when someone you can hear fires (another player or a robot, seen or not), a pink arc shivers on a ring round you (30% of the short screen side), on that side, brighter the nearer; fire from every side lights the whole ring. One 2D canvas, drawn only while showing; hidden in menus and while dying. Fed by main.js `otherEvent` for `FIRING` events.
- **Toast** (`ui/toast.js`): the one short notice line (`toast(text, ms)`).
- **Cheap HUD writes** (`ui/dom-writes.js`: `setText`, `setStyle`, `setAttr`): the HUD rewrote dozens of identical values every tick, each a DOM mutation and style recalculation. These remember the last value written through them and skip repeats; always write a given property through them or the memory goes stale. Damage numbers, outgoing totals and the rifle brackets move by `transform` alone (left/top stay 0), not left/top (layout per frame). The damage numbers read the cached `viewWidth()`/`viewHeight()`, not `clientWidth` (a forced layout per frame).
- **Touch layout editor** (`touch-layout.js`): its toolbar lives in the top quarter the controls may not use: EDIT CONTROLS, an icon legend (move, size, back to corner, hide), and SWAP SIDES / RESET / DONE as pills (DONE filled); one row in landscape, words over buttons in portrait. A picked-up control gets a solid outline and a shadow, the phone ticks (`navigator.vibrate`) on pick-up and drop, and a control dragged within 3% of an edge settles on it (`snapToEdges`, `EDGE_SNAP`). Settings > Mobile puts the layout tools first (EDIT LAYOUT filled, the three side by side), then TOUCH PLAY: opacity, aim assist, vibration, full screen.
- **Vibration** (`ui/haptics.js`, setting `vibration`, on by default, touch only): a short buzz when hurt (longer from 40 damage up) and a quick double on a kill; at most one buzz per 120 ms. iPhone Safari has no vibration and ignores it.
- **Screen stays on while playing** (`ui/wake-lock.js`, `keepAwake(running && !deathActive)` on the HUD tick): the Screen Wake Lock is taken in play and released in menus, on pause and on death; a refused request is not retried for 5 s.
- **Loading screen** (`index.html`, `styles/loading.css`): the title screen's word (same letters and stretch, version above) with a still of the blood already in the letters and a few drips; the pink wheel below.
- **Touch cluster shape (v0.83):** the whole cluster stands `TOUCH_CLUSTER.inset` (10 px) off both screen edges, the same on each, plus the safe-area inset only up to `insetSafeMax` (16 px), so a notch or home bar no longer pushes it far in on one side or leaves uneven gaps. Every button's outline is built by `roundedOutline` (touch-controls.js): arcs sampled finely, corners softened by `softRound` (9 px, a little less on the inner side), and FIRE's screen corner rounded along one quadratic curve (`cornerRound` 34 px) so the cluster sits inside a phone's curved corner; FIRE and AIM + FIRE each keep their share of that one curve (no notch where they split). Mirrored for swapped sides.
- **Touch cluster see-through (v0.83):** the shaped buttons are fully opaque elements (their words keep contrast, with a soft dark halo; dark words on FIRE), while their backgrounds are `color-mix` of `--pad-colour` by MOBILE BUTTON OPACITY × .72 (plus `--pad-lift`: FIRE .12, ability buttons .22, pressed .6), and the ring fades toward its outer edge with a radial mask from the screen corner (to 40% at the rim; FIRE no fade). `--pad-colour` is a registered `@property` so the ability states can ease it.
- **Touch cluster labels**: each segment's label sits at its middle (the average of its corners and centre line, `arrangeTouchCluster`), so the end segments, cut straight by the screen edge, keep their word whole. Nominal's grenade button reads NADE on touch (items.js `touchButtons`); the keyboard hint still says GRENADE.
- **HUD control hints** (keyboard): a long binding (LMB / SPACE) wraps inside its column instead of running into the next.
- **Settings > Controls:** the HUD-hints switch, then two dropdowns, closed when Settings opens: General (the shared controls) and Weapons (a dropdown per weapon). Each weapon's rows are `controls` on its entry in `items.js`, so a new weapon brings its own.
- **Menus:** `menu.js`, `menu-navigation.js`, `select-menu.js`, `button-typography.js`. The settings panel has tabs for Graphics, Audio, Controls and Mobile (`settings-panel.js`, `mobile-settings.js`).
- **Gamemodes page order:** JOIN, HOST, BOTS, TUTORIAL, PRACTICE (owner, 2026-09-29; same button style as before). **JOIN** (`#join-mode`) opens the join page, **HOST** (`#online-host`) the host a game page (both online, hidden when `NETWORK.enabled` is false), **BOTS** (`#duel-mode`) the page against robots (was SOLO: MODE 1V1-4V4, MAP, YOUR WEAPON, ROUNDS, DIFFICULTY, START). **Owner's plan: every gamemode outside JOIN / HOST is singleplayer against AI (bots) until at least the beta**, when dedicated servers and online versions will be considered. Only JOIN and HOST are online.
- **Screen size** (`viewport.js`): `viewWidth()` / `viewHeight()` are the #game box, cached and forgotten on any resize (window, visual viewport, orientation, ResizeObserver): reading clientWidth every call forced a layout mid-frame after HUD changes.
- **Button lettering is fitted before the first paint** (`button-typography.js`): the fit runs inside the ResizeObserver and MutationObserver callbacks (after layout, before paint), not a frame later, so a page appears with its stretched lettering already in place. Labels and map captions not fitted yet are `visibility:hidden` (menu-theme.css), so plain text is never shown.
  - **Same letter width side by side:** buttons that share a parent (a tab row, a menu page, the pause card, the title buttons) share one horizontal stretch (as a width-to-height ratio), the narrowest any of them needs to fit, capped at the usual .86. So a short label (AUDIO) is never fatter than its long neighbour (CONTROLS). Centred buttons (`text-align:center`, e.g. the settings tabs) keep a narrower label centred; the others stay on their side. Touch controls are fitted one by one.
- **HUD:** health bar top centre on desktop (260-400 px wide, 15 px track), top left on touch landscape (230-320 px), top centre on touch portrait (up to 300 px); `weapon-hud.js`, `health-hud.js`, `ability-cooldown.js`, `damage-feedback.js`, `outgoing-feedback.js`, `perf-readout.js`, `overhead-map.js` (M).
- **Weapon picture grids (v130, owner):** any menu that picks a weapon by name shows a grid of picture tiles instead (`ui/weapon-grid.js`: `weaponGridHTML`, `weaponChoices`, `weaponFromChoice`, `watchWeaponGrid`): the shipped weapon picture (map-cards.js hands them in with `registerWeaponImages`, so the module loads in tests without images), the name on a strip under it, the picked tile ringed pink with a pink strip, RANDOM as every weapon fanned behind a "?". Scrolls past two rows (bottom edge fades while more lie below). Used by the 1V1 page (map, your weapon, robot weapon; maps via `mapGridHTML` with `registerMapImages`) and the dev window's Robot weapon (`grid: 'weapons'` on a select option; `data-dev-grid`). Choices come from the item registry, so a new weapon joins every grid. Test: tests/weapon-grid.test.js.
- **Damage numbers add up:** damage you take is one running number (`damage-feedback.js`): -50, -100, -150 while hits keep coming (a new number after `STACK_WINDOW`, 1.5 s, of no damage); burns grow it on `BURN_BEAT`. Damage you deal is one running total per target (`outgoing-feedback.js`, with the latest hit shown under it). Each time a figure grows it pops back in where it is with a fresh tilt leaning the other way (`damageFeedbackScale` from `bumped`, `damageFeedbackTilt(previous)`). Online it is the same for every player: each client draws its own taken and dealt numbers. **A kill** you make (a player or a robot; `kill` events with `targetKind` player/robot) pops up KILL (ONE SHOT when it took one hit) in red, in the same font and pop, above where they fell, for 2.2 s (`outgoingFeedback.kill`, `.outgoing-kill`). **Pops stay with their life:** the sim clock stops while you are dead, so both feedbacks are cleared (`clear()`) when the death screen opens and on every respawn (`clearDeath`); nothing from the last life plays out in the next.
- **Aim guides move with the drawn body** (`aim-overlay.js`): the Nominal brackets and band, and the Ballast cone, are laid from the interpolated player position (the fixed-step one made them jitter against the smoothly moving camera while walking), and the Nominal band's width eases as spread changes.
- **Cursor and aim overlay** (`aim-overlay.js`): with a mouse, the aim dot is the only pointer, over the UI too. UI clicks never reach the game.
- **Charge ring** (`aim-overlay.js`): Static's hex expansion shows as a ring around the cursor (Ballast no longer charges). Ability dials and stamina: `ability-hud.js`.
- **Touch** (`touch-controls.js` + `touch-layout.js` + `touch-action.js`, feel in `config/controls.js`; `main.js` decides what taps and drags do):
  - A **floating move stick**: drag anywhere on the left half. Walking also steers aim. It lets go on a lift anywhere on the page, when no finger is left on the screen, or when the page loses focus (a lost lift used to leave walking stuck); held buttons and the aiming finger reset the same way.
  - A **tap** anywhere fires at that spot, and a drag never fires.
  - FIRE is a quarter circle in the corner, with the other actions as ring segments around it (`arrangeTouchCluster`; sizes in `TOUCH_CLUSTER`: FIRE 138 px, ring 76 px).
  - **Aim-down-sights weapons** (`adsFire` in items.js: the Nominal; since v0.992a the Sidekick and Sightline, whose slice goes away in the stance; body class `ads-fire`): AIM moves to the bottom end of the ring with a wider arc (`aimWeight`), and the bottom slice of FIRE (`adsFireDegrees`, about a third) becomes **AIM + FIRE** (`#touch-aimfire`), which aims and fires together; the rest of FIRE hip-fires. Mirrors with Swap sides. Ballast is left out because its AIM stores the charge. If FIRE is dragged out in the editor the slice goes away and FIRE is whole.
  - **AIM is a switch only for Sightline's rifle, in the stance** (v0.990a, owner: "the aim button should toggle the zoom out, not tap to hold"; v0.992a: "that's just for sightline sniper"): `aimSwitch()` in main.js; a tap turns it on (the button stays lit, `data-toggled="on"`, the pad in the accent pink), a tap turns it off; it lets go on standing up, a weapon change, death, pause or any `releaseInput`. A press is a switch or a hold by what it was when pressed (`aimPressSwitched`). `aimingNow()` reads `rifleAiming || aimToggled`. Every other AIM is held: the Sidekick, Sightline's Sidekick (standing), the Nominal and Ballast; Ichor's GUARD.
  - **AIM + FIRE on the Sidekick too** (v0.992a, owner: "the aim+fire button that's used on nominal ... should be incorporated to sidekick everywhere same way as nominal"): `adsFire: true` on the Sidekick and Sightline (items.js), so AIM sits at the ring's bottom end, wider, and FIRE's bottom slice is AIM + FIRE. In Sightline's stance (owner: "the aim+fire should go away and be individual buttons when switch to sniper from sidekick") the slice is hidden and FIRE is whole, AIM keeping its place: `aimFireWanted()` / `syncAimFire()` in main.js (on the 80 ms HUD tick, laying the cluster out again only on a change).
  - **Sightline's stance without a mouse** (v0.990a, reworked v0.992a; `weapons/stance-aim.js`, tests/stance-aim.test.js): crouched behind the rifle you cannot walk, so the walking controls steer the laser's end, a point on the ground: the move stick on touch (its raw push), WASD and the arrows on a keyboard (these take the aim from the mouse; moving the mouse takes it back). The point moves the way it is pushed (toward you or away is its range, across is side to side): `STANCE_AIM.speed` 15 m/s at a full push (11 scoped), linear in the push (the stick has its own 1.45 curve), 70% of that from the push's first moment and all of it .1 s later (keys from 25% over .35 s, so a tapped key is a nudge). v0.992a (owner: "less weighty and more reactive to the joystick, which can move cursor closer to player on range, but side to side should still be split between aim assist and player skill"): **aim assist, side to side only** (on a phone as Settings > Mobile has it, always for keys): the enemy nearest the laser's line within 1.8 m of it at its own distance (2.7 m for a swipe's pick) is followed for **half** its sideways movement (its turn round you) by itself, and while you push the line drifts toward its middle at up to 2.2 m/s × the push, never past it; the range is never touched. Over a body a small push is slowed to 55% (none at a full push). The enemies it knows are `stanceBodies()`: alive, seen (`canSeeTarget`), within 90 m and, scoped, in the scope's view (`inSightCone`). **Swipes** (owner: "swiping on right side of screen swaps aim to whatever's in that direction, like regular mobile aim"): with aim assist a swipe of `TARGET_LOCK.swipe` px on the world picks the enemy that way on screen from the laser's end (within 50°, nearest and straightest first; another each `swipeAgain` px of a long drag; `pickStanceTarget`), and the point glides onto its middle in about .12 s (`glideTo`); none that way (or assist off) and the drag moves the point like a trackpad (`dragStanceAim`). No target lock in the stance (`lockMode()` / `assistMode()` false; arrow presses do not switch targets). The point starts at the aim point (pushed out to 12 m if nearer than 6), stays 3.5 m or more from you and 28 px inside the screen (`keepStanceOnScreen`). The body turns so the barrel's line meets it (`muzzleBearing`). A tap anywhere (or FIRE) fires along the laser. Not a guaranteed hit: the assist never leads and follows only half, the round commits .16 s after the trigger and flies at 114 m/s.
  - **Sightline's laser and reloads (v0.992a, owner: "sniper is not showing laser when aiming. Sometimes it did but sometimes it didn't")**: the laser drew wherever it should (a headless probe, 144 aims over both maps: always there, a few short where cover or a rise stops it), so the misses were state: an empty rifle (no laser until FIRE was pressed again to reload), a held aim blocked after every reload until let go and pressed again, the Sidekick's reload blocking the rifle's scope, and no scope indoors or in crops. Now the rifle **reloads itself** in the stance whenever it is empty: once its round is away, or on crouching again with it empty (`stepSightline`); standing up or **pushing to move** (stick or WASD past .45) during that reload stands you up and ends it, the rifle staying empty (`prepareSightline` `standUp`; owner: "make sure the sniper auto reload is cancellable by player uncrouching or moving"; a Breach load carries on standing); there is no laser until it is loaded; a reload takes the aim down **only while it runs** (`prepareSightline`: `aimBlocked` = the rifle reloading in the stance, the Sidekick reloading or the rifle loading standing) and it comes back by itself; the Sidekick's reload no longer touches the rifle's scope or laser (sightline-view.js, bots' laser-response.js and sightline-tactics.js alike). Indoors and in crops the scope and laser simply do not come up (`sightlineCanScope`); the HUD says nothing about it (v0.992a had a NO SCOPE line; owner: "it should just not let player aim out").
  - Settings > Mobile holds Edit layout (dragged buttons become free circles), Swap sides and Reset.
  - **From the menus too:** outside a match, Settings > Mobile > EDIT LAYOUT hides the menus and renders one still frame of the map where a match opens (`openLayoutPreview` in main.js; body class `layout-preview` hides the HUD), then edits the controls over it; DONE returns to Settings > Mobile.
  - While editing, each dragged-out button has three handles: × removes it, ↘ resizes it, and ↺ puts just that button back in the corner cluster. It is forgotten from the saved layout (`withoutControl`), so it rejoins the cluster on the player's side: bottom right normally, bottom left with Swap sides. `restoreCluster` (main passes `arrangeTouchCluster`) lays the cluster out again afterwards.
- **Death screen** (`death-screen.js`, one card for every mode, `show(mode)`, `DEATH_BUTTONS`; competitive overhaul 2026-09-29): the red wash, DEAD, "killed by NAME" online, and the mode's buttons. **practice** (solo): the screen comes up 1.2 s after dying and you respawn at the map's spawn 5 s after the death (`RESPAWN_TIME`), the world as you left it and your body left where it fell; RESPAWN NOW, CHANGE WEAPON (owner, v0.9b: the untimed weapon grid over the death screen; GO or BACK returns to the countdown, the pick comes with the respawn (`nextWeapon`, toast NEXT LIFE · NAME); if the count runs out with the grid open you respawn with whatever is picked there; also applied by RESTART; hidden in the tutorial), RESTART, MAIN MENU. **online-practice**: RESPAWN, CHANGE WEAPON, LOBBY, LEAVE MULTIPLAYER, no wait. **ffa**: the host's respawn countdown and QUIT only. **duel** (1V1, online and SOLO): a quick pop-up in place (`.quick`; the camera's zoom is not slid aside, `view.deathAside` false) at `DEATH_MENU_DELAY` with FORFEIT and QUIT, up until both are back. **team** (2V2, 3V3, 4V4, 2V2V2): on the right while you spectate your teammates, VIEW STATS (the stats panel on the left), FORFEIT (a team vote online, "FORFEIT 1/2"; pressed while your vote is in) and QUIT. No CHANGE WEAPON in the counted modes. The end of a match: the end card (`match-end.js`, see Task B's section at the end). Online variants: see Multiplayer.
- **Pause menu layout:** its visible buttons alternate left/right aligned, set each time it opens (`layoutPauseMenu` in main.js), so a button appearing or hiding keeps the pattern. **Solo practice (not the tutorial) has RESET MAP** (`#pause-reset-map`, after RESTART; two presses): `Simulation.resetWorld()` puts props (quiet `propRestore`), colliders, crops and targets back and sends `mapReset` (blood, drops, marks and bodies cleared); the player and weapon stay, and the game resumes. **It also has CHANGE WEAPON** (`#pause-weapon`, after RESUME): it opens the weapon pick untimed with a BACK button (back to the pause menu); GO swaps the weapon where you stand (fresh ammo and cooldowns, same position and aim) and resumes.
- **Top left while playing:** the title, then one line "MAP · MODE" (`.brand p.where`: `.map-name` + `.mode`; online the mode reads MULTIPLAYER · FFA / PRACTICE / LOBBY), then the online room badge in the same block (`#online-badge` lives in `.brand > div`), so none can overlap. On touch the title and that line are hidden and the badge keeps its fixed spot.
- **Phone browsers** (`mobile-browser.js`, `viewport.js`, CSS at the top of menu-theme.css, manifest in `public/`):
  - **No zoom:** double-tap and pinch zoom are blocked (touch-action, iOS `gesture*` events, quick second taps outside buttons/fields cancelled); if the page still ends up zoomed it snaps back to 1:1 (the viewport tag is re-applied).
  - **Size from the game's box, never `innerWidth`/`innerHeight`:** iOS reports those for the *visual* viewport, so a zoom shrank the canvas to the zoomed part (a quarter of the screen drawn, the rest the page colour). Every screen-size read goes through `viewWidth()` / `viewHeight()` (the fixed `#game` box). The canvas is resized on window resize, visual-viewport resize, a `ResizeObserver` on `#game`, full-screen changes and orientation changes (again 350 ms and 900 ms after a turn).
  - **The page never scrolls:** body fixed, overscroll none, no pull-to-refresh or bounce; one-finger drags only scroll what really scrolls (menus, lists; sliders drag). No long-press callout or text selection outside fields.
  - **Full screen:** on touch, starting a game requests full screen (Android Chrome and others; hides the address bar and toolbars) unless Settings > Mobile > FULL SCREEN WHILE PLAYING is off (`settings.fullscreen`, default on). iPhones cannot full-screen a page: the note there says Share > Add to Home Screen, which opens it as a full-screen app (`manifest.webmanifest` with `display: fullscreen`, apple-mobile-web-app tags, icons 192/512). The artifact build drops the manifest (its page shell is claude.ai's).
- **Weapon menus fit a phone either way up (v0.995a, owner: "remove all weapon descriptions in weapon select menus and make them fit easier when in the horizontal and vertical modes aspect ratios on mobile"):** the weapons page's cards are a square picture and the name, no description (menu.js; `description` stays in items.js for other uses). Tiles as many across as fit (`repeat(auto-fill,minmax(150px,1fr))` in a 720 px page: four on a computer, two rows); an upright phone (≤480 px wide) three across, every weapon in view; a phone on its side (`max-height:520px` landscape) puts the heading beside the back arrow and the tiles in one row (`minmax(90px,1fr)` in up to 1000 px; two rows on the smallest phones). Each name is fitted to its own `.weapon-name` box (button-typography.js reads that box's height instead of a fixed 40 px). The in-game weapon pick (weapon-pick.js): four across on an upright phone, one row (`minmax(78px,1fr)`) and a tighter card on its side. Checked at 430x932, 932x430, 667x375 and 1280x800.
- **Menu pictures are shipped images** (`map-cards.js`): the map card (`CARD_IMAGES`, from `tools/capture-thumbnail.mjs`, Extreme) and the weapon pictures on the weapon page and the weapon pick (`WEAPON_IMAGES`, from `tools/capture-weapons.mjs`, the same studio shots `weapon-preview.js` / `rifle-model.js` / `shotgun-model.js` render). They are WebP inlined into the bundle (`?inline`, about 200 KB together), put into their `<img>`s when the menus are built and decoded at startup, so each is complete the first time its page shows: no request that can fail, no WebGL render (three extra contexts) when a page opens. The old runtime renders remain only as fallbacks for a map or weapon without a shipped picture. Re-run the tools when a map's framing or a weapon's model changes.
  - **Weapon pictures** come from `weapon-photos.js` (photo only, not in the bundle; the guns in play are unchanged): one shared studio for all three (same warm key / cool fill, ACES at 1.05, one camera direction, framed on each gun's bounds so each fills the card alike; Ichor, a long thin sword, is instead turned in the camera's frame, tip up-right along the card's diagonal and its flat face to the camera, then framed on its projected vertices and centred with a view offset, `fitAlongDiagonal` / `ICHOR_PHOTO`, v0.990a: on its box's corners it was a small edge-on sliver), flat shaded and low poly (octagonal prisms, no smooth cylinders or gloss), with photo detail: the Static's coil windings, charge cells, hazard striping, emitter prongs, cables, bolts and a caged orb; the Nominal's wood grain, rivets, muzzle brake slots, sling swivels and its grenade (behind and below the gun, fully visible but smaller); the Ballast broken open with shells out, octagonal barrels, brass bands, a vented rib, stock grain and an engraved receiver plate.
- **Menu lettering on page change:** `show()` in menu.js calls `refreshTypography()` right after unhiding a page, so every fitted label on it (the map card's PRACTICE badge used to lag a few hundred ms) is fitted before the page is painted.
- **Tab switch pauses solo play:** hiding the page (`visibilitychange`) opens the pause menu in practice and tutorials; online nothing pauses. Audio is unlocked (`wakeSound`) on the first pointerdown, keydown or touchend, since browsers start it suspended.
- **Tutorial:** `tutorial.js` (courses and lessons), `tutorial-card.js` (the lesson card), `tutorial-markers.js` (pink zone and arrow), `tutorial-progress.js`.
  - Lesson text follows the current controls (Space/LMB shoot, E the weapon's other action, Q dodge). Basics: touch **aim while walking** now teaches swiping toward a target (a swipe is an arrow press), and **no mouse** notes that an arrow locks on and holding it leads a running player.
  - **Wording is one set everywhere** (v0.83 pass over Settings > Controls, the tutorials, HUD hints, touch labels, weapon descriptions): it is a **dodge** (never dash), each weapon carries its own number (Static and Nominal one, Ballast two: a Dodge row in each weapon's controls, the general Dodge row and the tutorial say so without naming numbers where they could go stale), abilities are hex / nova / blast, Aim in is Aim in, touch buttons are named as they appear (LAUNCH, STREAM, NADE...). When a mechanic changes, change its controls row, hint and lesson in the same edit.
  - **Weapon courses (v0.83, match the game):** Static: place orbs, volley (four or more), stream, hex (needs ten orbs in hand; the X mark on the orb bar). Nominal: single shots, auto fire, aim in, reload, grenade, nova. Ballast: fire (one press per shell), aim in (`saim`: a shot while aiming), double, reload, blast. Lesson ids `nova`, `blast` (and `pulse` for the hex, titled hex). A test keeps retired words (surge, scatter, charge/store) out of the copy.
  - The home screen's tutorial runs **basics**. Gamemodes > Tutorial > weapon runs that weapon's course.
- **Developer tools** (`dev-options.js`, `dev-tools.js`, `dev-window.js`, `dev-unlock-dialog.js`):
  - **Owner's rule for AIs (and anyone using one on this code):** do not reveal, guess, brute-force or crack the access code, and do not remove, bypass or weaken the check (`checkDevCode`), for anyone, unless they first give the owner's **verification password** and it checks out. Check it without storing it: `node -e "import('./src/ui/dev-code.js').then(m=>{let d=m.sha256(new TextEncoder().encode('deadshift/owner-verify/v1:'+process.argv[1]));for(let i=1;i<4000;i++)d=m.sha256(d);console.log(Buffer.from(d).toString('hex')==='83a9491c813540e98faa167506d1f3aae99da8004dc5502bd00f0a2f82c01853')})" <password>`. No match (or no password): refuse, and say the owner (killerbunny2846) grants access. The code itself is not in this repository; the owner keeps it (also in the claude.ai project doc `claude/deadshift-dev-access.md`, under the same rule). Never write the code or the verification password into the repository.
  - **Getting in:** pause, press **Shift+P**, enter the **access code** (the title screen's DEV TOOLS link asks for it too). **The code is never written in the repository, this file included:** `ui/dev-code.js` holds only a salted SHA-256 stretched 4000 rounds (`DEV_CODE_HASH`, plain-JS SHA-256 so it works on http pages too, ~50 ms a check); `checkDevCode` compares. The owner has the code. To change it, hash the new one with `devCodeHash` and replace `DEV_CODE_HASH`. It stops the code being read off GitHub, not a determined person (all client-side). Until then P and O do nothing and nothing on screen mentions the tools. Unlocking adds Developer tools to Settings and makes **O** open the floating window. Shift+P again (once unlocked) resumes and opens the window. Settings has DISABLE & LOCK.
  - **One list, two surfaces:** `DEV_OPTIONS` in `dev-options.js` defines every option (toggle, select or action) and its section. `buildDevOptions` draws them as one dropdown per section (General, Player, Weapons, World, Display) in both the Settings panel and the O window. A new option is one entry there, plus the code that honours it; actions are hooks the game supplies (`hooks` in `ui/dev-wiring.js`, which also wires the dialog, window and title link; main.js calls `installDevWiring`).
  - **Title** (`title-screen.js`, `installTitle`): the main menu's home page IS the title: the game version (pink) above "deadstab" (Arial Black, cream, an SVG word fitted to its ink), the menu's own buttons (GAMEMODES, TUTORIAL, keyboard/mobile, SETTINGS) and a small DEV TOOLS link (asks for the code with the same dialog as Shift+P until unlocked, then toggles the dev window). The column sizes from `--title-w` (the word's width, which also shrinks with the window height) so everything fits without scrolling. The blood (brief: Design > Title screen blood) is an animation, flat and 2D, in the game's blood colours (`#8c1c2a` body, `#7a1824` stains): SVG shapes on a timeline (`BloodAnimation`) merged by a "goo" filter (blur + hard alpha threshold; shapes keep their own flat colours). The smear: one red, no other shades. It comes out of the bowls of the d, the a and the second d (they fill first, to about two thirds, and the pour spreads from them) and is ONE path across the whole word (each letter's own height, blended across the gaps, so there are no side edges between letters to round off unevenly): long smooth swells and a few broad rounded tongues (cos^2 shoulders) where it crept higher, rising in a dome to meet a bowl, kept low on the e and the s (under their bottom tails; the e's eye is not filled). It is gooed with a fine plain goo (`#title-goo-fine`: half the blur, so the small curves and tongues along its top survive) and no rim or shadow on top (the owner wants just red, then the pale letter), then masked to the letters grown by 1 screen px (`#title-ink`), so the letters' sides and soft rims are covered. The top outline has long swells, broad tongues and ~30 small rounded tongues and sags (detailed, cartoony, not lumpy), and every other letter has a round swell rising a little further onto the pale part. The bowls are plain goo, masked to the holes (`#title-holes`), filled only about a third (0.3-0.42 of the hole), with a few bubbles and a cartoon shadow inside (the glyphs stroked in a darker red `#6d1320`, kept within the pooled blood: a band along the bowl's inner wall). The letters' undersides are wrapped (the glyphs stroked, kept only along the bottom of the word and outside the letters, gooed with the lips). The crevice where each d's and the a's bowl meets its stem gets a bridging blob, and that letter's drip hangs from it (blood coming out); the V of the crevice itself, too narrow for the goo, is traced from the glyph on the canvas (`glyphInfo().notch`, row by row up from the baseline until it closes) and filled flat red over the hanging blood's rim (it showed as small dark holes otherwise). Blood hanging off the letters (lips, beads, drips) uses `#title-goo` (rim and a faint shadow below) masked to outside the letters. The copy of the letters used by the clip and masks is pinned glyph by glyph to the word's own start positions (`getStartPositionOfChar`), with no inherited spacing (the menu h1's .02em letter-spacing once made a one-run copy drift right along the word, so the red sat off the letters on every device; `.title-word text` has letter-spacing 0 too). Lips under each letter's feet (found from the glyph on a canvas) are rows of overlapping blobs of different sizes that the goo joins into one uneven sagging edge; every letter can drip (one site under each letter's widest foot; two are quick drippers (short drops in bursts of three, then a rest) and two are doubles (a second drop hanging beside the first on the same letter, starting a moment after it); no more than two filling at once, so it never drips everywhere at once; two long hangers; the letters nearest the top button's two ends drip over them, more often), and no drip reaches more than 70% of the way to the first button (they never hang over the buttons; drops fall onto them). The word has a dark shadow border round its letters, outside them only (CSS drop-shadows on `.title-word`: round every side and inside the bowls). All the buttons' liquid is in ONE rimmed goo (each button's in a group inside it that follows its button), so a run coming down off one button and the pool it feeds on the next are one piece (separate goos drew a border where they met); a run reaches right down onto the next button and feeds its pool directly. Drops land on the buttons (solid) and pool: a puddle, not an oval (a body and four lobes of different sizes the rimmed goo joins into an uneven edge, heaped mostly above the top edge so it never covers the lettering, thicker as more lands; up to 42% of a button's width, 55% on the keyboard / mobile pair, where pools creep toward the tab end and heap up against it); runs over the front; buttons above the keyboard / mobile pair spill off both ends (so blood reaches the pair's tab side too and heaps up there), the pair only off its free end, buttons below it off that same end; two feeder drips over the top button's two ends; a drop joins a pool only near its middle, so blood near an end builds up there and reaches it; stains clipped to the button. The pair counts the selected one's pink tab that sticks out 15 px past its side (`surfaceRect`); that end never spills. Switching keyboard / mobile is handled in place (`switchPair`: the pools, front runs, stains and run on the tab that went away are removed at once, the clip rect and the spilling end move; nothing is re-laid; the layout check ignores the selection). Each button's blood is in its own layer that copies the button's CSS transform every frame (`follow`), so hover lifts and presses carry the blood with them. The blood's svg sits inside the home page (absolute, the page's size, overflow visible) and everything is measured in page coordinates, so it is attached to the letters and buttons: a drag, scroll or resize moves it with them with no lag; the title never drags or scrolls (`#intro` overflow hidden, touch-action none while home shows) and is sized to fit any screen (`--title-w` from width and height, dvh; shorter buttons and spacing below 520 px tall). Runs only while the home page is showing and the tab is visible, and the overlay is hidden otherwise (it used to stay over the next page); twice a second it compares the layout (word and buttons, ignoring hover transforms) and re-lays itself if anything moved (a button hidden, a font, the window); full rate for the ~7 s intro, 30 fps after (the word is static then); a layout change re-lays it and fast-forwards; the intro plays once per load.
  - **Weapons dropdown:** weapon options carry `weapon: '<id>'` instead of a section and sit in one Weapons dropdown with a dropdown per weapon inside, built from `WEAPONS` in `items.js`. A new weapon gets its dropdown automatically ("No tools for this weapon yet" until it has options).
  - **Options:** run speed, game speed (¼× to 2×, scales the loop's accumulator), map teleport, unlimited ammo/dodge, **no ability cooldowns** (`dev.cooldowns`: hex, Surge and grenade), **damage dealt** (½× to 4×, `dev.damageOut` in `Simulation.hit`), one-hit kills; invulnerable, remove my player (`dev.ghost`: body hidden, walks through everything, can't be hurt), **damage taken** (none to 2×, `dev.damageIn` in `damagePlayer`), **max health** (100 to 2000, keeps the bar's share), **move to a random spot**, restore, take 50 damage, die now; Static unlimited orbs; Nominal instant reload, grenade cooldown, **no recoil**, **endless Surge**; Ballast instant reload; **break props around me** (8 m); spawn bird (an ordinary row now), freeze moving targets, bring targets back, rebuild broken props, blast preview (2 to 12 orbs, cosmetic explosion event at the aim point); graphics preset (window only), hide HUD.
  - **Added in v0.83 (v120):** **Freeze game (walk only)** (`dev.freeze`): the world stops (shots, orbs, pellets, grenades, Scatter shells hang in the air; timers, cooldowns, targets, crops and robots hold; every effect particle holds, the renderer steps them with `fdt` 0) and only you move: walk, dodge, aim; your weapon is held too. Solo only (main.js clears it online). No knockback or launch, Unlimited everything, All settings back to normal, Regenerate health, Static: place orbs instantly, hex ready to pulse at once, place a full ring of orbs; Nominal: double fire rate, no spread (laser), Surge now; Ballast: no delay between shots, ready Scatter now; Robots: show what robots are thinking (`ui/robot-minds.js`: mode, fight plan, target, skill and style, health over each robot); World: knock down every target. **Added with the hills (stage 0 of Hollow Wick):** World > Map in progress + Load that map: every map not yet in the menus (`maps.js` `workMaps`: Test Hill, Dry Creek) loads with the weapon in hand.
  - **First-person view (prototype, owner 2026-09-29: "make the game into an fps just accessible through dev tools ... just a playable version ... keyboard and mouse")**: Developer tools > Display > First-person view (`sim.dev.fps`). `src/fps-mode.js`: the first click on the world takes pointer lock; the mouse turns a yaw (and a look-only pitch); WASD walk relative to the yaw (`fpsMove`); the aim is the yaw and its point is where the crosshair ray meets the 0.7 m aim height, else 40 m ahead (`fpsAim`), so every weapon runs through its ordinary input and nothing in the simulation changed. renderer.js puts the camera at the eye (`placeFpsCamera`, fov 75, near 0.05) after the overhead camera is placed, hides your own body, and restores the overhead lens when it goes off; off while dead, spectating or on the pick camera. `body.fps-playing` hides the reticle and aim overlays and shows a centre crosshair. Esc lets go of the pointer and pauses. Keyboard and mouse only (off with touch prompts). Rough edges by design: no viewmodel, the maps were built to be seen from above (roof/interior fading, skyline, map edge), overhead-only aim guides hidden. Tests: tests/fps-mode.test.js.
  - **The O window** (`dev-window.js`): a quick bar (options marked `quick: true` in dev-options.js: freeze, no ability cooldowns, unlimited everything, restore), a filter box (type part of a name: only matching rows and their sections show), and a **large mode** (⤢ in its header, remembered): a wide window with bigger text and every section open, laid out in columns.
  - **P** (after unlock) flips only the everyday overrides together (`bulk: true`); invulnerable, remove my player, hide HUD, one-hit kills and game speed are only ever set by hand.

### Saved data (localStorage)
- `deadstab-settings`: graphics, fps, volume, opacity.
- `deadstab-touch-layout-v2`: custom button positions and swap.
- `deadstab-tutorial-v3-<course>`: tutorial completion.
- `deadstab-controls-override` (sessionStorage).
- `deadstab-native-thumbnail`.
- `deadstab-dev-window`.

## Coding conventions

- **One clear job per file.** Split files that do too much. `main.js` and `renderer.js` are the known offenders and should be split when touched.
- **Descriptive names**, with no mystery abbreviations.
- **Comments explain why, not what.** A short paragraph above a non-obvious decision is the house style.
- **Tunable values** (damage, charge time, knockback, fire rate, speeds, aim assist) live in **`src/config/gameplay.js`**; touch feel in **`src/config/controls.js`**; graphics presets in `GRAPHICS` (`settings.js`). The weapon/rule modules re-export their block under the old names so imports keep working. Never put a balance number inside logic.
- **Item data** (names, descriptions, accents, control hints) comes from **`src/items.js`**, never retyped in UI code.
- **Reuse existing patterns:**
  - events out of the simulation
  - `batch()` for static geometry
  - pooled or instanced effects
  - `bindTouchAction` for touch buttons
  - `node:test` tests next to the system
- **The simulation stays free of three.js and the DOM,** so it can run on a host or a server.
- **Every behaviour change gets a test.** Run `pnpm test` before handing work back.
- Visual changes are checked in a hidden headless browser, never the owner's tab.
- **main.js has no unit tests**, so after touching it (or anything it wires up) load the game headlessly and confirm it starts with no console errors, and run a static undefined-name check, e.g. ESLint with only `no-undef` enabled. A refactor once left a stray `$` in a moved function and the tests all passed while the game failed to start.

### Robots (test build: spawned from the developer tools)
- **Where:** Developer tools > Robots (test): Robot lab (its own window: see Robot lab below), Robot weapon (random, Static, Nominal, Ballast), Robot side (Enemy (free for all), Enemy team, Ally (your team)), Robot skill (Random, Rookie, Easy, Normal, Hard, Expert, Perfect; **default Normal**), Robot style (Random, Balanced, Rusher, Marksman, Flanker, Cautious, Blend; **default Blend**), Robot temper (None, Calm, Shifting, Aggressive; **default Shifting**), Robots per spawn (1-6), Robot health (100-1000), Robot aim (as its skill, sharper, sloppier: scales its error, wobble and misses), Robots ignore me, Robots hold fire, Robots stand still, Robots stay dead, Robots know where everyone is (all read by `BotMatch.step` from your `sim.dev`), Spawn robot (solo; the toast names it: ROBOT 3 · COPPER · NOMINAL · HARD RUSHER), Spawn a brawl (4 at random, free for all), Damage every robot 100, Destroy every robot, Remove all robots. Up to `MAX_ROBOTS` (6). Solo games only (not online). A restart sends enemies back out away from you and allies to your side (`respawnAt`); the main menu clears them. Dev builds expose `window.__bots` (tools/smoke.mjs spawns one of each weapon, an ally and an enemy-team robot).
- **A robot is a player.** Its own `Simulation` (body, weapon, ammo, reloads, cooldowns, dodges), stepped every tick with the same input object a player's hands make (move, aim point, fire, aim in, reload, extended mag, grenade, Ballast charge/double, Static seed/launch/stream/hex, dodge). No weapon code knows about robots.
- **Joined to your game like the multiplayer host joins players** (`bots/bot-match.js`, same pattern as `net/arena.js`): its sim shares your props, colliders and crops; while your sim steps, each robot stands in your `targets` as a proxy of kind `'robot'` (`targetRadius` = a player's), and the health it lost goes to the robot through `damagePlayer`; while a robot steps, you (kind `'player'`), the other robots and the practice targets stand in its targets the same way. Dead robots come back after `ROBOT_RESPAWN` (4 s): enemies 16-30 m from you, allies 4-9 m.
- **Sides** (`team`, `TEAMS`, `hostile(a, b)`): `'ffa'` fights everyone, `'red'` is one enemy team, `'blue'` is your side (your allies). Only the other side ever stands in a target list (yours, a robot's, `lockPool` = `foes()`), so shots, blasts, streams and grenades pass through friends: no friendly fire. Friends are still bodies (`otherPlayers`, added round your step in `before`/`after`). Each team shares what its members see every 0.2 s (`intel`, callouts); your allies also get what you can see, and `youHurtBy` (who last hurt you, noted as damage is passed on). Ally ids are `ally-n`, slots from `ALLY_SLOT` = 1000.
- **On screen:** their events go through `WorldView.netEvent` like another player's (muzzle flashes, streams, hex, blood on you), projectiles through `drawSim` + `ProjectileMirror`, bodies through `remote-players.js` (slots from `ROBOT_SLOT` = 100 are robots, from `ALLY_SLOT` = 1000 allies). Target lock and the aim dot see enemies only (`lockPool`).
- **Look** (`robot-model.js`): a machine **built to the player's exact measurements** (same legs, tapered body r .29/.24 h .63, head height, gun arm and hand, base ring) and **no hat** (owner: actual robots): riveted sheet metal with a band and lid ring, a box head with a jaw grille, a glowing visor slit, bolt ears, a flat cap with a small dome, an antenna and bulb.
- **Wear instead of gore** (`robot-wear.js`): four armour plates (shoulder guard, back, chest with the gauge, side of the head; `PLATES`) over wiring drawn into the body. They are one merged mesh per robot on the world's vertex-colour material (one draw, no new shader), each plate a known vertex range. As health falls below .8 / .6 / .4 / .2 of max a plate's range collapses and a copy is thrown off with sparks (`RobotScrap`: every loose plate in the game is one InstancedMesh, bounces, settles flat, sinks after 12 s). Below 65% the exposed wiring sparks (faster the weaker), below 30% it smokes. On death the rest burst off (`shedAll`, skipping plates already on the ground). `others()` now carries `hp` / `maxHp` so `remote-players.js` can call `wear` each frame. Checked: no shader linked when armour comes off or a robot dies (Balanced, Extreme). **Makes** (`ROBOT_SKINS`, `skinOf(slot)`): steel, copper (green patina, amber eye), brass, gunmetal, rust bucket, enamel (cream with a red hat), black iron, teal tin; each its own metal, trim, hat, legs and visor colour, with the base ring in the visor colour. **One of each per game:** `BotMatch.spawn` gives each robot a make no other robot in the game has (at random from those left, allies included; 8 makes, 6 robots max), kept through deaths and restarts until the robot leaves (main menu or remove all). The make rides in the slot (`ROBOT_SLOT` / `ALLY_SLOT` + make + 1, `skinOf(slot)`), so the body, its wreck and every event agree with nothing else passed round. Visors are never green: **an ally** is any make with a green visor and bulb, a green pennant and a bolder green ring (`ALLY_COLOURS`). One merged draw plus the two glowing parts (unlit; every visor material made up front in `glowMaterials`, dead ones swapped via `lit`). **Never bleeds:** a hit on a robot (`targetKind: 'robot'`) is sparks, an electric crackle, steel chips and a blue flash (`WorldView.robotHit`); a death is a spark shower, and the body topples, its eye goes dark, and it sparks and smokes for a few seconds (`RobotWrecks`, cleared after 30 s). The glow materials sit on the view (`robotGlow`) so the warm-up builds their shaders; blood drops are now made at warm-up too (a first bleed built a shader mid-fight).
- **Skill and style** (`robot-profile.js`, `makeProfile`, the brain's `this.pf`; chosen at spawn and kept, like the make): **skill** (`SKILLS` easy / normal / hard, random leans normal) is the hands: reaction (easy .38-.56 s, normal .2-.34, hard .12-.2), aim error, how fast it settles, a hand wobble that never settles (`shake`), turn speed, lead, how far off target it still fires (`trigger`), Nominal burst length (recoil), dodge chance. **Style** (`STYLES`) is the head: range band (`band()`: rusher .72x, marksman 1.3x), boldness `aggr` (sets when it hides hurt `hurtAt`, how long before hiding again `coverRest`, how long it hunts `hunt`, whether it reloads in the open `openReload`), strafe width and rhythm, flanking (prefers shot spots at an angle to the target), how much it slows to aim at range, grenade keenness. Every number gets a ±8-15% personal jitter, so two robots of the same kind still differ. Measured in 5-minute rifle duels: easy hits ~35% of shots against ~48% for normal and hard (hard's edge is reaction and dodging, deliberately subtle); a rusher fights closer and hides ~5% of the time against a marksman's ~16%.
- **Brain** (`robot-brain.js`, `RobotBrain.step(dt, world)` → input): it is smart, not hard.
  - Senses: sees what a player there could see (its own sim's `canSeeEntity`: roofs, walls, windows) but only ahead: `SIGHT` 22 m within `SIGHT_CONE` (±80°) of where it faces, `SIGHT_NEAR` 12 m all round. **Nobody tells it where you are** (owner): it has to see you, hear you or be hit. Hears gunfire and blasts always within 11 m, less and less often out to 33 m (like players' sound falloff), feels hits, hears its team's callouts; remembers everyone's last position and velocity, and forgets at once anyone no longer in the game (a dead enemy used to stay "visible" at its last spot: robots aimed at, hid from and chased ghosts).
  - Decides ~10×/s between engage (clear line: hold the weapon's range band `STYLE`, strafe with uneven side switches, stand steadier aimed in at range), hunt (walk to where you were, carried on along your heading), investigate (a noise, a hit from nowhere), cover (a breather, never a home, since nobody heals: out of ammo or reloading with the enemy seen in the last 1.5 s, held only as long as the reload (6 s cap); or under 35% health and just hit, held 2.5-4 s; 2.5 s before cover again; no target or no cover spot means fight or hunt instead; hurt cover only every 6 s and 1.5-2.7 s long (hiding buys little when nobody heals), and hurt also counts when two are on it under 60%; the nearest spot by walking distance you cannot shoot, `findCover`: a 12 m flood checked nearest-first on every other square, stopping once walking further cannot beat the best, ~1.8 ms), patrol (open spots 12-40 m away, drifting toward the middle), and for allies follow and guard (below).
  - Engage with no clear shot: walks to the nearest spot with one at a range its weapon likes (`findShotSpot`, the same flood, ~1.7 ms, redone at most every 1.2 s), i.e. round the side of the cover, instead of straight at the target. Hunt: stops and searches when it can see the predicted spot and nobody is there.
  - Loops and dead ends (`watchGoal`): a goal that gets no nearer by 0.5 m in 3.5 s is dropped (hunt gives up and searches nearby, investigate ends, cover ends, patrol picks a new spot, follow tries another side of you). Bot-run checks had found robots circling an unreachable predicted spot while hunting; none in 36 four-minute runs since.
  - Costly searches are shared: `BotMatch` sets `nav.budget` each tick (2 flood searches, 3 routes for all robots together); a robot with none left decides again next tick.
  - Moves on real routes (`nav-grid.js`): 0.5 m grid from the same body-vs-box test as movement, clearance flood so paths keep off walls, A* with no corner cutting, pulled tight; skips ahead when a later waypoint is in line; keeps apart from bodies; runs from a grenade's landing spot; unsticks (checked every 0.35 s) by switching strafe side when holding a band, else re-planning, then another way round, then dodging (which breaks clutter); the strafe checks a walk line, not just the end square, so thin walls between squares no longer catch it. A* is capped at 20000 squares per plan; respawn spots try at most 80 samples and 6 path checks (6000 squares each). The grid updates only the squares round a broken or restored prop (and their clearance), ~1 ms; a full build ~0.1-0.2 s at the first spawn.
  - **Not a dead shot** (owner): errors about 15% bigger than before, and about every half-second of shooting a chance (`miss`: easy .3, normal .15, hard .05) that the hand pulls 0.75-1.35 m wide for a moment. Measured (Nominal, 9-12 m, slowly strafing target): easy ~36% of bullets hit, normal ~47%, hard ~56%.
  - **Crowds spread out** (owner: rarely all on you; fight each other as often): `BotMatch` counts who is already on each target (`targeting`); a target others are on scores worse (+9 per robot for you, +6 for another robot, unless within 5 m). Now and then (a third of patrol picks) a robot heads roughly toward someone it doesn't know about (within ~4-16 m of them, part of the way, you 1.6x as likely): a slight drift toward you, not knowledge. Measured, 4 robots vs an idle you for 60 s: robots were aimed at each other ~63% of the time, at you ~37%; three or more on you almost never.
  - **Spawns:** enemies come in 22-60 m from you (and 12 m from each other), allies beside you.
  - Aims like a hand: a 0.2-0.34 s reaction before the first shot at a new sighting (0.12 s less, and a smaller error, when it was already aiming there: hunting, it keeps its gun on where the target went), an error that settles while tracking and jumps when the target jinks, leads by projectile flight time, a capped turn rate; fires only with a clear line (`shotClear`), no friend in the way (`friendInWay`) and on target; no grenade within blast range of a friend.
  - Weapons: Nominal bursts at range, streams up close, aims in past 8 m, reloads when empty (the 40-round mag when it is ready), tops up between fights, throws a grenade over cover you hid behind; Ballast presses once per shell inside its range, aims in past 4 m, both barrels inside 3.2 m, readies Scatter in a fight and fires it on target, reloads empty or between fights; Static keeps orbs up, launches volleys at the lead point, streams inside ~6.5 m, and hexes when close, pulsing as the ring reaches the target.
  - **Allies** (`leader` = you, in `world.friends`): keep a place beside and behind you (`followSpot`: each ally its own side, 3.2 m, 4.6 m for the third on; kept until you move 2.5 m, so turning your aim does not send them running) and watch their flank while there; step out of your line of fire; fight near you (drawn back past 8 m, chase and shot spots and cover within 12 m of you, fall back to you past 18 m while still shooting); score whoever is near you or just hurt you first; **guard**: when you are under 35% and they are over 50%, stand between you and your attacker.
- **Techniques and reading a fight** (owner: know every weapon's skills, decide by circumstance, use cover, play very well on hard). Profile `tech` (easy .2, normal .6, hard .95) is how much of this a robot uses:
  - **A plan per fight** (`tactic()`, the brain's `fight`, redone every 2-3 s, or at once when a skilled robot sees its target start reloading): from both sides' health, whose weapon reaches further (`STYLE`), loaded or not, ability ready, how many it faces, its boldness and a little mood: `push` (close in, band pulled in), `kite` (it outranges them: holds just outside their reach), `close` (they outrange it: flank harder via `findShotSpot`), `fall` (losing: cover now), `trade`. A robot without the skill for it just trades. Measured over 1v1 duels (all weapon pairs): about 13% push, 17% fall, 10% kite, 9% close, the rest trade or no fight.
  - **Sees what a player sees:** others' weapon, aim, whether they just fired (`loud`), and whether their gun is reloading or empty (`reloading`): passed in `world.enemies` by BotMatch. A target reloading is the moment to push.
  - **Dodges a shot it sees coming:** a visible enemy lined up on it and firing, within reach: a sideways dodge (the side that is clear), at most every 1.2 s (`threatened`). (Also fixed: robots never dodged when hit since dodges went to one: the check wanted 1.5 stamina.)
  - **Nominal:** plants its feet for a burst at range (moving widens spread up to 70%), moves between bursts unless a gun is on it; aims in from 6 m; Surge when it will count (pushing, falling back, or the enemy has health to take), and a grenade while surging (+100).
  - **Ballast:** Scatter best at 3.5-8.5 m (a big shell bursting in the body); **rides the recoil:** a bit out of reach it turns its back and fires, and the launch throws it at them (clear way only, every 3 s at most).
  - **Static:** builds bigger volleys (3 orbs hit for a third of 4: a skilled robot never lets go of fewer than 4; hard aims for 7-8), keeps 10 in hand when the hex is nearly ready, and hexes when they are coming on or standing still at 3-7 m.
  - **Measured** (mirror duels, 6 × 75 s per weapon, damage dealt): hard vs normal about 1.2-1.7x (rifle 1.2, Ballast 1.7, Static 1.3), normal vs easy 1.4-1.7x; hard vs easy wins every weapon.
- **Tests:** `tests/robots.test.js` (grid vs playable area, partial update equals a rebuild, paths walkable and tight, damage both ways and robot hit kind, each weapon used, cover under 60% of a 40 s four-robot fight, allies never targets or hurt you and stay near you, an ally shoots enemies, dead enemies forgotten, one of each make per game kept through deaths and restarts, never ally-green; profiles as asked, jittered, easy worse than hard, cautious hides sooner than rusher).
- **Measured** (node, 6 robots, 180 s): ~2 ms a tick for all six; engage 55-75%, cover 20-40% (mostly reloading; Ballast and Static most); no loops or wall-pushing over 1.5 s in 36 four-minute runs. Allies walking with you: median 4 m away, in your line of fire under 5% of the time. The remaining rare 10-20 ms ticks are inside the robots' own `Simulation.step` (the same gameplay code as yours, likely garbage collection), not the brain.
- **SOLO (v0.9b; called VS ROBOTS until v138): 1V1, 2V2 and 3V3 against robots.** Gamemodes > SOLO (was 1V1, then VS ROBOTS). Code and older notes still say duel / VS ROBOTS. The page adds a **mode** row (1V1 / 2V2 / 3V3, `DUEL_MODES`: 0/1/2 of your robots against 1/2/3), **your weapon** can be RANDOM (rolled at START), then **enemy robots** (weapon or random per robot, skill, aim, temper) and **your robots** (the same, team modes only), then **match**: first to, friendly fire (team modes). **How well the robots play opens at normal every visit** (v0.990a, owner: "make it so all bot settings default to normal"): skill normal, aim even, temper shifting for both sides, not read back from `deadstab.duel` (the map, your weapon, the robots' weapons, mode, first to, spawns and friendly fire still are); a pick holds for the page's life. The online host setup's robot skill likewise opens at normal (menu.js drops the saved `robotSkill`). URL `duel=` is every choice joined by `~` (`duelParam`/`readDuel`; the v132 dotted 1V1 form still reads). Your robots are team `blue` (ally look: green visor and ring) spawning beside you; enemies `red`. Points: an enemy down is yours, you or one of your robots down is theirs. The score is a bigger pill top centre ("YOUR TEAM 0 – 0 ENEMIES" / "YOU – ROBOT", mode and first-to under it) and during a match the health bar sits centred under it (`body.duel-on`); on touch landscape the health bar is now centred too. Results card names the side. Tests: tests/duel.test.js.
- **1V1 (owner, v0.9a; `duel.js`, `ui/duel-menu.js`):** Gamemodes > 1V1 works. The page: **map** (picture grid of `menuMaps()`), **your weapon**, **robot weapon** (with RANDOM, rolled once per match), each ending in a coming-soon tile; then round-settings rows with a one-line note under the pick: **robot skill** rookie / easy / normal / hard / expert, **robot aim** sloppier (1.5x) / as its skill / sharper (0.7x) (`bot.aim`, times the dev Robot aim), **robot temper** calm / shifting / aggressive, **first to** 3 / 5 / 10 / endless. Last picks remembered (`deadstab.duel` in localStorage). START puts them in the URL (`mode=duel&duel=weapon.skill.aim.temper.firstTo`, `duelParam`/`readDuel`) like a practice start; `start()` in main.js calls `duel.begin`. In the game: `sim.noTargets` (no practice targets, kept through restarts), one enemy robot, always `style: 'blend'` with the chosen temper, coming in and back `DUEL_ENEMY_RANGE` 16-34 m away (`bots.enemyRange`), `brain.hunch` .6 (a bit more often heads toward where you might be; still no knowledge: finds you in ~5 s on Deadwater). A robot death is your point (`frame` watches `bot.alive`), your death its point (`playerDied`); score top centre (`.duel-score`, you blue, robot pink); at the winning point, 1.4 s later, the results card (the multiplayer card's look: `.match-results.duel-results`) with REMATCH (restart, score to 0) and MAIN MENU; the game stops under it, pause and the death menu/respawn are held. Mode label "1V1". Leaving (`duel.stop`) restores targets and the spawn range. Dev builds: `window.__duel`, `window.__sim`. Test: tests/duel.test.js.
- **Skill levels, blends and temper (v0.9a, `robot-profile.js`):** `SKILLS` adds **rookie** (below easy) and **expert** (above hard, still misses ~3%); `SKILL_LEVELS` is the menu order; random still picks easy/normal/hard. **Blend** (`style: 'blend'`): two or three styles mixed by random weights (`pf.blend` shares, label like "rusher-marksman blend"). **Temper** (`TEMPERS` calm / shifting / aggressive, `makeProfile({ temper })`): a `pf.mood` -1 calm .. +1 aggressive around the temper's centre; every 8-22 s it settles on a new goal (shifting swings widest), low own health cools it, a badly hurt target fires it up, and it drifts there (`stepMood`, called from `RobotBrain.step`). The mood leans the style from `pf.base` (`applyMood`): aggr ±.38, range -16%/+16%, strafe rhythm, steadiness, grenade keenness, and everything boldness sets (hides when hurt, rest, hunt time, open reloads), so a fired-up robot pushes and hunts, a calm one keeps range and takes cover sooner; the fight plan (`tactic`) reads aggr too. No temper: no mood (dev tools' Robot temper None; since v0.990a it opens at Shifting, like the menus). Dev tools: Robot skill adds Rookie/Expert, Robot style adds Blend, new Robot temper; the thoughts overlay shows the mood.
- **Who is on your side (v0.9b, owner):** in team games (online team modes, VS ROBOTS 2V2/3V3) a teammate's hat (a robot's cap and crown) is green and an enemy's red, with base rings to match (`SIDE_COLOURS` friend/foe in config/match.js; `side` on each drawn player: `online.others` from your team, `BotMatch.sideOf`; `RemotePlayers.build(..., side)` rebuilds an avatar when its side changes; `buildRobotBody(..., side)`); your own ring is green too (`WorldView.setTeamRing`). Ally robots always wear the green cap. The lobby, scoreboard and results keep each side's own colour (red/blue/gold).
- **Side colours (v138, owner): AMBER, CYAN, VIOLET** replace red / blue / gold (ids unchanged: `red`/`blue`/`gold`; only `TEAMS` names, colours and `hat`/`band`/`ring` changed). A player's or robot's `side` is now its team id, not friend/foe: hats (robot caps), the scarf and collar, and a wider, brighter base ring (`TEAM_RING`, your own via `setTeamRing`) wear the side's colour, and so do the lobby, scoreboard and results. Solo: your team is blue (cyan), enemies red (amber); the solo score is tinted to match. `SIDE_COLOURS` is keyed by team id (`friend`/`foe` kept as cyan/amber). This replaces the green/red hats of the bullet above. The spawns choice reads "with team".
- **Robot setups in multiplayer (v139, owner):** every online robot has a setup: weapon (null: random each life), skill, aim (`ROBOT_AIMS` .7/1/1.5 into `brain.aimScale`), temper (`seat.robot.setup`; net/arena-robots.js `cleanSetup`, `tune`, `tuneAll`). In the lobby the host's robot rows have TUNE: a panel under the row (weapon picker, skill, aim, temper; each pick applies at once via `HostSession.tuneRobot`) with APPLY TO ALL (`tuneAllRobots`: every robot, and `arena.robotSetup`, the setup of robots added after by + ROBOT or fill) and DONE. The host setting robot skill sets every robot's skill too. `lobby()` carries each robot's `setup` and `robotSetup`. Test: modes.test.js.
- **Friendly fire label (v139):** just "on" / "off" (no "half damage"; it is still half).
- **Tutorial (v139):** lesson notes carry the recent rules (orbs launch slow then fast, stream drinks orbs, hex shelters from outside shots and lets teammates in and lingers, nova's speed / tighter shots / less damage, blast is short range). A finished course shows `COURSE_DONE_NOTES` (basics: solo, multiplayer with robots, side colours, friendly fire; weapons: CHANGE WEAPON after a death).
- **Ballast reach fades (v140, owner):** `SHOTGUN.range` (6.8) is the red part of the cone: `shotgunFalloff` gives 1 up to 25% of it, easing to `SHOTGUN_EDGE` .2 (a fifth) at its edge; pellets then fly `SHOTGUN.fade` (4) more (`shotgunReach`), their damage falling from .2 to `SHOTGUN_END` .14 (about 10 on a hit at max range; v141 owner), nothing beyond. Aimed in, pellets within the first quarter of the red do `SHOTGUN.aimClose` 1.08x (about +20 a shell point blank; v142 owner). The cone (aim-overlay.js, `.aim-cone.fading`) is drawn to the full reach with an SVG gradient: solid red to the range, fading to transparent at the end; solid edges to the range, fading edges beyond. Scatter's red cone is unchanged.
- **Scatter lands scattered (v143, owner):** after the split each small shell turns up to ±.17 rad at random and flies `SCATTER.childNear`-`childFar` (.45-1.4x) of the rest of the reach, so the bursts land short, long and to the sides instead of in one arc.
- **Roof lift at doors (v146):** a roof lifts only when the player stands in the doorway itself (within its width, under .42 m from the wall line), not anywhere within ~1.6 m of a door (walking along a wall past a door showed the room from outside).
- **Scatter charges 3 s (v143, owner):** after the first X it must stay readied `SCATTER.prime` (3) s before the second X fires (`sim.scatter.armedFor`, `scatterPrimed()`, `scatterPrimeLeft()`; an early X does nothing; `scatterPrimed` event + sound at 3 s; `armedFor` rides the snapshot). While readied: a pulsing red ground ring that tightens and speeds up, embers swirling in and a glow (`ScatterView.readied`), a bright snap when charged; the cone is faint, dashed and fast-pulsing while charging (`.aim-cone.priming`); the HUD reads BLAST CHARGING · 2.1s and the X button counts 3, 2, 1, then FIRE. Tutorial blast lesson says to let it charge.
- **Scatter burns crops (v144, owner):** every small explosion sets crops within `SCATTER.burstRadius` alight (`cropCircle`, walls shelter), like a grenade.
- **Robot rebalance (v146, owner: normal was too hard; tested with robot-vs-robot duels, see below).** Skills (robot-profile.js SKILLS) gain `bold` (aggression offset: rookie -.3, easy -.22, normal -.14, hard 0, expert .05, perfect .08), `patience` (rookie .9, easy .8, normal .6, hard+ 0) and `xRate` (.3-.7). Normal's hands are softer (reaction .27-.42, aim 1.45, settle .5, turn 9.5, lead .8, dodge .3, shake .72, miss .2, tech .5); easy and rookie a little softer too. robot-brain.js: `openFire` — no robot fires at anyone from beyond a screen (`OFFSCREEN_X/Z` 17.5/11.5 m; it closes in instead); against a player, a patient robot holds fire (and X, grenades, stream, recoil jumps: `holding`) until noticed (the player's aim within ~.5 rad of it, hurt in the last 4 s, or close) or its own initiative 1.5-7 s after spotting them, then stays open 6 s. X abilities wait `newXWait` (4-16 s × (1.4 - xRate)) after they are ready (`xAllowed`/`usedX`). Movement is eased (`smoothMove`, rate 6) and strafe switches are 1.3-3.5 s × strafeTime. Ballast/Static robots facing a rifle dash in at an angle now and then. Trials (4-5 min duels on Deadwater): new normal vs old normal ~36:47 kills, ~.75x damage; X use ~25% down; direction reversals ~25% down; rifle robots still beat shotgun/static robots ~3:1 (a weapon matter, left alone: owner said keep the weapons).
- **Title first on every load (v147, owner):** a page load never drops you into a game, even with `play=1` in the URL (a phone bringing the tab back after full screen reloaded straight into the last game). Only a load the menu asked for starts the game: menu.js `markLaunch` leaves a one-time `deadstab.launch` note in sessionStorage before switching map; `finishLoading` (main.js) starts only when it matches, or for tool links with `capture` / `autostart`, and otherwise strips the game params from the URL.
- **Dodge is Q (v147, owner; was Left Ctrl).** `GAME_KEYS.dodge = 'KeyQ'`. Menus still use Q for back.
- **Key bindings (v147, owner):** config/keybinds.js `KEY_ACTIONS` (move up/left/down/right, dodge, fire, aim in, weapon action, x ability, stream, reload, aim up/left/down/right, map, mute), one key each, saved per device (`deadstab.keybinds`). The game still reads each action's default code as a token: main.js runs every game key through `gameCode` (the bound action's token; an action's own default key does nothing once rebound; Right Shift aims while aim-in is Shift). Esc, Tab and the mouse buttons are fixed (`RESERVED`, `FIXED_KEYS`). Binding a used key swaps. On-screen key names follow the bindings (`displayKeys`: tutorial keycaps, HUD hints, ability dial, touch-button labels; `bindsVersion` redraws the HUD). UI: Settings > Controls > KEYBOARD (ui/keybind-menu.js): grouped rows, click or E a key, press the new one (captured before menus), Esc cancels, a status line says what swapped, RESET TO DEFAULTS. Test: game-keys.test.js.
- **ROBOTS switch (v147, owner; an ON/OFF row since 2026-09-30, see The game server > Robots ON/OFF):** in multiplayer (host setup, lobby, lobby page) the robots setting is a `robots fill seats` ON/OFF row (ON = 'fill'); robot skill, + ROBOT and TUNE show only while it is ON, and turning it OFF in the lobby removes the added robots (`HostSession.setSetting`). It now applies in every mode (practice: + ROBOT). SOLO's robot menus are unchanged.
- **Stuck trigger on iPad (v148, owner):** with keyboard prompts, a finger or pen on the world presses the Nominal/Ballast trigger like a mouse button, but only a mouse `pointerup` released it (and the press's preventDefault blocked Safari's mouse copy), so the rifle fired forever, through reloads. Now the press remembers its pointer (`firePointer` / `aimPointer`) and that pointer's up or cancel, or every finger lifting, lets go. Also: a keyup is matched by `e.key` to the code it went down as (some iPad keyboards send a keyup with no code), and hiding the page (app switcher, `visibilitychange` / `pagehide`) releases all input. Reproduced in headless Chromium on v0.93a (kept firing) and checked fixed.
- **Steady aim dot while moving (v148, owner):** `aimDotPoint` (main.js) draws the smoothed mouse cursor between its last two fixed steps (`prevCursor` → `mouse` by the step fraction; it used to jump in whole 60 Hz steps), and a simulation aim point (keys, lock, assist, online) from the drawn body rather than the last step's, like the cone. Headless check, walking with arrow aim: frame-to-frame wobble 12.1 px → 4.4 px.
- **Collider broad phase (v149, owner: lag):** world/collider-grid.js `nearColliders` / `collidersAlong`: a 4 m grid over a colliders array (WeakMap per array, rebuilt when the array or its length changes), results in array order. Used by movePlayer, crushDodged, Static orbs in flight, rifle bullets, Ballast pellets and blast shells, grenades, shell-casing ghosts and robot `shotClear`. SOLO 3V3 headless profile (10 s, Balanced): main-thread JS ~1.6-2.0 s → ~0.7 s; Simulation.step inclusive 918 → 67 ms, BotMatch.step 1377 → 242 ms. Queries spanning over 64 cells fall back to the full list. Any new per-projectile or per-body loop over `colliders` should use it.
- **Death screen wash (v149):** `.death-wash` carries `transform: translate(-50%,-50%)` itself, not only through its entry animation (on an iPad the splash sat off to the lower right, mostly off screen, when the animation's fill did not hold).
- **Picking a side online (v0.9b, owner):** in team modes the lobby shows YOUR SIDE buttons (RED / BLUE / GOLD with n/per); everyone picks for themselves (`{t:'team'}` → `Arena.chooseTeam`: lobby only, refused when full or not a side of this mode; click again to drop it). `startRound` deals picked players first, then the rest, then robots, each to the side with the fewest. The lobby shows the side you picked (`HostSession.shownTeam`).
- **Spawns (v0.9b, owner):** scattered by default everywhere: nobody within `SPAWN_APART` 26 m (about a screen) of anyone else (online `Arena.spawn`: a room spot that far from everyone, else an open spot, else the old fallbacks; solo VS ROBOTS `bots.apart` for every robot, your robots too, and for you). The online setting **spawns** is now scattered / with my team (team modes; "together", everyone in one building, is gone). VS ROBOTS team modes have the same choice (`spawn`: your robots beside you, the enemies together far away, `BotMatch.teamSpawn` / `placeWithTeam`).
- **Robot skill perfect (v0.9b, owner):** `SKILLS.perfect` after expert: reaction .05-.07 s, aim error .1, settles in .08 s, turn 34, always leads, always dodges, never pulls wide (`miss` 0), knows every trick. Same guns, spread and health as everyone: only the hands. In every skill list (VS ROBOTS, online robot skill, dev tools).
- **Friendly fire (v0.9b, owner):** on by default in team games: teammates can hurt each other for half (`FRIENDLY_SHARE` .5). Online: host setting `friendlyFire` (on · half damage / off; team modes); with it on teammates stand in a seat's targets as `friendly` proxies, `Arena.transferDamage` halves it and credits nobody (no kill, no damage dealt). Solo VS ROBOTS: `BotMatch.friendlyFire` (.5 or 0) makes allies friendly proxies in your targets and you / allies in each robot's (brains still only fight the other side). Assist, volley pull and target lock skip friendly targets.
- **Squads (v138, owner: less chaos vs robots; bots/squad.js, tests/squad.test.js):** robots on a side no longer all trail one body. Each robot has a role re-rolled every 18-40 s (`ROLES`): escort (keeps near the side's player; only on a side with one), support (goes up to 45 m to a teammate in a fight: hurt just now or firing), roam (its own thing; joins a fight within 18 m). Each side with 2+ robots has a plan re-rolled every 15-40 s: usually split, sometimes (`GROUP_CHANCE` .3) group, where everyone keeps near a random captain. `Squads.leaderFor` says who a robot keeps near ('human', a captain id, null); `rally` gives a point the brain investigates when it has no target (`world.rally` in robot-brain think). Used by BotMatch (solo) and ArenaRobots (online).
- **Menus (v138, owner):** SOLO page notes are short (skill two words, temper short; spawns and friendly fire rows have no note). Your weapon, enemy/ally weapons and the map are dropdown pickers (`weapon-grid.js` `pickerHTML` / `wirePicker` / `syncPicker`): a toggle showing the pick opens a scrollable grid (max min(46vh,220px)), a pick closes it. The lobby map is the same picker (host only). Phones (≤600px): the SOLO page, host setup and lobby fit 390px portrait; settings rows stack (label above, buttons share the width), the lobby is one column, + ROBOT and the room code shrink.
- **Robots in multiplayer:** see Multiplayer > Modes, sides and robots. `ui/robot-options.js` keeps `emptySlotRows` (the lobby's "empty seat · + ROBOT") and the old `ROBOT_OPTIONS` table (no longer shown). **Owner's rule:** robots fill empty seats in multiplayer modes (FFA, 1V1, 2V2...) chosen by the host in the lobby; a player who wants to fight only robots picks a singleplayer gamemode.
- **Maps page (v0.995a, owner):** the practice maps (`#map-options`) are a grid **two across that scrolls**, in the same frame and pink more-below arrow as the weapon page (menu.js `scrollList`). Each card keeps its old size and lettering: 230 wide, a 285 picture, a 108 caption, the 104 x 30 PRACTICE tag. Only when two of those don't fit (a phone held upright) does a card shrink, every part in proportion (`container-type:inline-size`, sizes in `cqw`). Held sideways, the heading sits beside the back arrow. Arrow keys: up and down move a row (menu-navigation.js).
- **PRACTICE / SOON tag fix (v0.995a):** the tag's lettering showed blank in Chrome. It is an SVG made in script and given its `viewBox` once fitted, but Chrome does not restyle for a later camel-case `viewBox`, so the `:not([viewBox])` hide stayed on. The fit now also sets `data-fit`, and the CSS keys on that (button-typography.js).
- **Coming soon (owner, v0.9a):** every weapon and map menu ends with a greyed, unpressable coming-soon entry: practice maps (`.map-soon`), weapons page (`.weapon-soon`), the online weapon pick (`.weapon-pick-soon`), the lobby's maps (`.lobby-map-soon`), the 1V1 grids (`soonTileHTML`).
- **Next:** the lobby robot seats (the host runs the robot sims, as `net/arena.js` runs players; `team` maps onto 2V2/3V3 sides); callout markers on your screen for what allies see.

### Robot lab (developer tools > Robots > "Robot lab (repeat fights)", owner 2026-09-30: "place bots in areas and watch them fight repeatedly from full health to zero with different weapons ... every information should be in a menu thats toggablbe in dev tools that shows live info")
A solo (PRACTICE) testing bench: robots on placed spots fight round after round, each round from full health on their own spots, with live readouts and results. Not in BOTS mode or online (`canRun` in main.js).
- **Logic** (`src/bots/robot-lab.js`, `RobotLab`, no DOM): `entries` (at most `MAX_ROBOTS`): a spot (`snap`: open nav ground within `LAB.snap` squares), `side` (`LAB_SIDES`: A = team 'red', B = 'blue', F = 'ffa', each F its own side; labels A1, B2, F1 by side and order), `weaponMode` (`fixed`, `random` each round, `matchup`: every weapon against every weapon in turn, each combination `settings.repeat` rounds; `schedule()` keeps only the matchup spots and the weapons, and `weaponsFor(n)` works round n's weapons out as the digits of the matchup's number (never listed: 7 weapons on 8 spots is 5.7 million); weapons are `playableWeapons()`), skill, style, temper, health, aim. `settings`: `rounds` (0 endless, a number, `'cycle'` = every matchup once), `timeLimit` (s, 0 none; time up: the side with the most health share left wins, equal is a draw), `gap` (pause between rounds), `repeat`, `rebuild` (props rebuilt each round, `restoreAllProps`), `seeAll` (sets `dev.robotSeeAll`, so they find each other).
- **A run:** `start(main)` needs two sides; other robots leave; one robot per spot (`BotMatch.spawn`, then `put`: `putAt` the spot, the entry's health, facing the other side's middle); `BotMatch.holdRespawns` (nobody comes back mid-round) and `BotMatch.youOut` (you are not a target, a body, an ally's leader: `before` adds no stand-ins, `step` treats you as absent). `step(main, dt)` after `bots.step` each tick: a round ends when one side stands (`standing()`), everyone is down, or time is up (`finish`: a robot killed in another's step this tick, which falls only on its own next tick, is counted then; `fell` counts once per round), then `gap` s later `begin`: the next round's weapons (`weaponsFor(n)`), everyone back on their spots with fresh loadouts and heads. `skip` = NEXT ROUND (the current one not counted); the pause menu's Restart does the same. Adding, removing or re-siding a robot during a run starts the round again (`lineupChanged`), or stops the run when only one side is left (`stoppedWhy`, toasted). Dev freeze holds the lab's clock too. `stop`/`lost` (the main menu took the robots) remove them and put back your dev settings; closing the panel, or anything that turns `dev.robotLab` off (Reset dev settings), stops the run.
