- **You watch:** while a run is on, `watching(main)` keeps `dev.ghost` and `dev.invulnerable` on (put back after), main.js passes your input through `watchInput` (every attack, ability, reload and dodge off; walking moves your ghost = the free camera, through walls, at Fly speed = `dev.speed`), and `body.lab-watching` hides your HUD, reticle and aim. **Follow:** `lab.follow` = an entry key; `watched()` feeds main.js's spectate branch (`spectateView`), with `view.spectateCentre` (renderer: no death-card offset, the robot in the middle); leaving it puts your ghost where the camera was.
- **Numbers** (a round's numbers are kept in `pending` and added to the totals only when it ends, so a skipped or restarted round counts for nothing; a kill is credited only for a hit in this round, `put` clears `lastHitBy`; BotMatch hooks while a run is on: `onDamage(ownerId, victim, took)` from `deal`, `onEvent(bot, e)` for each robot event, `onFell(victim, killerId)` from `fell`): per robot (`stats.robots`), per weapon (`stats.weapons`, by the weapon held) and per line-up (`stats.matchups`, "rifle vs shotgun"): rounds, wins, losses, draws, kills, deaths, dealt, taken, attacks (`ATTACK_EVENTS`: trigger pulls, swings, volleys), hits (a tick in which it hurt someone, so streams count many), abilities used (`ABILITY_EVENTS`, dodges included), time to kill (the victim's first hit this life to its death), time alive. `averages(s)`. `log`: the last 60 rounds (who, weapons, winner, why, time, each robot's health left, dealt, taken, kills). `csv()`: the log plus the weapon table.
- **Live readout** (`src/bots/lab-readout.js`): `weaponReadout(sim)` (ammo, reload, each ability as `{ name, state, left }` in the HUD's states: ready, cooldown, charging, active, locked; one branch per weapon, read from the same clocks the HUD reads), `mindReadout(bot)` (mode, fight plan, cover reason, target and whether it is seen, mood, profile), `labReadout(bot)` (plus health, dodges, dodging, speed).
- **Panel** (`src/ui/robot-lab-panel.js`, shown while `dev.robotLab` is on in a started solo game; its × turns the option off; a dev window on the left, dragged by its bar, – folds it to the status): status (state, round/limit, matchup, time, last result), START / STOP / NEXT ROUND / RESET STATS, and tabs. SETUP: PLACE ROBOTS (main.js's capture-phase pointerdown on the world: left click adds, right click removes the nearest spot, a card's MOVE then a click moves it; `labPanel.place`), the next robot's choices, the round settings, the camera (free or follow), fly speed, game speed, freeze, health bars, spots, robots' thoughts, CLEAR ALL SPOTS. ROBOTS: a card per robot, redrawn ~8 times a second: health bar, ammo, reload, dodges, speed, ability chips, head, this round's numbers, totals and averages, abilities used; FOLLOW, MOVE, ×, and edit (next round). RESULTS: by weapon (rounds, win %, K/D, damage a round, hits per attack, TTK), line-ups (wins each, draws, average time), the last 12 rounds, COPY CSV / DOWNLOAD CSV. Over the world (`.lab-overlay`): a bar and number over each standing lab robot, a dashed ring with its label on each spot.
- Dev builds expose `window.__robotLab` and `window.__labPanel`. Tests: tests/robot-lab.test.js (placing, START, round ends, counted deaths, time up and draws, the weapon schedule, a real fight's numbers adding up, the readout for every weapon, the wiring).

## Multiplayer (rounds: FFA, practice, 1V1, 2V2, 2V2V2, 3V3, 4V4; robots)

**On.** `NETWORK.enabled` is `true`: Gamemodes > JOIN / HOST. Up to 8 players (`NETWORK.maxPlayers`, `MAX_SEATS`; eight colourways in `PLAYER_COLOURS`). **Since 2026-09-30 games run on the game server** (below: The game server); peer-to-peer (one player's page hosts) is kept behind the developer tools. Everything in this section is the same either way: the server runs the very same HostSession. Rounds of every mode in `MODES` (FFA, practice, 1V1, 2V2, 2V2V2, 3V3, 4V4), run from a lobby, with robots filling seats (v0.9b).

### Modes, sides and robots (v0.9b)
- **Modes** (`config/match.js` MODES: `teams`, `per`, `size`, `fillTo`): FFA (any number, robots fill to 4), practice (targets, nothing counted, never filled), 1V1 (2 seats, each alone), 2V2 (2 sides of 2), 2V2V2 (3 of 2), 3V3 (2 of 3), 4V4 (2 of 4, eight seats, 2026-09-29). Every mode but practice keeps score (`COUNTED`: syphon, stats; the match clock, kill limit and respawn wait are FFA only now; every other counted mode is `ROUNDED`, played in rounds, see Competitive rules below). The host picks a mode on the host setup page (remembered, `deadstab-host-mode`, passed as `mode` to HostSession) and in the lobby (a line under the modes says what it is, `MODE_NOTES`).
- **Starting** (`Arena.startRound`): robots added to fill the last round leave; a fixed-size mode refuses more players than seats (`startError`: "1V1 is for 2 players"), spare robots step out; with **robots: fill empty seats** (`SETTINGS.robots`, default fill) robots take the empty seats up to `fillTo`; still short with robots off → refused ("2V2 needs 4 players: add robots, or set robots to fill"; the host gets it as a toast). Sides (`TEAMS` red, blue, gold) are dealt round-robin, players first (host, then join order), then robots.
- **Sides:** only the other side stands in a seat's targets (`Arena.hostile`), so no friendly fire (shots pass through teammates; damage from a teammate is dropped too); teammates are still bodies. Each side spawns in its own building (`teamRooms`, a different one per side while there are enough). Kill limit counts a side's kills together (`teamScores`); results name the winning side ("red team wins", "· your side"), or a draw. The match clock shows each side's kills ("10:00 red 0 · blue 0"). Lobby, scoreboard and results show each player's side as a coloured tag; everyone's base ring (yours via `WorldView.setTeamRing`, others' via `p.ring`) wears their side's colour. Target lock and aim assist see only the other sides (`online.foes`).
- **Joining mid-round** in a fixed-size mode: a robot filling a seat steps aside and the player takes its side; with no robot to replace they sit out on the bench (`seat.bench`) until the next round. A player leaving mid-round with robots on fill: a benched player or a new robot takes their seat and side. A player joining a full room (6 seats) pushes a robot out.
- **Robots online** (`net/arena-robots.js` ArenaRobots): a robot is an arena seat with its own sim, stepped by the host each tick with its RobotBrain's input exactly like a joiner's seat (so weapons, damage, deaths, respawns, feed and scoreboard need nothing new). + ROBOT on an empty lobby seat adds one that stays (REMOVE takes it out; `HostSession.addRobot` / `kick`); fill robots are `auto` and leave with the round. Skill from `SETTINGS.robotSkill` (rookie..expert), always a blend of styles with a shifting temper. Each wears a make no other robot in the room has; its slot is `ROBOT_SLOT + make + 1`, so every screen draws it as a robot (sparks, wreck, armour). They pick a weapon at random at once. They hear LOUD events from every seat, share what their side's robots see every 0.2 s, keep near the first human on their side (`leader`), dodge grenades' landing spots; one NavGrid for the room with a shared search budget. Snapshots carry `team` and `robot` per player and `match.teams`. Tests: tests/modes.test.js (every mode starts, fill and sides, refusals, + ROBOT, no friendly fire, robots fight and team kills add up, a late joiner takes a robot's seat, results name the side).

### Files

```
src/config/network.js   every network setting: transport, signalling server, STUN/TURN, room codes, rates, timeouts
src/net/transport.js    the Transport interface (the only thing sessions talk to), room codes, an in-memory loopback for tests
src/net/peer-transport.js  WebRTC via PeerJS 1.5.5 (loaded only when someone goes online)
src/net/local-link.js   two windows of the same browser: BroadcastChannel instead of WebRTC (joining knocks here first, 0.5 s; hosting listens on both)
src/net/protocol.js     message shapes, input cleaning (playerInput), usernames, snapshot/loadout packing
src/config/match.js     the modes (MODES, `ready`), the host's round settings (SETTINGS with their values), pick and results times
src/net/arena.js        THE MATCH RULES: round phases, weapon picks, shared world, players and practice targets as targets, damage, deaths, respawns, kill feed, scoreboard, map reset
src/render/pick-view.js        the weapon-pick camera spot (map.pickView or its thumbnail spot, 44 m up) and the no-spawn area it shows
src/net/spawn-points.js random spawn spots inside buildings, clear of furniture
src/net/projectiles.js  everyone's orbs/bullets/pellets/grenades packed for snapshots and drawn through a "draw sim"
src/net/host-session.js the authority: steps every seat through the arena, numbered event log, snapshots per joiner
src/net/client-session.js a joiner: movement prediction, host-fired weapon, events/feed/scoreboard/world sync
src/net/online.js       picks the wire (`via`: 'server', the game server's WebSocket; 'p2p', PeerJS) and starts the right session
src/net/socket-transport.js  the game server's WebSocket (see The game server)
src/ui/robot-options.js    robot option rows and empty lobby seats (a preview: inert, tagged "soon")
src/online-play.js      page glue: menu requests (with the host's settings), badge, lobby/round/pick actions, events and projectiles (and practice targets) for main.js
src/ui/lobby-screen.js     the lobby screen between rounds (players + ping, mode, map, settings; START ROUND for the host)
src/ui/lobby-panel.js      the lobby page of the pause menu during a round (players + ping, settings; host: REMOVE, RESET MAP, END ROUND)
src/ui/lobby-settings.js   the settings rows shared by the host setup page, the lobby screen and the lobby page
src/ui/weapon-pick.js      the weapon pick (grid of rounded squares, 10 s timer, pink GO); untimed with BACK for solo practice's pause CHANGE WEAPON
src/ui/multiplayer-hud.js  kill feed, scoreboard with ping and colours (Tab / SCORES on touch), match clock, round results
src/remote-players.js   how other players are drawn: one colourway per slot (PLAYER_COLOURS), no name tag, holding the model of the weapon they really use (rifle/shotgun models at detail 2, Static's gun; one template per weapon, cloned). Who is drawn is renderer.js's `sees`: from indoors only through the openings, on hills not behind the ground, and (s6-roofs) nobody inside a closed Hollow Wick building unless you are in it or its doorway (roof-fade.js `shownInside`)
tests/net.test.js       host + joiners over the loopback: join, lobby, picks, modes, settings, spawn rules, loading grace, stall forgiveness, shoot to death, multi-kill line, self-kill, time, props, map reset, results, lost packets
```

### How a game goes
- **Menu:** Gamemodes > JOIN: USERNAME (saved in `deadstab-username`), then ROOM CODE + JOIN (no list of rooms: owner, 2026-09-30, "remove the list of servers from join. keep host and join") (an invite link `?join=CODE` lands here). Gamemodes > HOST: **host a game** with the same USERNAME (`#host-name` mirrors `#online-name`; CREATE GAME without one says "Enter a username first."), MAP, MODE, the mode's one row (ROUNDS 3 5 10 ∞ or FFA's MATCH LENGTH; none in practice), CREATE GAME (a new room on the game server; the developer tools' PEER-TO-PEER box, under the code box on JOIN and above CREATE GAME on HOST, plays peer-to-peer instead). No password: the room code is the only key. The code box always shows capitals. Names are unique per room ("Sam 2").
- **Playing alone:** a host can start FFA or practice with nobody else in the room. With **no matchmaking server** reachable (offline, a blocked network, or 8 s without an answer) the room still opens (`transport.offline`, badge says OFFLINE): the host plays alone, and other windows of the same browser can still join over `local-link.js`. Other errors (a taken code) still fail.
- **Hosting:** HOST A GAME opens **host a game**, the round settings (below; remembered in `deadstab-host-settings`), then CREATE GAME opens the room. Nobody is put in the map yet: everyone lands on the **lobby screen**.
- **The room's map (v0.990a, owner):** the host picks it on **host a game** (a map picker above the mode, `deadstab-host-map`) and can change it in the lobby. A map other than the page's loaded one means a reload: CREATE GAME on another map stores the room's mode and settings (`saveCarry`, sessionStorage `deadstab-room-carry`, read back once within 2 min) and loads `?map=<id>&host=1&autohost=1`, where the menu opens the room at once with the saved username (`menuFlow.autoRoom`, run from `finishLoading`). In the lobby the host's map pick calls `online.moveRoom(id)`: it sends `moveMap` to everyone (host-session.js `moveMap`), carries mode, settings, the robots the host added (their setups; fill robots refill themselves) and the room code, and reloads onto the map, where the room opens again with the same code (retrying for up to 15 s while the old page's hold on the code clears: `unavailable-id`). A joiner told `moveMap` reloads to `?map=<id>&join=<code>&autojoin=1` and joins again, retrying for up to 25 s while the host is away (`peer-unavailable`). A joiner who types a code while on another of our multiplayer maps is sent the same way: the welcome's `map` differs from theirs (client-session.js `moveTo`), instead of the old "different map" refusal (still given for an unknown map or another build of it). The PeerJS error `type` rides on the thrown error for those retries (peer-transport.js). Tests: tests/room-map.test.js.
- **Lobby screen** (`lobby-screen.js`, while the round's phase is `lobby`): live list of players with their colour, host / you tags and ping, and the host's REMOVE; the room code (click copies an invite link); MODE (FFA, PRACTICE; 1V1, 2V2, 2V2V2, 3V3 listed, not startable), MAP (the multiplayer maps; only Deadwater now) and SETTINGS. Joiners see mode and map read-only and "waiting for the host"; **the settings are the host's only and are hidden from joiners** (here and on the LOBBY page). The host presses START ROUND.
- **Settings** (`config/match.js` SETTINGS, host only, changeable on the host setup page, the lobby screen and the pause menu's LOBBY page): **rounds** (3/5/10/∞, default 5; the round modes) and **match length** (5/10 min, default 10; FFA) are the only rows everyone sees (`PLAIN_SETTINGS`); the rest carry `dev: true` and show only with the developer tools unlocked: spawns (random building each / together in one building), kill limit (FFA only: none/10/20/30, default none), health (250/500/750), respawn wait (FFA only: 6/8/12/16 s, default 6), syphon, friendly fire, robots. Rows that do nothing in the chosen mode are dimmed.
- **Weapon pick** (`weapon-pick.js`): every round starts with it, and a joiner arriving mid-round gets it. A see-through grey panel over a top-down view of the world from 44 m up (`pick-view.js`, above the birds); rounded squares, five to a row, scrolling, only picture and name; hover or pick lifts the square and its picture. A 10 s timer at the top: pink GO goes in at once with the pick; at zero you go in with your pick, or a random weapon. **Nobody spawns inside the ground that view shows.** FFA: weapons change only after dying (CHANGE WEAPON on the death screen opens the pick; the respawn waits for it). **Practice: any time** from the pause menu's CHANGE WEAPON (`arena.pickAgain`/`choose` allow a living practice seat: it leaves the world while picking, then GO puts it back in).
- **FFA:** kills count. The match ends when the clock (match length, 5 or 10 min) runs out (or, only if a developer sets one, someone reaches the kill limit); the results (winner, standings) stay up until everyone is READY (a new match, same settings) or `READY_WAIT` (90 s), then the lobby screen. Respawn after the respawn wait (6 s by default) with the same weapon.
- **Practice:** the map's targets are out (shared: everyone sees and hits the same ones; the host's arena runs them once per tick and they are mirrored to joiners every snapshot). Players can hit each other, but nothing is counted (no kills, deaths, feed or clock). Death has no wait: RESPAWN on the death screen puts you straight back.
- **Same computer:** two windows of one browser connect through `net/local-link.js` (BroadcastChannel), so local testing never depends on WebRTC. Players on other devices still use WebRTC.
- **Timeouts:** 6 s of silence drops a player (`timeout`), but a joiner still loading the map gets `loadGrace` (45 s) before its first message, and a freeze on our own side (loading, hidden window; any gap over 1 s between our own ticks) is not counted as the other side's silence. Ping replies alone do not count as being heard (a frozen tab still answers them).
- **Spawning:** at a random spot inside a building (rooms at least 6 m across, outside the pick view), preferring spots 6 m from anyone alive; with spawns "together", all in one building (picked per round) a body apart. Health from the health setting.
- **Death:** the same death screen as practice (`death-screen.js`): the red wash, DEAD, "killed by NAME" (or "you took yourself out") and, in FFA, the respawn countdown with a draining bar. Buttons: FFA: CHANGE WEAPON, LOBBY, LEAVE MULTIPLAYER; practice: RESPAWN, CHANGE WEAPON, LOBBY, LEAVE MULTIPLAYER. Your body stays where it fell after you respawn (settled), until your next death, a map reset or leaving. **At most one body and one bloodstain per player:** each player's last stain stays as long as their body (`BloodSplatters.add(..., owner)`: 'you', or 'slot'+slot for others), and their next death fades the older one out in 0.6 s while the old body goes, so the ground there is clean again. The camera cuts to the spawn, it does not glide across the map.
- **Pause:** your own only; the round goes on and you can be hit. RESUME, SETTINGS, LOBBY, LEAVE MULTIPLAYER; in practice also CHANGE WEAPON (after RESUME).
- **LOBBY page** (`lobby-panel.js`, pause menu or death screen): everyone sees the players with ping; only the host sees (and changes) the settings, and can REMOVE a player (`HostSession.kick`: refused for the rest of the room), RESET MAP (every prop and crop back, blood, marks and bodies cleared on every screen) and END ROUND (everyone back to the lobby screen). The two resets need a second press.
- **Names:** no name floats over players. Names show in the lobby, the scoreboard, the kill feed and "killed by"; each player has a colourway (coat, arms, band, scarf, base ring; `PLAYER_COLOURS` by slot) shown as a dot next to their name.
- **Kill feed:** one-shots (from full health to dead in one hit: that tick's damage from one attacker, or a volley the sim marks `oneShot`) get their own line, "X one shot Y" (`oneShot` on the feed line; `arena.died(..., oneShot)`), and the death screen reads "one shot by X". Bottom left on desktop; on touch it sits top right under MAP / SOUND / PAUSE (the bottom left is the move stick), in portrait a little lower. 6 s per line, "KILLER killed A, B" (everyone one attacker killed in the same tick is one line) or "NAME died". Your name blue, everyone else pink; names keep their case.
- **Scoreboard:** hold Tab (SCORES button on touch). The stats panel (ui/stats-panel.js, competitive overhaul 2026-09-29): one narrow vertical card, everyone in one list ranked by kills (then fewer deaths, more damage dealt, name), each row rank, colour, name (yours blue), kills big on the right and under the name deaths, damage dealt and taken, time in game, most used weapon, ping (whatever is known). The game around it is not dimmed; the card is slightly see-through (`#232827d1`) with a text shadow. Team games: each row has its side's colour as a left stripe and a faint tint. Time in game counts only while in the world (dead included, pause and settings included, weapon picks excluded).
- **Match clock:** top centre in an FFA round (red in the last 30 s), "results" between the round and the lobby, hidden in the lobby and in practice.
- **Own blasts hurt you** online too; dying to them is a death, not a kill.
- **Developer tools are the host's only** online (the host's sim is the authority): the host unlocks and uses them as offline (Shift+P, O, map teleport); joiners' dev settings are reset every tick and they get "DEV TOOLS ARE THE HOST'S ONLINE".

### How it works
- **One world, one sim per player (arena.js).** Each player keeps their own `Simulation` (body, weapon, ammo, orbs, cooldowns: all the single-player weapon code, unchanged). Before a player's sim steps, the arena hands it the shared `props`, `colliders` and `crops`, and puts every other living player in its `targets` as a proxy of kind `'player'` (radius `RULES.radius + .04`, see `target-radius.js`). Every weapon already hits targets, so all of them hit players. After the step, whatever health a proxy lost is dealt to the real player with `damagePlayer` (dodge reduction, damage type and direction from the kill/hit event, so the right death plays), and a changed collider list is kept for everyone.
- **World authority:** player sims have `worldAuthority = false`; crops burn and prop flashes fade once per tick in the arena's own world sim (`endTick`), whose proxies are every living player, so fire damages everyone once.
- **Host tick:** main.js calls `online.input(raw)` (host: `beforeLocal`) then steps its own sim, then `online.afterStep()` (host: `step()`): the arena finishes the host seat, steps every joiner's queued inputs, burns the world, runs respawns, clocks and the kill feed, and every 3rd tick sends each joiner a snapshot.
- **Rounds (arena.js):** `phase` is `lobby` (nobody in the world), `playing` (a round of `mode`: `ffa` or `practice`) or `results` (until everyone is READY, or `READY_WAIT` 90 s, then `lobby`). `startRound` resets the map, the scores and the practice targets and puts every seat on a pick (`seat.picking = { left, weapon, go }`); `tryEnter` spawns once the pick is done (GO or time up, random weapon if none) and any respawn wait is over. `counting` (every mode but practice) decides whether stats and the feed run; the clock is FFA's only (round modes: `clock` Infinity, `matchState().timed` false). Snapshots carry `match` (phase, mode, time left, results, and in round modes `rounds`, `played`, `forfeit` votes; in results `ready`) every time, `lobby` (players with ping, settings, mode, map) every 30 ticks, your `picking`, and the practice `targets` (id, x, z, hp, flash) every time.
- **Ping:** the host sends `{t:'ping', s}` once a second to each joiner, who answers `{t:'pong', s}`; the host smooths the round trip per joiner and reports it in the lobby and on the scoreboard.
- **Practice targets online:** the arena owns them (`arena.targets`); each seat's sim sees stand-ins (like other players) so it never moves or revives them, and damage to a stand-in lands on the real target. Drawing: the host passes them through `drawSim` (`foreign.targets`); joiners hold a mirror in their own sim (`applyTargets`: never moved or revived there).
- **Joiners send full inputs** (`playerInput`: moves, aim point, every button), numbered, repeated 4 times. Their own sim only walks (prediction and reconciliation as before). **Weapons are fired by the host**, so hits have one truth; your own shots show one round trip late (simple on purpose; "favour the shooter" rewind can come later).
- **Snapshots carry:** every player (position, aim, hp, weapon, present, dead, life), your `loadout` (ammo, reloads, charges, cooldowns for the HUD), everyone's projectiles (`pack`), events not yet acknowledged, the last kill-feed lines, the scoreboard (every 30 ticks) and the world (broken props and crop fires, every 30 ticks and on joining).
- **Events are reliable:** the host numbers every shared event (`SHARED_EVENTS`) and keeps 3 s of them; each joiner's inputs carry `ack`, and the host resends everything newer. So shots, deaths and kill-feed lines are never lost on the unreliable channel.
- **Protocol 11** (Hollow Wick's stream): player states carry `below` (1, only when wading under a deck). **Protocol 10** (hills): terrain maps' rounds carry `stop` in snapshots, grenade `y` is a world height, and `welcome` carries the map fingerprint (`mapHash`; a joiner on another map or build is turned away). **Protocol 9** (v0.9b): players carry `team` and `robot`, match state `teams`. **Protocol 8** (`PROTOCOL_VERSION`): the `scatter` press replaced `storeCharge` (7: `surge` replaced `extendedReload`), and the loadout carries the surge and scatter state. Bump it whenever message shapes change; mismatched players are refused.
- **Snapshots must fit one message** (PeerJS refuses anything over ~16 KB: "Message too big for JSON channel", and that snapshot is lost). This froze other players' games whenever someone used Static's C stream: it made ~180 events a second, the unacknowledged backlog outgrew one message on a real connection, and every snapshot after that was refused. Now (protocol 6): events travel packed (`packEvent` / `unpackEvent` in protocol.js: numbers to millimetres, a stream's arcs as a flat list of end points), repeats before the next snapshot fold into one (`foldKey` in host-session.js: stream arcs, and electric hits, dealt and taken damage per target), and each snapshot takes only as many waiting events as fit `WIRE_BUDGET` (15000 bytes), oldest first; the rest go next time (one too big for any message is skipped). A stream now costs ~74 events a second. The host's own screen still gets every event unfolded. Test: `tests/net.test.js` (a stream on a slow link never outgrows one message).
- **Hidden indoors:** from inside a building you see out only through doors and windows (the grey shroud); other players (and robots) out of that view are not drawn, nor their blob shadows (`RemotePlayers.update(..., sees)`, the same `canAimAt` rule, body centre and both sides).
- **Drawing others:** `view.netEvent(e, shooter, slot)` plays another player's event with them standing in for you (muzzle flashes, trails, arcs), and `drawSim` hands the views your sim plus everyone else's projectiles (orb ids made unique per slot). A joiner's own events come back from the host and go through the ordinary `event()` path (hit markers, damage numbers, death reaction).
- **Other players' deaths** leave their body with the proper reaction (`remote-corpses.js`, see Deaths), their stain and their dropped weapon.
- **Removing a player:** REMOVE on the lobby screen or the LOBBY page (`HostSession.kick`): they get `{t:'removed'}`, leave with "The host removed you from the game.", and that peer is refused for the rest of the room.
- **Developer overrides are the host's only.** Joiners' sims (on the host and their own) get `sim.dev = {speed:1}` every tick; the host's own sim keeps its settings (including a respawn: `arena.spawn` skips the host seat). A game-speed override on the host speeds up everyone, since the host runs everyone.
- **Local testing without the internet:** run a PeerJS server bound to 127.0.0.1 (`PeerServer({port:9000,host:'127.0.0.1',path:'/'})` from the `peer` package) and open the dev build with `?peerhost=127.0.0.1:9000` in two separate browser processes. On Potato the sandbox runs fast enough to play a round.

### NAT traversal and relays (peer-to-peer only; the game server needs none)

- WebRTC finds a route with ICE. `iceServers` in `config/network.js` lists Google's STUN servers, which let most home routers connect directly.
- **Some networks refuse direct connections** (many phone carriers, strict office or school Wi-Fi). Those need a **TURN relay**, which forwards the traffic. None is configured yet: add a `{ urls, username, credential }` entry from a relay provider (Cloudflare Calls TURN, Metered, or a self-hosted coturn). Without it, expect a minority of pairs, mostly on mobile data, to fail with "The host did not answer".
- **Signalling** (the introduction) uses the free public PeerJS cloud (`peerServer: null`). It only carries the handshake; if it goes down mid-game the game continues but nobody new can join. For reliability later, self-host a PeerJS server and set `peerServer`.

### Keep in mind
- **Other players' effects anchor to their own gun.** `WorldView.netEvent` gives each slot its own muzzle point (`netMuzzles`) and passes it through `eventMuzzle`; handlers use `eventMuzzle || staticMuzzle`, and `muzzleLight` takes the shooter. Never borrow `staticMuzzle` (your live gun) for someone else's event: effects keep a reference to it and are drawn for several frames, which is how another player's Static stream came to draw as a white bolt out of your own gun. The shooter's flash light is placed at their gun the same way. Test: `tests/render-savings.test.js`.
- **SCORES button:** `.top-actions .icon-button` forces `display:grid!important`, so `[hidden]` and the keyboard rule need their own `!important` rule (it showed in solo games before v0.9a).
- **A joiner's sims only predict (`sim.predictOnly`, v129 audit).** `client-session.js` marks its local and scratch sims so they never run the nova or blast (the host fires them and sends the state); reconcile copies `surge` into the replay. The bullet pack carries `surge` so other players' nova shots draw as white beams.
- **Audit rules (v129, tests/audit-fixes.test.js):** a death ends the nova (the cooldown still starts), disarms the blast, and projectiles already flying (blast shells, grenades, bullets) keep flying while you are dead. The blast cap counts damage after the dev damage multiplier. The hex dial/touch button read 'cooldown' until you hold `RULES.hexCost` orbs (the orb-bar mark sits after that orb). Other players' nova ends on their death (`playerDeath` netEvent) and their shell leaves with them (`surgeView.dropBody`). The nova shell copies its body's pose every frame. Dev "Lock settings" restores max health. Time scale and Freeze are solo only. Positioned touch buttons drop `touch-shaped`.
- **Audit, v0.9b (tests in modes.test.js):** a side's kills are tallied on the round (`Arena.teamKills`, from `died`), so a seat leaving or a robot stepping aside no longer takes its kills with it. A teammate's share of friendly fire is applied in the attacker's sim (`Simulation.hit`: a `friendly` proxy's `share`), so damage numbers, markers and KILL match what landed (arena and BotMatch no longer halve afterwards). The hex push now moves the real body (arena `transferDamage` and BotMatch copy a proxy's shift back), so enemies really are kept out. `startRound` does every check before touching seats. Seats the rules take out (`Arena.dropSeat`) are broadcast as `leave`; robot kicks go through `HostSession.removeRobot`; a benched player takes a leaver's seat with robots off too; the lobby shows no side on robots between rounds. Scattered spawns in the open avoid the weapon-pick view (`Arena.openOutside`). Living seats and hex shields are worked out once a tick (`Arena.tickShared`). VS ROBOTS "with my team": an enemy never comes back beside its mate if that is near you, and a restart re-places every robot afresh. Rendering: the rich blast effects show on Quality too (`fx.level` ≥ 1), orb beams and blast shells hide indoors (and are compiled at warm-up), beams clear on restart and map reset, a dead teammate keeps the green hat, a damaged robot is not rebuilt for a new side (its armour would fall twice), the lobby side buttons are no longer rebuilt every frame, a team match ending while you are dead closes the death screen (the result card takes the keys), a late key lock after MAIN MENU lets go, the death screen hides CHANGE WEAPON in the tutorial, fewer per-frame allocations (projectile mirror, robots packed once a tick, the side map cached per tick). Static orbs still pass through teammates (bullets, blasts and the hex hurt them at half).
- **Testing multiplayer headless:** two pages in one browser context link over BroadcastChannel; the host needs signalling, so run a local PeerJS server (`PeerServer({ port: 9000, host: '127.0.0.1' })`, IPv4) and open `?peerhost=127.0.0.1:9000`. Move players with real input: writing a position into the host's sim doesn't reach the joiners.

- Peer-to-peer: the host's tab must stay in front. Browsers stop animation frames in background tabs, which stops the host's simulation for everyone (joiners are dropped after 6 s). The game server removes this.
- Other players are not hidden by walls or cover the way targets are.
- Names are typed usernames until accounts exist.
- Headless testing: Chromium runs only one game page at full speed; the others' frames freeze (joiners then time out). Check two-player timing with unit tests (`tests/net.test.js`) or two real windows.

### Next steps

1. **Favour the shooter:** rewind other players to where the shooter saw them (about 100 ms) when checking hits.
2. **Your own shots instantly:** fire your own weapon locally for the flash, sound and ammo, and let the host confirm hits.
3. **Hiding players out of sight** on the authority (stops wallhacks). (1V1 / 2V2 / 2V2V2 / 3V3 and robot seats are done, v0.9b.)

### The game server (2026-09-30, owner: "full game server ... room codes and a few servers on a list that are always open and fill with bots")

Matches run on the owner's VPS instead of a player's page, so nobody hosts: no host advantage, no game ending when the host leaves or hides the tab, no blocked peer-to-peer networks. It lives in `server/` (Node, imports the game's own `src/` modules) and runs at **wss://play.deadstab.com** (`NETWORK.gameServer`). How to put it online: `server/deploy/SETUP.md`.

**How it works**
- **The same match code.** Each room is a `HostSession` with `local: null` (no host seat: every player is a joiner, slots start at 0, `step()` is the whole tick; `dedicated` is true, and nothing is kept for a host screen). The arena, robots, storm, rounds and protocol are untouched, so everything above holds on the server.
- **One process, one loop** (`server/index.js`): 60 ticks a second, stepping every room with a player in it (empty rooms cost nothing), catching up at most 4 ticks after a hiccup. A full 4V4 with seven robots costs ~2.5 ms a tick on a laptop core; the loop warns in the log past 70% of a tick. A robot's first NavGrid in a room costs ~65 ms once (one late tick, caught up).
- **The wire** (`src/net/socket-transport.js`): one WebSocket to `/play`. Its first message picks the room, `{ t:'join', code }` or `{ t:'create', map, mode, settings }`, with the browser's player id (`src/net/player-id.js`: random, kept in `deadstab-player-id`, never shown to others) and `PROTOCOL_VERSION`. The server answers `{ t:'room', code, name, public, map, id, lead }` or `{ t:'nope', reason }`; after that it is the ordinary protocol, plus `room` (the leader changed), `note` (a refused leader action, toasted) and the leader's `lead` controls. `ClientSession` runs on it unchanged (`transport.lostText` gives "Lost connection to the game server."). A WebSocket is TCP: nothing is lost, a lost packet only delays the ones behind it; inputs are skipped while the socket is backed up (each repeats the last ten), and the server skips a player's snapshot while 256 KB wait on their socket. **Build trap (v0.1.1):** the transport literal defines `onMessage() {}` / `onLeave() {}` as empty placeholders that the session replaces later; Vite 7.1.7 tree-shook direct calls to them (`transport.onMessage('host', message)`) out of the production build, so the live page connected, got `room` and `welcome`, never passed them to `ClientSession`, and gave up after 8 s ("left the game"). Unit tests run unbundled and passed. The calls now go through a local (`const handler = transport.onMessage; handler.call(transport, ...)`), and `tools/check-build.mjs` (run by the deploy workflow after `pnpm build`) fails the deploy if the built `socket-transport-*.js` lacks `onMessage.call(` or `onLeave.call(`. Don't call a placeholder method on an object literal directly in new transports; call through a local, and extend the check.
- **The leader** (`server/room.js`): a room's maker while they are in it (by player id, so it survives a map move's reload), else whoever has been in longest; only a player the session has let in (`admitted`, just after their hello) can lead, and a connection not let in within 10 s (`ADMIT`) is closed, so silent sockets can't hold a room full or lead it. At most 8 leader controls a second per player, a map reset or restart at most once a second per room, and a map move (the picker's or the vote's) at most once every `MOVE_GAP` (5 s) per room (each rebuilds the world; a vote's move waits for it). A leader's new START, mode or map drops a vote's pending auto-start, and none fires while a vote is open (online-play.js does the same peer-to-peer). A same-map vote whose round can't start tells the leader why (`HostSession.voteError`). The leader gets the host's lobby controls: `online.leads` is true, the lobby screen and LOBBY page show the controls, and `online-play.js` sends each as `{ t:'lead', op }` (`mode`, `setting`, `start`, `end`, `reset`, `restart`, `robot`, `tune`, `tuneAll`, `kick`, `map`). Anyone else's are refused with a note. The lobby marks the leader with the host tag (`lobby().players[].host`). Changes reach the lobby at once (`HostSession.lobbyDirty`). Developer tools stay off online for everyone (`online.isHost` is false on the server).
- **Listed rooms: the JOIN list** (owner, 2026-09-30: "every combination of gamemode and map ... the list allows anyone to join"; list rooms have a "fixed map, auto-start"). `Rooms` (server/rooms.js) keeps one room for every multiplayer map x `SERVER.listedModes` (FFA, 1V1, 2V2, 3V3, 4V4, 2V2V2), each with a random 5-number code; when every room of a kind is full it opens another (up to `listedPerKind`, 6), and spare empty ones close (`balance`, twice a second). A listed room has no leader and refuses every lead op ("This room starts by itself."): its map and mode never change, every developer setting stays at its default, robots always fill the seats the mode needs (`robots: 'fill'`) and players can't be removed (`lobby().publicRoom`). It runs itself (`Room.runItself`): with a player in the lobby it counts down `AUTO_START` (8 s, the lobby shows "starting in N": `lobby().startsIn`), and `RESULTS_GAP` (15 s) after a match ends it starts the next. Seats: the mode's size (`capacity`, 8 for FFA; `lobby().capacity` is what the lobby screen shows). The countdown starts over when the lobby empties. A new room is `fresh` (nothing to reset); `publicReset` (20 s) after the last player leaves a used one, it starts over. `GET /rooms` lists them (`{ version, rooms: [{ code, map, mapName, mode, modeName, phase, players, robots, max }] }`).
- **The JOIN page's list** (`src/ui/join-list.js`, sorting in `src/ui/join-rooms.js`): under the code box, one row button per listed room: the map's card picture on the left, the mode and "players/seats" and "starting soon" / "in a match" / full in the middle, the MAP NAME in capitals on the right. Filters: a preferred map, a preferred mode (each with ANY) and players FEWEST / MOST first (ties by map, then mode), remembered in `deadstab-join-filters`. They are laid out like the host page's mode picker (owner, 2026-09-30: the fitted letters must fit and read like the rest of the menus): modes four over three, the map row with room for the names (a long name shows its first word, `filterName`: DEADWATER), and all three rows one lettering (`data-fit-group` on `.join-filters`: button-typography.js fits every button inside it with one shared stretch, as it does for buttons side by side). The room list is at most min(34dvh, 300 px) tall, so the page shrinks less to fit. It asks `online.rooms()` (`fetchRooms`) when the JOIN page opens and every 4 s while it stays open (menu.js `show`), and says so when the server can't be reached or runs another version. A row needs a username, then joins that code over the game server (a room on another map reloads onto it, as a code does). Rows are `plain-text` (their own type sizes, not the fitted menu type).
- **Room codes are 5 numbers** (`NETWORK.codeLength`, `codeAlphabet: '0123456789'`; the code box opens the number pad). 100,000 codes against at most ~40 player rooms: guessing one is unlikely and harmless (a guesser only joins a lobby, which the leader can REMOVE them from), so guesses aren't limited beyond the per-address message and connection limits.
- **The map vote** (owner, 2026-09-30: "like before weapon select ... the name of player who voted for it ... the number of votes each map has on the thumbnail ... if theyre tied it should be a 50/50"; it opens "when the host presses START"). `HostSession.startVoting(mode)` (START on the lobby screen, peer-to-peer too): PRACTICE, or fewer than two multiplayer maps, starts at once (the host picks the map with the lobby's map picker, the only mode where it is shown; `.lobby-map-note` says so in the others). Otherwise, if the round could start (`arena.startProblem`, the same checks as `startRound` with no side effects), a ballot opens: `VOTE.time` (12 s), every player (not robots) votes with `{ t:'vote', map }` (`castVote`; the host page calls `vote`), and it closes early `VOTE.settle` (1.2 s) after everyone has. Most votes wins; a tie (and nobody voting counts as all tied) is `random()` among the tied. This map: `startRound(mode)`. Another: `pendingMove = { map, mode }`: on the server `Room.moveMap(id, { autoStart })` moves the room and starts the round once everyone is back (or 20 s); peer-to-peer the host page's `moveRoom(id, { autoStart })` carries `autoStart` and `expect` through the reload (`pendingStart` in online-play.js). Every snapshot carries `vote` (`voteNow()`: `{ mode, left, total, maps: [{ id, votes, names }] }` or null; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 24). The screen (`src/ui/map-vote.js`, shown by main.js `syncOnlineScreens` while there is a ballot): the weapon pick's see-through card, a timer, one card per map with its picture, the count on the picture (it flips to a new number, `map-vote-flip`) and the voters' names under it; clicking a card votes, clicking another moves the vote.
- **Robots ON/OFF** (owner: "robots have a on and off switch for filling up server. this doesnt apply to the server list"): `SETTINGS.robots` is a plain setting now (`robots fill seats`: ON/OFF), shown to every host at setup and in the lobby; listed rooms force it ON.
- **Player-made rooms** (HOST): a fresh code (`Rooms.create`); the maker leads and may set any setting. REMOVE keeps that player id out of the room. `privateIdle` (60 s) empty and the room closes. At most `maxPrivateRooms` (40); 6 new rooms a minute per address.
- **Moving map** (the lobby's map picker, leader, in the lobby): the server tells everyone (`moveMap`), opens a fresh session on the new map under the same code with the mode, settings and added robots, and treats the old connections as stale (ignored, closed after 15 s). Every page reloads to `?map=<id>&join=<code>&autojoin=1` and joins again (the leader gets the lead back by player id).
- **Versions:** a page whose `PROTOCOL_VERSION` differs is told "The game was just updated. Reload the page to play online." The server updates itself from GitHub `main` (`server/deploy/update.sh`, every 2 minutes, waiting up to 20 minutes for an empty server), so a push updates the page and the server together. **Bump `PROTOCOL_VERSION` whenever message shapes change**, as before; a map change without a bump is caught by the map fingerprint.
- **Limits** (`server/config.js`): 400 connections, 32 per address (a school can share one), 90 new connections a minute per address (`connectsPerMinutePerIp`, else HTTP 429), 180 messages a second and 8 KB a message per player, 10 s to say which room. Origins: deadstab.com, www, kbun2846.github.io and local dev pages (`ALLOWED_ORIGINS` adds more; `ALLOW_ANY_ORIGIN=1` for tests). A room whose tick throws is closed (its players told), and a message that throws closes only that player's connection: never the whole server (setting names are checked with `Object.hasOwn`, so 'constructor' or '__proto__' go nowhere). The server keeps nothing a host page would drain (join lines, feed: `Room.step` drains them).
- **Admin** (`server/admin.js`, `server/admins.js`; owner, 2026-09-30: "secure to me only and who i share with"): https://play.deadstab.com/admin lists every room and player (name, ping, player id, address) with KICK (out of that room for good), BAN (player id and address, every room; `DATA_DIR/bans.json`, with who banned) and UNBAN. Only with a key: the owner's `ADMIN_TOKEN` (/etc/deadstab.env), or a named key he makes on the VPS (`server/deploy/admin.sh add|list|remove <name>`; `DATA_DIR/admins.json` keeps only SHA-256 fingerprints, re-read when it changes, so a removed key stops at once). Every action is logged with the key's name; 10 wrong keys in 10 minutes lock an address out for 10 minutes; with no key anywhere the page is off (404). The page keeps a key for its tab only (sessionStorage), has SIGN OUT, and is served no-store, no-referrer, never framed. Keys are the owner's alone: never in the repo, never handled by Claude.
- **Peer-to-peer** is unchanged behind the developer tools' PEER-TO-PEER box; its invite links carry `&p2p=1` (anyone opening one joins peer-to-peer, tools or not).

**Files**
```
server/index.js     HTTP (/rooms, /health, /admin) and WebSocket (/play), limits, the 60 Hz loop, clean shutdown (players told)
server/rooms.js     every room: the listed ones (a room per map x mode, more when full, spare ones closed), HOST's new ones, idle closing, a crashed room
server/room.js      one room: HostSession with no local player, connections, leader, lead controls, the vote's map move, a listed room running itself, resets
server/admin.js     the owner's page and its API
server/admins.js    who may use it: the owner's token and named keys (also the command line behind deploy/admin.sh)
server/bans.js      the ban list (a JSON file)
server/config.js    listed modes and rooms per kind, limits, environment settings (HOST, PORT, ADMIN_TOKEN, DATA_DIR, TRUST_PROXY, ALLOWED_ORIGINS)
server/deploy/      SETUP.md (the owner's step-by-step), install.sh, update.sh, admin.sh (admin keys), deadstab.service, deadstab-update.{service,timer}, Caddyfile
src/net/socket-transport.js  the client's end (connectServer, fetchRooms)
src/net/player-id.js         this browser's player id
src/ui/join-list.js          the JOIN page's list of open games (join-rooms.js: its filters and order)
src/ui/map-vote.js           the map vote screen
tests/join-list.test.js      the list's filters, order and row layout
tests/game-server.test.js    no-local HostSession, leader rules, the map vote (early close, coin toss, practice, move + auto-start), listed rooms (every kind, auto-start, overflow), player-made rooms, numeric codes, admin keys, the real server over WebSockets (list, HOST, JOIN, bans, versions, origins, admin)
```

**Testing locally:** `pnpm server` (or `ALLOW_ANY_ORIGIN=1 node server/index.js`), `pnpm dev`, then open `http://127.0.0.1:5173/?server=ws://127.0.0.1:8787` in two separate browser processes. On the 2-core sandbox, keep the windows small (800x500, Potato): two SwiftShader pages at full size starve the loop and the server drops a player whose page stops sending for 6 s (the same rule as peer-to-peer).

**Not done yet / later**
- One process for every room. When one VPS is not enough: a room per worker thread, or more servers with the list pointing at each.
- No compression: a player in a busy 8-seat match downloads ~115 KB/s (~400 MB an hour). WebSocket per-message deflate (`perMessageDeflate` in index.js) would cut that several times for some CPU.
- Accounts, owned items and the store: the server is now the authority that would check them (Items, cosmetics and a future store).
- The old Next steps (favour the shooter, own shots at once, hiding players out of sight) apply to the server exactly as to a host.

## Adding a weapon

1. `src/items.js`: a `WEAPONS` entry (id, name, description, controls rows, HUD hints, `input: 'orbs' | 'trigger'`, `smoothCursor`, and for trigger weapons `touchButtons`; optional `adsFire`, `storesCharge`, `tutorial`). Unknown ids fall back to `DEFAULT_WEAPON` everywhere (`weaponOrDefault`), so menus, online messages and saved progress accept the new id automatically.
2. `src/config/gameplay.js`: its tuning block (named by `stats`).
3. `src/<weapon>.js`: its rules, a `step<Weapon>(sim, input, dt, geo)` added to `WEAPON_STEPS` in `simulation.js`, and a reset.
4. Visuals: `<weapon>-view.js` wired in renderer.js; its HUD rows in `weapon-hud.js` / `ability-hud.js` / `aim-overlay.js` where it differs; a 3D preview function in `menu.js` (`previews`; without one the card shows text only).
5. Deaths: its hits carry a `damageType` (`death-reactions.js`). Dev tools: options with `weapon: '<id>'` in `dev-options.js` get their own dropdown. Tutorial: a course in `tutorial.js` if it needs one.
6. Tests: `tests/registry.test.js` checks the entry is complete.
7. **Hills** (see Hills): rounds get `roundOnGround` at fire time and hit through `roundSees` / `roundMeets` (a hit needs mutual sight; `stop` ends them in the ground and is packed in net/projectiles.js); blasts measure with `blastReach` (3D, crests, the flat pre-test); sight for lock and assist is `sim.sees`; its views add `groundY` to every height, lay flat marks with `tiltToGround` / `layFlat`, and draw flying rounds with `roundGlide`; robots fire only through `openFire` (the off-screen rule). All of it behind `ground.flat` / `hilly(view)`, so Deadwater runs the old code (tests/golden-flat.test.js).

## Adding a map

1. `src/maps/<id>.js` exporting the map as plain data (copy `dry-creek.js`; helpers and prop types come from `map-kit.js`). Include `look: { warmth }`, a `spawn`, and a `thumbnail: { x, z, height }` spot for the menu picture.
2. `src/maps.js`: import it and add it to `MAP_LIST` with `modes` (`'practice'`, `'multiplayer'`) and `menu` (listed on the Practice map page).
3. **Its menu picture:** with the dev server up, `node tools/capture-thumbnail.mjs <id>` photographs the `thumbnail` spot in Extreme (post-processing on, the player and moving effects hidden, a vulture placed over open ground with the dust cleared around it) and writes `src/assets/thumbnails/<id>.webp` (690 x 855); add it to `CARD_IMAGES` in `src/ui/map-cards.js`. Until then the card falls back to a render of the loaded map.
4. That's all the wiring: the map page builds one card per `menuMaps()` entry, multiplayer uses `multiplayerMaps()`, `?map=<id>` loads any map, and unknown ids fall back to `DEFAULT_MAP`. Multiplayer spawns come from the map's building interiors (`net/spawn-points.js`), so give it buildings with rooms, or from its authored `bases` and `ffaSpawns` (`net/map-spawns.js`, see below).
5. Tests: `tests/registry.test.js` checks every map has modes and basics.
6. **A map with hills:** add a `terrain` spec (see Hills), give its buildings `baseY`, author every steep edge as a level's `cliffs` side or an `edges` polyline (never leave a steep bank a body can reach), set `maxSlope` (0.78) so meeting features are eased, run `node tools/bake-terrain.mjs`, and keep tests/terrain.test.js green (it walks every reachable point of every terrain map).

**Building a new map, in stages (owner, 2026-09-25).** One map at a time. Show the owner the result at the end of each stage before moving on, and fix bugs and optimise along the way, not only at the end. At the end of **every** stage: the tests, a headless check (`tools/smoke.mjs`), a phone-size check, frame cost on all 5 presets (`node tools/perf.mjs <preset> <weapon> <map> [x z]`, from the map's widest and densest views), publish to the game page, one review agent (owner, 2026-09-25: one per stage, not four critics plus verifiers), the golden replay (tests/golden-flat.test.js) once at the end of the stage, and this file updated. Nice-to-haves wait for stage 5.
0. If the map needs a new engine system (Hollow Wick needed hills), its core first, on a small dev-only test map (Test Hill).
1. **Ground:** size and border, ground colours and texture, paths, heights, water. Walk it on every preset.
2. **Placeholders:** grey blocks for every building, landmark and cover piece. Test routes, sightlines and ranges per weapon, robot walking, spawns (two and three teams, FFA) and targets. Settle the layout here.
3. **Mechanics and style:** the map's own systems and its look (light, fog, sky, palette, sound), tuned on all 5 presets.
4. **Development:** real buildings with finished interiors, real props and breakables (debris, sound, marks), the set pieces, the menu picture and card, the overhead map.
5. **Detail:** clutter, ground scatter, small props, decals and life, until it is at least as detailed as Deadwater; compare them side by side.

Every new map: a new vibe, no copies of Deadwater's features (no crops); breakables and interiors kept; Deadwater's art style, camera and quality or better; built for all 5 presets with phones in mind; compact to medium (about 130 m); every mode; fights at every range and several routes between areas. **Maps in progress stay out of the menus** (`menu: false` in `MAP_LIST`) and are loaded from Developer tools > World > Map in progress (`maps.js` `workMaps`), or `?map=<id>` on a dev server.

**Authored spawns: bases and FFA points (Hollow Wick, s2-spawns).** A map may give `bases` ([{ id, x, z, poly, points }], 6-8 points each), `teamBases` ({ sides: [base ids in TEAMS order] }), `ffaSpawns` ([{x, z}], ~20), `noSpawn` ([{ id, poly }]) and a `pickView` (Hollow Wick's data: `src/maps/hollow-wick-spawns.js`, spread into the map). With any of them the map's spawns come from `net/map-spawns.js` instead of building interiors (Deadwater has none and is untouched: tests/golden-flat.test.js):
- **Online** (`Arena.authoredSpawn`): team modes put each side at its base whatever the spawns setting (Hollow Wick: 2V2 / 3V3 at A town yard and C south bank, 2V2V2 at A, B graveyard foot and C; B is ~35 m from the summit, the others ~72 m); a free point, teammates a body apart; bases are exempt from the pick-view rule. **Never in an enemy's sight while another point will do** (v0.975a, stage 4 audit: base A sits on the main street and every respawn there came up in view of an enemy standing in it): `baseSpot(..., { enemies })` passes over a point within `BASE_SAFE` 12 m of a living enemy or on their 16:9 screen with the ground clear and no building wall between (`exposedTo`); every point exposed: the FFA point nearest the base (within 30 m) that is not; only then an exposed point. Everyone else (FFA, 1V1, practice) comes in at an FFA point `SPAWN_APART` from everyone alive (a screen away: off every screen), half the time one the ground also hides from them (it used to be always, when there was one: a third of all respawns came in at the one point behind Church Hill), leaving out the last three used (`recent`, kept by the arena and BotMatch); else a sampled open spot that far (`openSpot`); else the valid point furthest from everyone. Base A's south row stands off the main street's road (z -7.5).
- **SOLO** (`BotMatch.authoredSpot`, `youSpot`; `duel.js` sets `bots.baseSpawn` in the team modes): your side at `teamBases[2][0]`, the enemies at `[1]`; 1V1 and scattered at FFA points. main.js puts you at yours when the game starts and after a death (the same enemy-sight rule: your enemies are the robots of the other side). Dev-tool robots (no `apart`) keep the old spots, but on a hills map those must pass `spawnProblem` too (`BotMatch.spot(..., colliders)`: they came in in the water, on steep banks, in the graveyard and once in the tavern).
- **The rules** (`spawnProblem`): playable, dry (`bankDistance >= 0`), not on a deck, slope at most 0.3 at the point and half a metre round, not in a `noSpawn` area or a building, 1 m from every collider. Every point is re-checked against the live colliders at each spawn, so props added later can never bury one; `openSpot` applies the same rules on any hills map or map with `noSpawn` (flat maps unchanged).
- **Menus:** (Hollow Wick left this state in v0.990a.) A map in progress with `'multiplayer'` in its modes stays dev-only: `multiplayerMaps(current)` / `soloMaps(current)` list it only when it is the loaded map (reached from Developer tools > World > Map in progress, then SOLO or HOST A GAME there); the invite link carries `map=` when it is not the default map.
- **Robots** on Hollow Wick reach every base and the summit on the nav grid (slopes and water cost more). The fallen log is a narrow deck on the grid like any ground; no one-at-a-time rule, since stepping off it is only wading. The mill dam's walk is 1.8 m between its wall faces (at 1.6 m no nav square fitted and robots waded beside it; tests/hollow-wick-spawns.test.js checks its squares). **Stage 4 audit fixes (v0.975a, `bots/robot-brain.js`):** cover and shot spots cost more in the water (`wetCost`: up to +3 at `WADE.depth`) and never under a deck in the stream (robots hid and fought from under the bridge, unseen); a shot spot the robot reached with still no line from where it stands is marked bad for 4 s and it looks again (`markBadSpot`/`isBadSpot`; a spot must also have a line from 0.3 m round it, the arrival radius): robots stood a minute doing nothing below the dam, by base A's yard wall and at the terrace ramp. (On every map since v0.990a, owner: it was gated to hills maps; Deadwater's `robots` golden replay was re-recorded for it.) A deck is see-through while anyone the view draws is under it, not only you (`updateCrossingDecks`). A few props were moved out of gaps a body fits but a robot's nav does not (the farm's skeps and crocks, the barn's pumpkins and barrow, the farm haystack); small pockets remain between some detail stones.
- **Pick view:** Hollow Wick's weapon-pick camera looks at the stubble south of the fence (`pickView` { x 0, z 64, height 24 }), so it rules out no spawn.
- **Tools and tests:** `node tools/spawn-check.mjs [map]` (every point valid with its height and slope; FFA spacing and ground-hidden pairs; the longest sightline out of each base on the real 16:9 screen, over 18 m flagged: until sheds and walls stand, A, B and C each show an open line of 25-30 m; building walls count as stopping sight or fire since v0.975a). tests/hollow-wick-spawns.test.js: points valid, nothing in water, on decks or in no-spawn areas, team modes at the right bases, FFA a screen apart, nav reach, practice targets (from the green at 6/10/14/20 m; from Church Hill's south-west foot one 2.6 m above and one 2.9 m below), menus, SOLO, and a 60 s robots 2V2.

## Items, cosmetics and a future store

- **Registry (built): `src/items.js`.** Every weapon and skin has a unique ID and its data defined there. Menus, the HUD and the tutorial read from it. Each weapon's `stats` names its tuning block in `config/gameplay.js`. Each weapon has a `<id>.default` skin (how it looks today). `ownedItems(list)` turns the authority's list into a set, dropping unknown ids; with no list, a player owns the starter set (everything today).
- **Two kinds of item:**
  - **Gameplay items** are weapons, with stats that point at the tuning configs.
  - **Cosmetics** are visual only: skins, which never change stats.
- **Ownership:**
  - It is decided by the authority, the host now and a server or backend later. It's **never trusted from the client**, so editing the game or storage can't unlock paid items.
  - It will tie to player accounts, a login, once a backend exists.
- **Store:** a future store and payment provider plug in on the backend. The backend grants ownership, and the game only reads the owned list the authority sends.
- **Online:** a player's equipped skin ids will travel in the `hello` message. The authority checks them against the owned list it got from the backend (never from the client) and falls back to `<weapon>.default` for anything not owned, so other players only ever see skins that were paid for.
- **Not built yet:** accounts, a store, and skins actually changing models. A new skin is a new `SKINS` entry plus the model code that reads its `visual` data.

## Known bugs, unfinished work, priorities

**Next up, in order:**
1. Multiplayer is live (free-for-all). See Multiplayer > Next steps.
2. Keep splitting when touched: `main.js` (~1090 lines; dev wiring and the toast moved out; next is keyboard/mouse input) and `renderer.js` (~1810 lines after world building, warm-up and vision moved to mixins).

**Queued:**
- **Title screen, next:** it is the main menu now. Still to check on a real phone (the goo filter is the only costly part; it already stops when hidden and drops to 30 fps after the intro).
- A loading screen after picking the map and weapon, with the world build moved into it (the owner wants it styled with the title screen, after the title is settled).
- A shadow-box margin.
- Fix point-blank Static orbs spawning off the muzzle when fired into props.
- Explosive barrels: reddish, 30 HP, a larger grenade-style blast, 1.8% spawn, and dashing pushes them.
- Clarify "make ADS slightly more aim in".

**Known issues:**
- **Hex (X) performance:** the spinning hex's sides and its flying orbs break props they cross; that used to test every prop against every collider six times a step (the whole frame on a laptop, a spike on the second X). `Simulation.breakablePropsAlong` now walks one cached list of prop colliders with a bounding-box reject (about 12x less work in profiles), and `segmentBox` (every bullet, orb and hex side against boxes) allocates nothing.
- Check before handing over: `node tools/smoke.mjs` (dev server up) plays every preset headlessly and must print no errors or warnings. It caught two that are now fixed: the shader warm-up cloned post-pass materials that sample a render target (three warned and dropped the texture; those now skip the indoor copy), and GTAO's depth texture was sampled before it had a size on Extreme (GL_INVALID_VALUE; it now gets its target's size up front).
- Load time on slow laptops and phones, mostly world building (shared box geometry cut ~15% at 4x CPU slowdown; `tools/load-profile.mjs`). On Balanced, the first fire death still links 2-3 programs (clipped variants); Extreme links none.
- Draw calls on Performance at phone size: about 90 (was 159 before merging the player and targets, 119 before the empty-draw skip and weapon/roof bakes). Breakable props are now drawn in batches (`prop-instances.js`), which took about 20 draws off busy spots.
- Roof and wall line shimmer: the camera pixel snap is the current fix, not yet confirmed by the owner.


### Local weapon preview revision 19 (2026-09-27)

The isolated codex/omen worktree now adds room-aware bots, Sightline scanning, and cursor-placed Breach. Current details and complete merge inventory are in CLAUDE_HANDOFF_OMEN.md and SIGHTLINE_DESIGN.md; no commit/push/pull/merge authorized.
- Bots: src/bots/interior-tactics.js remembers witnessed doorway entry, never hidden room coordinates, then pushes/holds/probes with expiry. All indoor attacks have short bursts and longer pauses (BOT_INTERIOR). Allies cannot relay a hidden indoor location to an outside bot; unseen indoor players do not guide patrols.
- Sightline bots: src/bots/sightline-tactics.js uses only player inputs, normal setup/reloads and ammo. Travel, plant to scan, sweep slowly with pauses, acquire/settle/lead moving targets, scan the last seen lane briefly, and reposition or draw Sidekick under pressure. Scoped sensing uses the player's 45-degree cone and reach. The old off-screen firing safeguard remains. BOT_SIGHTLINE holds timings.
- All buildings, including open sheds, conceal foreign occupants and never make a roof transparency circle around them. Your roof still fades when you enter. See roof-fade.js, renderer.js and roomShowsEntity in vision-polygons.js.
- Breach lands at cursor distance unless intercepted by a body or solid cover. Regular aimed Sightline still travels its extended range. Slight scoped cursor weight (rate 28, 3400 px/s); Sidekick is unchanged. Empty rifles have no laser, including networked bodies (rifleAmmo in the existing sightline player state).
- The local guide draws above fog and depth as a placement overlay but still ends at the first collision-tested blocker. This prevents the lower animated barrel connector from clipping into a hill and reappearing. Other players' beams and all projectiles retain depth concealment; no new render pass.
- Regressions: bot-interiors.test.js, bot-sightline.test.js, sightline.test.js, roof-fade.test.js, aim-cursor.test.js. Only hosted Deadwater golden hash changed intentionally for the requested room behavior; audit and reason are in golden-flat.test.js and the handoff.

### Local weapon preview revision 23 — standalone Sidekick (2026-09-27)

Sidekick is a sixth selectable weapon; Sightline still keeps its separate, unchanged 25 +/- 2 backup. Standalone Sidekick deals 30 +/- 2, manual fire, 10 rounds, 0.28 s interval, 22 m range, 65 m/s projectiles and a 2 s reload. It uses the same pistol model and the existing studio for its selection art.

E plants a small ground disc under the player. One-second arming, 25-second cooldown, two mines per owner across the map. Placing a third replaces the oldest. The trigger radius is 0.7 m; allies/owner never trigger or take this blast. A single 170–230 roll is shared by the blast: full damage within 0.9 m, falling to 20% at 3.6 m. Walls and terrain block it; another deck level cannot trigger it. Friendly dots flash green and enemy dots red. Death, respawn/loadout change and map reset clear that player's mines; leaving clears their mirrored list. Existing grenade smoke, fireball, sound and directional explosion death are reused.

X is Rush: reach out for 0.55 s, then eight full seconds with a second gun. A white silhouette grows in the outstretched hand and resolves into coloured steel/wood. Running is 20% faster, normal bullets retain 30 +/- 2 damage, and LMB/Space may be held for alternating automatic fire at 1.75x cadence. Each gun has ten rounds; the new gun arrives loaded, the original retains its ammunition. A 2 s reload refills both while Rush is active. On expiry bonus ammunition disappears; the original retains its remaining rounds and returns to manual fire. Cooldown is 45 s from summoning. A dodge does not consume or cancel Rush; death and loadout reset end it. These tuning defaults can be adjusted after playtesting.

Motion echoes are merged low-poly player silhouettes, in one instanced batch capped at 48, lasting 0.4 s. Performance/Potato emit every 0.10 s; Quality/Extreme every 0.06 s. No new dynamic lights or render passes. Mine discs, rims, indicator dots and bullets use capped instance pools. Existing room/scope shader clipping applies to them; hidden bodies do not emit visible echoes. The white reveal is a shader uniform over the original vertex colours, so it needs no geometry rebuild. Natural fixed-length arm solving is shared with Sightline. Remote models animate both guns; private reveal materials are cleaned when avatars change weapons or leave.

Networking protocol is now 14: sanitized mine/Rush inputs, predicted speed state, host-owned ammo/cooldowns, remote gun state, mine allegiance and projectile snapshots. Bots use their normal perception, tap outside Rush, hold in Rush, place mines when pressured nearby, and use the same cooldowns. Shared room fire pacing includes Sidekick actions. Four short tutorial steps teach shooting, reload, mines and Rush. HUD uses the shared ammo pips and E/X filled circles (20 rounds during Rush).

New files: src/weapons/sidekick.js, src/weapons/sidekick-model.js, src/weapons/sidekick-view.js, src/assets/weapons/sidekick.webp, tests/sidekick.test.js, SIDEKICK_DESIGN.md.

Latest Ichor requirements supersede revisions 27/28 above. Details are in ICHOR_DESIGN.md. Hold/tap repeats identical broad slashes (no thrusts), 0.24 s cadence/contact 0.067 s, 30–85 damage. Dash/0.24 s landing grace uses a unique slash with 15% extra damage and 0.3 m reach. X keeps eight hits/200–600 total/80 health cost/no healing, now around 1.3 s; WASD adds directional movement pulses without forced motion. ### Local weapon preview revision 31 — flowing Ichor combat, timed parries and blood saturation (2026-09-27)

Current tuning supersedes earlier Ichor entries: basic cuts retain 30–85 damage and 0.24 s cadence, now with varied smaller/broader arcs and occasional 360 spins. Ten authored forms include diagonal descents, rising rolls, backhands, dash cuts and body-led spins. The blade rests on its finishing side; subsequent forms start from that side. Walking slows 12% during attacks while Frenzy retains faster WASD movement. Successful attacks within 0.85 s build an extra 7/14/21% blood, advancing once per attack rather than per victim. Above 90% blood regenerates 6 HP/sec, capped at max and disabled throughout Frenzy.

X now runs twelve strikes over 2.4 s, 180–540 total direct damage (10% lower), 22% secondary damage, unchanged 80-health cost and 50 s cooldown. Three sequences combine crossed white/red crescents, rising fans, broken spirals and finishing wheels. Normal slashes mix white and muted reds below 60% blood and turn red above it. Both ribbon edges follow terrain with normal depth testing; the below-deck level remains intact. Geometry remains capped and batched; two sampled edges replace six repeated evaluations per segment.

A timed 55 ms slash crossing can deflect one front-facing regular bullet, with 0.28 s recovery. Host and bot collision proxies share that recovery state. The original bullet is consumed without damage or hit credit; an outward random spent ricochet, clipped to cover, plus metallic ping/glints shows it. Nominal/Sidekick/Sightline normal rounds qualify; pellets, curses, electricity and explosive rounds do not. No reflected damage or new death type is introduced.

Organic hits spray more directional blood; robots remain metal with yellow/blue sparks. At high blood the wielder's existing hat/body/arm vertices become drenched, nearly all red at full, with no additional draw calls. Private geometry/material copies avoid affecting other avatars; eight display stages limit uploads. Full blood keeps boots wet for 24 steps, with extra local drips; under-bridge prints stay below. Respawn cleans the player while corpse snapshots keep the blood and release their own copies on removal. Human AI remains behind the existing solo developer unlock.

See ICHOR_DESIGN.md and CLAUDE_HANDOFF_OMEN.md for detailed balance, verification, inventory and artifact checksum. All work stays in codex/omen; no commit, push, pull, merge or public deployment.

The blade coating is now flush to both steel faces in blade coordinates, without an offset/scaled shell. It follows blade flips and guard poses; swing-shed droplets are narrower liquid streaks. Supersedes revision 33's guard budget/HUD: each raise rolls 40–60 damage capacity (integer), holds indefinitely, and slows walking by 10%. Releasing or exhausting it starts a fixed 20-second cooldown. Excess projectile damage passes through when capacity runs out; Ballast spends capacity pellet by pellet before aggregation. Omen physical shots qualify; electricity, active curses, explosions and melee still bypass it. Existing timed-cut parry remains independent. Solid cover clips all cut ribbons/filaments and their blood emission with one 128-sample polar mask per cut, built from nearby colliders and reused by every layer. It retains cover through that cut even when a prop breaks. Damage uses the existing solid-cover rule; prop victims are captured before breaking any so one cut cannot penetrate a front crate into a rear one. Rotated carts/walls protect people; headstones remain breakable over their low permanent stumps.

Slashes use a filtered steel scrape with quiet, nearly steady metal partials instead of the old octave-down squeak. Higher blood raises pitch and lengthens the tail; high-blood human kills add a wet splash, while robots keep metal-only contact audio. Blood HUD gains stronger irregular sloshing, darker liquid veins, thin wet edges and bounded falling drops as it rises. E/X gain animated blood edging, beads and runs progressively with blood. HUD shape updates remain capped at 30 Hz, meter drops at 36, and world glints/sparks reuse the existing capped line batch; no extra lights.

### Ichor after the merge (v0.990a fixes, owner, 2026-09-27)
- **The blade cuts crops.** Every standing crop tile a cut's sweep reaches is cut down (`contact` in weapons/ichor.js; `ichorSweepMeets` in ichor-cut.js samples the sweep near to far, all round for a spin), from the ground only (not from under a deck), and the blood wave (E) mows a path along its way. `cutCrop` (crops.js) sets the tile `gone` as the Static's arcs do and pushes `cropCut` (the tile, and the blade's way): straw and chaff thrown along the cut (renderer.js), a dry rustle (audio.js). A burning tile burns on. `cropCut` is a shared event (host-session.js SHARED_EVENTS); the tile's state reaches clients with the world as before. Tests: tests/ichor-field.test.js.
- **The guard turns only what meets the blade.** `tryIchorDeflect` keeps its facing cone (`parryFacing` .55), and a raised guard now also needs the round's way in (from 3 m before it meets the body to the point of its path nearest the body's middle) to pass within `thick` (.14 m) of the blade as it is drawn in the block pose (`ICHOR_GUARD_BLADE`, [forward, right] from the body's middle: the hands at .25, .39, the tip at .63, -1.7; measured on the model). So a round at the back, or at the back of the uncovered right side, goes through to the body, even from inside the cone; a round from the front-left that crosses the blade out past the body is turned. A timed cut's parry needs the round to meet the front half of the body (`meetsFront`). A round with no position (x, z) is taken to pass through the body's middle. If the block pose changes (ichor-motion.js ICHOR_BLOCK), measure the blade again and update `ICHOR_GUARD_BLADE`.
- **The blood wave's price (owner: "when the wave leaves the player's blade it causes the player to splash blood and lose half the damage the thing would've dealt"; "only the damage of that hit").** The wave still hits for its normal rolled damage (`waveDamage` 70 ± 10). As it is launched the wielder loses `waveCost` (.5) of that one hit's rolled damage (30-40), whoever it meets or misses: a `playerDamage` of `ichorCost` (as Frenzy's drain: no hurt sound, no damage arrow), never below 1 health, nothing with the developer tools' invulnerability. The `ichorWave` event carries `cost` and the wielder's `id`, and the view throws a blood splash off the body (backward, and some along the wave) with a stain at their feet, for anyone who can see them. Robots launch it only above 140 health (`waveDamage` x 2). Tests: tests/ichor-field.test.js.
- `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 16 for these (the new event; the host's rule).

### Owner fixes after v0.990a (2026-09-27), not yet released
- **Roofs lift whole at a door.** Entering, the roof came off in parts: stepping onto the stoop faded the hood and the front section, and only at the doorway (.42 m from the wall) did the whole roof go. The whole-roof lift now starts `DOOR_LIFT` (out 1.8 m, sides .35 m past the door) from a door (renderer.js), a step before the hood alone would fade, and a roof lifting whole (`entry.lifting`) or not yet back (`opacity < .995`) fades no section on its own (roof-fade.js updateSections: its values are held at 0), so it lifts and comes back as one.
- **The lobby's backdrop:** `lobbyView(map)` (pick-view.js): a map's `lobbyView`, else its pick view. Hollow Wick's is the meetinghouse and burying ground (-40, -18, 40); its pick view stays on the stubble outside the fence (no spawn is ever under it). Nobody is in the world during the lobby, so the lobby view has no spawn rule. Deadwater's lobby keeps its farmhouse and corn.
- **Blood on the gun in your hand** (`makeGunStains`): flecks lie on each shown part's own top, sized from that part and never over `FLECK_MAX` (5.5 cm). Before, one box round everything shown (Sightline's slung rifle and its pistol: most of the body) sized flecks up to half a metre and hung them in the air round you: the "enlarged, bugged blood particles" a friend saw online. Test: tests/gun-stains.test.js.
- **Omen's curse marks** are drawn on the body as it is drawn (`drawnAt` in omen-view.js: you, an avatar's interpolated root, a practice target), not at the simulation's step or the last snapshot's position, so they no longer lag or jitter under someone, above all online. The curse's tick pulses follow the same.
- **Everyone prints the dirt:** robots and other players leave footprints as you do (`stepPrints(key, x, z, vx, vz, ok)`, renderer.js, called by remote-players.js for each avatar outdoors on dry ground, not under a deck, not dead), in the same pool (`FOOT_CAP` 320).
- **Stream fog:** Hollow Wick's fog sheets gather harder over low ground (`lowBias` 1.6, was .7; tries = 1 + 2 x lowBias, up to 5; up to 2.6 x as thick over the lowest ground) and are a little stronger (opacity .4).
- **A hex stops rounds at its wall** (`Simulation.shieldStop` / `shieldEntry`): a round fired by someone outside a hex ends where it first crosses into it, with a `hexBlock` spark there (`wall: true`): the rifle's bullets, the Ballast's pellets, Sidekick and Sightline rounds, Omen's bolts, launched Static orbs and Ichor's wave. (Damage from outside was already refused at the body, hit's `shieldedFrom`; now nothing flies in.) Golden hosted re-recorded (e448e157). Test: tests/hex-shield.test.js.
- **Crop fields hide people:** players and robots in a field are drawn for another player only by `cropEntityVisible` (crops.js: from inside the same field, and only within its visibility), as the practice targets always were; your own side always shows. Robots see an enemy in a field by the same rule (robot-brain.js).
- **Deadwater's dust** is a drier ochre: its fog sheets #d8bc70 / #efd690, and every kicked-up puff, dash trail, gust and dust devil pulled .32 of the way to #dcb858 (map look `dust`, renderer.js `kickedDustColor`; Hollow Wick has none).
- **The corner text in game** is the version alone (no DEADSTAB), and the version reads `v` + VERSION everywhere (v0.990a; the letter is the stage: a alpha, b beta), set from version.js at load (main.js), not the page's text.
- **Ichor's slashes over the ground's marks:** its effect batches draw at `ICHOR_FX_ORDER` (3), after the blood stains, splatters and prints (order 2), so those show under a slash, not over it.
- **Warm-up:** Hollow Wick's breakables' debris pool (`HollowBreakFX`, an instanced Lambert batch) is made during the warm-up where the map has any of those props (it was made on the first break and built its shader then).
- **Favicon:** public/favicon.svg (a pink tile, #e8afb9, with a blood splatter in its lower right corner), favicon-32.png, and the same picture for icon-192/512.png (the manifest and the home-screen icon); `node tools/make-icons.mjs` renders the PNGs from the SVG.

### Owner fixes after v0.994a (2026-09-28), not yet released
- **Roofs stay put against a wall** (owner: "the roof when going up against some buildings still disappears"). A slope's fade shape was solid all the way down (`y0` -Infinity), so anyone within `reach` of an eave, even on the camera's side with nothing over them on screen, faded the whole slope and showed the ceiling.
