A slope's slab is now the roof itself: `band` (`SECTION_FADE.band` .5 m) deep under its top (colonial-buildings.js `plane` sets `band` on slopes; gable ends keep their eave floor and stay solid). `slabHides` takes the least and greatest gap (slab top less the line's height) over the part of the line inside and hides only where the gap is between 0 and `band`. The head's line tests slopes and gable ends with `SECTION_FADE.edge` (.08 m) instead of `reach` (.3): only when the roof is over the middle of it, not a brim's width off; the waist's line keeps `reach`. Result: hugging a front (camera-side) wall or corner fades nothing; behind a building (north side, the roof really over you) the slope still fades, as before (owner chose to keep that). tests/roof-fade.test.js (the test slopes carry `band`), tests/hollow-wick-buildings.test.js (the cape's ceiling now checked from its back wall; its front wall fades nothing).
- **A faded section is a soft patch, not the whole section** (owner, with a picture: "the regular roof on the building disappears, though some interior ceiling remains ... it should have the regular roof with a seamless transition into a faded portion just for where the edge of it is"). The sections are still decided as above (`updateSections`, eased values in the shared table), but a section's value is now a gate: the roof's material (`sectionFade`, both variants) no longer drops a faded section's vertices; in the fragment shader, where `vSectionFade` is over `cut`, the old patch loop (`holeLoop`, now with `ROOF_PATCH`: fully open within .8 m of the point on the character's line to the camera, whole again past 1.9 m) times `vSectionFade` is `sectionOpen`: the opaque draw leaves it out (discard), the blended copy draws only it at 1 - `sectionOpen` x .85. So the roof stays and fades smoothly into a see-through patch over whoever it hides. **The ceilings are gone** (colonial-buildings.js `ceiling` removed; `CEILING_FADE` and `ceilingMaterial` are unused): with only a patch open, the boarded lid read as the roof missing. Tests: tests/roof-fade.test.js (the shader), tests/hollow-wick-buildings.test.js (no ceilings).
- **Corner boards** (owner: "4 clippings in the corners of the interiors"): each corner was one box T + .1 square through the corner, .05 m into the room and a pale square on top. Now the walls' corner square is filled flush in the wall colour and two trim boards sit on the outside faces only.
- **Favicon:** a stale root `favicon.svg` (the old teal diamond) was what `index.html`'s `favicon.svg` resolved to in the build, so the pink icon in `public/` never shipped. The root file is gone; the icon links carry `?v=2` so browsers drop the cached one.
- **Graphics change never finishing** (owner: after switching to AUTO the world stayed blank under APPLYING GRAPHICS): `warmProgramsParallel` waits on `compileAsync` at most `WARM_WAIT` (2.5 s) a step (warm-up.js), then the ordinary compile finishes; main.js `changeGraphics` also lets drawing resume and hides the note after 20 s whatever happens. The note is now plain HUD text under the health bar (no pill over the score).
- **Touch always brings the touch controls** (owner): `createInputPreference.observe('touch')` drops a KEYBOARD pick from the menu and switches to touch (a key never takes away a MOBILE pick; a tap on the controls choice itself is the choice). tests/input-preference.test.js.
- **The VS ROBOTS score** (owner: "make it match rest of game ui, it looks too ai generated"): no pill, rim or glow; laid on the world under the health bar's drop shadow, numbers in the health number's face (Bahnschrift), a thin rule between the sides, and in first-to games a row of health-track segments under each side (`duel-track`, up to 10, filled from the middle out as rounds are won). duel.js `render`, menu-theme.css `.duel-score`.
- **Hotspot lag** (owner: joiners on a phone hotspot "have high ping ... lags around the map and glitches ... super slow response"). PROTOCOL_VERSION 18. Inputs go packed as arrays (protocol.js `packInput`/`unpackInput`, `INPUT_FLAGS`; ~87 B a message instead of ~1.1 KB), `inputRedundancy` 10. Snapshots leave out zero motion fields (`ZERO_OMITTED`, applyPlayerState defaults them to 0), round weapon states, send names only on slow snapshots (host `seatState(seat, lastSeq, named)`), and loadouts carry only the matching weapon's block. peer-transport.js opens a second, unreliable unordered channel per peer (`FAST_LABEL` 'ds-fast-', `maxRetransmits: 0`, patched in `fastChannels()`), used for `FAST_TYPES` (snapshot, input, ping, pong) and falling back to the reliable one when it is not open or is `congested` (over 24 KB buffered); everything else stays reliable. The host drains input queues gently (`queue>12`: down to 6; `>4`: 2 a tick; else 1) and smooths remote bodies (`smoothed`, `SMOOTH` .045, snaps past 2 m). The joiner times snapshot lateness (last 40) and sets its interpolation delay from it (`interpolationDelayFor`: p90 + a snapshot gap + .02, between `interpolationDelay` and `maxInterpolationDelay` .3, eased), and extrapolates up to .12 s along velocity when a snapshot is late. tests/net-wire.test.js; tests/golden-flat.test.js hosted re-recorded (only the wire and the joiner moved).
- **Sniper passes all obstacles except buildings** (owner: "Make sniper fire through ... all obstacles in game. This includes trees and walls and fences but discludes buildings and building walls"). map-kit.js `buildingWalls` marks a building's round-stopping walls `wall: true`. sightline-flight.js `sightlineMeets` meets only `wall` boxes and breakables (destructible, or a prop with hp: the round still cuts through and damages them); `sightlineGuideEnd` (the laser, the placement guide, robots' `sniperLineClear`/`sniperClear`) stops only at `wall`. Trees, fences, fieldstone walls, rocks, graves, furniture, carts, the mill works: passed. The ground, bridges and a hex still stop it; the Sidekick is unchanged. Robots therefore take only buildings as cover from a sniper. Tests use `wall: true` for a solid wall (sightline, bot-sightline, bot-laser-response).
- **Dynamic buffers upload only what is drawn** (owner: "optimize ... make fps increase across platforms"). render/upload-used.js, installed on the game scene (renderer.js): before each render, every InstancedMesh's changed instance attributes, and every non-indexed dynamic geometry's changed attributes, get an update range for just `count` instances / the draw range, unless they set their own ranges; if the drawn part grows with no new write, the grown slice is sent. Candidates are rescanned every 30 frames. (The Sheath's 24000-vertex ribbons and the 700-line batch no longer send the whole pool each frame.) tests/upload-used.test.js.
- **Meeting road meets the door** (owner, with a picture: "the path doesn't meet the doorway"): Hollow Wick `nw-branch` ran into the meetinghouse's south-east corner; it now bends north along the east wall into the east door (hollow-wick.js; terrain rebaked).
- **SOLO menu: difficulty only** (owner: "Remove the enemy aim and enemy aggression parts from solo menu ... just do difficulty"): duel-menu.js shows one row per side, "enemy difficulty" / "your robots' difficulty" (the skill levels); aim and temper stay at their defaults (even, shifting). The lobby's round settings keep aim and temper.
- **The player is a plain white figure** (owner: "Redesign player avatar ... just a white player skin ... pure white. Also make one of these in a slight blueish color. Should be same size same hitbox"). render/player-figure.js `buildFigure`: round legs with rounded feet (x ±.13), pelvis, tapered torso (.3-.86 m), a squashed shoulder dome, neck, a ball head (top about 1.31 m, where the hat's crown was), and the gun arm as a capsule (same place as before; its rotation is baked into the geometry so its +y is up). `FIGURE` holds the sizes; blood-wading.js `makeBloodStains` places every fleck from it, flat on the surface. One material per figure (`figureMaterial`, not a colour-keyed world material, so it is never baked into vertex colours), shared by the body, the static arm and the RiflePose arms (`player.userData.figureMaterial`; with it the pose's arms are round tubes and hands). Pure white on screen: `figureBalance(look)` inverts the map's light colour (sky, bounce, sun) so a lit figure is neutral, `FIGURE_LIFT` 1.12, plus a white glow `FIGURE_GLOW` .3 (IchorDrench fades it as blood covers the body). Your own (world-build.js makePlayer) and every other human player (remote-players.js: all share `view.remoteFigure`; player identity is the base ring's slot colour, a side the wide team ring; PLAYER_COLOURS' coats are unused on the body) use it; robots keep their tin gunslinger. Death effects fitted: gore and corpses in the figure's colour, BallastBlood's neck stump at .94 m and legs at ±.13, death-bones hands white, Sidekick's remote sleeve white. **Blue skin:** developer option Player › Player skin (`playerSkin` 1: `FIGURE_SKINS.blue` #d3e3ff); renderer drawFrame applies it with `setFigureSkin` (local view only). Hitbox untouched (simulation).
- **Firing and blasts without hitches** (owner: "frame loss especially when firing multiple projectiles or causing explosions ... smoother across the board without sacrificing visual quality"). Nothing drawn changed; the work moved off the frame:
- **Never stuck loading** (owner, v0.994a on a MacBook, a WebKit browser with side tabs: the loading screen never left, the word cut off at "deads", the menu dead under it). Two causes, both fixed: (1) the shader warm-up waited on `compileAsync`, which on some drivers never reports done (warm-up.js `WARM_WAIT`, above); bootstrap.js now also races `app.ready` against 20 s and `document.fonts.ready` against 3 s, so the game always opens. Reproduced headless by faking KHR_parallel_shader_compile that never completes: v0.994a hangs for good, this build opens. (2) Stretched SVG words (textLength + lengthAdjust) drew at natural width there: ui/svg-fit.js `fitSvgWords`/`watchSvgWords` measure each such word and, only where the drawn width is off by more than 3%, replace the stretch with a horizontal scale (the loading word's clip copy gets the same); run on the loading screen, then the whole page at reveal and on later DOM changes. Chrome/Firefox: nothing changes.
  - **Bullet and blast marks** (surface-marks.js) were the big one: every mark raycast the whole world with three.js (thousands of objects a ray; inside the 24-40 m merged cells every triangle) and cut its decal with DecalGeometry over the entire merged mesh it hit (two vectors made per vertex of the cell), and a blast did it 25 times; and `flush` walked the whole scene's matrices each frame a mark waited. Measured in the browser: rays 0.84 → 0.045 ms, decals 1.6 → 0.28 ms (a blast about 37 → 4 ms). effects/triangle-grid.js: a lazily made 1.5 m grid of each big mesh's triangles (with each triangle's height range; `raycastGrid` walks only the squares along the ray, Amanatides-Woo), `nearTriangles` (the decal is cut from only the triangles near it), and `objectIndex` (every mesh of the world, props and roofs by its world bounding sphere in 4 m squares; SurfaceMarks.cast uses it, the targets are raycast directly as they move). Grids and the index are made while loading (warm-up.js calls `prepareGrids(400)`) and otherwise a little each idle frame; the index is made again when the props are (clear, or a different prop count). Same hits, same order: tests/triangle-grid.test.js compares against three.js, and in the game 1600 rays on Deadwater and Hollow Wick gave identical first hits. Mark batches are now fixed buffers of `MARK_VERTS` (13000) vertices written onto the end with an update range (DynamicDraw, not frustum culled) instead of mergeGeometries copying the whole batch per mark. `flush` updates only the targets' and props' matrices.
  - **Static orbs and beams are pooled** (renderer.js `shotPool`, `beamPool`/`retireBeam`, up to 48 and 32): a volley made a group, a material and a buffer per orb and three materials and a buffer per beam, then disposed them. Cleared (and disposed) with the rest on a reset.
  - **Hex spin** (`breakablePropsAlong`): reads the broad-phase grid (collider-grid.js `collidersAlong`) instead of every prop collider, six sides a step. Same results (goldens unchanged).
  - **Less garbage every step**: `isPlayable` reads the outline by index (array destructuring made an iterator per body per step), `stepCrops` returns at once when nothing burns (it spread the bodies and walked every crop per body every step), tumbleweeds test only the colliders near them with one point object.
- **Skins: the cowboy stays; the figure is rigged and dev-only** (owner: "the skins should remain the previous skin, the dev tool should allow me to change skin ... redesign ... realistic and simple, but cartoony proportions ... animations for everything"). Supersedes "The player is a plain white figure" above. render/player-skin.js: `PLAYER_SKINS` cowboy / white / blue, `buildCowboy` (the original build, parts tagged `userData.skin`), `setPlayerSkin(view, skin)` (renderer.js calls it every frame from `sim.dev.playerSkin`, 0 cowboy, 1 white, 2 blue; dev-options.js "Player skin"). Everyone spawns as the cowboy; robots and other players keep their looks. render/figure-rig.js `FigureRig`: capsule limbs on a lathe torso with a sphere head (`RIG` proportions), two-bone IK (`solveTwoBone`) for arms and legs; feet planted in player space (a walk cycle along the facing, `STEP`), so moving any way keeps the steps under the body; run lean and bob, a dash lunge (lead and trailing foot, tucked weapon), a landing settle, idle breathing, a sniper's kneel for Sightline's stance. Hands take their goals from RiflePose (`rig.setGoals`: right/left targets and elbow hints, free flags, a throw), else the gun grip, else Omen's support hand; the chest twists (hips a third) to reach far grips, and a hand still short slides along the weapon. Idle hands: the weapon breathes with the chest, drifts on two slow beats, and re-grips every 4-8 s (a dip, a nudge, a settle); free hands drift. Blood: `makeRigStains` (flecks on the limbs, WADING stages). Deaths copy whatever body is drawn (limbs tagged `limb: 'arm±1'` are what Sheath severs); death-view.js takes gore colours from the figure's material. rifle-pose.js hides its own arm segments when a rig is present (`throwReleaseRig` for grenades).
- **Sightline stance any time** (owner): E crouches or stands whenever (not during a dodge or a commit), moving or not, loaded or not; the rifle reloads in the stance and keeps reloading standing and on the move (no gold effects); standing R with an empty rifle reloads it. `aimBlocked` only by the weapon in hand's reload. The rifle is shown while it reloads (sightline-model.js `held`, sightline-ammo.js, weapon-hud.js).
- **Loading screen** (owner): the word is plain text (no pour or drips), the wheel's colour runs #b3202f to #e8afb9 and back (loading.css `loading-tint`), no minimum splash (bootstrap.js). ui/svg-fit.js fits stretched SVG words on WebKit (skipped while `body.playing`: the HUD's changes re-measured hidden menu words every frame).
- **Menus** (owner): skills are easy / normal / hard only (robot-profile.js `MENU_SKILLS`; SOLO, lobby and robot options; the lobby's aim and temper rows are gone). Choice rows (skill, 1v1/2v2/3v3, host modes, lobby settings) are `button.choice-button` segmented bars sized like the other buttons (menu-theme.css, 40 px, 36 px on phones). Map and weapon pickers open as an overlay panel over the page (`.picker-panel`, absolute, scrolls inside, max 52vh), one at a time; outside press or Escape closes (weapon-grid.js `wirePicker`).
- **V toggles full screen** (owner: "remove everything pertaining to full screen and windowed, even in the controls ... V toggles"): keybinds.js `fullscreen` (KeyV, group other) is the only control; the SCREEN setting, LOCK BROWSER SHORTCUTS and FULL SCREEN WHILE PLAYING are gone (index.html, menu.js, mobile-settings.js, settings-panel.js). main.js: a capture keydown on the bound key (not repeat, not with modifiers, not in a field) leaves or enters full screen. Touch games still go full screen on start (`settings.fullscreen`).
- **Cursor in menus** (owner): any menu (not running, the death screen, a weapon choice, settings open) shows the plain pointer (`body.menu-pointer` hides the reticle, cone, spread and Omen timers); the weighted game cursor exists only in play, and pointer moves over the UI in play never place the reticle.
- **Crops hide nobody** (owner: "remove all features that involve going into crops to hide"). Supersedes "Crop fields hide people" above: `cropEntityVisible` is always true; vision.js draws no crop darkness. Crops still burn and spread.
- **Blades cut the stalks they meet** (owner: "they shouldn't break like holes, like squares ... in the parts where the melee weapon actually cuts ... maybe a little bit more"). crops.js: a cut is a shape, `{kind:'line', ax,az,bx,bz, r}` (Sheath's draw-cut, Ichor's blood wave) or `{kind:'arc', x,z, cx,cz, reach, arc}` (swings), sent as one `cropCut` event per stroke (`cutCrops(sim, cut, tilesMet, dx, dz)`); tiles are no longer taken away by a cut. `cropCutMeets(cut, x, z, margin)` with `CROP_CUT_MARGIN` .25 m past the blade. crop-view.js `cut(e)`: each stalk inside (a ragged margin per stalk) tips over along the cut (scattered ±25°, `FALL_TIME` .42 s, a small bounce), is thrown .35 m and shrinks to loose straw; the bed shows the swath (the cleared mask draws each cut's shape, blurred). Straw bursts run along the shape (renderer.js). PROTOCOL_VERSION 19.
- **Frame rate** (owner: "20 FPS on one of my laptops in quality ... double FPS ... keep the shadows ... Balanced minimum 70 FPS ... potato look better"):
  - **One frame in flight** (renderer.js `gpuBusy`/`fenceFrame`): the fence pacing waited for the previous frame before drawing the next, which serialised page and GPU (browsers report fences only between tasks; Safari late). Now up to two fences; a frame is held only while two are unfinished, at most `FENCE_PATIENCE` 50 ms.
  - **Sand grain painted in** below Extreme (renderer.js `prelitGround`, `PRELIT`): the relief heights lit by the map's sun into the ground's colour tile (same repeat), material colour lifted by `headroom`; no ground bump map (it was the ground's costliest part). Wood on Quality: `woodGrain()` as a colour map (`WOOD_GRAIN`) instead of a bump. Extreme keeps both bumps. Potato gets the painted grain (a 512 tile).
  - **Potato** goes through the crisp pass too (crisp-output.js `CRISP.potato`: FXAA-lite and sharpening at output ratio 1, max 1.3 M px) instead of the browser's stretch.
  - Quality's shadow map redraws at 30 fps (was 45). Balanced's multisampled buffer resolves colour only (`resolveDepthBuffer` false).
  - DOM: the Omen timers' `hidden` is written only on change; cone paths are rounded to 0.1 px (an unchanged path is not rewritten); hit-direction canvases draw at no more than 1.25x.
  - The frame cap: `RenderBudget` draws a frame due within `BUDGET_SLACK` (1.5 ms; an early frame is owed back), and the default cap is 0 (the display's rate) so 60 is not a ceiling.
- **No white flashes; one loading wheel** (owner: "sometimes when I load into games or change graphic settings ... a brief white screen ... any loading necessary should be the ... loading screen without the text and just the circle spinning slightly larger and centered"). The page's own background is the loading colour #191c1c (index.html inline `<style>` + `color-scheme: dark` before any CSS, style.css `:root`), so a canvas that is cleared or not yet drawn shows dark, never the old tan. The loading screen is the wheel alone (84 px, centered; no word or version). ui/busy-screen.js `showBusy`/`hideBusy` (nested holds) show the same wheel as `#busy-screen` (instant on, .16 s fade out): during a graphics change (main.js `changeGraphics`, replacing the APPLYING GRAPHICS note) and before every page navigation into a game or room (menu.js, online-play.js, dev-wiring.js). bootstrap.js makes the game live under the loading screen, starts it, and removes the screen only after two painted frames (fading .16 s).
- **Frame rate, round two** (owner: "do all of these ... make sure it's actually good ... when I load into the game, it should look mostly similar and keep extreme mode a little bit more extreme"). All below Extreme; Extreme draws as before.
  - **Kept sun shadows** (render/shadow-cache.js `ShadowCache`, `SHADOW_CACHE`; renderer.js `shadowCache.follow` in place of the per-frame sun placement, `setEnabled` in setQuality for Performance/Balanced/Quality). The map covers a region (the view's shadow box + `margin` 3 m) on a world-fixed texel grid at the old texel size; the still casters (frozen scenery roots, prop batches synced by prop-instances.js) are drawn into it only when the view leaves the region, and their picture is kept in `pristine`; each shadow update copies the pristine texels back where moving things were drawn last time and draws the moving things (players, robots, targets, grenades, anything not known still) on top. Watched still things that change are drawn as moving for `calm` 1 s, then patched into the kept picture. Receiving shaders, filter and bias are unchanged. Hooked by wrapping `renderer.shadowMap.render`.
  - **Matte lighting** (render/matte-lighting.js `setMatte`, `MATTE_SURFACE` define): roughness-1, metal-0 standard materials skip the roughness derivatives and use GGX's closed form at roughness 1; indirect specular with no environment map (always zero) is skipped (`NO_ENV_SPECULAR`). Same picture to one 8-bit step. setQuality marks materials (not Extreme).
  - **No shadow lookup facing away from the sun** (shader-savings.js patch 3): the sun's shadow filter is skipped where dot(normal, sun) <= 0 (the light there is zero anyway). All presets; identical picture.
  - **Smaller world, FSR upscale** (settings.js `scale`/`maxPixels`: Balanced .82 / 1.21 M, Quality .77 / 2.2 M; crisp-output.js): Balanced and Quality draw the world off-screen and bring it to the old screen size with an FSR 1-style chain: antialiasing at the world's size (4x MSAA in the buffer; FXAA 3.11 with an edge-end search on Apple touch devices), EASU edge-adaptive upscale (12 texels, clamped to the nearest four), RCAS sharpening (`rcas` stops). Potato and Performance keep their one `simple` pass at their old sizes (three passes cost Performance more than the smaller world saved). No temporal upscaling (most moving things here have no motion vectors: it would ghost).
  - **iPhone/iPad anti-aliasing** (crisp-output.js `appleTouch`, `crispSpec`): on Apple touch devices the multisampling tiers use FXAA instead (a resolved MSAA buffer is costly on tile GPUs).
  - **Fog sheets drawn where there is fog** (fog-sheets.js `sheetGeometry`, `SHEET_CELLS` 16 x 8): each sheet is rows of quads over the picture's cells that hold fog (and their neighbours), same UVs, instead of a full rectangle blended over the scene.
  - **Chunked culling** (render/chunk-cull.js `ChunkCull`, `CHUNK_SIZE` 32 m): the frozen scenery roots' children are grouped in chunks (at the root's transform) with their meshes' spheres; before each draw, chunks the camera cannot see are hidden (restored after), so three skips whole subtrees. The shadow pass sees every chunk (or, on Extreme's own redraw, those its box reaches). Never changes what is drawn.
  - The simulation stays on the main thread: moving it to a worker would put every input, the network code, robots, the tutorial and dev tools behind a message hop for a small share of the frame.
- **Elimination and spectating** (owner: "in 1v1, after whoever dies, both players respawn at full health random again. for 2v2, after two players on a team die, everyone respawns ... 2v2v2 and 3v3 ... until last team or last man standing, and whoever the last man's team is gets the point ... all gamemodes in solo and multiplayer except for ffa ... they should see the death screen, which turns into a spectating screen ... arrow keys or click or tap to change which teammate they're spectating (can't spectate other team)").
  - **Multiplayer** (net/arena.js): `eliminationMode(mode)` is every counted mode but FFA (1v1, 2v2, 2v2v2, 3v3; practice unchanged). A death sets `respawnIn` Infinity (no own respawn). `stepElimination` each tick: sides are teams (1v1: each seat); a seat standing = not dead and picking or present with health; when one side (or none) stands, it scores (`points`, `pointWon` event), and after `ROUND_BREAK` (5 s) `newPoint` brings every non-bench seat back at fresh spawns at full health (an open pick taken as it stands). `matchState` adds `elimination`, `sides` (points, best first), `roundBreak`, `roundWinner`; results count points (`results.points`, `winner.points`); a kill limit no longer ends a round mode (2026-09-29: ROUNDS decide, see Competitive rules). A pick made while down closes when done (`go`) and waits for the next point. The respawn-wait setting is FFA only. multiplayer-hud.js shows points beside the clock and "wins with N points".
  - **SOLO VS ROBOTS** (duel.js): `frame(dt, youStanding)` scores a side when the other has nobody standing (you count for yours); `DUEL_ROUND_BREAK` later `hooks.newRound` (main.js `newDuelRound`: you placed first, then every robot, your picked weapon applied); `pointBreak` for the score flash; bots.holdRespawns keeps fallen robots down (bot-match.js). While you are down the fight goes on (main.js `soloWatch`: the sim and robots step with your hands off). No RESPAWN or countdown on the death screen (death-screen.js `show(mode, { elimination })`).
  - **Spectating** (ui/spectate.js): `SPECTATE_AFTER` (3.5 s) after the death screen comes up, with a teammate standing (SOLO your robots; online your side, `online.others`), the world behind the death screen follows them (owner: "behind the death screen, not replace it": the death screen stays as it is); the bar over it names the teammate, pink arrow buttons either side; ← / → (A / D), the arrows, or a click / tap anywhere but the death card switch. main.js draws `spectateView(sim, mate)` (your sim with the player at the teammate: camera, room, roofs, sight follow them; your body stays where it fell); renderer.js skips the death camera while `view.spectating` and frames the teammate beside the card (x + 0.3 height wide, z + 0.22 height narrow, as death-view.js cameraFrame).
  - **Score flash** (ui/score-flash.js, owner: "it shouldn't say point to enemies but it should show the new score updating ... same text/format as the top ui ... bigger ... the change in numbers as an animation"): between points a copy of the top score (duel.js `scoreElement`, or the match clock's `.clock-teams` with `data-side`) in the middle, scaled 2.2x (1.7x narrow; beside the death card when down), the number that went up rolling from old to new with a pop, "next round in N" under it. Breaks are 5 s (ROUND_BREAK, DUEL_ROUND_BREAK: owner, "a short bit more time in between respawns").
- **Rounds, the round popup and the dull view** (owner: "the gameplay after player dies and is spectating is more dull, including game ui ... should go away after respawn ... after each respawn ... a popup in center screen for 2.4 sec ... round 1 or round 12 ... round number should also be shown in leaderboard/tab menu"). arena.js `round` (1 at startRound, +1 in newPoint; `matchState().round`); duel.js `round` (+1 as everyone comes back; the VS ROBOTS score's line reads "… · ROUND n"). main.js `showRound` puts `.round-popup` (mode name small over "ROUND n", 2.4 s animation) up once per round while you are up and playing. `body.watching` (down in an elimination round, from the death screen on; cleared with the death) dulls `#world` and the top score / clock / kill feed with CSS filters; the death screen, menus, spectating bar and score flash stay bright. multiplayer-hud.js shows "round n" in the scoreboard title (`.board-round`).
- **Short address** (v0.996a, owner: "remove all this crap from the game url when selecting bots and stuff, make it shorter"). src/launch.js: the menus' launches (`launchTo(query)`, menu.js, dev-wiring.js) keep the game's settings (`GAME_KEYS`: map, weapon, play, mode, duel, course) in sessionStorage (`deadstab.launchQuery`, with a one-time `deadstab.launch` = '1' note) and load the page's plain address; `readLaunch()` (main.js boot) reads them (`boot.launched` starts the game; a plain reload opens the menu on the last map) and takes those keys off the address bar, keeping anything else (join codes, dev flags; never stored). A same-map start uses `setLaunch`; main.js reads the duel's settings from `launchParams()`. Links that still carry the keys (old links, ?capture tools, online-play's room moves) work as before.
- **Extreme's first seconds** (v0.996a, owner: "my pc ... used to get 240fps all times on extreme ... now on a new map load it starts at 20fps and after 20ish secs it gets up to 240"). The warm-up gave up on the driver's parallel shader compiles after 2.5 s a step, so the game opened while hundreds of Extreme programs still compiled in the background. warm-up.js `programsSettled(renderer)` now waits while any program (shadow and AO variants included) keeps finishing, giving up only after `WARM_STALL` (4 s) with none finishing (the never-reporting driver) or `WARM_MAX` (60 s); bootstrap.js READY_WAIT 65 s. Also the fence pacing (renderer.js gpuBusy) never holds a frame longer than two display frames (`view.frameInterval`, the rAF interval's average, set in main.js), so a late fence can't cap a fast screen at 1/FENCE_PATIENCE.

### Match rules: rounds, forfeit, ready, 4V4 (competitive overhaul, 2026-09-29)
Task D. Owner: "3 rounds, whoever has most wins"; "ffa should have same respawn system" as 1V1; 4V4 "two teams of four". Spec: COMPETITIVE_SPEC.md (Modes, Rounds, Death and respawn, End of match).
- **Rounds** (`config/match.js` `roundsDecided(wins, played, total)`, used by arena.js and duel.js `DuelScore`): `SETTINGS.rounds` 3/5/10/∞ (0), default 5, for `ROUNDED` (every counted mode but FFA). The match is over when the leader leads the next best side by more than the rounds left, or all rounds are played and someone leads; a tie after the last round plays on (sudden death, a round at a time); 0 never ends. A draw round (both sides out at once) is `played` with no point. Round modes have **no match clock** (`clock` Infinity; `matchState` `timed: false`, `left` 0) and ignore the kill limit. arena.js `stepElimination` counts `played`, sets `decided`; the deciding point still gets its break (`DUEL_BREAK` 5.6 s in 1V1, `ROUND_BREAK` 5 s otherwise), `newPoint` is not called, then `matchOver` puts the results up. `matchState` adds `rounds`, `played`, `forfeit` (`{ side: [ids] }`).
- **FFA** keeps its clock (`roundLength` 5 or 10 min, default 10; most kills wins; `killLimit` is a dev setting, default 0 = off) and now respawns after **6 s** (`respawn` default 6, dev setting).
- **Forfeit** (`arena.forfeit(id, on)`, `checkForfeit(side)`, `forfeitVotes()`): round modes only, while playing and not `decided`. 1V1: immediate. Team modes: a vote, every human on the side must have voted (robots and benched seats do not vote); `on: false` takes the vote back; a teammate leaving re-checks the vote. The side goes into `forfeited`, `decided`, a `forfeit` event goes out, and the results put it last (`results.forfeit` = side id), whatever its points. Ignored in FFA/practice, after `decided`, and outside `playing`.
- **Ready** (`arena.setReady(id, on)`, results phase only): the results card lasts until every human seat is ready (robots never need to be), then `startRound(this.mode)` (same mode and settings, `matchNumber` +1, scores reset), or `READY_WAIT` (90 s) then the lobby. `matchState` carries `ready` (ids) in the results phase. A start that cannot happen (a seat left with robots off) goes to the lobby.
- **4V4**: MODES 4v4 (teams 2, per 4, size 8, fillTo 8), `MAX_SEATS` 8 (`addRobot`, robot fill), `NETWORK.maxPlayers` 8, eight `PLAYER_COLOURS` (7: slate blue, 8: sand coat with brown trim; none near the side colours). A joiner takes a robot's seat and side, the ninth sits on the bench. SOLO 4V4 (duel.js `DUEL_MODES` 4v4) needs bot-match.js `MAX_ROBOTS` of at least 7.
- **Protocol 20**: `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 20; joiners send `{t:'forfeit', on}` and `{t:'ready', on}` (`readMessage`: `on` defaults true, only `false` unticks; host-session.js routes them, client-session.js `forfeit`/`setReady`); `forfeit` event; match state fields above.
- **Tests:** tests/rounds.test.js (roundsDecided table, arena rounds, clocks, FFA respawn), tests/forfeit-ready.test.js (forfeit, ready, 4V4, protocol, colours), tests/duel-rounds.test.js (`DuelScore`, URL), helper tests/rules-harness.js (scripted arena).
### Stats panel (competitive overhaul, 2026-09-29)
- **`src/ui/stats-panel.js`** (owner: "a newly designed stat menu that is vertical ... same data as when you press tab", "color coded by team, but ... most kills to least", FFA "first and second place ... with just a background color"). `createStatsPanel(parent)` -> `{ root, open, show(opts), update(opts), hide() }`, opts `{ rows, myId, mode, sides, final, flip, place, round }` (`round` is an addition: the title's "round n"). Pure helpers, no DOM at import: `sortStatsRows(rows)` (kills, fewer deaths, dealt, name) and `statsTableHTML(rows, { myId, mode, final })` (the bare `ol.stats-list`, for the end-of-match card; ranks its rows itself). Team rows: `--team` stripe and `--tint` (teamById colour + `26` alpha; SOLO uses 'blue' yours, 'red' theirs). FFA `final` (mode 'ffa', or no mode and no row with a team): row 1 `.stats-gold` (#e6cf7a), row 2 `.stats-silver` (#c9cfd3), dark text, nothing else marks them. `sides` (elimination points, 1V1 the two players) draw a score line in the team colours; `flip { side, from, to }` rolls that number (CSS `.stats-roll`, like score-flash.js) and is kept while the side's points equal `to`. `update` redraws only what changed (list scroll kept, a roll is not restarted by an identical update); `show` always replays. `place: 'left'` sits at 30% (the death card is on the right), the top half on narrow screens. CSS is the last block of menu-theme.css (phones: 390x664 shows 8 rows; 844x390 a wider card, one line a row).
- **multiplayer-hud.js**: the Tab board is this panel (id `scoreboard`); `showBoard/hideBoard/setBoard/boardOpen` as before; `setMatch` keeps the mode, round (elimination) and sides (`match.sides`), and a side whose points went up while the board is open rolls (`flip`); `final` while the results phase shows. `scoreboardRows` stays exported (old table markup, unused by the HUD now).
- **SOLO stats** (bots/bot-match.js): `bot.stats`, `youStats` `{ kills, deaths, dealt, taken }`. Damage is counted where it is passed on (`deal`, capped at the health the robot had; your hurt in `step`), a death once when a robot's `alive` goes false (`fell`) or yours (`main.player.dead`, once, `youDown`), the kill to whoever last hurt the victim within `STAT_CREDIT` (10 s) and against its side (no credit for friends or old hits). `resetStats()` (also in `clear()`), `statsRows(main, youName = 'YOU')` returns panel rows (you 'you'; team null in an all-FFA game, else 'blue' for you and each robot's own). Nothing in the tick allocates for it. Tests: tests/stats-panel.test.js, tests/solo-stats.test.js.
### Task A: menus (competitive overhaul, 2026-09-29)
- **Gamemodes** (owner: "join at the top, then host, then below host there is bots, and then below bots there is tutorial and practice ... same format, same design"): index.html `data-page="modes"` holds `#join-mode` JOIN, `#online-host` HOST, `#duel-mode` BOTS, `#tutorial-mode`, `#practice-mode`; SOLO and MULTIPLAYER are gone. Pages: `join` (was `online`; heading "join", no HOST button), `host-setup` ("host a game"), `duel` (heading "bots"). Back: join / host-setup / duel / maps go to gamemodes, gamemodes home (menu.js `back`). `?host=1` without autohost opens host a game.
- **Rows** everywhere (host a game, BOTS, lobby, pause LOBBY): lowercase label over a full-width bar of fitted `choice-button`s. Mode order everywhere is lobby-settings.js `MODE_ORDER` (1V1 2V2 3V3 4V4 2V2V2 FFA PRACTICE); seven modes wrap to two rows (4 over 3) in the lobby always and on host a game on phones (menu-theme.css, Task A block).
- **Plain vs developer rows** (lobby-settings.js): `settingsRowsHTML()` puts `PLAIN_SETTINGS` first and every `dev: true` row (robots box included) in `<details class="dev-only round-dev">` "developer". `.dev-only` shows only with `body.dev-unlocked`, which dev-wiring.js `showDevEntry` toggles on unlock/lock. A row not in the chosen mode is hidden (not dimmed); `render()` returns how many plain rows show, and the lobby screen / lobby page hide their "settings" heading when that is 0 (practice). `rowsFor(mode, {dev})` lists them. CREATE GAME (and autohost without a carried room) sends `withDevDefaults(settings)`: locked, every developer row goes at its default whatever an unlocked visit left in `deadstab-host-settings`.
- **BOTS** (duel-menu.js): `duelOptionsHTML` = MODE (4V4 note added), MAP, YOUR WEAPON, ROUNDS (`firstTo` holds 3/5/10/0), DIFFICULTY (`skill`), then the developer group: enemy weapon, your robots' weapon and difficulty, spawns, friendly fire (the last four team modes only). START sends `lockedPicks(picks, unlocked)`: locked, `DUEL_DEV_KEYS` play at `DUEL_DEFAULTS`; the picks' shape (duelParam) is unchanged.
- **Endless ∞**: the fitted lettering stretched the ∞ glyph into an "00", so `choiceLabel('∞')` draws `INFINITY_MARK` (the ammo readout's faceted mark, stroked in the button's ink, `height:calc(100% - 8px)`), and the button is `aria-label="endless"`.
- **Keyboard**: menu-navigation.js lets ArrowUp/ArrowDown leave a text box (the host page's username sits above the rows); letters, left/right and E/Q still type.
- Tests: tests/menu-flow.test.js (page order, join/host markup, host rows by mode, developer group and CSS, BOTS rows, ∞); tests/net.test.js's gamemodes check now looks for JOIN and HOST.
### Task B: death flow and match end (competitive overhaul, 2026-09-29)
Owner: "After any kill in 1v1, it should show the aftermath for a solid 3 seconds, then the rest should happen, a 2-3 second death screen for the player ... and then they respawn, and same for the winning player"; team modes "wait for your team to die or win ... spectate ... the death screen should be off to the side and should hold the buttons"; "after a match ends there should be a play again option ... ready up ... For bots ... a button saying start ... a way to quickly enter a change settings menu".
- **1V1 (online and SOLO)**: both see 3 s of aftermath (`AFTERMATH`, duel.js). The one who fell: the zoom onto their body without the slide (`view.deathAside` false) and the death card popping up in place at `DEATH_MENU_DELAY` (2.6 s): FORFEIT (SOLO `duel.forfeit()`, online `online.forfeit(true)`), QUIT. The one who took the round: hands off (main.js `startAftermath`: `running` false, SOLO stepped like `soloWatch`, `body.aftermath` hides the aim marks and touch controls, pause is held), the camera eases onto the body and zooms in (renderer.js `view.aftermath` { x, z, start }, `death-view.js killCamFrame`, nothing allocated); at 3 s the stats panel opens by itself in the middle with their number flipping old → new (`openStats('aftermath', { flip })`). Both are back together `DUEL_BREAK_1V1` / arena `DUEL_BREAK` (5.6 s) after the kill. The top score keeps the old number through the break and turns over as both come back (duel.js `held`/`roll`, `before`; online main.js `syncClockRoll` on `#match-clock [data-side] b`; `rollHTML` in score-flash.js, `.score-roll`). No centre score flash in 1V1 (score-flash stays for team modes). The last kill of a SOLO 1V1 plays its 3 s, then the end card (`finalBreak`); a decided SOLO match counts nothing more (it used to score its last point again and bring everyone back under the card).
- **Team modes**: the death card on the right with VIEW STATS (the stats panel on the left, refreshed every 0.25 s; again closes it), FORFEIT (online a vote: `online.forfeit(!myForfeitVote())`, the count from `matchState().forfeit` and your side's people in `online.lobby()`; SOLO at once), QUIT; spectating teammates only as before (`deathScreen.mode === 'team'`).
- **FFA**: the countdown and QUIT only. Practice (solo and online) unchanged.
- **The end card** (`ui/match-end.js`, one design): a lowercase title (`onlineOutcome` / `soloOutcome`: "you win", "your team wins", "amber team wins", "draw"; the score and "... forfeited" under it), the final stats table (`statsTableHTML`) and the buttons. Online: READY n/m (`online.setReady`, pressed while yours is in; robots never hold it up), the host's LOBBY (`online.endRound()`), LEAVE; it shows while the host's phase is 'results' and goes when it moves on (`syncMatchEnd`). SOLO: START (new match, same settings: the old rematch), CHANGE SETTINGS (back to the bots page, picks kept: `menuFlow.showPage('duel')` when menu.js has it, else a click on `#duel-mode`), QUIT. duel.js no longer has a card: `hooks.over(outcome)`, `resultOpen` = ended. The old `#match-results` (multiplayer-hud.js) is hidden in CSS; its code and the `.duel-results` rules can go.
- **"Sometimes the death menu doesn't show"**: (1) a finished SOLO match left `duel.over` true after MAIN MENU (duel.stop kept the score and reset() is a no-op once stopped), which held practice's death card and its respawn for good: `over` needs a match on and `stop()` clears the score; (2) online, a death whose playerDeath event was not seen here left you down with no card: multiplayerFrame begins the death when the host says you are down (`me.present && me.dead`); (3) the card's CHANGE WEAPON line un-hid it in every mode (online FFA too); (4) on a phone on its side the practice card ran off the screen (shorter buttons there).
- **Respawn without the stutter**: `online.frame` (which calls `reviveOnline` on a new life) runs before the frame is drawn, so a respawn the host just made (yours, or the survivor moved at a new round, who was drawn one frame between the old and new spot with the camera gliding) is cut to at once; a camera cut redraws the shadows the same frame (renderer.js); other players' avatars snap their height on a new life or a jump of more than `RESPAWN_JUMP` (3 m) in a frame (remote-players.js). Still needed in net/ (coordinator): client-session.js `others()` should not blend across a life change (use the newer snapshot when `b.life !== a.life`) and pass `life`; host-session.js should blend from `previous` only within one life.
- Tests: tests/death-flow.test.js, tests/match-end.test.js.

### Competitive overhaul: coordinator notes (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
- The owner's running spec for this work is the claude.ai project doc `claude/deadshift-competitive-spec.md` (decisions, proposals not yet confirmed, open questions). The four sections above (rules, stats panel, menus, death flow) were built in parallel and merged; this is what ties them together.
- **PLAY** (owner: "turn the game modes button [to] just PLAY and make it bright white ... the only thing that's layered above the blood even though it's on the button"): `#gamemodes` reads PLAY and has `.title-play` (white #f7f6f2, dark lettering; hover/focus #e6e3dc). Its `.button-label` has z-index 2 over the blood svg's 1 in the same stacking context (the button itself must keep no transform, z-index, filter or opacity, or the label is trapped under the blood). The blood still pools and runs on the button.
- **No glide on a respawn:** `blend()` (net/host-session.js, used by the host and joiners to draw others) draws a body at its new place at once when it moved further than `BLEND_JUMP` (4 m) between the two states (a respawn, a new round's spot).
- **SOLO Tab** opens the same stats panel (main.js `openStats('tab')`, BotMatch `statsRows`); `bots.resetStats()` on a restart/START; `MAX_ROBOTS` 8 (SOLO 4V4: three allies and four enemies).
- The old multiplayer results card is gone from multiplayer-hud.js (ui/match-end.js replaces it).
- Not built yet (owner's order): the storm (the owner designs it), the 1V1 duel circle, floor loot and the knife, Stab Royale and the fourth team colour (green), moving KEYBOARD|MOBILE off the title.
- **Readable fitted lettering** (owner, 2026-09-29: "see how these are so stretched in they can't be read? make it easier to read. do this everywhere"): button-typography.js never squeezes a label narrower than `MIN_LETTER_RATIO` (.3) of its height (horizontal over vertical scale; the usual buttons are about .44); a label that would need more is drawn shorter, centred in its button (`readableScale`). Narrow segments keep more of their width for the label (side room 14% of the button, 6-20 px, instead of a flat 20 px). The seven-mode bar on host a game is two rows everywhere now (it was only on phones), as in the lobby.
- **Scoreboard only when asked for; bigger** (owner, 2026-09-29: "make scoreboard bigger and easier to read, and dont show it for a player after winning, only show it if a player hits tab, or whenever a player is in the death screen waiting if they press tab it will show ... include the top down fitted text where it makes sense"): the stats panel no longer opens by itself for a 1V1 round's winner (main.js `stepAftermath` is empty); the winner's number turns over on the top score at once (duel.js: `roll` set when you take the point; online `syncClockRoll`: `now` when you are `roundWinner`), the one who fell sees theirs turn over as both come back. Tab (held; keyboard) shows it online and against robots, alive or down (beside the death card then: `place` 'left' while the card is up); touch has the SCORES button (now also against robots) and, while down, VIEW STATS (hidden for keyboard players; their card says "hold TAB for the scoreboard"). The panel is 520 px wide, rows bigger, and the title, round score, ranks, names and kill counts use the stretched display lettering (`.stats-fit`: the text scaled .86 wide by --sy tall in a box sized for it, names ellipsised); the stats line stays plain. The end card lists rows on one line so a full 4V4 fits.
- **Bots' end card: PLAY** (owner: "when game ends in bots stuff remake start to be play"): START reads PLAY (same action: a new match, same settings).
- **No friendly fire** (owner, 2026-09-29: "make friendly fire not count anymore at all"): teammates never hurt each other, online or against robots. The `friendlyFire` setting is gone (config/match.js SETTINGS; an old saved value is dropped by cleanSettings), arena.js `before()` puts only the other sides in a seat's targets, duel.js sets `bots.friendlyFire` 0 always (the SOLO address keeps the old field for old links, ignored), the bots page's developer row is gone. `FRIENDLY_SHARE` and the `friendly` proxy code stay unused.
- **End card never clips a row** (owner, 2026-09-29: "when enough damage numbers end up on here things start getting cutoff"): the card is 760 px wide where the screen allows (800 px wide and 521 px tall or more), a row's name takes 36% and its stats wrap onto a second line when they don't fit (never clipped).
- **SKINS on the title** (owner, 2026-09-29: "add a button that functions the same way the tutorial button does with the blood also ... move the buttons down to make room not up ... should say SKINS"; "just a regular ui with a back arrow that takes back to main page, it should be prepped as the tutorial weapons page is for future"): `#title-skins` after TUTORIAL, the same button (title-screen.js's blood finds it like the others). The home page is centred, so it is let down by half a button (`top: calc(var(--title-slot) / 2)`, 52 px / 42 px on short screens; not while menu-fit scales it) and the word "deadstab" stays exactly where it was (checked: 1280x720 and 390x664 unchanged; 844x390 is scaled to fit). Its page `data-page="skins"`: back arrow (to the title), heading "skins", and `#skin-options`, the weapons page's scrolling card grid (same widths and phone layouts), empty for now: a skin will be a `.weapon-card` like the weapons'.
- **Nothing from a game over the menus** (owner, 2026-09-29: "when going into a game and round one and stuff come up and then leaving to main menu right after, the round one and other ui elements still show briefly"): `returnToMenu` takes down the round pop-up, the score flash, spectating and the damage/kill pop-ups at once, and CSS hides `.round-popup`, `.score-flash` and `.outgoing-kill` whenever the body is not `.playing` (the round pop-up sat at z-index 43, over the menu's 20).

### The 1V1 duel circle (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
The owner's brief: "for 1v1 mode, there should be a second boundary that is like a circle that spawns in one of any part of each map. The size of this circle should be about 1/4-1/5 the size of the actual map. The edges should turn red when you get close to them, and it should be like a see through foggy vertical reddish at bottom fog wall. The parts closer to players, like the south side of the circle should work on the ground but fog shouldn't interfere with view and camera. This should be a forced setting, and also, it shouldn't ever be cut off by map border, it should always have the full circle. It's fine whatever's in it. Regular maps should keep their zones/storms but this 1v1 map shouldn't have a storm"; "the near side of the circle should still have the fog effect and reddish mist at the bottom and a bit up as well"; "the circle size should be a close range duel box"; SOLO 1V1 against a robot gets it too.
- **Rules** (`src/duel-circle.js`, pure): radius `duelCircleRadius(map)` = 22% of the map's mean side across, 14-30 m (Deadwater 26 m, Hollow Wick about 14.6 m). `pickDuelCircle(map, colliders, { random, skip, avoid })`: a centre anywhere with the whole circle `DUEL_CIRCLE.border` (1.5 m) inside the map's edge, the centre playable, at least 72% of 36 sample points inside it playable, and two spots found by `duelSpawns`: opposite each other through the centre, 55-80% of the radius out, both `openAt` (net/spawn-points.js: the same tests as `openSpot`: playable, clear of colliders, not in a tiny shell, the hills' spawn rules) and not refused by `skip`; not where the last round's was (`avoid`) if another place will do. A boundary, not a damage zone: `confineToCircle` keeps the body's whole radius inside and takes away its speed outward; `Simulation.movePlayer` calls it every sub-step while `sim.boundary` is set (null: nothing changes; goldens unchanged). Shots, blasts and everything else pass (it is mist). (Whether it should be a damage zone instead was asked and not answered; a wall was built.)
- **Online** (net/arena.js): 1V1 only. `startRound` and `newPoint` pick a new circle (`newDuelCircle`, the host's random, spots clear of the weapon pick's view), `handWorld` hands it to every seat's sim as `boundary`, `spawn` puts the first non-bench seat on spot 0 and the other on spot 1, `endRound` clears it; `matchState().circle` = `circleState` ({x, z, r} in cm) while playing. Joiners set it on their own sim and the replay sim (client-session.js, from each snapshot's match), so their prediction stops at the edge too. PROTOCOL_VERSION 21.
- **SOLO** (duel.js `placeDuel()`, `circle`): in a 1V1, main.js calls it at the start, every new round (instead of the random spawns) and on a restart: a new circle, you on spot 0 facing the robot, the robot on spot 1 (`BotMatch.putAt`), both sims confined; `stop()` clears it. Other modes: returns false and clears.
- **Drawn** (`src/render/duel-circle-view.js`, `view.setDuelCircle(c)` from main.js every frame, cheap; the view is made in the WorldView constructor, hidden, so its one program is compiled with the warm-up): one mesh and one ShaderMaterial for a 6 m wall of drifting mist round the circle plus a 1.4 m band on the ground inside the edge; vertices rewritten only for a new circle, following the ground (`view.gy`). Red (#a3121f) at the foot and a way up, a pale haze (#cbb2ad) above with slow puffs; the camera's side (south, +z) is thinner and lower (`near`); within `warnFrom` (7 m) of you the edge turns a brighter red (#ff2638) and thicker. Transparent, no depth write, no shadows, no team colours. The M map shows the ring and dims the outside (`duelCircleSVG`, overhead-hills.js, used by both map drawings; empty otherwise, so the maps' hashes are unchanged).
- Robots keep their goals and routes inside the circle (see the follow-ups below).
- Tests: tests/duel-circle.test.js.
- **Duel circle, second pass** (owner, 2026-09-29: "slightly less reddish ... a little bit less opaque ... when a player goes up near them ... they should get more solid ... and a little bit more red where they land ... make the circle a little bit bigger ... it should never be able to go outside of the map border ... the fence should never be within [it]"): radius 26% of the map across (16-34 m: Deadwater 30.7 m, Hollow Wick 17.3 m). The whole circle must lie inside the map's playable outline, `border` (1.5 m) clear of it, where the fences stand (`circleFits`: 64 points round the edge, and rings inside for an outline's notch); if the full size fits nowhere it tries 8% smaller, down to three quarters of minR. The look: foot #9c3a42 (less red), mist #cdbfbb, thinner (far side body .42, near side .14); within 7 m of you the wall firms up (up to about +.55 alpha) and turns red (#e8263a), most at the foot; the ground band fainter until you are close.

### Competitive overhaul follow-ups (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
- **Robots respect the duel circle** (bots/robot-brain.js `inside(spot)`, `ROBOT_EDGE` 1.6 m): with `sim.boundary` set, a goal (before `watchGoal`) and every waypoint of a planned route are pulled in along the line to the centre, a body and a step inside the edge, keeping their flags. Without a circle it returns the same object (nothing changes in other modes). Measured: 0 of 7200 ticks pressed against the wall (5429 before).
- **Room to fight** (duel-circle.js `openShare(map, colliders, x, z, r)`, `DUEL_CIRCLE.open` .6, `grid` 2 m): a picked circle must have at least 60% of its floor free for a body (playable, not in a wall, a solid tower, a crate: spawn-points.js `blocked`, now exported). Failing that in every try, the most open one found (a repeat of the last circle counts .5 less). Before this, circles on Hollow Wick and Lumen could be mostly buildings (min 14-17% open); now min about 60% on every map, 1-4 ms a pick.
- **Warm-up: robot avatars** (render/warm-up.js): stand-in RemotePlayers for slots 1 and `ROBOT_SLOT + 1` are in the warm rack, so a robot's first appearance links nothing. A stray physical program ('scope-world-gray', scenery batch meshes) seen now and then in SOLO duel runs also appears on the base build: an adaptive-quality recompile, not new; `tools/program-check.mjs` shows 0 on Extreme, Performance and Potato, including a SOLO 4V4 (its new 5th argument: extra query string).
- **4V4 robots cost** (node bench, 7 robots, you ghosted): about 0.25 ms a tick on average once warm, p99 3-4 ms; the rare 6-10 ms ticks are a single route or cover search (`plan`, `findCover`, already under `nav.budget`) or GC, and budget stacking is rare (13 of 7200 ticks). In the browser 4V4 on Performance/Potato stays under a 16.7 ms frame at p50 (SwiftShader numbers are noisy). No change made. `tools/perf.mjs --extra '&mode=duel&duel=4v4~...'` measures a SOLO match.
- **Lumen merge check**: the Lumen WIP patch (claude/lumen-wip.patch) applies on this work with two conflicts, both old menu code it carried as context: `src/ui/menu.js` (the back mapping: keep ours, join/host/bots/maps go to gamemodes) and `src/styles/menu-theme.css` (host-setup and landscape rules: keep ours; nothing of Lumen's is lost). Merged: 1455 tests pass. On Lumen the circle is 16.6 m, fits its outline, both spots open and never in a room; SOLO 1V1 plays to the end card with no errors and no programs linked in play. Lumen's `sameRoomGroup` changes in simulation.js, robot-brain.js and main.js touch lines next to ours but do not conflict.


### Owner's list, 2026-09-29 (competitive overhaul, v0.1.0-alpha)
- **Weapons under maintenance** (`src/weapon-maintenance.js`; owner: "disable the sniper weapon by putting a maintenance sticker over it in all spots, make a disable maintenance option in dev tools"): `MAINTENANCE` ['sightline']. Such a weapon keeps its place in every weapon menu with a paper sticker over its greyed picture (`stickerHTML()`, `.maintenance-tile`, `data-maintenance="<id>"`): the weapons page (practice and tutorial, menu.js), the in-game pick (weapon-pick.js: CHANGE WEAPON and the online pick) and every `weaponGridHTML` grid (BOTS and host dev rows, the dev window). main.js refuses a click/Enter on a `[data-maintenance]` element in the capture phase and shakes its sticker. Nobody gets it: `?weapon=`, CHANGE WEAPON and the death pick go through `playableOr`; random picks through `randomPlayableWeapon` (duel.js, duel-menu.js START, bot-match.js `spawn` even when named, arena.js robots, pick timeouts and `newPoint`); the host's `choose()` turns it into a random one. Developer tools: **Lift weapon maintenance** (`dev.maintenanceOff`, not in the P set) → dev-wiring.js `devChanged` → `setMaintenanceLifted`, which also sets `body.maintenance-lifted` (stickers hidden, pictures back). Online the host's switch decides. A new weapon to shelve is one id in `MAINTENANCE`. Tests: tests/weapon-maintenance.test.js.
- **The deciding point shows** (owner: "if playing to 10, the game stops at 9 in the scoreboard and ui points because it doesn't count the last point won"): SOLO 1V1 held the old top score for the break, and the deciding point has no break, so the final number and its segment never turned over. duel.js now rolls the deciding point at once for whichever side took it (team modes too); online, main.js `syncClockRoll` turns it over at once for both players when `roundsDecided` says the match is over. Test: tests/duel-rounds.test.js.
- **Death card's first beat** (owner: "the initial dead screen should show only the dead word"): `DEATH_WORD_ONLY` 1 s (death-screen.js) with `.word-only` on #death-screen: the blood and DEAD only (the actions keep their space, `visibility:hidden`, so DEAD doesn't move); then who killed you, the buttons, the countdown and the hint fade in and the first button takes focus.
- **"hold tab for the scoreboard"** moved in from the splat's ragged left edge (`margin-left:16%`, 15 px, just under the buttons) so it sits on solid red.
- **Nominal: 20 rounds** (was 28). All five golden-flat replays re-recorded for it (every sim's state carries the magazine; with 28 the old hashes still match); tests/solo-stats.test.js reloads now.
- Decided, not built: F always swaps between the floor weapon and the knife; no revives (Stab Royale duos); the SKINS page stays empty for now.
- **First-person view merged** (from the owner's other chat, project doc `claude/fps-dev.patch`; its note is in the Developer tools section). `git apply --3way` onto this work: one conflict, the known-toggles list in tests/dev-window.test.js (keep both `maintenanceOff` and `fps`). One change on merge: renderer.js `fpsView` also waits for `!this.aftermath`, so 1V1's killer aftermath (the camera on the body) stays overhead. The duel circle reads from eye height as a see-through mist band; its "thin on the side toward the camera" rule is the overhead camera's (south), so from the eye the south arc is lower and fainter. Checked: 1226 tests, smoke clean on every preset (a fresh dev server: a long-running one gave a stale "Multiple instances of Three.js" warning), program-check 0 on Extreme, Potato and a Performance SOLO 1V1; headless SOLO 1V1 in first person: W walks along the look, the aftermath goes overhead and back, off restores fov 40 / near 2, no console errors.

### 100 health: every health and damage number a fifth (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
- Owner: "scale all damage taken and given down to 100 health per player. Adjust every single weapons attacks and stuff to this new number proportionally so it's just the numbers that change". A player has **100** health (500 before). Every damage, heal, drain, health pool and health threshold is **exactly a fifth** of what it was: practice target 50 / dummy 60, tutorial targets 20 / 15 (per-weapon tutorial health 80 / 160 / 220), breakables 1 (Hollow Wick's 1 / 1.6 / .6 / 2.4), crop fire 5/s, the host's health setting 50 / 100 / 150, every weapon number in config/gameplay.js, the robots' hurt/dodge thresholds, the dev health lists (max health 100/200/400/20, robot 100/20/50/200, target 20/50/100/200, "Take 10 damage").
- **Numbers older notes in this file give are at the 500 scale** unless they say otherwise: divide any health or damage figure above this section by 5.
- What used to round to whole points rounds to fifths now: `HP_STEP` (.2), `hpRound(x)` (= Math.round(x*5+1e-7)/5; the nudge keeps an old n.5 rounding up), `hpRoll(base, spread, random)` (± spread in fifths, same count of equally likely values as the old whole-point roll, same random draws). "At least 1" / "minus 1" health became HP_STEP (dodge damage, blast minimum, Ichor's Frenzy/wave floor, syphon). boostedHexDamage rounds to 1/500. The wire sends hp to 1/50 (was 1/10 of the old unit).
- Proof: a replay harness (`/tmp/claude-0/scale/trace.mjs` in that session; all eight weapons solo against targets and props, three robot battles incl. Hollow Wick, a hosted match) ran on the old and the new code: every position, tick, mode and death identical and every health/damage figure the old one / 5 within 1e-6. Golden replays re-recorded for it (tests/golden-flat.test.js header).
- Display: damage numbers stay whole points of the new scale (a Nominal hit reads −4, carrying the .4); hits under one point add up on the burn beat. Health readout rounds up.
- **Merging Lumen**: its props (`src/world/lumen-props.js` and any city prop with a `health`), and any damage it adds (rain? barricades?), must be divided by 5 too; its tests likewise.

### Robots: aim rebalance and their full kits (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
- Owner: "robots aim is too good, especially in normal it should be more balanced. dont make it easy but make it miss a bit more, especially with melee and ranged weapons. weapons like omen shouldnt be hitting so regularly because its hard to hit with as a real person too. make easy extra easy and make hard actually hard, make normal balanced and fun." bots/robot-profile.js SKILLS retuned (rookie < easy < normal < hard < expert still holds: tests/duel.test.js): easy's hands much looser (aim 4.6, shake 2.7, miss .62, slower reaction), normal looser than v146 (aim 2.3, shake 1.15, miss .36), hard sharper (aim .5, reaction .1-.17, turn 17, dodge .8, tech 1). New profile numbers: `whiff` (melee misjudgement: easy .6, normal .45, hard .05), `rest` (trigger rests: easy .6 s, normal .2 s after every ~1.6 s of firing; not Static), `feel` (how much a hard-to-aim weapon throws it: 1.15 easy, 1 normal, .45 hard). `whiff` is drawn last in makeProfile so every other rolled number is unchanged for a seed.
- robot-brain.js: `WEAPON_AIM` (omen 1.7, sightline 1.35, sidekick 1.05, rifle/shotgun/melee 1, static .8: Static's volleys land where thrown, so aim error costs it most) scales aim error, wobble and pulled shots through `hand()`. `meleeSwing()` (Ichor, Sheath): each swing rolls once; a misjudged one starts `MELEE_WHIFF.early` m out of reach or is cut `wide` of the target, then `recover` s before the next.
- Measured (tools-like bench: a robot against a weaving, dodging person, 20 seeds × 45 s, hits per attack / damage per second): normal Nominal 46% → 39%, Omen 42% → 31% (−30% dps), Ichor 81% → 65%, Sheath 97% → 74%, Sidekick 51% → 40%; easy about a third to two thirds less damage per second on every weapon; hard Nominal/Static/Ballast +20-37% dps, melee about the same, Omen −17% (it stays hard to land).
- Robots use each weapon's whole current kit (checked per weapon: main fire, reloads, E and X for all): new, **Ichor's guard** (`ichorGuard()`: held while someone shoots it with a gun from beyond its reach and it closes in; lowered in reach, when it breaks or after 1-2.5 s; readiness from `tech`) and **Static's quick shot** (one orb at once to finish someone at 4 health or less, `tech` ≥ .4).
- tests/robots.test.js: "a robot left to itself ... fights" runs 30 s (a normal robot waits its patience out). Golden robots/hosted re-recorded.

### Ballast's Scatter timer at the cursor (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
- Owner: "for the ballast's X ability charge thing, add the time indicator to the cursor too like how other weapons have it ... the 3 secs to load after initial x press". ui/aim-overlay.js: a third `.omen-cursor-timer` shows from the first X until Scatter is primed (`scatterPrimeLeft`, SCATTER.prime): the ring filling, the seconds left and X, in the charging colour; gone once primed.

### Shadow flicker on the hills (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
Owner: "in the maps, especially in hollow wick i keep getting these shadow flickerings across places" (while walking; building/ground; every preset, Extreme included). Cause: on terrain maps the sun's shadow box (shadow-snap.js settleShadowBox) changed size or depth every ~10 m of walking on Hollow Wick, since the view's heights change with each step. Every change moved every texel by a fraction, so every shadow edge on screen jumped at once. Below Extreme it also forced the kept map (shadow-cache.js) to redraw at a new texel size.

Fix:
- A terrain map's outdoor camera now uses one box size and depth: `shadowReach` + `shadowSpan`, measured once per screen shape over the map's playable ground every 4 m (about 10 ms).
- Texels are whole 4096ths of a metre, so the arithmetic is exact.
- `placeShadowBox` moves the box only in whole texels, so the grid stays fixed to the world.
- Rooms, the scope, the pick view and flat maps keep the settled box, as before.

Cost: texels about 10% bigger than a level stretch's box on Hollow Wick at 16:9. Headless, walking 40 m on Hollow Wick: 0 box changes on Extreme and Balanced (4 before on Extreme). Test: tests/shadow-coverage.test.js ("one box size for the whole map").

### BOTS FFA and syphon amounts (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
Owner: "ffa should be a mode in bots menu too btw, and it should have siphon it should have 50 siphon off each kill". Clarified: "ffa only, other gamemodes with siphon should do 25 siphon".

- **Syphon:** `config/match.js` defines `SYPHON` and `syphonAmount(mode, hp, maxHp)`. A kill gives its killer a flat 50 health in FFA and 25 in the other modes, capped at full health, floored to HP_STEP. This replaces "half the lost health". The online host uses it (net/arena.js syphon, which also clamps to maxHp), with the setting still on by default in every counted mode. The hosted golden was re-recorded for it (tests/golden-flat.test.js note).
- **BOTS FFA:** `DUEL_MODES.ffa` in duel.js has 5 robots on team 'ffa' (bot-match.js `hostile`), so everyone fights everyone. It has a MATCH LENGTH row (5/10 MIN, `length` at the end of the URL's `duel=` param; the ROUNDS row is hidden) and the most kills at the end wins. A tie at the top is a draw (match-end.js soloOutcome `ffa`).
  - Respawns after FFA_RESPAWN (6 s), as online: you through main.js' practice respawn with the 'ffa' death card; robots through `bots.respawnWait`.
  - Respawns land inside the storm once it has started closing: `storm.js` `stormSafeSpot`/`stormSafe`, also used by the online arena now. Robots go through `bots.respawnSpot`; for you, `duel.safeSpawn` is called in `randomPracticeSpawn`.
  - Syphon in SOLO runs through the new `bots.onKill` hook, called from `BotMatch.credit`. Robots get their 50 too; only yours shows its +50.
  - The FFA storm plan is kind 'ffa' over the match length. The storm HUD's final-zone countdown reads `duel.left`.
  - `eliminationNow()` is false for SOLO FFA, so there are no round pop-ups and no spectating.

Tests: tests/duel.test.js ("BOTS FFA"), tests/net.test.js (syphon), tests/menu-flow.test.js.

### Hollow Wick's goat can be killed (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
Owner: "give the goat that lives in hollow wick gore and make its head drop in the pile of gore when its killed. it can be shot or killed with blades that pass through the fence".

- **Pure mind:** `world/goat-mind.js` now holds GoatMind, GOAT and PEN_INSIDE, moved out of hollow-life.js, which re-exports them.
- **Critters:** `src/critters.js` `Critters` holds the world's animals, one goat per `goatPen` prop, 30 health.
  - Whoever holds the world steps the minds: SOLO the game's own sim (main.js sets `sim.critters`; `Simulation.step` ticks it when `worldAuthority`), online the host's arena (`endTick`).
  - `Simulation.step` (the old body is now `stepSelf`) adds each living goat as a target for the length of a step: `kind: 'goat'`, `critter: true`, the same proxy object in every sim that shares the critters. So every weapon that hurts a target hurts it.
  - Critters are never an aim-assist or lock target (`assistTargets`), and a goat kill counts no kill (`hit`).
  - Dead stays dead until `resetWorld`/`reset` (SOLO) or the arena's `resetWorld`.
  - Robots' sims don't carry it, so robots never shoot it.
- **The pen:** `LIFE_TYPES.goatPen` is `screen: true`, so its hurdles are body-only colliders. Rounds and blades pass over and through them (the hurdle test was updated to match).
- **Online:** arena seat sims share `arena.critters` (addSeat, handWorld; the world sim doesn't). Snapshots carry `critters: [[x, z, heading, dead]]` (PROTOCOL_VERSION 23). A joiner's own sim has none (`client-session`, `online-play` puts the page's own back on leaving). Its view places its goat from `client.critterState()`; the death arrives as the host's shared `kill` event (targetKind 'goat').
- **The view** (hollow-life.js `goat`, `goatDeath`):
  - It draws the world's mind when there is one (`view.critters`, set each frame in main.js), otherwise its own placed from `view.critterState`.
  - On death the body collapses into a heap: `effects/gore.js carcassPile` (torn hide, flesh, ribs, a leg, a soaked pool). It is one lit vertex-coloured draw in the gore material, so nothing new compiles.
  - The head (with ears and horns) is thrown up off the neck, tumbles, lands on the heap with a small bounce and lies on its side. Seen dead from the start, it is already lying there.
  - The renderer's `goatHit` adds bleeding, a blood stain and a blood-red burst. There is no kill ring.
- **Warm-up:** it now stages a blood splat (floor and wall) too. The first death of any game used to build the splat's program mid-game; found here, and it applies to player deaths as well.

Checked headless: a goat kill builds 0 programs on Performance, Balanced and Extreme; smoke is clean on every preset on Hollow Wick. Tests: tests/critters.test.js (rifle over the hurdles, Sheath through them, no lock, online shared goat, joiner state, the heap is one draw).

### The storm (2026-09-29, v0.1.0-alpha)
Owner's spec, in short:
- "the shape should close into a new circle each round. it should be constantly shrinking. it should take a total of 2 mins to get to its final zone, in which it stays in until the 2 and a half minute timer gives up. the 30 seconds should be in that final storm."
- Damage: 15 a second (his pick, lowered from 75), in quick small ticks.
- Look: "all red ... a harsh red over everything", crackling electricity creeping in, "actual detail like electricity bolts", and cheap to draw.
- Sound: static and anxious, rising with the damage taken, fading in and out.
- "the ongame indicators of sound/damage shouldnt show this damage."
- Team modes, when the round timer runs out: sudden death.
- FFA: "a storm that goes slowly until there's a minute to like 30 sec left, then it should stay there", with effects building tension.

- **Rules:** `src/storm.js`, pure.
  - A plan is a straight line from a circle round the whole map to a final circle (`pickStormFinal`: inside the playable outline, open ground). Centre and radius move together, so every circle sits inside the one before.
  - Team rounds: close 120 s, hold 30 s, then sudden death closes it to nothing in 20 s.
  - FFA: one close over the match, reaching a 12 m final zone 45 s before the end, then held.
  - Modes: every counted mode but 1V1 (`stormMode`), online and BOTS. The developer tools can turn it off: online the host's `storm` setting, SOLO "Storm off".
  - Respawns land inside the storm: `stormSafeSpot`/`stormSafe`.
- **Damage:** `Simulation.stepStorm`, 20 bites a second (`STORM.tick`). Its `playerDamage` events carry `storm: true`; the damage numbers, the hurt sound and the vibration skip them. The kill feed says "fell to the storm".
- **Online:** the host sends `storm` (the plan) and `stormT` in the match state (PROTOCOL_VERSION 22). Joiners work the circle out themselves and extrapolate up to 0.3 s (`client-session stormNow`). SOLO: duel.js `newStorm`/`setStormClock`, a new plan each round.
- **Robots:** robot-brain.js `inside()`/`within()` keep their goals and paths inside the circle (STORM_EDGE 4 m); `escapeStorm` takes over movement when they are out in it or at its edge.
- **The look:** `render/storm-view.js`, three draws, all added hidden at load so the warm-up compiles them.
  - The wash: a full-screen 2× multiply. Each pixel's view ray meets the ground 1 m up; out in the storm the pixel goes harsh red, with clouds and a burning seam along the edge.
  - The bolts: a pool of 48 jagged, branching bolts, built on the CPU only when one strikes, faded in the shader. They crawl in over the edge and crack across the storm; now and then one strikes from the sky.
  - The wall: a thin curtain with arcs at the edge.
  - No team colours anywhere. Cost on SwiftShader is about +0.7–1.1 ms a draw on Potato/Performance (the full-screen pass; trivial on a GPU).
- **HUD:** `ui/storm-hud.js`, under the health bar: STORM CLOSING / FINAL ZONE / SUDDEN DEATH with a countdown bar. `body.storm-final` / `storm-sudden` pulse the clock and breathe a red vignette, faster as tension rises (FFA's last 45 s, sudden death). The overview map (`overhead-hills.js stormSVG`) shows the red outside, the circle and the dashed final zone.
- **Sound:** audio.js `stormVoice(on, level)` is a persistent WebAudio voice: bit-crushed, band-passed noise with a square stutter, a sine whine and a sawtooth buzz. Its pitch and rate rise with the dose you've taken, and it fades in and out as you enter and leave. `pulse` is a heartbeat under tension; `zap` is the crack of a bolt striking within 16 m.
- **Lumen (city maps), when merged:** a city map opts in just by having a playable outline and open ground for a final circle (`pickStormFinal` checks `circleFits`/`openShare`; with none it falls back to the middle). Lumen's WIP (claude/lumen-wip.patch) predates the 100-health rescale: divide its props' health and any damage numbers in it by 5 when it comes in.

Tests: tests/storm.test.js. Headless checks: no damage pop-ups in the storm, sudden death shown, 0 new programs (Deadwater and Hollow Wick, desktop and phone).

## The name: deadstab (renamed from deadshift, 2026-09-30, owner)
- The game is **deadstab** (lowercase in the title lettering and tab title, Deadstab in prose). It was dustshift, then deadshift. The planned Shift Royale mode is **Stab Royale**. The GitHub repo is `kbun2846/deadstab` (Pages: kbun2846.github.io/deadstab/).
- Everything is renamed (owner: "rename everything else u can"), in two passes: the rename chat's (what people see: title and loading word, app name, README, docs) and then, at v0.1.0-alpha, everything the running game relies on:
  - every saved key is now `deadstab-...` / `deadstab....` (settings, keybinds, the BOTS page, touch layouts, tutorials, username, host picks, dev window, thumbnails, launch notes);
  - the online room prefix (`roomPrefix: 'deadstab-'`, local-link `deadstab-room-`; the protocol bump already splits old and new builds);
  - internal globals (`__deadstabFast`, `__deadstabShaderChunks`, `__deadstabMatteChunks`) and the Extreme pass name (`DeadstabGradeOutput`);
  - the tools' settings key; `vite.artifact.mjs` now finds the repo itself (no `/home/claude/deadshift` path).
- **Saves carry over:** `src/storage-migration.js` copies every `deadshift-`/`deadshift.` key to its `deadstab` name when the new one is empty (the dustshift chain first). `bootstrap.js` runs it on localStorage and sessionStorage before the game module loads, and main.js runs it again (harmless). The old keys are left in place. Tests: tests/storage-migration.test.js.
- **Kept on purpose:**
  - `DEV_CODE_SALT` (`'deadshift/dev-tools/v2'`) and the owner-verify salt, which changing would invalidate the dev code and the verification password;
  - the claude.ai project doc paths (`claude/deadshift-*.md`), which other chats (the map agents) still read;
  - names of real past files in history notes (`Deadshift-Ichor.html`, `Deadshift-weapons-r35-merge.zip`);
  - past CHANGELOG entries.
- **Merging older work** (the Lumen map patch and anything else written before this): run the rename script (project doc `claude/tools/deadstab-rename/rename_to_deadstab.py`) over the merged tree, then change any `deadshift-`/`deadshift.` saved key, `__deadshift` global or `DeadshiftGradeOutput` in the incoming code to the deadstab name; the migration already covers players' old saves.

## Versions (owner, 2026-09-30)
- Numbered like **v0.1.0**, with the stage after a dash where the full form shows: **v0.1.0-alpha**. The first release under the new scheme is v0.1.0-alpha (after alpha v0.996).
- `src/version.js`: `VERSION` ('0.1.0'), `STAGE` ('alpha'), `VERSION_FULL`, and `TAB_TITLE` ('deadstab').
- Where each form shows:
  - **the browser tab:** no version at all (`deadstab`);
  - **the title screen and the corner in game:** `v0.1.0` (`.game-version`, set from version.js by main.js);
  - **the full form** `v0.1.0-alpha`: the foot of Settings (`.settings-version`), README's first line, the CHANGELOG heading, package.json's `version`, and the GitHub commit.
- `claude/tools/release.sh` still assumes the old `0.996a` form: bump by hand (version.js, index.html's two `.game-version` texts and Settings line, package.json, README, CHANGELOG) until it is updated.
